## Official REA: core and setup

- [CLI and Evidence](https://github.com/morluto/rea/blob/main/docs/cli.md) — documentation · app, binary, firmware, game, website — CLI workflows for saving Evidence and projecting smaller views from completed analysis. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rea-cli-evidence/))

- [MCP contracts and generated catalog](https://github.com/morluto/rea/blob/main/docs/mcp-contracts.md#generated-catalog) — documentation · app, binary, firmware, game, website — Canonical tool contracts, provider discovery and Evidence semantics for integration authors. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rea-mcp-contracts/))
- [Official reverse-engineer-anything skill](https://github.com/morluto/rea/tree/main/.agents/skills/reverse-engineer-anything) — integration · app, binary, firmware, game, protocol, website — Target routing, connection recovery and evidence practices for agents using REA. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rea-official-skill/))
- [REA — Reverse Engineer Anything](https://github.com/morluto/rea) — project · app, binary, firmware, game, protocol, website — The upstream CLI and MCP server for evidence-based investigations of shipped software and runtime behavior. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rea-upstream/))

<a id="workflow-guides"></a>
## Official REA: investigation guides

- [Browser observation guide](https://rea.tools/guides/browser/) — documentation · website — Follow a user-triggered export request in the supplied Notes browser example and connect it to the downloaded result. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rea-browser-guide/))
- [Cross-layer JavaScript application workflows](https://github.com/morluto/rea/blob/main/docs/javascript-application-workflows.md) — documentation · app, website — Trace features and compare authenticated JavaScript application graphs while preserving static-analysis limits. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rea-js-workflows/))
- [Firmware analysis](https://github.com/morluto/rea/blob/main/docs/firmware-analysis.md) — documentation · firmware — Inspect firmware regions with Binwalk and explicitly extract with Unblob, retaining tool and artifact identity. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rea-firmware-guide/))
- [Flutter build identification](https://github.com/morluto/rea/blob/main/docs/flutter-analysis.md) — documentation · app — Identifies a Flutter APK’s Dart snapshot hash, per-ABI library digests and engine build provenance, then inspects one ABI’s AOT snapshot sections, through REA’s pure-parsing CLI and MCP tools. The string-pool output is a bounded byte-pattern projection of package and SDK URIs, not deserialized Dart classes or recovered source. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rea-flutter-guide/))
- [Historical web network captures](https://github.com/morluto/rea/blob/main/docs/web-network-captures.md) — documentation · app, protocol, website — Inspect saved web captures offline; recorded URLs are not fetched and no proxy is started. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rea-web-captures/))
- [JavaScript and Electron: follow a CSV export](https://rea.tools/guides/javascript/) — documentation · app — A source-owned Notes fixture teaches the renderer, preload, IPC and CSV path with linked analysis evidence. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rea-electron-guide/))
- [Managed-code analysis](https://github.com/morluto/rea/blob/main/docs/managed-code-analysis.md) — documentation · app — Read PE/CLI metadata and CIL, compare managed members and inspect declared native boundaries without loading the assembly. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rea-managed-guide/))
- [MIPS ELF analysis through Ghidra](https://github.com/morluto/rea/blob/main/docs/ghidra-mips.md) — documentation · binary, firmware — Documents REA’s Ghidra lane for MIPS ELF executables, relocatable objects and MIPS16 shared images, including a pinned 32-bit language for recordless legacy firmware executables that Ghidra would otherwise import as 64-bit. Admission is bounded by declared header and ABI-record facts, and verification compares source-owned fixtures with independent readelf output rather than executing or rehosting firmware. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rea-ghidra-mips-guide/))
- [Native binary investigation](https://github.com/morluto/rea/blob/main/docs/native-investigation.md) — documentation · binary, game — Locate a function, inspect instructions and pseudocode, and follow references with a selected native provider. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rea-native-workflow/))
- [NativeAOT metadata recovery with Ghidra](https://github.com/morluto/rea/blob/main/docs/ghidra-nativeaot.md) — documentation · binary — Recovers .NET 8 NativeAOT metadata from x64 PE executables during REA’s Ghidra analysis: MethodTable relationships, virtual slots and frozen strings from RTR 9.1 data, through a built-in read-only parser or an optional headless adapter that annotates the Ghidra database. Generated names, inferred prototypes and recovered native pseudocode stay separate from the unknown original C# names, fields and source. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rea-ghidra-nativeaot-guide/))
- [Packet capture summaries](https://github.com/morluto/rea/blob/main/docs/pcap-analysis.md) — documentation · protocol — Summarize a saved pcap or pcapng file’s endpoints, DNS questions and addresses, TLS SNI, protocol stacks and HTTP URIs into paged Evidence views. It does not capture traffic or fetch recorded targets. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rea-pcap-summaries/))
- [PSP static ELF analysis through Ghidra](https://github.com/morluto/rea/blob/main/docs/ghidra-psp.md) — documentation · binary, game — Documents REA’s bounded Ghidra profile for static PSP ELF executables, which leaves Allegrex instructions, PSP loading and calling conventions to a caller-installed ghidra-allegrex extension while reusing REA’s CLI, MCP, Evidence and snapshot workflows. It admits one PSPSDK header and ABI declaration on Linux x64 and verifies selected static facts on a self-built fixture, not commercial games or PRX modules. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rea-ghidra-psp-guide/))
- [Recorded packet capture inspection](https://github.com/morluto/rea/blob/main/docs/packet-captures.md) — documentation · protocol — Select TShark fields, display filters and decode-as rules over an existing network, USB or Bluetooth capture, and look up installed field names. It does not capture traffic, open an interface or control a device. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rea-packet-captures/))
- [Recorded signal decoding](https://github.com/morluto/rea/blob/main/docs/signal-recordings.md) — documentation · firmware, protocol — Run caller-chosen sigrok protocol decoders, such as UART or I2C, over an existing logic-signal recording and keep annotation text with sample ranges. It does not acquire samples or communicate with hardware. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rea-signal-recordings/))
- [Static Android APK analysis](https://github.com/morluto/rea/blob/main/docs/android-analysis.md) — documentation · app — Inspect Android package identity, classes, methods and references through REA's JADX integration. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rea-android-guide/))
- [Static HarmonyOS package inventory](https://github.com/morluto/rea/blob/main/docs/harmony-analysis.md) — documentation · app — Inventories HarmonyOS HAP, HSP and App Pack files through REA’s hardened ZIP reader and projects an execution-free application graph: component paths, IDs and hashes, Stage/FA packaging hints, runtime-family hints and path-based native-bridge hypotheses. Every claim rests on path presence; manifests, Ark bytecode, resources and signatures are not decoded or verified. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rea-harmony-guide/))

<a id="official-case-studies"></a>
## Official REA: case studies

- [Aegis: trace a login-code calculation](https://rea.tools/showcase/aegis/) — case-study · app — An official APK investigation follows TOTP methods in Aegis and publishes artifact identities and selected Evidence. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rea-aegis-case/))
- [Dinosaur runner: recovering one acceleration rule](https://github.com/morluto/rea/blob/c360cfcd6d7901514b16dd6113eba2e0b0fdf422/website/evidence/calculator-and-dino.md#dinosaur-speed) — case-study · game, website — REA’s documented teaching case inspects the HTTP t-rex-runner edition and reconstructs its speed-update rule. A separate controlled original-game check and a new demonstration compare update counts, including the floating-point step that crosses the nominal speed threshold. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rea-dinosaur-speed-case/))
- [Does reverse engineering need a bigger model? General and specialist models in Touhou reconstruction](https://rea.tools/blog/reverse-engineering-models/) — case-study · binary, game — An essay on REA’s site by N0zoM1z0 (13 October 2026) uses dated Touhou reconstruction commits to ask whether exact function matching needs a larger general model, a compiler-specialist model or better retrieval. It walks through TH09, TH095, TH04 and TH08 cases where later GPT-6-series sessions closed stubborn matches, and the TH08 audit that found accepted functions with wrong floating constants. Specialist and reinforcement-learning comparisons are proposed as future experiments; no trained specialist was evaluated. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rea-bigger-model-essay-case/))
- [DownUnderCTF: masked-squares flag checker](https://rea.tools/showcase/ctf/) — case-study · binary — An official educational challenge investigation connects binary checks to equations and records an original-program validation. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rea-ctf-case/))
- [DX-Ball: reconstruct sound panning](https://rea.tools/showcase/dx-ball/) — case-study · binary, game — Recover one sound-pan function from instructions and constants; upstream cites 3,205 cases and a 63-byte match. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rea-dxball-case/))
- [Notion: trace the clipboard bridge](https://rea.tools/showcase/notion/) — case-study · app — Trace a packaged Electron clipboard path using REA findings and separate source inspection; the dynamic wrapper remains a stated limit. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rea-notion-case/))
- [TH04: recover a DOS bullet-ring helper](https://rea.tools/showcase/th04/) — case-study · binary, game — Inspect a 16-bit PC-98 DOS helper and relate its angle calculation to a pinned reconstruction and historical compiler checks. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rea-th04-case/))
- [Touhou reconstructions: an agent-driven method across three games](https://rea.tools/blog/touhou-reconstruction/) — case-study · binary, game — An essay published on REA’s site, with pinned evidence notes, describes one author’s agent-driven reconstructions of TH08, TH095 and TH04. It dates milestones from public commits and progress ledgers, separates exact-comparison and playable-build oracles, and documents a TH08 check that missed wrong floating-literal data. Project results remain author-reported, and REA is credited with supplying analysis evidence, not the reconstructions themselves. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rea-touhou-reconstruction-method-case/))
- [Windows Calculator: reconstructing percentage rules](https://github.com/morluto/rea/blob/c360cfcd6d7901514b16dd6113eba2e0b0fdf422/website/evidence/calculator-and-dino.md#windows-calculator) — case-study · app, binary — REA’s case traces the installed Windows Calculator model DLL to explain different percentage behavior for multiplication/division and other operations. An analyst-selected function is interpreted against public Microsoft source and demonstrated with fixed browser examples. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rea-calculator-percent-case/))

<a id="community-integrations"></a>
## Community REA integrations

- [App X-Ray](https://github.com/Eliot5566/app-xray) — integration · app, binary — Builds evidence-linked HTML/JSON reports, summary cards and diffs from REA analysis of app packages, native build metadata and model files. Its component and configuration findings come from static inputs; they are not observed runtime traffic, a complete security audit or reconstructed software. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/app-xray-rea-evidence-reports/))
- [Co-op Anything](https://github.com/cranebeaker/coop-anything) — integration · game — Agent playbook, skill set and stdio MCP server for adding online or split-screen co-op to single-player games the players already have installed. Agents either revive an existing co-op mod or reverse engineer the game and engine to build one, test it with in-game bots and send their notes back, while the MCP server searches a directory of 223 co-op projects and 32 open gaps, detects a game’s engine from its install folder and records the executable’s identity. Its README and reverse-engineering skill pair it with REA for native code, and two game write-ups report observations made through REA’s Ghidra lane. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/coop-anything/))
- [crashlens](https://github.com/mingleiw/crashlens) — integration · binary — A crash-triage tool that turns Linux core dumps, runtime panics, exception traces and sanitizer reports into Markdown bug reports naming the crash type, the first frame in the user’s code and a stable signature. Its core-dump path calls the REA CLI to read the recorded crash and the binary layout, and can ask REA’s Ghidra provider for pseudocode of stripped functions; a Claude Code plugin bundles a skill and read-only MCP tools. It is a debugging integration around REA, not a reconstruction case, and its diagnostic accuracy remains author-reported. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/crashlens-rea-crash-triage/))
- [dsh-mcp-rea](https://github.com/Ersith/dsh-mcp-rea) — integration · app, binary, firmware, game, website — A community MCP bridge presents a smaller selected tool surface and forwards explicit commands to the REA CLI. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/dsh-mcp-rea/))
- [Eleven](https://github.com/Zfinix/eleven) — integration · app, binary — An agent-first desktop reverse-engineering app that opens Mach-O, ELF and PE binaries, Android packages and GPU kernels, and lets any Agent Client Protocol agent read code, rename functions, add notes and propose patches in a shared window. Native code is decompiled by Ghidra through a bundled REA build, with Rizin and jadx as alternative engines. It is a workbench around existing engines, not a new decompiler or a reconstruction case. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/eleven-agent-re-workbench/))
- [REA plugin for Hermes Agent](https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-official-plugins/tree/main/rea) — integration · app, binary, firmware, website — Nous Research’s Hermes Agent plugin package for REA. One catalog install registers REA’s MCP server, pinned to rea-agents 6.4.0 and launched through npx over stdio, with REA’s workflow skill adapted for Hermes and a separate crash-triage skill. About 170 REA tools stay behind Hermes’ on-demand tool search, and the adapted skill asks before tools that run programs, drive apps or change a connected Android device. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/hermes-rea-plugin/))
- [REA Reconstruction Companion](https://github.com/ShadowSwaps/REA-Reconstruction-Companion) — integration · app, binary — A third-party Agent Skill that works alongside REA’s own skill to turn reverse-engineering evidence into scoped requirements, design, staged implementation and validation, from one feature or format to a whole app. An optional installer can add a marked routing block to the user’s REA SKILL.md so that relevant reconstruction requests load both skills. Its bundled evaluation kit consists of source-exposed, maintainer-authored examples with literal oracles, not a model benchmark. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rea-reconstruction-companion/))
- [REA Workspace Template](https://github.com/dobest1024/rea-workspace-template) — integration · app, binary, website — A clone-first Chinese workspace template for analyzing one target per workspace with REA from Claude Code or Codex. It ships project-level REA MCP settings, agent rules that require CONFIRMED/GUESS labels with evidence locations, seven task prompts and standard-library scripts that hash imported originals, stage writable copies and strip values from HAR captures. It organizes analysis rather than performing it, and its acceptance run used only synthetic samples. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rea-workspace-template/))
- [rea-casebook](https://github.com/pablodiazjorge/rea-casebook) — integration · binary — A Claude Code mod collects received REA Evidence, tracks unknowns and records findings that cite delivered evidence IDs. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rea-casebook/))
- [rea-jev](https://github.com/juan-viox/rea-jev) — integration · app, website — A Claude Code plugin adds Jev-based routing and evidence checks around a pinned REA MCP integration. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rea-jev/))

<a id="community-labs"></a>
## Community REA labs

- [REA & AI RE Tools Guide (unofficial)](https://reaguide.com/) — learning-resource · app, binary, website — An unofficial, independently run guide to installing REA and connecting it to coding agents, in 32 pages: install paths for npx, npm and Bun including Windows, MCP setup for Claude Code and Desktop, Codex, Cursor, Windsurf and Gemini CLI, a config generator for 24 clients, Ghidra, IDA and JADX provider setup, and troubleshooting pages that match exact error text to upstream REA issues. Several pages record the author's own runs, such as Android APK analysis through headless JADX on rea-agents 6.5.0 and a Claude Code setup re-test on 6.8.0. The site also compares REA with Ghidra, IDA and other reverse-engineering MCP servers and documents look-alike REA copies. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/reaguide-unofficial-rea-guide/))
- [rea-lab](https://github.com/pablodiazjorge/rea-lab) — research · binary — A Windows REA/Ghidra lab with seeded challenges, published studies, upstream contributions and recorded limitations. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rea-lab/))
- [rea-playbook](https://github.com/jinshi666888/rea-playbook) — learning-resource · app — An Electron practice target, Evidence extractor and MCP probe; offline fixture mode is distinguished from real REA analysis. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rea-playbook/))

<a id="community-case-studies"></a>
## Community REA investigations

- [Claude Code background lab: an REA-informed reimplementation of a desktop animation](https://github.com/Henry-fan88/claude-code-background) — case-study · app — Reimplements the glyph-wind background of the Claude desktop app’s Claude Code New session view as an offline WebGL2 page with a 38-slider control panel. The author reports a static REA investigation of the desktop shell and its remote web bundle, followed by a newly written renderer that reuses recovered timing and layout values. It shares the original’s visual vocabulary and interaction model but is not a pixel-identical extraction. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/claude-code-background-lab-case/))
- [Desktop Games Nostalgia: a macOS and browser port](https://github.com/ucodia/desktop-games-nostalgia) — case-study · binary, game — A Swift/macOS and JavaScript/browser port recreates the Desktop Games screen-smashing toy from a captured image. The author documents Claude and REA/Ghidra work on the Peter interpreter, a decompiled program tree and extracted assets, with explicit visual differences and no project license. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/desktop-games-nostalgia-rea-port-case/))
- [Dinosaur runner: an REA-inspected speed rule rebuilt with Codex](https://github.com/zhxunzh84/rea-dino-reverse-engineering) — case-study · game, website — The author uses REA’s MCP browser tools to passively capture the JavaScript loaded by wayou’s open-source T-Rex Runner page, then has Codex rebuild its acceleration rule in one self-contained HTML5 Canvas game. The report identifies the captured script by SHA-256 and separates verified source facts from reconstruction choices; only the speed rule is claimed, not full gameplay equivalence. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rea-dino-runner-community-case/))
- [DiskLens: a WinDirStat-derived disk usage analyzer](https://github.com/KarlssonChristoffer/DiskLens) — case-study · app, binary — A Rust/Tauri disk analyzer combines REA/Ghidra investigation of WinDirStat 2.5.0 with source-code ports of its treemap and color handling. Its documentation distinguishes binary-confirmed findings from source-derived algorithms and leaves several file actions and Windows integrations untested. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/disklens-windirstat-rea-case/))
- [DX-Ball: an REA-guided function-level reconstruction with Codex](https://github.com/N0zoM1z0/dx-ball) — case-study · binary, game — The author has Codex use REA’s Ghidra tools to inspect the English DX-Ball 1.07 executable and recover it as typed C/C++, then compiles each function with the original-era Visual C++ 4.0 and compares it byte for byte with the original after mapping relocations. The same source also builds a MinGW Windows game and a portable core library. Exact reconstruction is still in progress; REA’s separate sound-pan showcase covers just one function. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/dx-ball-rea-reconstruction-case/))
- [FastView-REA: a Rust reconstruction of Fast View 5](https://github.com/dmytrobsp/fastview-rea) — case-study · app — A Rust desktop reconstruction of Mekhanotronika Fast View 5 uses REA and Ghidra research to reproduce oscillogram analysis and relay-characteristic views. The project publishes behavioral specifications, factual indexes, screenshots and cross-platform builds, with explicit format and visual-fidelity gaps. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/fastview-rea-relay-analysis-rebuild-case/))
- [Feishu: an author-reported isolated second app instance](https://benx.ai/blog/posts/rea-reverse-engineer-feishu-dual-instance/) — case-study · app — The author reports using Codex and REA analysis to adapt a separate macOS Feishu copy with its own application identity, data directory and single-instance lock. The account describes local signing and separation checks while retaining the original app unchanged. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/feishu-dual-instance-case/))
- [GunZRs: a Rust reimplementation of GunZ: The Duel](https://github.com/OpenRsG/GunZRs) — case-study · game — A Rust/Bevy port reads GunZ: The Duel files from the user's installation and provides native releases, browser build tooling, screenshots and a documented REA/Ghidra investigation framework. Its LAN protocol and inferred movement behavior remain distinct from retail-server compatibility and proven gameplay parity. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/gunzrs-rea-rust-reimplementation-case/))
- [Horizon: a Rust rewrite of Minecraft: Java Edition 26.3 with REA bytecode queries](https://github.com/ludovic111/horizon) — case-study · game — Rewrites Minecraft: Java Edition 26.3 as a native Rust client, dedicated server and launcher that prepare their resources from the user’s own copy of the game. Contributors are told to read the vanilla code first, and tools/rea_vanilla.py runs a pinned REA release with a headless JADX engine over the shipped client and server jars to inspect classes, methods and callers. Differential harnesses run Java oracles against the real game and Rust probes against Horizon; README reports all 281 passing, which covers only what they compare. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/horizon-minecraft-rust-rewrite-case/))
- [iCSee LocalMod: a stock-firmware camera control extension](https://github.com/Vittuu/icsee-localmod) — case-study · binary, firmware, protocol — An owner-authorized XM530V200 camera case uses REA/Ghidra to investigate native Sofia command handlers, then adds local control and Home Assistant integration while preserving stock drivers and recording. The source-only extension documents separate static, emulated-protocol and physical-device evidence. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/icsee-localmod-rea-camera-case/))
- [keen-clone: a Commander Keen 1 C/SDL2 port](https://github.com/carlhako/keen-clone) — case-study · binary, game — A native Linux and browser port of Commander Keen Episode One is documented as a function-by-function REA/Ghidra reconstruction of KEEN1.EXE. Published analysis artifacts accompany the C/SDL2 game; the scope is the first episode, rather than the entire series. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/keen-clone-rea-commander-keen-case/))
- [Ledger Reborn: an independent accounting app informed by QuickBooks study](https://github.com/lxistired/ledger-reborn) — case-study · app — The maintainer reports using REA and Ghidra to study selected QuickBooks Desktop for Mac behavior, then independently implements a local accounting application. Its Python/SQLite engine persists double-entry journals, invoices, inventory and payments, while public synthetic fixtures test bounded accounting and rollback behavior. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ledger-reborn-accounting-case/))
- [MXQ PRO RK3229: an Android firmware investigation and Linux repurposing](https://github.com/bigtower17/mxqpro-rk3229) — case-study · firmware — An owner documents identifying an RK3229 TV box, investigating its stock Android ROM and repurposing it with a userspace gamepad driver and Armbian. REA assisted ADB inventory and Android-provider routing; APK analysis and device changes are reported as a mixed workflow, not firmware reconstruction. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/mxqpro-rk3229-rea-repurposing-case/))
- [Notes CSV export: an author-reported REA check of reconstruction oracles](https://x.com/i/article/2110962506604929025) — case-study · app — A Chinese article breaks three versions of a Python CSV exporter that reproduces the Notes teaching app shipped with REA. It shows that a simple export-and-read-back test passes all three, while byte comparisons against the official JavaScript formatter on 215 fixed inputs catch each one. The author also connects REA 6.5.0 over MCP, reruns its JavaScript analysis and checks that an edited evidence record is rejected on import; this is a study of acceptance checks, not a new reconstruction. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rea-notes-csv-oracle-mutation-case/))
- [Open Claude UI: REA-informed interface reference components](https://github.com/Caho1/open-claude-ui) — case-study · app — Publishes React components, motion helpers and design-system notes derived from the author’s reported REA investigation of Claude Desktop. It is useful as an interface-reconstruction case, with version-specific observations rather than proof of a complete assistant application or backend clone. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/open-claude-ui-case/))
- [Open Scrantic: reconstructing the Johnny Castaway screensaver engine](https://github.com/glauberlima/open-scrantic) — case-study · app — Reconstructs the Johnny Castaway screensaver engine in Rust, with author-disclosed REA and Claude Code use. It reads original files to run recovered animation scripts and calendar-based story state on modern desktops; documented deviations and limited tests stop short of complete behavioral equivalence. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/open-scrantic-screensaver-case/))
- [OpenBattlefield2: an unfinished Refractor 2 engine reimplementation](https://github.com/Moxnatiy/OpenBattlefield2) — case-study · binary, game — A C++20 reimplementation of Battlefield 2’s Refractor 2 engine reads original game data and documents menu rendering, level loading and server interoperability. Published crosshair research cites REA/Ghidra evidence IDs and binary addresses; major gameplay systems remain unfinished. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/openbattlefield2-refractor-engine-case/))
- [OpenBSD netcat: static REA and Ghidra investigation](https://github.com/NullLabTests/OpenBSD_netcat_REA) — case-study · binary — A REA/Ghidra report studies the Ubuntu netcat-openbsd 1.234-1 ELF binary and publishes evidence envelopes, decompiled functions and a function inventory. Its output is a static investigation, rather than a rebuilt netcat application. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/netcat-openbsd-rea-static-case/))
- [PicoPrint: LX-D02 printer investigation](https://github.com/bigtower17/picoprint) — case-study · app, protocol — The author describes REA-assisted Android investigation followed by separate Hermes decompilation to support a Windows BLE printer. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/picoprint-rea-investigation/))
- [Pocket Tasks: reconstructing task state and persistence](https://github.com/ShadowSwaps/REA-Reconstruction-Companion/tree/main/evals/reference/reconstruction) — case-study · app — A teaching case with disclosed prior source access carries a REA-assisted investigation of a small command-line app into a separate Node implementation. It preserves observed task editing, persistence and selection behavior, then compares scoped outputs against fixed expectations and captured reference sessions. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pocket-tasks-reconstruction-case/))
- [re2ds: a native Nintendo DS engine for Resident Evil 2](https://github.com/eray1605/re2ds) — case-study · game — Reimplements Resident Evil 2’s PS1 game rules in a native Nintendo DS engine, with converters that turn data from the user’s own disc into DS formats. A helper wraps the PS1 executable as an ELF for REA’s Ghidra lane, analysis notes name the PS1 functions and tables each part follows, and the Reddit post linking it credits REA and Claude Code. Leon’s disc 1 is reported playable, while Claire’s disc, most enemy types and some menus remain unfinished. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/re2ds-nds-engine-case/))
- [REA in Claude Code: an isolated static-analysis A/B trial](https://zenn.dev/keigoly/articles/8738d8d241b5a1) — case-study · app, website — A Japanese field report connects REA 6.4.0 to Claude Code in a separate project directory, denies 43 of its 170 tools and common shell launch forms, and compares REA-enabled and REA-disabled runs on two real tasks: an Electron terminal’s packaged files and a saved HAR capture. Both arms answered correctly, while the REA arm took up to 2.00× the time and 1.67× the API-equivalent cost. It is an analysis-evaluation and safety-configuration case, not a reconstruction. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rea-claude-code-isolated-ab-case/))
- [ReaCut: video-editor reconstruction](https://github.com/Xiangyu-CAS/rea-capcut) — case-study · app — An independent video editor whose authors document REA-assisted investigation of Jianying/CapCut behavior and project formats. The public implementation includes a local timeline, FFmpeg export and an experimental, lossy draft importer. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/reacut-reconstruction/))
- [rec-rs: an OBS-informed Rust recorder for macOS](https://github.com/michaelshimeles/rec-rs) — case-study · app — A macOS recorder is informed by OBS architecture research using REA package inspection, platform tools and public source references. It offers screen/camera recording and streaming with a deliberately smaller scene/source feature set; the research notes record unavailable native-analysis providers. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rec-rs-obs-rea-recorder-case/))
- [samp-client-re: a partial SA-MP client reconstruction](https://github.com/NathanKanaeru/samp-client-re) — case-study · binary, game — A documented REA/Ghidra investigation maps SA-MP 0.3.DL-R1 client subsystems to a new C++ DLL, loader and smoke-test executable. The result is an architectural reconstruction with explicitly unresolved network and graphics behavior, not an established retail-compatible client. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/samp-client-rea-partial-reconstruction-case/))
- [Screen Studio: an author-reported REA study of auto-zoom rules](https://x.com/i/article/2109834677343432704) — case-study · app — The author reports using REA to map Screen Studio’s Electron package, reading six auto-zoom and cursor rules from its obfuscated code with Claude Code’s help, and having Claude Code write an independent recorder and renderer from those rules. The article describes the commands, evidence, a cursor-alignment check and the clone’s gaps; neither the clone nor the decoded code is published. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/screen-studio-auto-zoom-case/))
- [sh23ds: a native 3DS reconstruction of Silent Hill 2](https://github.com/ankushKun/sh23ds) — case-study · game — sh23ds rebuilds Silent Hill 2: Director's Cut as a native C engine for Nintendo 3DS using the player's PC game data. Game logic, camera, lighting and events were reconstructed from the PC executable; the research notes report analysis with Ghidra and REA. The author reports a complete playthrough in Azahar, while real-console testing and some PC behaviors remain unfinished. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/sh23ds-case/))
- [Show Your Work: a measured dissection of REA's evidence architecture](https://no-flag.com/2026/10/18/REA-paper/) — case-study · binary — An unofficial field study that reads REA's source and then drives the running server through 1,012 recorded calls across 27 artifact classes, measuring its content-addressed Evidence records, revision-chained unknown ledger, typed refusals, cold starts and response limits. The author maps those mechanisms onto vulnerability-research agents as an argument for not overclaiming. It evaluates REA's tool surface; it is not a reconstruction or a vulnerability finding. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/noflag-rea-evidence-architecture-study/))
- [Sketch: a Paper-shell-informed native Rust canvas](https://github.com/michaelshimeles/sketch) — case-study · app — A Rust canvas models Paper-like editing behavior after REA investigation of the Paper Desktop shell. The hosted editor was not recovered: research separates observed shell integration from inferred editor features, and the new geometric document model differs from Paper HTML/CSS. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/sketch-paper-rea-canvas-case/))
- [Snowball War: a bounded REA-assisted minigame reconstruction](https://github.com/tuunanet/rea-ski-or-die-reconstruction) — case-study · game — The author uses REA’s Ghidra workflow to investigate the DOS Ski or Die binary, document Snowball War rules and rebuild that one minigame in Rust with SDL2. The public notes distinguish recovered tables and mechanics from approximations and deliberate presentation changes. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/snowball-war-rea-case/))
- [Sofa TV Browser: a remote-friendly BrowseHere alternative](https://github.com/sonalder-darlene/sofa-tv-browser) — case-study · app — An Android TV browser follows BrowseHere's remote-control interaction design with its own implementation. The author reports using REA to inspect the BrowseHere APK and Claude to build Sofa; the repository documents APK releases, screenshots and current feature limits. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/sofa-tv-browser-browsehere-case/))
- [SoundConnect Desktop: a Windows client reusing a reviewed headset engine](https://github.com/B-Howell/soundconnect-desktop) — case-study · app, binary — A Windows Sony-headphone client adopts existing libmdr/libmdr-bt engine DLLs after a documented REA-assisted binary inventory and source review. The desktop interface is modeled on Sound Connect, while protocol implementation and device support are inherited rather than newly reconstructed. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/soundconnect-desktop-rea-engine-case/))
- [Terminal: a Rust terminal informed by TradingView chart research](https://github.com/michaelshimeles/terminal) — case-study · app, website — An independent Rust market interface uses REA browser-scenario research on public logged-out TradingView chart interactions. The notes retain observed/documented/inferred/unknown labels; the result supplies its own chart engine and data providers rather than recovering TradingView or Bloomberg server software. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/terminal-tradingview-rea-chart-case/))
- [TicTacToe-AI: a bounded REA-fork reconstruction of one game method](https://github.com/heaven-hm/re-agent/tree/develop/benchmarks/tictactoe) — case-study · game — The author’s REA fork, posted as rea-engine and since renamed RE-Agent, decompiles the shipped .NET executable of the GPL-licensed TicTacToe-AI game, recompiles the recovered C# and resources, and repairs one decompiler error against the original IL. A differential probe runs original and rebuilt code on all 19,683 board assignments for one status method and compares six form renders. The report passes only that method; the whole game remains partial. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rea-engine-tictactoe-case/))
- [UltraSunnyMoon: a partial, REA-led Pokémon Ultra Sun reconstruction](https://github.com/tylergraydev/UltraSunnyMoon) — case-study · game — The author uses a pinned REA build with Ghidra to analyze a user-supplied Pokémon Ultra Sun base executable, rebuild it byte for byte from split objects, and swap in two small compiled C runtime routines. Both replacements are behavior-tested but nonmatching and every other byte stays original; one recorded input movie drives the original and hybrid builds to the same bedroom movement in the Azahar emulator. Retail compiler identity, exact matching, module loading and broader gameplay remain open. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ultrasunnymoon-rea-case/))
- [Untangle: a browser rebuild from REA and Ghidra evidence](https://github.com/az9713/untangle-rebuild) — case-study · binary, game — An author-documented REA/Ghidra investigation of the shipped Untangle Windows binary produces a single-page playable rebuild and a claim-by-claim grading receipt. The report separates frozen findings, a later source-code answer key and a prior-knowledge audit. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/untangle-rea-evidence-rebuild-case/))
- [Virtual Desktop Streamer: rehosting the platform layer on Linux](https://github.com/cudanexus/vd-streamer-linux) — case-study · app — An author-reported Claude Code and REA workflow replaces the macOS streamer’s capture, encoding, audio and input layer with Linux implementations while reusing vendor protocol libraries. The documented result targets desktop streaming and video playback, not Windows-only VR game streaming. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/vd-streamer-linux-case/))
- [Windows loader: static malware analysis with REA and Codex](https://codegeeksai.github.io/en/blog/static-malware-analysis-rea-codex/) — case-study · binary — Fedele Mantuano reports using Codex with REA in an offline Docker lab to statically analyze a public Windows malware sample without executing it. The article separates behavior supported by the extracted JavaScript bundle from claims that would depend on remote components that were never obtained, and reclassifies a suspected infostealer as a loader. It is a defensive analysis case built on an author-reviewed agent report, not a reconstruction or a dynamic detonation. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/windows-loader-static-analysis-case/))

<a id="reconstruction-cases"></a>
## Independent reconstruction cases

- [2 Ship 2 Harkinian: a native Majora’s Mask port built on the decompilation](https://github.com/2ship2harkinian/2ship2harkinian) — case-study · game — Builds native Windows, Linux and macOS versions of The Legend of Zelda: Majora’s Mask from an adapted copy of the zeldaret/mm decompilation, with libultraship reimplementing N64 SDK functions. Game data comes from a user-supplied ROM and is packed into an archive that custom asset packs can override. It targets a playable PC port with enhancements, not a byte-identical ROM. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/2ship2harkinian-mm-port-case/))
- [3S Decomp: a byte-compared PS2 decompilation of Street Fighter III: 3rd Strike](https://github.com/crowded-street/3s-decomp) — case-study · game — Decompiles the PlayStation 2 executable of Street Fighter III: 3rd Strike from Street Fighter Anniversary Collection into C that period Metrowerks and EE GCC compilers rebuild, then compares the rebuilt executable word by word with the user’s original. README reports the game code fully decompiled and the CRI middleware partly so. The separate 3SX native port describes itself as this project’s continuation. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/sfiii-3rd-strike-ps2-decomp-case/))
- [3SX: a native Street Fighter III: 3rd Strike port continuing the PS2 decompilation](https://github.com/crowded-street/3sx) — case-study · game — Builds Street Fighter III: 3rd Strike as native C for Windows, macOS and Linux, continuing the 3s-decomp PS2 decompilation, with SDL3 for video, input and audio and GekkoNet for rollback netplay. README stresses that it is neither a recompilation nor emulation. The port needs the player’s own PS2 game files and works toward arcade-accurate balance, which an optional Statcheck build tests against CPS3 RAM states recorded from Fightcade replays. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/3sx-third-strike-native-port-case/))
- [A.O. Smith water heater: local CTA-2045 control and protocol findings](https://github.com/ericboehs/aosmith-water-heater) — case-study · protocol — The A.O. Smith water-heater project implements local monitoring and demand-response control of an HPS10-50H45DV over its CTA-2045 RS-485 port, publishing readings to MQTT. Its documentation records frame structure, checksums, control responses and observed behavior of this specific tank. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/aosmith-water-heater-case/))
- [a0rfid: an open driver for A0-protocol UHF RFID readers](https://github.com/uaih3k9x/a0rfid) — case-study · protocol — Reconstructs the A0 serial protocol of R2000-family 915 MHz UHF RFID desktop readers, which ship with only Windows software and an Android app, from the vendor app's native command encoder rather than its misleading Java SDK. A userspace CH340 driver, a Python CLI and GUI, and a small Android app then talk to the reader directly for inventory and tag-memory reads. docs/PROTOCOL.md records the frame layout, checksum, command table, response formats and the different field orders of READ and WRITE. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/a0rfid-uhf-reader-protocol-case/))
- [ACiQ K18W: reconstructing a mini split's UART protocol](https://github.com/codypendant/aciq-minisplit-protocol) — case-study · protocol — ACiQ K18W documents the UART protocol between a mini split's indoor board and TCL WBR1 Wi-Fi module. Captures and decoders map 115200 8N1 traffic, 0xA5 frames, CRC-16/XMODEM, acknowledgments, and appliance fields; ESPHome configurations provide passive monitoring or local control after replacing the module. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/aciq-minisplit-protocol-case/))
- [Age of Empires Free Software Remake: a re-implementation on the original data](https://github.com/FolkertVanVerseveld/aoe) — case-study · game — Re-implements Microsoft’s Age of Empires and its Rise of Rome expansion as a cross-platform C++17 engine on SDL2 and OpenGL that loads the original game’s DRS archives and language.dll resources. README says it mimics the Windows API and the Genie engine for compatibility and reconstructs some code from the disassembly; the repository adds format notes and Python tools for inspecting the original data files. Only very basic gameplay through a multiplayer session exists so far, so it is an early engine case rather than a playable replacement. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/aoe-free-software-remake-case/))
- [Agent decompilation: a byte-matching retrospective](https://momo5502.com/posts/2026-10-09-game-decompilation/) — case-study · game — Maurice Heumann describes replacing plausible-looking agent output with compiler-based checks of reconstructed functions, relocations, data and types. The retrospective reports 99% function presence and 83% of all functions byte-exact, while keeping the reconstruction code private. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/agent-decompilation-oracle-case/))
- [ak820: reconstructing AJAZZ AK820 Pro HID control on macOS](https://github.com/gusleig/ajazz-ak820-pro-mac) — case-study · protocol — ak820 implements macOS control of the AJAZZ AK820 Pro keyboard through its vendor HID feature-report protocol. The Python CLI sets the clock, RGB lighting, and sleep timer over USB; accompanying notes describe Windows-driver analysis, packet framing, transaction handshakes, and the unresolved key-remapping commands. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ajazz-ak820-pro-mac-case/))
- [akp02: reconstructing the Ajazz AKP02 display protocol](https://github.com/deonspengler/akp02) — case-study · protocol — akp02 reconstructs the Ajazz AKP02 secondary display's USB HID protocol as a Linux Python library. It documents command report framing and implements full or partial image updates, brightness and power controls, firmware queries, orientation and keepalive. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/akp02-display-protocol-case/))
- [Animal Crossing: a native PC port of the GameCube decompilation](https://github.com/flyngmt/ACGC-PC-Port) — case-study · game — Runs Animal Crossing’s decompiled GameCube C code natively on 32-bit x86, replacing the GX graphics API with an OpenGL 3.3 translation layer and console services with SDL2-based platform code. Game assets are read at startup from the user’s own disc image instead of being shipped. It is a port built on ACreTeam’s ac-decomp, not a matching rebuild of the original executable. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/animal-crossing-pc-port-case/))
- [Animal Crossing: matching GameCube decompilation](https://github.com/ACreTeam/ac-decomp) — case-study · game — Reconstructs Animal Crossing’s GameCube code as C and C++ source that rebuilds the USA release’s main executable and its foresta.rel module. The dtk-template build reads the original binaries and assets from a user-supplied disc image, then checks both outputs against SHA-1 hashes. A separate native PC port builds on it; completeness figures remain project-reported here. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/animal-crossing-gamecube-decomp-case/))
- [Animal Crossing: Wild World: matching Korean DS decompilation with differential runtime checks](https://github.com/banatic/acww-decomp) — case-study · game — Reconstructs the ARM9 game code of Animal Crossing: Wild World’s Korean DS release as one C or C++ source per function, checked against the user’s ROM with the original Metrowerks compiler and linker. Each function also has a readable Clean rewrite, and an included DS interpreter compares the ROM code, natively compiled sources and rewrites on recorded play. Data sections, the ARM7 binary, the linked SDK libraries and regrouping into original source files remain outside the reconstruction. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/animal-crossing-wild-world-decomp-case/))
- [Animal Forest: an in-progress matching N64 decompilation](https://github.com/zeldaret/af) — case-study · game — Rebuilds the Japanese Nintendo 64 release of Animal Forest (Dōbutsu no Mori) from C code reverse-engineered from the ROM, combined with assets and assembly extracted from a user-supplied ROM. Default builds compare the uncompressed and recompressed ROMs with stored MD5 checksums. The README rules out use as a port base or for commercial purposes, and decomp.dev reported 18.52% of code matched at the reviewed commit. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/animal-forest-n64-decomp-case/))
- [AOL 7.0 Retro Server: reconstructing the P3 server session](https://github.com/pioneerman2k1/AOL7-Retro-Server) — case-study · app, protocol — AOL 7.0 Retro Server implements local Python servers for the 2001 AOL 7.0 client. It reconstructs the proprietary P3 login and security exchange, the handoff between login and BOS servers, and post-login data used to display the welcome screen and buddy list. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/aol7-retro-server-case/))
- [ArkWeb: a two-game movement and collision experiment](https://github.com/luki-1/ArkWeb) — case-study · game — Runs Spider-Man Remastered and Arkham Knight together, streaming Gotham collision into Spider-Man’s physics world and compositing his rendered frame into Arkham. This experimental runtime bridge is not a recovered standalone game or new engine. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/arkweb-two-game-case/))
- [Atlantis Native: porting the Atlantis and Atlantis II engines to Rust](https://github.com/ripleyXLR8/atlantis-native) — case-study · game — Atlantis Native ports the reverse-engineered CryOmni3D Atlantis engine to Rust for Atlantis: The Lost Tales and Atlantis II. It reads files from the user's own game copies and runs their scripts, panoramas, cinematics and puzzles on desktop systems and in browsers, with optional display and bug fixes. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/atlantis-native-case/))
- [aw5d-linux: driving an iBUYPOWER cooler LCD from USB-HID research](https://github.com/claygorman/aw5d-linux) — case-study · protocol — aw5d-linux drives the round LCD on an iBUYPOWER AW5/AW5D cooler from Linux using a 64-byte USB-HID output report. Its research notes document report ID 0x10 and byte positions for CPU usage, clock, temperature and gauge styling. The author reports a working display on one 3402:0407 unit; other device variants remain unconfirmed. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/aw5d-linux-case/))
- [Banjo: Recompiled — an N64Recomp Banjo-Kazooie native port](https://github.com/BanjoRecomp/BanjoRecomp) — case-study · game — Uses N64Recomp to statically recompile the US 1.0 Nintendo 64 release of Banjo-Kazooie into a native port on N64ModernRuntime, rendered by RT64 and extended with mod support. Like Zelda 64: Recompiled, it is not built from decompiled source; a symbols repository generated from the Banjo-Kazooie decompilation supplies function names for locating code in the ROM and writing patches. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/banjo-recompiled-case/))
- [BaoCut Design: porting reconstructed canvas and design-system editors](https://github.com/JimLiu/baocut/tree/7aa049e82e1367da013b7aba80f0b79033fa04ad/packages/design-runtime) — case-study · app, website — Documents BaoCut’s design-canvas and design-system editors, which the project says it ported 1:1 from TypeScript reconstructions of Claude Design’s editors and hosts in agent sessions with annotation and export. Its runtime notes record identifier fixes checked against the original scripts and a structural comparison matching 19,111 of 20,463 canvas main-page functions. Unreviewed differences, the unpublished reconstruction record and reuse rights remain open; no hosted-backend recovery is claimed. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/baocut-design-canvas-case/))
- [BaoCut Slides: adapting an interactive web editor](https://github.com/JimLiu/baocut/tree/b8585e0c8d7a82bdee9f0ed019cf6edb075e41c1/packages/slides-runtime) — case-study · app, website — Documents BaoCut’s interactive slide editor, whose author says the implementation draws on Claude Slides. It adds a local deck store, HTML/CSS compiler and annotation-to-agent bridge for browser and desktop hosts; the design reports a Web-flow check, without establishing original backend recovery or full behavioral parity. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/baocut-slides-editor-case/))
- [Batla 3D server emulator (2015 client)](https://github.com/Serega25511s/Batla-3D-server-emu) — case-study · game, protocol — C# server emulator for the 2015 Steam client of Batla 3D, covering HTTP account, shop and room services and Lidgren UDP gameplay. It includes a client-assembly patcher and protocol notes that distinguish recovered client evidence from emulator rules. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/batla-3d-server-emulator-case/))
- [BattleShip: an AI-assisted Super Smash Bros. native port](https://github.com/JRickey/BattleShip) — case-study · game — Ports the existing Super Smash Bros. N64 decompilation using libultraship and Torch. The author’s earlier README describes agent-built graphics/audio comparison tools and differential debugging against emulator behavior, rather than claiming AI recovered the game from a cartridge without prior community work. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/battleship-port-case/))
- [Benro Polaris Firmware Patcher: rebuilding camera control and mapping the app protocol](https://github.com/blaineam/benro-polaris-firmware-patcher) — case-study · app, firmware, protocol — Benro Polaris Firmware Patcher investigates the Polaris camera controller and repacks a user-supplied firmware image with a newer libgphoto2 stack and targeted pgphoto changes. Its documentation maps Benro Connect app behavior and the TCP messages used to control the device. The author reports end-to-end testing with a Canon EOS R5 Mark II. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/benro-polaris-firmware-patcher-case/))
- [bf-private-server: a Brave Frontier revival server](https://github.com/joppe2001/bf-private-server) — case-study · game, protocol — bf-private-server recreates a self-hosted server for Brave Frontier client version 2.19.6. Its C++23 service answers the game's HTTP requests, stores saves in SQLite and works with CDN workers and a patcher for the user's Android client; request tables and boot-flow notes document the protocol investigation. The server builds on an earlier emulator fork. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/bf-private-server-case/))
- [BFM-decomp: Brave Fencer Musashi matching decompilation](https://github.com/Druthulu/BFM-decomp) — case-study · binary, game — Documents an agent-assisted reconstruction of the USA PlayStation game with a pinned compiler and whole-binary SHA-1 gates. The verification recipe covers the main executable, resident engine and disc-loaded code modules, with explicit library and assembly exceptions. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/bfm-decomp-case/))
- [BikeStation: local Echelon Connect control over Bluetooth LE](https://github.com/nathanbrittin/BikeStation) — case-study · protocol — BikeStation is a local Windows client for Echelon Connect exercise bikes built on community research into their proprietary Bluetooth LE protocol. It reads cadence and resistance telemetry, queries the bike's resistance range, writes motorized resistance commands and records rides without the vendor cloud. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/bikestation-case/))
- [bluefluff: reverse engineering Furby Connect's Bluetooth LE protocol](https://github.com/Jeija/bluefluff) — case-study · protocol — Documents the Bluetooth Low Energy interface of Hasbro's Furby Connect toy, worked out, according to its notes, from the companion app's native library, sniffed BLE traffic and trial and error. It maps the GATT services and the command and response bytes of the toy's Nordic radio and GeneralPlus controller, covering action sequences, antenna colour, mood values, sensor reports and a hidden debug screen. fluffd, a Node.js daemon built on the noble BLE library, exposes these commands through an HTTP API and a browser control page. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/bluefluff-furby-connect-ble-case/))
- [BMS RS485 Service Suite: reconstructing a battery-service bus protocol](https://github.com/zonfacter/bms-rs485-service-suite) — case-study · protocol — BMS RS485 Service Suite documents an observed ASCII RS485 protocol for battery-management services and implements frame building and decoding in Node-RED. The specification covers framing, length and checksum fields, request and response rules and decoded service IDs; the wider suite connects JK and DALY BLE data to MQTT and dashboards. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/bms-rs485-service-suite-case/))
- [bosectl: reconstructing Bose’s BMAP headphone protocol](https://github.com/aaronsb/bosectl) — case-study · protocol — Controls Bose headphones from Linux and macOS without the Bose app, account or cloud, through Python, Rust and C++ libraries and command-line tools that implement Bose’s BMAP protocol over Bluetooth RFCOMM. The author documents BMAP’s function blocks, operators, payload layouts and error codes in NOTES.md, reportedly worked out by probing RFCOMM channels, capturing Bluetooth traffic while changing settings in the Bose app and testing each operator, and the libraries cover noise cancellation, EQ, spatial audio, button remapping, profiles and device settings. Support is reported per model by the author and contributors. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/bosectl-bose-bmap-protocol-case/))
- [Bravo: a reverse-engineered Minecraft server](https://github.com/bravoserver/bravo) — case-study · game, protocol — Reimplements the server side of Minecraft’s Alpha- and Beta-era multiplayer in Python on Twisted; its documentation calls it a reverse-engineered implementation written from scratch, credits network traffic dumps and says general Minecraft data went to a community wiki. The final 2.0 release speaks the 1.4.x client protocol for login, chunk transfer, entities, server-side inventories, lighting, physics and Anvil saves, while deliberately changing behaviour such as chunk loading and item stacking. A pluggable architecture adds terrain generation, seasons and commands, and small scripts inspect packets, NBT and region files. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/bravo-minecraft-server-case/))
- [Breath of the Wild: per-function matching decompilation of the Switch executable](https://github.com/zeldaret/botw) — case-study · game — Reimplements part of Breath of the Wild’s Switch v1.5.0 main executable in C++ and checks each function against the original machine code. The project calls itself a hybrid decompilation/reimplementation: it contains no assets, its ELF holds only reimplemented functions and cannot run the game, and porting is an explicit non-goal. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/botw-decomp-case/))
- [Browserbase plugin: re-hosting Claude in Chrome’s browser bridge](https://github.com/browserbase/claude-code-plugin) — case-study · app, protocol — Stands in for the Claude in Chrome extension side of Claude Code’s local browser bridge and runs the requested actions in Browserbase cloud browsers. The author’s thread describes the reverse-engineered framing, a 4-byte little-endian length before each JSON tool call, and the plugin maps 17 named tools onto Stagehand/CDP operations. It is an archived interoperability shim with several placeholder tools, not recovered Anthropic code or a verified equivalent of the extension. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/browserbase-claude-chrome-bridge-case/))
- [Budokai Tenkaichi 3: a native PC port built on a matching decompilation](https://github.com/z3xox/Tenkaichi3Decomp) — case-study · game — Compiles the decompiled code of Dragon Ball Z: Budokai Tenkaichi 3 (PS2, USA release SLUS-21678) for PC on a new renderer, sound, input and platform layer rather than an emulator. The game code comes from the separate BT3-Decompiled matching decompilation, and the game's data is read from the user's own disc image. The README reports the full game loop and experimental online play and says AI helped make the project; these results remain project-reported. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/tenkaichi3-pc-port-case/))
- [BudsPro-Headtracking: streaming Galaxy Buds Pro head tracking over Samsung’s serial protocol](https://github.com/timschneeb/BudsPro-Headtracking) — case-study · protocol — Reimplements the Samsung Galaxy Buds Pro’s proprietary spatial-audio messages in a small Python script so that a PC can stream the earbuds’ head-tracking data in real time. It connects to the earbuds’ GEARMANAGER (or FACTORY) RFCOMM serial service, frames packets with start and end markers and a CRC16-CCITT checksum, enables the sensor stream with attach and two-second keep-alive messages, and decodes the rotation quaternion from the data events. A contributed script converts the quaternion to Euler angles and forwards it over local UDP to a Unity receiver; results are shown only as author-provided sample output. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/budspro-headtracking-case/))
- [c1: a C port of PlayStation Crash Bandicoot](https://github.com/wurlyfox/c1) — case-study · game — Ports the original PlayStation Crash Bandicoot game code to C function by function; its coding standard labels functions with the addresses of their original implementations. Only the PC build, using OpenGL, SDL2 and FluidSynth, currently works; it loads asset files from the user’s game disc and is only partially playable. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/crash-bandicoot-c1-port-case/))
- [C2C: hybrid recompilation and hand decompilation of Crash Bandicoot 2](https://github.com/ughman/c2c) — case-study · game — An early reverse-engineering effort on PlayStation Crash Bandicoot 2 that converts the engine’s MIPS machine code directly into a very large C file and runs it against a modified PCSX-R emulator library for hardware I/O. Translated functions are meant to be decompiled by hand and moved into ordinary C, gradually replacing the mechanical translation. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/c2c-crash2-hybrid-recompilation-case/))
- [Castlevania 64: Recompiled — a beta static-recompilation port](https://github.com/RevoSucks/CV64Recomp) — case-study · game — Uses N64Recomp to statically recompile the US 1.0 Nintendo 64 release of Castlevania into a native PC port rendered with RT64. Game-specific C patches build against headers from a separate Castlevania 64 decompilation. A beta release tagged at the reviewed commit reports a working build with known issues, while the README there still describes an unfinished project. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/cv64-recompiled-case/))
- [Castlevania: Symphony of the Night: matching decompilation across PlayStation, PSP and Saturn builds](https://github.com/Xeeynamo/sotn-decomp) — case-study · game — Recreates C source for Castlevania: Symphony of the Night from its PlayStation, PSP and Saturn binaries, aiming for code that compiles byte for byte to the original game files. The work-in-progress build combines decompiled code with assets and assembly extracted from a user-supplied disc image, then checks per-version SHA-1 hashes. Native PC experiments in the same tree are documented as unfinished and are not a released port. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/sotn-matching-decomp-case/))
- [Cat Printer BLE: reconstructing the MXW01 thermal printer protocol](https://github.com/MaikelChan/CatPrinterBLE) — case-study · protocol — Replaces the vendor phone app for the MXW01 “cat printer”, a small Bluetooth Low Energy thermal printer, with a Windows command-line client. The author reports starting from the iOS-only Moblin implementation and then reverse-engineering the vendor's phone app further, and the client sends CRC8-checked commands to one GATT service while streaming 384-pixel image lines in 1-bit or 4-bit grayscale. Several command IDs remain marked as unknown, and the author tested only this one printer model. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/catprinterble-mxw01-printer-case/))
- [cbyge: reconstructing the C by GE smart-bulb cloud protocol](https://github.com/unixpickle/cbyge) — case-study · app, protocol — Reimplements the cloud control path of the C by GE (now Cync) smart-bulb app as a Go library and a small self-hosted web app. The author reports decompiling the Android app with Apktool, finding its JSON HTTP API for login and device lists and a separate raw TCP protocol to the vendor's server, then working out that protocol's type-and-length message framing and packet encodings from proxied traffic and the Smali code. The resulting API lists devices, reads their status and sets power, brightness, color tone and RGB color. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/cbyge-c-by-ge-protocol-case/))
- [Centerpiece Projects: decoding Finalmouse Centerpiece USB control](https://github.com/LeiterConsulting/finalmouse_centerpiece) — case-study · protocol — Centerpiece Projects documents observed USB control messages for the Finalmouse Centerpiece keyboard and implements scripts around them. Its notes describe HID framing and message types for uploads, status, slot switching and preview chunks; PowerShell and Python helpers expose device operations. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/finalmouse-centerpiece-protocol-case/))
- [CF632BLE: a local client from scale-protocol research](https://github.com/guyru/cf632ble) — case-study · app, protocol — Reimplements the CF632BLE scale’s BLE protocol and body-composition calculations from research on the vendor’s Unique Health Android app. A static Web Bluetooth client receives measurements, manages device profiles and keeps its history in browser storage. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/cf632ble-case/))
- [Chariot: Age of Empires engine reimplementation](https://github.com/ChariotEngine/Chariot) — case-study · game — Chariot is an open-source effort to reimplement the engine of the original Age of Empires (1997). It aims to support ports and enhancements; the README describes it as a work in progress with asset-loading demonstrations, not a playable game. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/chariot/))
- [claude-rc-api: reconstructing Claude Code Remote Control endpoints](https://github.com/ThatCrispyToast/claude-rc-api) — case-study · protocol — claude-rc-api reconstructs the private HTTP interface used to control local Claude Code sessions from claude.ai/code. Its reference derives session endpoints, OAuth authentication, event envelopes, and streaming behavior from the CLI binary; a Python library and CLI use that interface to observe and steer sessions. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/claude-rc-api-case/))
- [Clickwill: reconstructing iPod library database editing](https://github.com/jakubwarzecha00/clickwill) — case-study · app — Clickwill reconstructs direct editing of iPod classic and iPod nano music libraries in a Swift Mac app. It reads and writes iTunesDB and ArtworkDB on the device, preserves unknown record bytes, handles hash58 signatures and publishes a byte map that distinguishes measured fields from uncertain ones. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/clickwill-case/))
- [CoD4 Rewrite: Rust/Bevy reconstruction](https://github.com/Nilbong/COD4-Rewrite) — case-study · binary, game — A Rust/Bevy reconstruction of Call of Duty 4 multiplayer systems, using assets from a local game installation. Source covers menus, local bots, movement and combat, and connects custom relay-based netplay to the game. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/cod4-rewrite-reconstruction-case/))
- [Conker's Bad Fur Day: Recompiled — an N64Recomp native port](https://github.com/sciaschi/CBFD-Recompiled) — case-study · game — Statically recompiles the US Nintendo 64 release of Conker’s Bad Fur Day with N64Recomp into a native Windows, Linux and macOS port on N64ModernRuntime and RT64. Function names and boundaries come from a vendored, still mostly assembly decompilation, while the recompiled code is taken from the user’s ROM so unmatched decompiled C cannot change behavior. The author reports heavy use of Claude Code across the project. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/cbfd-recompiled-case/))
- [cooler-lcd: a Thermalright display protocol implementation](https://github.com/kristjaningi/cooler-lcd) — case-study · protocol — Implements a USB picture-transfer driver for Thermalright Vision cooler displays, with a Rust dashboard replacing the vendor display application. The documented protocol sends a header and JPEG frames, with periodic resends to prevent the device reverting to its logo. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/cooler-lcd-case/))
- [Cooper Hunter AC: UART and infrared protocol investigation](https://github.com/kronhait/cooper-hunter-ac-esphome) — case-study · protocol — Cooper Hunter AC documents the COM1 UART and infrared control messages of CH-IF140RK and CH-IF140RK2 units with a Gree GRZQ3-R V1 board. The author inferred UART status fields by comparing packets while operating the infrared remote. The resulting ESPHome design receives UART status and sends control through an infrared signal path. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/cooper-hunter-ac-protocol-case/))
- [CorsixTH: an open reimplementation of Theme Hospital](https://github.com/CorsixTH/CorsixTH) — case-study · game — Reimplements the engine of Bullfrog’s 1997 Theme Hospital, with game logic in Lua and C++ for graphics, sound, movies and pathfinding, on top of the original game’s data files. The README reports that the full campaign can be completed, lists features that are still missing, and adds extensions such as high resolutions and a map editor. It recreates the game’s behaviour on original content rather than recovering Bullfrog source code. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/corsixth-engine-case/))
- [Cosmic Byte Firestorm Controller: a USB protocol client for mouse settings](https://github.com/electrondefuser/cbfirestorm) — case-study · protocol — Cosmic Byte Firestorm Controller implements a Linux client for the mouse's recovered USB settings protocol. Its Python CLI sends control and interrupt transfers to configure lighting effects, per-DPI indicator colours and sensitivity levels, while PROTOCOL.md records packet structures and command sequences. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/cosmic-byte-firestorm-controller-case/))
- [Cosmo: a native port of Cosmo's Cosmic Adventure](https://github.com/digows/cosmo) — case-study · game — Cosmo ports the reconstructed source of Cosmo's Cosmic Adventure: Forbidden Planet to macOS, Linux and Windows. It retains the game's logic and supplies an emulated EGA adapter, timer, keyboard controller, PC speaker and AdLib layer for the original code. The build uses the user's game data files. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/cosmo-native-port-case/))
- [CPQ IO Scheduler: reconstructing Xiaomi's block scheduler](https://github.com/Rasenkai/CPQ_IOSched) — case-study · binary — CPQ IO Scheduler reconstructs Xiaomi's proprietary cpq.ko Linux block scheduler from a stripped ARM64 kernel module. Its out-of-tree C implementation combines priority classes, thread-group queues, deadline handling, and blk-mq elevator integration, with a whitepaper and author-reported benchmarks. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/cpq-iosched-case/))
- [CPS-2 Recomp: static recompilation of Capcom CPS-2 and CPS-1 arcade games](https://github.com/tmcleroy/cps2recomp) — case-study · game — Translates the 68000 game programs and Z80 sound programs of Capcom arcade boards ahead of time into C++ and runs them on a machine model written for the project, with an interpreter for code that has not been translated. One launcher covers Street Fighter Alpha and Alpha 2, Super Puzzle Fighter II Turbo, Vampire Savior and its variants, Vampire Hunter 2, Alien vs. Predator and the CPS-1 Cadillacs and Dinosaurs, adding training modes, widescreen and three-player options. Translated code is generated from the user’s own ROM sets, and a check build compares it block by block with the interpreter. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/cps2recomp-arcade-static-recomp-case/))
- [Crash Bandicoot 2 Recompiled: a PSXRecomp native build](https://github.com/Zumbo06/Crash2Recomp) — case-study · game — A Windows launcher that uses the PSXRecomp recompiler and runtime to translate the user’s Crash Bandicoot 2: Cortex Strikes Back disc to C and compile it locally, so the game runs as native code with an emulated PlayStation hardware layer. The repository holds the launcher, a patched vendored runtime and a long bring-up log; it distributes no game code or data. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/crash2recomp-case/))
- [CTR Turbocharged: a PC sourceport built on the Crash Team Racing decompilation](https://github.com/CameronRedmore/Crash-Team-Racing-Turbocharged) — case-study · game — Extends the Crash Team Racing: High Octane sourceport, which derives from the CTR Native port of the CTR-ModSDK PlayStation decompilation, into Windows and Linux builds with a native 3D renderer, selectable aspect ratios and frame rates up to 240 FPS. The decompiled game code still assumes 32-bit pointers and loads assets from the player’s own NTSC-U disc image. Renderer, timing and option tests exist, but device coverage and fidelity remain project-reported. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ctr-turbocharged-port-case/))
- [Cursed Lands: a Godot engine for Evil Islands and Lost in Astral](https://github.com/TheWWWorm/CursedLands) — case-study · game — Cursed Lands reimplements the engine for Evil Islands and Lost in Astral in Godot, reading maps, models, audio, scripts and tables from the player's own game copy. Its native rendering, combat, AI, scripting and interface systems follow the original game's rules, with releases for desktop, Android and browsers. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/cursed-lands-case/))
- [d2mac-linux: porting the Diablo II Mac executable to 32-bit Linux](https://github.com/jaenster/d2mac-linux) — case-study · binary, game — d2mac-linux ports the Diablo II 1.14d Mac executable to 32-bit Linux by converting its i386 Mach-O container to ELF and reimplementing imported Darwin, Carbon and AGL interfaces in Zig. The shim translates graphics to GLX and adapts threading, files and sockets. The author reports that the game reaches its menu and character creation. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/d2mac-linux-case/))
- [D2R Mobile: rehosting Diablo II: Resurrected’s Switch program on Apple devices](https://github.com/jhaizhou-ops/d2r-mobile) — case-study · game — Runs the Nintendo Switch release’s own aarch64 program natively on iPhone, iPad and Apple silicon Macs, rewriting only instructions that depend on the Horizon kernel and supplying its system services and the NVN graphics API on Metal. Shaders are translated offline from the user’s files, and the project adds its own touch interface and endgame features. It is a rehosting compatibility layer, not a decompilation or recompiled source. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/d2r-mobile-switch-rehost-case/))
- [DABplay+: reconstructing USB DAB+ dongle control](https://github.com/jvspier/dabplayplus) — case-study · protocol — DABplay+ implements an Android DAB radio using a reverse-engineered USB dongle protocol. It drives the DTR-100 family directly, decodes broadcast data and audio, and documents additional dongle protocols alongside hardware findings and tests. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/dabplayplus-case/))
- [ddg-tenor-bridge: replacing Gboard's Tenor search endpoint](https://github.com/hxreborn/ddg-tenor-bridge) — case-study · app — ddg-tenor-bridge redirects Gboard's Tenor GIF searches to a service that returns DuckDuckGo animated-image results in Tenor v2 response form. Its documented workflow changes Gboard's persisted search URL flag while leaving other Tenor features on their original endpoint. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ddg-tenor-bridge-case/))
- [Dead Rising 2: Case West — a sibling Xbox 360 XBLA static recompilation port](https://github.com/wivi514/Dead_Rising_2_Case_West_Xenon_Recomp) — case-study · game — Builds a native Windows, Linux, Steam Deck and Apple Silicon port of the XBLA epilogue Dead Rising 2: Case West by statically recompiling its Xbox 360 code with patched XenonRecomp and translating its shaders with patched XenosRecomp. It shares most of its runtime with the author’s separate Case Zero port, executing the console GPU command stream on Vulkan with XMA audio and keyboard/mouse input. Completion and platform results are project-reported. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/dead-rising-2-case-west-recomp-case/))
- [Dead Rising 2: Case Zero — an Xbox 360 XBLA static recompilation port](https://github.com/wivi514/Dead_Rising_2_Case_Zero_Xenon_Recomp) — case-study · game — Builds a native Windows, Linux, Steam Deck and Apple Silicon port of the XBLA game Dead Rising 2: Case Zero by statically recompiling its Xbox 360 code with patched XenonRecomp and translating its shaders with patched XenosRecomp. A purpose-built runtime supplies a Vulkan renderer, XMA audio, keyboard/mouse input and online co-op through the author’s XenonLive service. Completion and platform results are project-reported. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/dead-rising-2-case-zero-recomp-case/))
- [DeckCraft: presentation workflow reconstruction and bounded PPTX interchange](https://github.com/storytold/deckcraft) — case-study · app — The authors describe DeckCraft as a clean-room Rust implementation of PowerPoint-style presentation workflows. Its slide model, rendering and command layers support desktop/browser editing and PPTX interchange; reviewed source tests check constructed decks and selected preserved package parts. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/deckcraft-case/))
- [Dell ControlVault 2 fingerprint on Linux: adapting a driver for BCM5880](https://github.com/grosa787/dell-controlvault2-fingerprint-linux) — case-study · binary, protocol — Dell ControlVault 2 fingerprint on Linux adapts a locally obtained Broadcom driver to the older BCM5880 fingerprint reader through five signature-matched byte patches. The accompanying investigation maps the device’s USB endpoints, command header and driver checks. The author reports working capture, while enrollment and matching remain unreliable or unconfirmed. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/dell-controlvault2-fingerprint-linux-case/))
- [Destiny 2 build 86657: Sunrise/Dawn reconstruction research report](https://github.com/isinternets/d2-research) — case-study · game, protocol — A dated research report on the Sunrise/Dawn reconstruction of the archived Destiny 2 build 86657 client, which emulates platform sign-on and the examined Bungie service contracts locally and adds reconstructed mission host policy. It records transport, decoding, object creation, presentation and gameplay results separately, and keeps superseded conclusions with their cited sources. It documents the case rather than shipping the runtime implementation or a recovered Bungie backend; playthroughs and native results remain project-reported. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/destiny2-build-86657-reconstruction-report-case/))
- [Devil May Cry: an experimental PS2 static recompilation](https://github.com/Gui-Tora/dmc-recomp) — case-study · game — dmc-recomp translates the PlayStation 2 PAL release of Devil May Cry into native PC code through PS2Recomp and a project runtime. Its pipeline rebuilds generated code locally from the user’s own game executable, while research notes and patches track boot, CD services, MPEG playback, rendering and gameplay behavior. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/dmc-recomp-case/))
