- [DevilutionX: porting Diablo’s reconstructed engine](https://github.com/diasurgical/DevilutionX) — case-study · game — Builds a modern Diablo and Hellfire port on the Devilution reconstruction, with platform support, bug fixes and optional quality-of-life features. The project documents the original symbol-assisted reconstruction and includes selected dungeon-generation and saved-game regression checks. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/devilutionx-case/))
- [DiabloWeb: a WebAssembly browser port built on Devilution](https://github.com/d07RiV/diabloweb) — case-study · game — Runs Diablo in web browsers by compiling a stripped-down fork of Devilution, the community reconstruction of the game’s original source, to WebAssembly behind a React front end. The fork removes the Storm, DiabloUI, DirectX and Windows dependencies and reworks event handling for the browser. It is a port built on a reconstruction and reports no matching or behavior-equivalence checks of its own. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/diabloweb-devilution-wasm-port-case/))
- [DJI MIMO BLE artifacts: reconstructing the DJI Osmo control protocol](https://github.com/xaionaro/reverse-engineering-dji) — case-study · protocol — Documents the Bluetooth Low Energy message format that DJI's proprietary MIMO app uses to control DJI Osmo devices, as artifacts produced while building the independent djictl CLI. The author derives the framing — a magic byte, length, protocol version, header CRC8, subsystem and message-id fields, a payload and a message CRC16 — from the author's own Android BLE logs, and ships a Wireshark dissector plus an annotated catalog of message types. Many message types are labelled as guesses or unknown; the author reports reaching working livestreaming on a DJI Osmo Pocket 3 and pausing further analysis. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/dji-mimo-ble-reveng-case/))
- [DKC1Recomp: static recompilation of Donkey Kong Country](https://github.com/elliotttate/DKC1Recomp) — case-study · game — Statically recompiles the North American v1.0 SNES release of Donkey Kong Country into native Windows, macOS and iOS applications with a pinned fork of snesrecomp, which translates 65816 code to C and links it against a modelled SNES hardware runtime. Function boundaries, entry flag states and jump-table targets are ingested from a byte-identical community disassembly instead of discovered heuristically, and the game code is generated locally from the user’s verified ROM. Optional widescreen, CRT filters, MSU-1 music and a Dixie Kong Country variant are host-side additions to what the README calls a playable bring-up. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/dkc1-recomp-case/))
- [DLSSNR-AMD: reimplementing DLSS 5 neural rendering for RDNA4](https://github.com/mochizuki0323/DLSSNR-AMD) — case-study · binary, game — Reimplements the neural-rendering network of NVIDIA’s DLSS 5 library as Vulkan compute shaders for AMD RX 9000 (RDNA4) cards and supplies it to games through an NGX-compatible core and ReShade or Vulkan-layer routes. The weights are not distributed: installers extract them, hash-checked, from the user’s own nvngx_dlssnr.dll 310.9.16 or 310.8.0. The project compares its output with NVIDIA’s DLL on an RTX 5090 and reports close, not bit-identical, pictures. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/dlssnr-amd-neural-rendering-case/))
- [DOAXVB Native PC Port: statically recompiling Dead or Alive Xtreme Beach Volleyball](https://github.com/NoRain211/doaxvb-re) — case-study · game — Lifts the original Xbox executable of Dead or Alive Xtreme Beach Volleyball to C with a fork of xboxrecomp and links it with a hand-written runtime that replaces kernel imports, audio and key Direct3D 8 and DirectSound APIs. Setup builds the runner locally from the user’s USA disc image and checks generated files against hashes in an authenticated recipe; releases carry only tools and source. Rewriting game logic as readable source has so far reached only the startup thread entry. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/doaxvb-native-port-case/))
- [Donkey Kong 64: Recompiled — an N64Recomp native port](https://github.com/Rainchus/Donkey-Kong-64-Recompiled) — case-study · game — Uses N64Recomp to statically recompile the US Nintendo 64 release of Donkey Kong 64 into a native port, with RT64 as the renderer. The port is not built from decompiled source, but borrows headers and some functions from the in-progress DK64 decompilation for patches and enhancements. Its recompiler configuration reads a checked-in symbol dump and the ROM rather than a decompilation ELF. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/dk64-recompiled-case/))
- [Driver Recompiled: a psxrecomp setup host that builds the game locally](https://github.com/alexbeavs-ps1-ports/driver-recomp) — case-study · game — Packages a psxrecomp static-recompilation project for Driver (PlayStation, USA) as a setup host: releases carry the recompiler, runtime and a recomp-ui launcher, and the player’s machine generates and compiles the game from the user’s own disc image. The repository holds configuration, Ghidra-derived function seeds and release automation rather than game code. Its documented status is thin: an operator-confirmed private build reaches gameplay, while package release gates remain open. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/driver-recomp-setup-host-case/))
- [Dusklight: a native Twilight Princess port built on the decompilation](https://github.com/TwilitRealm/dusklight) — case-study · game — Reimplements Twilight Princess as a native application for desktop and mobile platforms, building on the zeldaret/tp decompilation and the Aurora GameCube/Wii compatibility layer. It runs from the user’s own disc image with no bundled assets and aims to stay accurate to the original while adding options, enhancements and mods. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/dusklight-twilight-princess-port-case/))
- [EchoMuse: replacement software that turns the Echo Dot 2nd gen into a Home Assistant voice satellite](https://github.com/wilbowes/EchoMuse) — case-study · firmware — Replaces the software on an unlocked second-generation Amazon Echo Dot (2016, codename biscuit) so it works as a local Home Assistant voice satellite over the ESPHome API: EchoMuse's own Go firmware drives the microphones, speaker, LED ring and buttons, and a controller on the LAN handles wake words, devices and a dashboard. Its default emOS mode swaps Amazon's Android userspace for the project's own init and busybox while keeping the Dot's kernel from the owner's boot image. An engineering journal records how codec routes, the mute key and other hardware behavior were worked out by measuring the device and diffing the mixer and DAPM state while stock FireOS plays a sound. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/echomuse-echo-dot-voice-satellite-case/))
- [Elgato HDR: a userspace capture path for the Elgato 4K X’s 10-bit mode](https://github.com/talkaboutdesign/elgato-hdr) — case-study · app, protocol — A native macOS app that reaches the Elgato 4K X capture card’s 10-bit P010 video mode, which the project says Apple’s built-in webcam driver does not expose to apps. A root helper detaches that driver and runs the card’s USB Video Class streaming directly through libusb, feeding a Metal HDR viewer, an HDR10 recorder and an editor. Latency figures and device coverage remain author-reported. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/elgato-hdr-uvc-capture-case/))
- [epson-l3110-driver: investigating the printer's USB and ESC/P-R exchanges](https://github.com/mikel-btw/epson-l3110-driver) — case-study · protocol — epson-l3110-driver investigates the Epson L3110's USB printing exchanges while developing a Linux driver in C. Its notes use captured print-job traffic to identify the IEEE 1284 device ID, EJL session setup and ESC/P-R command framing. USB communication is still in progress, and the driver cannot yet print pages. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/epson-l3110-driver-case/))
- [ESPHome ↔ L&S Emotion Control: reconstructing a smart-light radio client](https://github.com/SergeyZh/emotion-control-ha) — case-study · protocol — An ESPHome component recreates L&S Emotion Control remote commands so an ESP32 with a CC2500 radio can control furniture lights and reflect physical remote presses in Home Assistant. Its documented radio configuration uses MSK at 2436.2 MHz, a sync word, and 14-byte payloads carrying the remote ID, command, and value. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/emotion-control-ha-case/))
- [Eufy Local Light: reconstructing BLE control for E10 and E22 lights](https://github.com/SudoSaturn/eufy-local-ha) — case-study · protocol — Eufy Local Light implements a Home Assistant client for Eufy Outdoor String Lights E10 and Permanent Outdoor Lights E22 using BLE traffic analyzed from the Eufy app. Its protocol notes describe GATT endpoints, packet framing, AES-128-CBC session setup, and light-control commands. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/eufy-local-ha-case/))
- [Even G2 protocol: reverse-engineering Even Realities G2 smart-glasses BLE traffic](https://github.com/i-soxi/even-g2-protocol) — case-study · protocol — Documents the Bluetooth Low Energy protocol between the Even app and Even Realities G2 smart glasses, worked out from captured traffic: GATT characteristic UUIDs, an 8-byte transport header with sequence, multi-packet and two-byte service-ID fields, a CRC-16/CCITT over the payload, and protobuf payloads with draft .proto definitions. A Python example built on bleak sends the seven-packet session handshake and drives the teleprompter service to show custom text. The README’s status table marks connection, handshake, teleprompter and calendar widget as working, notifications as partial, and Even AI and navigation as still under research. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/even-g2-ble-protocol-case/))
- [FAAC E145 Gate Connect: reconstructing EasyBoard gate commands](https://github.com/jens62/FAAC-E145-Gate-Connect) — case-study · protocol — FAAC E145 Gate Connect implements local control of an FAAC E145 gate through serial commands reconstructed from EasyBoard software traffic. The documentation lists POLL, OPEN, CLOSE and STOP byte sequences and describes their framing and checksum. The Python gateway adds position monitoring plus web, REST and MQTT interfaces. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/faac-e145-gate-connect-case/))
- [FFTARecomp: static recompilation of Final Fantasy Tactics Advance](https://github.com/Jorge262007/FFTARecomp) — case-study · game — Recompiles the user’s Final Fantasy Tactics Advance (USA) Game Boy Advance ROM ahead of time into native code for Windows, Linux and Steam Deck, building on JRickey’s gba-recomp toolkit. ARM and Thumb code is translated to C and compiled locally, while a Rust runtime models the console’s graphics, sound, DMA, timers and BIOS calls. The port adds widescreen, zoom, fast-forward and remappable controls. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/fftarecomp-gba-static-recomp-case/))
- [FH2MP: reconstructed Forza Horizon 2 online services](https://github.com/Charelene/FH2MP) — case-study · game, protocol — FH2MP restores Forza Horizon 2 multiplayer with a replacement server based on protocol behavior recovered from the Xbox One game client. An in-game shim redirects sockets and web-service calls, while Python and Go servers handle lobbies, worlds and online features; a launcher manages setup and updates. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/fh2mp-case/))
- [Final Fantasy VII: a matching PlayStation decompilation](https://github.com/Xeeynamo/ff7-decomp) — case-study · game — Reconstructs C source for the US PlayStation release of Final Fantasy VII, aiming for code that compiles back to the original main executable and its game overlays. The build splits the user’s disc files with splat, compiles with period cc1-psx compilers and checks every rebuilt file against its SHA-1. A native build against the PsyZ PlayStation-library replacement is compiled in CI but is not documented as a playable port. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ff7-ps1-matching-decomp-case/))
- [Firefighter: a decompiled DOS game ported to Rust](https://gitlab.com/esr/firefighter) — case-study · game — Eric S. Raymond’s terminal port of Firefighter, Eben Sprinsock’s 1985 DOS shareware game. The project history says ChatGPT 5.5 decompiled the DOS binary to Borland Pascal and translated it to Rust, after which Raymond modernized the interface and corrected a scoring mismatch. Unchanged gameplay is the author’s statement; the intermediate Pascal and any comparison runs are not published. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/firefighter-rust-port-case/))
- [fitdays: reconstructing a smart-scale cloud client](https://github.com/AboveColin/fitdays) — case-study · protocol — fitdays recreates read-only access to the Fitdays smart-scale cloud API from traffic observed in the vendor app. Its asynchronous Python client logs in with the service's password digest, follows regional redirects, renews sessions, and retrieves scale, profile, and body-composition measurement records. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/fitdays-protocol-case/))
- [flipper-telink: replacement firmware for Tuya Zigbee remotes](https://github.com/ngxson/flipper-telink) — case-study · firmware — A Flipper Zero app that bit-bangs Telink’s SWire debug interface to dump and reflash TLSR825x Zigbee devices, with static-analysis notes on the dumps and freshly written firmware for two of them. For Tuya TS0044/TS0046 scene remotes, the notes trace the stock ~500 ms single-click delay to a double-click window; the replacement firmware drops that window while presenting the stock model IDs to Zigbee2MQTT. The latest Zigbee build is marked unverified for pairing, actions and battery reporting. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/flipper-telink-tuya-remote-firmware-case/))
- [Ford Fiesta MK5 MS-CAN: decoding the dashboard and radio bus](https://github.com/roncapat/Ford-Fiesta-MK5-MS-CAN-bus) — case-study · protocol — Documents the medium-speed CAN bus that links the 6000CD radio and the dashboard of a restyled (post-2006) Ford Fiesta MK5, worked out by tapping the bus at the radio's QuadLock connector with a Seeed CAN-BUS Shield on an Arduino Uno. The README describes 18 partly or fully decoded message IDs on the 125 kbit/s bus, with byte layouts and scaling formulas: radio text sent to the dashboard LCD over ISO-TP, station names, volume, CD track and steering-column mode and seek buttons, as well as broadcast clock, door, lock, reverse-gear, indicator, light, handbrake, airbag-lamp, pedal, RPM, speed, coolant, key-position, battery and partial VIN data. An incomplete Kayak .kcd definition encodes only the clock and gas-pedal/RPM/speed messages. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ford-fiesta-mk5-ms-can-case/))
- [FreeRS42: reimplementing Ricochet Xtreme](https://github.com/NuVelocity/FRS42) — case-study · game — FreeRS42 is a C++ project to reimplement the PC game Ricochet Xtreme using the NuVelocity engine. The repository contains source for game objects, playfield behavior and screens, with a CMake build that includes the engine submodule. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/freers42-ricochet-xtreme-case/))
- [fuji-cam-wifi-tool: reconstructing Fujifilm X-series Wi-Fi remote control](https://github.com/hkr/fuji-cam-wifi-tool) — case-study · protocol — Reimplements the Wi-Fi remote-control protocol of Fujifilm X-series cameras as a C++ library and an interactive command-line client that joins the camera's access point. Its headers name the control, event and JPEG live-view ports, message types and camera property codes, and source comments keep hex traces of exchanges along with what "the app" sends; the client connects, fires the shutter, records video, adjusts exposure settings and focus point, and streams live view, optionally into a Linux webcam device. README calls the tool very limited and mostly undocumented, so the protocol knowledge lives in code and comments rather than a specification. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/fuji-cam-wifi-tool-protocol-case/))
- [Galaxy Buds Client: an unofficial manager built on Samsung’s earbud protocol](https://github.com/timschneeb/GalaxyBudsClient) — case-study · app, protocol — Reimplements control of Samsung Galaxy Buds over their proprietary RFCOMM serial protocol in a cross-platform desktop and Android manager. The author reports recovering the message format by analyzing the earbuds’ binary stream and disassembling Samsung’s official Android apps, and publishes partial packet notes for the 2019 Buds and the Buds+. Beyond the official app’s settings, it exposes battery statistics, diagnostics, factory self-tests, hidden debug information and firmware flashing for supported models. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/galaxy-buds-client-protocol-case/))
- [game-and-watch-patch: Nintendo Game & Watch custom firmware](https://github.com/BrianPugh/game-and-watch-patch) — case-study · firmware, game — Custom firmware for the Mario and Zelda Game & Watch consoles that patches new C code into the user's own stock firmware backups, so the original firmware runs alongside the retro-go emulator. A Python patcher hooks stock function calls, relocates and LZMA-compresses stock code and data to free flash, and can merge both models' firmware into one image. Committed symbol maps, a cross-reference mapper and an analysis-ELF builder support the reverse engineering, and a journal records the author's first firmware-patching work. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/game-and-watch-patch-cfw-case/))
- [GAMEKB-K17: reconstructing a macropad configuration protocol](https://github.com/dvdarkin/gamekb-k17) — case-study · protocol — Implements the vendor HID configuration protocol for the GAMEKB-K17/HCY-K017 macropad. It writes key mappings, firmware macros and RGB settings, then reads them back; protocol notes separate tested commands from unconfirmed observations and other-device findings. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/gamekb-k17-case/))
- [GameTrak Liberation: a client for the controller's HID protocol](https://github.com/CreativeInquiry/GameTrak-Liberation) — case-study · protocol — GameTrak Liberation implements a client for the GameTrak controller's reverse-engineered USB HID behavior. It sends the initialization and rolling-key keepalive messages needed for sustained reports, then exposes six-axis data through stdout, OSC, MIDI or WebSockets. Protocol notes, report captures and Processing and p5.js examples accompany the Python tools. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/gametrak-liberation-case/))
- [garmin-ble: a Python client for Garmin's GFDI watch protocol](https://github.com/gwerneckp/garmin-ble) — case-study · protocol — Python library that talks to Garmin watches over their proprietary GFDI V2 Bluetooth LE protocol, with no Garmin Connect, phone or cloud service. It implements the handshake, multi-link routing, COBS framing, CRC16 checks and protobuf service messages, streams typed readings such as heart rate, HRV, SpO2, stress, Body Battery and accelerometer data, and adds a simulator and recorded-session replay for work without hardware. Its acknowledgements credit Gadgetbridge for the protocol logic and schemas, while a published journal shows how the author decoded the accelerometer payload, which Gadgetbridge only logged, from labelled recordings. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/garmin-ble-gfdi-protocol-case/))
- [Ghostship: a libultraship native port of Super Mario 64](https://github.com/HarbourMasters/Ghostship) — case-study · game — Builds Super Mario 64 as a native PC, Switch and Android program by compiling decompilation-style game source against the libultraship platform library. A bundled extractor converts the user’s US or Japanese ROM into an O2R archive at first launch or build time. The project ships modern renderers, configuration menus and mod-archive loading, not a matching ROM. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ghostship-sm64-port-case/))
- [God of War PS2 static recompilation: a native Windows port via PS2Recomp](https://github.com/KIexster/god-of-war-recomp) — case-study · game — Statically recompiles the MIPS R5900 code of the NTSC-U PlayStation 2 God of War executable (SCUS-97399) to C++ with PS2Recomp and builds it as a native Windows program. Since October 2026 the port builds on Waffle565’s runtime, which replaces the PS2 hardware with native code: VU1 microprograms and, optionally, the game’s IRX modules are compiled ahead of time, and an OpenGL renderer stands in for the GS. The repository holds only the game-specific function map, recompiler configuration, overrides and runtime patches; the README reports gameplay on the opening ship level, with sustained 60 fps and a full playthrough still pending. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/god-of-war-ps2-recomp-case/))
- [GoldenEye 007: an agent-assisted native port from a decompilation](https://github.com/jkdansereau/goldeneye-pc-port) — case-study · game — Builds a native PC port on the existing GoldenEye 007 decompilation, with a host platform layer and assets extracted from the user’s ROM. The project documents agent collaboration, rendering comparisons, playtest scope and unresolved platform support. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/goldeneye-pc-port-case/))
- [GoldenEye 007: matching Nintendo 64 decompilation for three releases](https://github.com/n64decomp/007) — case-study · game — Reconstructs GoldenEye 007’s Nintendo 64 code as C source that rebuilds the US, Japanese and PAL ROMs. Assets come from a user-supplied US ROM, with Japanese and PAL differences taken from their own ROMs, and the default build compares each output with a recorded SHA-1. The README still calls the project work in progress, while a mutable status page reports all 208 files done; it is distinct from the separately curated PC port. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/goldeneye-007-matching-decomp-case/))
- [Gran Turismo 4: an agent-assisted PS2 engine matching decompilation](https://github.com/gustavocanepa/gt4-decomp) — case-study · game — Decompiles Gran Turismo 4’s PS2 engine executable (CORE.GT4, NTSC-U v1.01) into one C or C++ source per function for Sony’s ee-gcc 2.96, matching public libraries such as newlib and Expat from their own source. The README credits AI agents (Anthropic’s Claude) driving the project’s tools with most of the matching, each function accepted only on a compiler-and-judge comparison, and reports 95.4% of functions and 83.6% of code bytes matched for an October 2026 commit. A local full build links sources at their original addresses, fills the rest from disassembly and compares SHA-1 with the user’s executable. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/gran-turismo-4-matching-decomp-case/))
- [GridCraft: spreadsheet workflow reconstruction and XLSX round-trip limits](https://github.com/storytold/gridcraft) — case-study · app — The authors describe GridCraft as a clean-room Rust implementation of Excel-style spreadsheet workflows. A workbook model, formula parser and dependency-driven recalculation are connected to XLSX I/O and a shared editing-command layer for desktop, browser and automation interfaces. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/gridcraft-case/))
- [grsimwrite-mac: reconstructing GRSIMWrite for macOS](https://github.com/eliel9012/grsimwrite-mac) — case-study · app, protocol — grsimwrite-mac reconstructs the Windows GRSIMWrite 4.4.10 SIM-writing workflow in Python for macOS. Its research records card-family profiles and APDU write behavior, while the implementation identifies cards, reads elementary files, writes selected fields, and builds formatting sequences. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/grsimwrite-mac-case/))
- [Gymnasticon: bridging proprietary exercise-bike data to standard cycling sensors](https://github.com/ptx2/gymnasticon) — case-study · protocol — Node.js bridge that reads power and cadence from exercise bikes whose own apps or services are gone or closed, and re-broadcasts them as standard Bluetooth LE Cycling Power and Cycling Speed and Cadence sensors and as an ANT+ bike power profile, so that Zwift, TrainerRoad, bike computers and watches can use the bike. The author's development notes describe working out the Flywheel Home Bike's stats packets on its Bluetooth UART service after the vendor shut its home service down; Peloton support reads the bike's data cable through a USB serial adapter, while the Schwinn IC4 and Keiser M clients parse the standard Fitness Machine Service and Keiser's documented broadcasts. Bike and app compatibility is project-reported. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/gymnasticon-exercise-bike-bridge-case/))
- [Halo 2: an agent-assisted matching decompilation of an LTCG Xbox build](https://github.com/kirklandsig/halo2-decompiled) — case-study · game — A work-in-progress matching decompilation of Halo 2 for the original Xbox: contributors write C and C++ that the period compiler, building the whole program with link-time code generation, must turn back into the retail executable's bytes function by function. The README reports just under half of the game-function bytes matched and leaves a native port for a later stage. It discloses that much of the maintainer's work runs in Claude and Codex coding-agent lanes. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/halo2-matching-decomp-case/))
- [Halo: Combat Evolved: an incremental re-implementation patched into the original Xbox executable](https://github.com/halo-re/halo) — case-study · game — A research project re-implementing the original Xbox Halo: Combat Evolved in C, one function at a time. Rewritten functions are compiled into a separate image and patched into the user’s original executable with redirects from the original implementations, so the hybrid game can be run and debugged in xemu or on an Xbox as work progresses. The project reports an early stage, and its sources document no byte-for-byte comparison with the original code. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/halo-ce-xbox-reimplementation-case/))
- [hardsid4u: reconstructing the HardSID 4U USB protocol](https://github.com/pjthulin/hardsid4u) — case-study · protocol — hardsid4u reconstructs the HardSID 4U USB protocol and implements a libusb Python driver for its SID-chip sockets. The investigation combines vendor-binary analysis, USB captures, and hardware probes to document command framing, initialization, register writes, timing, and flow control; a MIDI instrument uses the driver. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/hardsid4u-case/))
- [Himuro: a matching Fatal Frame PS2 decompilation](https://github.com/Mikompilation/Himuro) — case-study · game — Reconstructs Fatal Frame’s PS2 executable as C for the US, EU and Japanese releases, built with the original EE-GCC 2.9 toolchain and checked by SHA-1 against the user’s own executables. Committed objdiff reports list the US and EU code as fully matched and the Japanese code as partial, and a few routines still rely on assembly or byte patches. Porting to modern hardware is a stated later goal, not part of this repository. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/himuro-fatal-frame-decomp-case/))
- [Hisense AEH-W41H1: local A/C control over a reconstructed RS-485 bus](https://github.com/AndrewDemsDS/hisense-w41h1) — case-study · firmware, protocol — Hisense AEH-W41H1 local control replaces the air conditioner's cloud-connected Wi-Fi module with ESPHome firmware for an ESP32 board or the stock RTL8710C module. The implementation speaks the A/C's RS-485 bus and exposes climate controls and diagnostics to Home Assistant. Protocol notes describe the frame envelope, checksum, bus roles, and command/status behavior derived from device observation. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/hisense-aeh-w41h1-local-control-case/))
- [hongtai-panel: reconstructing a cooler LCD's USB serial protocol](https://github.com/GOG1071/hongtai-panel) — case-study · protocol — hongtai-panel implements Linux control of a LovingCool AIO cooler LCD using a USB CDC-ACM serial protocol reconstructed from the vendor's Electron application. Its documentation describes control-frame layout, checksums, JSON replies, and opcodes; the client displays system statistics, media, or screen mirroring. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/hongtai-panel-case/))
- [hw-harness: scanner USB protocol investigation](https://github.com/cschalle/hw-harness) — case-study · protocol — Documents an author-reported scanner investigation comparing vendor-driver USB control transfers with Linux-driver traces to infer initialization, timing and resolution parameters. The released harness organizes reference capture and comparison; it does not automatically recover a complete protocol specification. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/hw-harness-reference-case/))
- [ICO decompilation: byte-identical PAL and Japanese PlayStation 2 boot ELFs](https://github.com/nathanialf/ico) — case-study · game — Rebuilds the boot ELFs of ICO’s PAL (SCES-50760) and Japanese (SCPS-11003) PlayStation 2 discs from one source tree with the game’s original compiler versions, and checks that every loaded byte and the whole ELF file match the disc’s SHA-1. The 73 data-only archive members are generated at build time from the user’s own ELF, so most read-only data still comes from the disc rather than from source. Separate branches target the USA, Korean and August 2001 prototype builds, and the catalog’s ICO PC port is a fork of this tree. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ico-matching-decomp-case/))
- [ico-pc: a native ICO port compiled from the PS2 decompilation](https://github.com/nathanialf/ico-pc) — case-study · game — Compiles the decompiled C of the PlayStation 2 game ICO natively for Windows, Linux, Android, iPhone and Apple-silicon Macs, with host code standing in for the PS2 hardware and Sony libraries. No PS2 code is interpreted or recompiled, and the program reads the player's PAL disc image on first start. It is a native port of a separately byte-matched decompilation, with documented divergences and optional enhancements. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ico-pc-port-case/))
- [iDotMatrix: Home Assistant control through a reconstructed BLE protocol](https://github.com/dallanwagz/idotmatrix-ha) — case-study · protocol — iDotMatrix Home Assistant integration controls the HXS-002 / NL-XSD-32 LED panel over a BLE protocol derived from analysis of the vendor Android app. Its vendored protocol module and hardware-capture tests support power, brightness, clock controls and chunked 32×32 image uploads with CRC32. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/idotmatrix-ha-case/))
- [impacto: a reimplementation of the MAGES. visual-novel engine](https://github.com/CommitteeOfZero/impacto) — case-study · game — Reimplements the MAGES. visual-novel engine, used by the Science Adventure and Memories Off series, in C++ with OpenGL, running supported titles from the user’s original data files through per-game Lua profiles in one binary. README says all endings can be reached in at least one game, Memories Off 6 T-Wave, but no game is fully complete. The project’s tracking issue lists partial support for titles including Chaos;Head Love Chu Chu, Chaos;Child Love Chu Chu, Steins;Gate and Robotics;Notes Elite. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/impacto-mages-engine-case/))
- [inFAMOUS Second Son native PC port](https://github.com/HayatoG/iss_pc) — case-study · game — Runs the PS4 inFAMOUS Second Son’s own x86-64 code natively on Windows and Linux: a runtime reimplements the PS4 system libraries the game uses, and a shadPS4-derived core translates its GPU commands and shaders to Vulkan. The project reports a playable, in-development port that reaches gameplay and the first chapters, with unstable performance and remaining graphics glitches. It is a compatibility-runtime port rather than a decompilation, and its results remain project-reported. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/infamous-second-son-pc-port-case/))
- [IPI STAY FLY Driver: reconstructing mouse configuration over HID](https://github.com/SpookyyQ/IPI-Stay-Fly-Driver) — case-study · protocol — IPI STAY FLY Driver reconstructs mouse configuration in a Windows app using reverse-engineered 16-byte HID frames. It exposes DPI, polling, button and sensor settings, documents a frame checksum, and includes a WebHID capture extension plus protocol notes for ATK FIERCE X. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ipi-stay-fly-driver-case/))
- [IsaacRecomp: translating Flash game bytecode to native C++](https://github.com/whylurking/IsaacRecomp) — case-study · game — Translates The Binding of Isaac’s Flash AVM1 bytecode into C++ functions and supplies a native rendering/input runtime. The project reads graphics and audio from the user’s original game and documents deterministic input, headless runs and screenshot regression checks. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/isaacrecomp-case/))
- [IW4L: a Rust runtime that plays Modern Warfare 2 from the user’s installed game](https://github.com/vladtrc/iw4L) — case-study · game — Implements a standalone runtime for Call of Duty: Modern Warfare 2 (2009) in Rust on Bevy and wgpu, loading maps, models, textures and weapons from the user’s installed game through native FastFile readers and translating its Direct3D 9 shaders to WGSL. Bots, demo recording and replay, and multiplayer over custom UDP with a QUIC master are implemented, while README warns that gameplay remains incomplete, with missing behavior, bugs and desyncs. README states that the whole project is written by an LLM; NOTICE names the community projects consulted and says the original binaries were read with Ghidra. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/iw4l-mw2-rust-runtime-case/))
- [iX500 Scan: a macOS client for the reverse-engineered ScanSnap Wi-Fi protocol](https://github.com/jonaswagnr/ix500-scan) — case-study · app, protocol — Replaces ScanSnap Home for a Fujitsu/PFU ScanSnap iX500 on macOS with a SwiftUI app and Python service that scan over Wi-Fi through the community-reverse-engineered VENS protocol, or over USB through SANE, and produce searchable PDFs. It vendors bramheerink’s GPL-2.0 driver, extends it for macOS, and documents further handshake and hardware-status fields, including the scan-button bit. Results are author-reported; the repository publishes no tests or captures. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ix500-scan-scansnap-wifi-case/))
- [j2208-band-tools: a direct client for the J2208 fitness-band protocol](https://github.com/brendangooden/j2208-band-tools) — case-study · protocol — j2208-band-tools implements a direct BLE client for J2208-family fitness bands using protocol findings recovered from the FitTrack Health companion app. Its Python tools read stored health history and live sensor values, probe GATT services and collect readings into SQLite. The author checked the documented 16-byte frame protocol against one Hume Band V2. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/j2208-band-tools-case/))
- [Jazz² Resurrection: a native C++ reimplementation of Jazz Jackrabbit 2](https://github.com/deathkiller/jazz2-native) — case-study · game — Reimplements Jazz Jackrabbit 2 (1998) as a native C++ game on an extensively modified nCine engine, converting the user’s original game files into its own cache on first start. It runs on desktop, mobile, web and many homebrew console targets, with multiplayer, an enhanced Reforged mode and partial JJ2+ and MLLE support. A two-sided trajectory probe checks its non-Reforged movement against the original game, and the project reports 376 of 406 scenarios within 25 px/s. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/jazz2-resurrection-engine-case/))
- [K1 Pro OpenDeck Suite: a Linux driver for the Stream Dock K1 Pro](https://github.com/leabergero/k1pro-opendeck-suite) — case-study · protocol — K1 Pro OpenDeck Suite includes a Linux OpenDeck driver for the HOTSPOTEK/Mirabox Stream Dock K1 Pro based on its reverse-engineered USB/HID CRT protocol. The driver handles connection and keepalive messages, 64×64 JPEG key displays, RGB ring controls, and three knob inputs. Four accompanying plugins add generic OpenDeck functions. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/k1-pro-opendeck-suite-case/))
- [KartPad: platform integration for a Mario Kart Wii recompilation](https://github.com/chrissotraidis/kartpad) — case-study · game — Builds Apple and Android integration around WiiCompiled’s ahead-of-time translation of Mario Kart Wii, with native graphics, input and optional Retro Rewind support. Its status and rights documents distinguish platform acceptance, unresolved device defects and game-content requirements. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/kartpad-case/))
- [KeyAxis: decoding a magnetic keyboard's analog HID stream](https://github.com/RigZeeel/KeyAxis) — case-study · protocol — KeyAxis documents the HID travel stream of keyboards using the 0416:7372 controller and turns key depth into virtual gamepad axes. Its protocol notes specify the vendor interface, ARM and DISARM commands, 64-byte host reports and row, column and depth fields recovered from captured traffic. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/keyaxis-keyboard-hid-case/))
- [King Kong Recompiled: an AI-developed Xbox 360 static recompilation](https://github.com/TekRantGaming/king-kong-recompiled) — case-study · game — Translates the Xbox 360 release of Peter Jackson's King Kong into native Windows code with the ReXGlue static-recompilation SDK, adding a launcher and display, control and graphics options. Project code completes streamed disc reads the way the console does, which README credits with getting past an opening freeze seen under Xenia. The author discloses that the port was made almost entirely with Claude Code, directed and tested by the repository owner. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/king-kong-recompiled-case/))
- [Kingdom Hearts 358/2 Days: a matching Nintendo DS decompilation](https://github.com/Yokimitsuro/khdays-decomp) — case-study · game — Reconstructs the ARM9 program and overlays of the EU Nintendo DS release of Kingdom Hearts 358/2 Days as C that recompiles byte for byte with CodeWarrior. README reports all 306 modules byte-identical, with about 99.5% of functions in real C and library assembly counted separately; the ARM7 binary is outside its scope. The repository holds no ROM, game assets or extracted disassembly and produces no playable build. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/kingdom-hearts-days-matching-decomp-case/))
- [Kingdom Hearts 358/2 Days: native PC port built on a matching decompilation](https://github.com/Yokimitsuro/khdays-port) — case-study · game — Compiles the C of the khdays-decomp matching decompilation for Windows x64 and supplies a native platform layer for the Nintendo DS hardware, SDK services and sound driver. README reports the whole game running without an ARM interpreter; its rendering, controls, mod support and network multiplayer are the port’s own additions. This is a decompilation-based port, distinct from the byte-matching DS reconstruction it builds on. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/khdays-native-pc-port-case/))
- [Kinoko: reimplementing Mario Kart Wii’s physics engine for exact ghost replay](https://github.com/vabold/Kinoko) — case-study · game — Re-implements Mario Kart Wii’s physics engine in C++ as a command-line simulator, continuing stblr/Hanachan’s goal of perfectly accurate time-trial ghost replay. Each test case replays a recorded ghost through the engine and compares kart position, rotation, velocities, speed and checkpoint state frame by frame with a trace captured from the original game. It is a behavioural reimplementation of the physics only, not a matching decompilation, and has no graphical frontend. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/kinoko-mkwii-physics-reimplementation-case/))
- [LASRecomp: Link’s Awakening static recompilation](https://github.com/peiikai/LASRecomp) — case-study · game — A native desktop reconstruction of the 2019 Switch version of Link’s Awakening using static recompilation. The author attributes most code, tooling and reverse-engineering work to Claude Code with human direction and review. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/lasrecomp/))
- [LBA2 Remake: a Little Big Adventure 1 and 2 reimplementation in TypeScript and Three.js](https://github.com/LBALab/lba2remake) — case-study · game — Reimplements the engine of Little Big Adventure 2, and now also Little Big Adventure 1, in TypeScript, Three.js and React so that it runs in a browser on resources imported from the user’s copy of the game. README calls it a full re-engineering rather than a disassembly-based approach: every island and building can be explored and most graphics and part of the gameplay work, but the game cannot yet be completed. It adds an editor with an actor-script debugger, VR support and fan-made 3D replacement models for the isometric scenes. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/lba2remake-little-big-adventure-case/))
- [LED Controller: a BanlanX BLE client with audio-feed protocol findings](https://github.com/Antrubtor/LED-controller) — case-study · protocol — LED Controller uses BanlanX BLE commands to control SP611E-family LED strips from a desktop app. Its documentation maps GATT commands and state notifications, and reports probing an audio-feed message used by the controller’s music effects; included tools inspect advertisements, GATT state and Bluetooth captures. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/led-controller-banlanx-case/))
- [Left 4 Dead 2: recovering the AI Director's behavior](https://github.com/zeljkovranjes/l4d2-director-system-research) — case-study · binary, game — This Left 4 Dead 2 study reconstructs the AI Director's pacing, intensity, encounter placement and music rules from an identified x86 server.dll and sanitized runtime traces. It publishes disassembly-derived findings, analysis scripts, evidence files and implementation specifications; a reference game library is in a separate repository. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/l4d2-director-research-case/))
- [Legend of Legaia: a Rust port with disc-local browser tooling](https://github.com/AndrewAltimit/legend-of-legaia-re) — case-study · game — A from-scratch Rust port of the North American PlayStation Legend of Legaia includes native/browser play, asset extraction and disc patching. The author documents Ghidra/emulator research and a full-game ladder; this is a fresh implementation, not a matching decompilation or static recompilation. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/legend-of-legaia-rust-port-case/))
- [LEGO Island, portable: a multi-platform port of the decompiled source](https://github.com/isledecomp/isle-portable) — case-study · game — Adapts the LEGO Island decompilation into a portable build for desktop, mobile, console, DOS and Web targets. Windows-only subsystems are replaced with SDL3, miniaudio, iniparser and a custom Direct3D Retained Mode re-implementation, while core gameplay is meant to stay unchanged. It ports reconstructed source rather than checking byte-identical binaries, so it is distinct from the exact-rebuild decompilation case. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/lego-island-portable-case/))
- [LEGO Island: source reconstruction with separately reported exact rebuilds](https://github.com/isledecomp/isle) — case-study · game — Reconstructs the English LEGO Island 1.1 source for its original 32-bit Windows target. The project distinguishes function-comparison reports, ReproBit’s reported byte-identical retail rebuilds and ordinary developer builds; its modern portable adaptation is a separate repository. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/lego-island-decomp-case/))
- [LGwebOS-menubar: a native macOS remote for LG webOS TVs](https://github.com/gabrielgreco1/TVmenubar-MacOS) — case-study · app — A native macOS menu-bar app that pairs with an LG webOS TV over its local encrypted WebSocket interface to launch apps, switch inputs, adjust volume and picture settings, send pointer input and cast selected media. The project credits the public Connect SDK for webOS protocol behavior and reuses PyWebOSTV’s registration manifest. Testing covers one TV on webOS 5.0. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/lgwebos-menubar-remote-case/))
- [lib-osmo-ble: DJI Osmo Pocket 3 Bluetooth LE protocol library](https://github.com/yigitkonur/lib-osmo-ble) — case-study · protocol — Reconstructs the DUML-over-BLE protocol that DJI's Mimo app uses with the Osmo Pocket 3 camera as a Node.js library and CLI for scanning, pairing, gimbal commands and roughly 20 Hz position telemetry without the vendor app. The author reports working from a third party's Wireshark captures and dissector of Mimo traffic, a Go client and trial-and-error debugging; PROTOCOL.md documents the frame layout, CRC parameters, BLE characteristic roles and command IDs. A write-up and bundled patch trace seven bugs that kept the earlier node-osmo library from working with the camera, and the author reports that the patched library completed pairing, Wi-Fi setup and RTMP streaming. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/osmo-pocket-3-ble-protocol-case/))
- [libd2: Diablo II 1.14d engine-core libraries](https://github.com/jaenster/libd2) — case-study · binary, game, protocol — Zig libraries reconstructing Diablo II 1.14d seeded maps, item generation, saves, simulation rules and network formats. Selected components expose native and WebAssembly interfaces; verification notes distinguish retail map comparisons from weaker subsystem evidence. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/libd2-engine-core-reconstruction-case/))
- [libfprint-goodixtls: a reconstructed Goodix GXFP5187 fingerprint driver](https://github.com/Sigfrodr/libfprint-goodixtls) — case-study · protocol — libfprint-goodixtls reconstructs communication with the Goodix GXFP5187 SPI fingerprint sensor in the Huawei MateBook X Pro as a libfprint TOD driver. It reads a PSK from sensor RAM, establishes the sensor's TLS-PSK channel, decodes captured images, and uses local descriptors for host-side matching. The author reports enrolment and verification through fprintd and GNOME Settings; detailed reverse-engineering notes are kept outside the repository. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/libfprint-goodixtls-case/))
- [LibreMP: an Epson EasyMP screen caster from packet-capture research](https://github.com/MuazTPM-YT/libre-mp) — case-study · protocol — LibreMP casts desktop screens to Epson EasyMP projectors using a protocol reconstructed from Windows client packet captures. Its Rust core handles registration and login messages, projector QR decoding, JPEG tile streaming and changed-region updates; a CLI and Tauri interface use the same core. The project documents control on TCP 3620 and video on TCP 3621. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/libremp-easymp-case/))
- [librw: a re-implementation of the RenderWare Graphics engine](https://github.com/aap/librw) — case-study · game — Re-implements a large part of the RenderWare Graphics middleware API as a portable C++ library that reads and writes its DFF model and TXD texture files for PS2, D3D8, D3D9 and Xbox and is meant to convert them between platforms. Direct3D 9 and OpenGL backends render the scenes, while PS2 rendering works only as a test. README names rendering GTA as its use; BSP world files are not supported. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/librw-renderware-reimplementation-case/))
- [Lighthouse: a Banjo-Kazooie PC port built on the decompilation](https://github.com/HarbourMasters/Lighthouse) — case-study · game — A native Windows, Linux and macOS port of Banjo-Kazooie that compiles game C sources, many tagged with their Banjo-Kazooie decompilation file paths, against libultraship, a reimplementation of the N64 SDK. Torch extracts the user’s ROM into an asset archive, and the port adds Anchor multiplayer, romhack and language-pack support and a randomizer. Unlike Banjo: Recompiled, it builds from decompiled source rather than statically recompiling the ROM’s code. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/lighthouse-banjo-kazooie-port-case/))
- [LineWars II: a portable SDL3 reconstruction](https://github.com/fredangstadt-lang/LineWarsII-SDL) — case-study · binary, game — Provides an SDL3 backend for a portable-C reconstruction of the 1994 DOS game. Its build configuration links the reconstructed engine to video, input and audio adapters; the upstream documents differential gates against original machine code. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/linewars-ii-sdl-case/))
- [LinkCam: controlling Insta360 Link 2 through UVC extension research](https://github.com/fmontes/linkcam) — case-study · protocol — LinkCam implements macOS menu-bar and command-line controls for the Insta360 Link 2 webcam. It uses standard UVC controls for pan, tilt and zoom, while its extension-unit investigation identifies a feature-mask bit and selector for AI tracking. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/linkcam-case/))
- [Little Planet: Sonic CD recompiled from a matching disassembly](https://github.com/carlosrmz009/littleplanet) — case-study · game — Disassembles every executable file on the Sonic CD disc from runtime coverage and recursive descent, reassembles them byte-identically, and translates the code statically to C on a from-scratch Mega-CD runtime that replaces the BIOS with a high-level implementation. Recompiled blocks run only where memory still holds the code they came from, and a script compares 96 stage runs frame by frame against the interpreter. The project also patches in widescreen and per-zone saves; results remain project-reported. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/little-planet-sonic-cd-recomp-case/))
- [Littlebird.ai static home-page rebuild](https://github.com/AkashNaickar/crdb-sample-frontend) — case-study · website — Rebuilds the Littlebird.ai home page in plain HTML/CSS/JavaScript from captured markup and assets, with source-wired mobile navigation, FAQ toggles, scroll reveals and OS-dependent call-to-action presentation. It illustrates a bounded website reconstruction, with uncaptured routes and product functionality left outside the scope. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/littlebird-static-rebuild-case/))
- [Lost Odyssey Recomp: statically recompiling an Xbox 360 RPG into native ports](https://github.com/freefrank/LostOdysseyRecomp) — case-study · game — Translates Lost Odyssey’s Xbox 360 PowerPC executable with patched XenonRecomp tooling and runs the generated code on a native runtime that renders through Direct3D 12, Vulkan or Metal. Windows, Linux, macOS and Android packages import the user’s own disc data. The maintainer documents development with Claude Code and Codex CLI and records each release separately from validation and acceptance. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/lost-odyssey-recomp-case/))
- [LSX II Volume Fixer: reconstructing volume behavior](https://github.com/roberdorfer/kef-lsx-volume) — case-study · app — Uses measured USB-volume behavior and a macOS audio proxy to map volume keys to a KEF LSX II’s LAN control. The project reports more even steps while forwarding USB audio; the speaker firmware is unchanged. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/kef-lsx-volume-case/))
- [lux-lat-long-log: reconstructing LUMIX S5 BLE GPS communication](https://github.com/tobiasbrummer/lux-lat-long-log) — case-study · protocol — lux-lat-long-log reconstructs the Panasonic LUMIX S5 Bluetooth LE GPS exchange in an ESP32 camera client. The project documents GATT identifiers, session ordering and GPS/time packets from Android HCI captures, then uses a GNSS module to send positions without the phone app. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/lux-lat-long-log-case/))
- [MacroPad: reconstructing a USB macropad configuration protocol](https://github.com/Probler-Yt/MacroPad) — case-study · protocol — MacroPad reconstructs the USB HID configuration exchange of a 12-key, two-knob macropad identified as 1189:8840 and implements it in a Linux GUI. The author documents capturing MINI_KEYBOARD.exe traffic with usbmon, decoding configuration and commit reports, and replaying captured packets in tests. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/macropad-usb-protocol-case/))
- [Magene L308: reconstructing a smart taillight's BLE control protocol](https://github.com/mchardysam/magene-l308-taillight) — case-study · protocol — Magene L308 interop tools reconstruct the smart taillight's BLE control protocol and light_mode.bin pattern format. A protocol specification records GATT characteristics, command frames and upload sequences; browser, Python and Garmin clients use those findings to control settings and patterns directly. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/magene-l308-taillight-case/))
- [Majora’s Mask: matching US N64 decompilation](https://github.com/zeldaret/mm) — case-study · game — Recreates a C source base for The Legend of Zelda: Majora’s Mask from information in the game and static or dynamic analysis, then rebuilds the US Nintendo 64 ROM. The build takes assets and assembly from a user-supplied ROM and checks the output against stored MD5 checksums. The team states it will not produce a PC port; the separately curated Zelda 64: Recompiled only credits it for headers and some functions. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/zelda-mm-decomp-case/))
- [Malware Analysis: a trojanized FL Studio installer and Go stealer](https://github.com/AllobatesZaparo/malware-analysis) — case-study · app, binary — This malware-analysis report investigates a trojanized FL Studio installer that bundles an Electron/NSIS wrapper and a Go information stealer. The author traces the wrapper statically and studies the payload in an isolated Windows environment with process, registry and network observations, recovering its reported command-and-control mechanism. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/fl-studio-stealer-analysis-case/))
- [Malware Analysis: njRAT and Casdet behavior investigation](https://github.com/phour44/Malware-Analysis) — case-study · binary — A malware investigation examines njRAT and a UPX-packed Casdet executable with PEStudio static triage and ANY.RUN sandbox telemetry, alongside a malicious PDF and benign comparison files. The author reports process, registry and network behavior, extracted indicators and MITRE ATT&CK mappings in a report and screenshots. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/malware-analysis-njrat-casdet-case/))
- [Mario Kart Wii: an in-progress matching decompilation of the PAL release](https://github.com/snailspeed3/mkw) — case-study · game — A work-in-progress matching decompilation of Mario Kart Wii that targets the PAL release (RMCP01), rebuilding main.dol and the StaticR.rel module from C and C++ with Metrowerks compilers and checking both against stored SHA-1 values. The build splits these binaries from a user-supplied disc image, and objdiff compares objects during development. The README’s progress badges point to a decomp.dev page that did not resolve at review, and the default branch was last updated in September 2025. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/mario-kart-wii-decomp-case/))
- [Mario Kart: Double Dash!!: a matching GameCube decompilation of the debug build](https://github.com/doldecomp/mkdd) — case-study · game — A work-in-progress 1:1 decompilation of Mario Kart: Double Dash!! that rebuilds the GameCube main.dol of the US debug build (MarioClub_us) from C and C++ with the original Metrowerks compilers, checking it against a stored SHA-1. The build splits the binary from a user-supplied disc image, and objdiff compares objects during development. decomp.dev reported 47.09% of code matched at the reviewed commit; a European retail target is configured but marked as not working yet. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/mario-kart-double-dash-decomp-case/))
- [Mario Party 4: matching GameCube decompilation of the USA releases](https://github.com/mariopartyrd/marioparty4) — case-study · game — Reconstructs Mario Party 4’s GameCube main executable and REL modules as C source that rebuilds both USA releases. The build analyzes the user’s own disc image and checks every linked output against version-specific SHA-1 lists. PAL and Japanese work is stale and no PC port exists yet; matching results remain project-reported here. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/mario-party-4-decomp-case/))
- [MarioKart 64: Recompiled — a native port through N64Recomp static recompilation](https://github.com/sonicdcer/MarioKart64Recomp) — case-study · game — Statically recompiles the US Nintendo 64 release of Mario Kart 64 into a native Windows, Linux and macOS application with N64Recomp, rendering through RT64. The README distinguishes this from a source port: game code is translated from the user’s ROM, while headers and some functions from a decompilation fork support patches and enhancements. Enhancement, fidelity and input-lag claims are project-reported. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/mariokart64-recompiled-case/))
- [MechRewired: rebuilding MechWarrior 2 from original game data](https://github.com/deanthecoder/MechRewired) — case-study · game — MechRewired rebuilds the MechWarrior 2: 31st Century Combat experience around data imported from a user's DOS copy. Its code loads original resources and missions, while providing a new cockpit, terrain rendering and combat simulation for modern platforms. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/mechrewired-mechwarrior-2-case/))
- [MediEvil decompilation: an early checksum-gated PlayStation project](https://github.com/MediEvilDecompilation/medievil-decomp) — case-study · game — Decompiles the US PlayStation release of MediEvil toward C source that rebuilds its main executable, game executable and 26 overlays. splat splits the user's own disc files, and the default build compares all 28 outputs with SHA-1 checksums. README describes the project as a work in progress and publishes no progress figure. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/medievil-decomp-case/))
- [MediEvil Recomp: a PlayStation static recompilation to C#](https://github.com/BlackLabelHQ/MediEvilRecomp) — case-study · game — Statically recompiles the North American PlayStation release of MediEvil into C# with the RecompOne recompiler and runtime. The runtime stands in for BIOS, GPU, CD-ROM and other hardware services, and project patches change rendering, widescreen and terrain behavior. README stresses that this is a recompilation, not a decompilation, and labels it an open beta. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/medievil-recomp-case/))
- [Mega Man X Recompiled: a snesrecomp static recompilation](https://github.com/mstan/MegaManXSNESRecomp) — case-study · game — Statically translates the 65816 CPU code of the SNES Mega Man X (USA Rev 1) to C with the snesrecomp framework; the PPU, audio, DMA and other hardware run in the framework’s runtime, and a LakeSnes-derived interpreter executes whatever the static pass cannot prove. The C is generated locally from the user’s ROM, with function names imported from community DiztinGUIsh annotations. Playable Zero, co-op and rollback netplay, X2/X3 boss weapons and adaptive widescreen are layered on top, using assets extracted locally from the user’s own sequel ROMs. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/megaman-x-snes-recomp-case/))
- [Melee Unlocked: a Windows source port and static recompilation of Melee](https://github.com/Hero88go/melee-unlocked) — case-study · game — A beta Windows build of Super Smash Bros. Melee (NTSC 1.02) with two engines: a source port that compiles the doldecomp decompilation as a native library, and a static recompilation that translates the retail DOL's PowerPC code and Slippi's Gecko codes into C++. Both run on a native runtime with Direct3D rendering, unlocked display rates and natively implemented Slippi rollback play. The user's own ISO supplies all game data, and the README says the work was AI-assisted. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/melee-unlocked-case/))
- [Metaforce: a Metroid Prime engine reimplementation rebuilt on the decompilation](https://github.com/AxioDL/metaforce) — case-study · game — Started in 2015 as a reverse-engineered reimplementation of the Metroid Prime engine, Metaforce has been rewritten on top of the PrimeDecomp matching decompilation. Its native app compiles that game code as non-matching C++ for desktop and mobile through the Aurora GameCube compatibility layer and loads a user-selected disc image. The README says builds are unavailable while the rewrite stabilizes. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/metaforce-metroid-prime-case/))
- [Metroid Dread: a hand decompilation in progress](https://github.com/tsuyoi-w/DreadComp) — case-study · game — DreadComp is an in-progress hand decompilation of Metroid Dread that recreates game source code in C++. The repository includes initial source files, build tooling and symbol data; building requires assets and the version 2.1.0 executable from the user's own game copy. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/dreadcomp-case/))
- [Metroid Prime Port: an AI-assisted native port of the PrimeDecomp decompilation](https://github.com/Odrannnn/MetroidPrimePort) — case-study · game — Compiles the PrimeDecomp decompilation of Metroid Prime’s game and engine code as native C++20 for Linux, Windows and Android, with an experimental macOS build. Aurora supplies the GameCube SDK compatibility layer and rendering, and the port reads a user-supplied disc image at run time. The author states that parts were written with AI coding assistants under their oversight. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/metroid-prime-port-case/))
- [Metroid Prime: the PrimeDecomp matching GameCube decompilation](https://github.com/PrimeDecomp/prime) — case-study · game — Reconstructs C++ source for Metroid Prime’s GameCube executable and its NES-emulator module so that CodeWarrior builds match the originals, across the USA, Korean, PAL and Japanese releases, with early configurations for demo discs and three Wii releases. Matched units are compiled from source and linked with objects split from the user’s disc, then checked against per-version SHA-1 hashes; the repository contains no game assets or assembly. The separately curated Metaforce and Metroid Prime Port build their native ports on this code. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/primedecomp-metroid-prime-decomp-case/))
- [MikuPan: a native Fatal Frame PC port built on the Himuro decompilation](https://github.com/Mikompilation/MikuPan) — case-study · game — Ports the PS2 game Fatal Frame to native PC builds without emulation; its repository description names the Himuro decompilation as its basis. Stubs replace PS2 kernel calls and a new SDL3-based layer handles rendering, audio and UI, while game data comes from the user’s own disc. The README calls the port very early work and publishes no matching or fidelity checks. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/mikupan-fatal-frame-port-case/))
- [MinecraftLCE: an early-stage Minecraft: Nintendo Switch Edition decompilation](https://github.com/GRAnimated/MinecraftLCE) — case-study · game — Decompiles Minecraft: Nintendo Switch Edition v1.0.17, a Legacy Console Edition release, into C++ that is compared function by function with the original executable using a patched Clang 4.0.1 and the open-ead Switch decompilation tools. The README explains the choice of the Switch build for its matchable Clang toolchain and type information, with names ported from the symbol-bearing Wii U Edition. At the reviewed commit the function list marks about 11% of functions decompiled, most of them matching; the result cannot run the game. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/minecraft-lce-switch-decomp-case/))
- [Monix: reconstructing Audient iD USB mixer control on Linux](https://github.com/sKuhLight/monix) — case-study · protocol — Monix implements Linux mixer control for Audient iD USB audio interfaces through a companion kernel module, protocol library and GUI. Its protocol documentation maps USB control requests from official-app captures and macOS binary analysis, covering routing, faders, metering and device profiles. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/monix-audient-id-case/))
- [MS5 WiFi Microscope: reconstructing the camera's UDP video protocol](https://github.com/Fyfar/ms5-wifi-microscope) — case-study · protocol — MS5 WiFi Microscope reconstructs the MS5 camera's UDP command and video protocol and supplies a Python client that serves its reassembled JPEG stream as MJPEG. The documentation specifies command headers, the OpenVideo port handshake, chunk layout and resolution queries; the author reports 1280×720 streaming at roughly 25–30 fps on MS5 hardware. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ms5-wifi-microscope-case/))
- [MSI Mystic Light on Linux: reconstructing B860/X870 lighting control](https://github.com/Picachuchu69/msi-mystic-light-b860-x870-linux) — case-study · protocol — MSI Mystic Light on Linux implements lighting control for an MSI motherboard from USB traffic captured while MSI Center ran in a Windows VM. Its Python script sends a 290-byte HID Feature report to controller 0db0:0076, while the documentation maps modes, RGB palette bytes, brightness, direction and the flash-save flag. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/msi-mystic-light-b860-x870-linux-case/))
- [MSP CSV Export Format: reconstructing My Stocks Portfolio exports](https://github.com/Izero/msp-csv-format) — case-study · app — MSP CSV Export Format reconstructs the undocumented CSV export of the My Stocks Portfolio & Market app. The author compared eleven iOS exports, app displays and public translation strings to describe columns, blocks and transaction replay. A dependency-free Python parser and synthetic export exercise the documented rules and flag malformed input. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/msp-csv-format-case/))
- [MTG Arena Collection Export: reconstructing the Frontdoor collection request](https://github.com/Ikreb1/mtg-arena-collection-export) — case-study · game, protocol — MTG Arena Collection Export reconstructs the Frontdoor request needed to export owned cards from Magic: The Gathering Arena. The Python client uses an account refresh token, opens a TLS Frontdoor socket, authenticates with the 2026 version 4 frame and protobuf command envelope, and requests Card_GetAllCards. A separate protocol document records the recovered wire format. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/mtg-arena-collection-export-case/))
- [MyJBxHeadsetControl: JBL Quantum 950 controls over USB HID](https://github.com/666mille/MyJBxHeadsetControl) — case-study · protocol — MyJBxHeadsetControl provides Stream Deck controls for the JBL Quantum 950 Wireless headset through its USB dongle's reverse-engineered HID exchange. It receives status packets for battery and sound modes, sends feature reports for settings, and uses the user's QuantumENGINE DLL to calculate EQ coefficients. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/myjbxheadsetcontrol-case/))
- [Nanolathe: a documented Total Annihilation engine reimplementation](https://github.com/nanolathe-gg/nanolathe) — case-study · game — Reimplements a Total Annihilation-compatible strategy-game engine in Go, with public file-format research and separate gameplay profiles. It distinguishes intended retail 3.1 behavior from community-patch research and deliberate modern changes, and separates synthetic checks from tests requiring original game data. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/nanolathe-case/))
- [nfs-mw: a Rust reconstruction of Need for Speed: Most Wanted](https://github.com/47PADO47/nfs-mw) — case-study · game — nfs-mw reconstructs Need for Speed: Most Wanted 2005 PC v1.3 in Rust using data read from the user's installation. Its format readers decode bChunk, JDLZ, HUFF, cars, textures, and the streamed city; the current runtime provides viewers, driving, menus, and free roam. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/nfs-mw-case/))
- [NiimBlue: an independent web client for NIIMBOT label printers](https://github.com/MultiMote/niimblue) — case-study · app, protocol — Replaces NIIMBOT's own label app with a browser client that designs labels and prints them over Web Bluetooth or Web Serial, with Android (Capacitor) and Windows (Tauri) builds of the same interface. Printer communication comes from the same author's NiimBlueLib, which the README calls the most complete implementation of the NIIMBOT protocol; the protocol is documented on the NIIMBOT Community Wiki, and owners of models that do not print are asked for a packet capture of a print made with the official app. The README says the client runs entirely in the browser and keeps label data there. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/niimblue-niimbot-printer-client-case/))
- [NJFE: a Soulear camera protocol bridge for desktop inspection](https://github.com/WildweaselMS/NJFE) — case-study · protocol — Reuses otoscope's reverse-engineered i4season/Soulear protocol in an ESP32-C3 bridge and Python desktop viewer. The author credits Claude Code for implementation and reports streaming one Hopefox Find T to a Windows PC, with recording, orientation and LED controls. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/njfe-ear-camera-bridge-case/))
- [NOOP: an offline WHOOP strap client built from Bluetooth protocol research](https://github.com/ryanbr/noop) — case-study · app, protocol — Replaces the WHOOP companion app with offline macOS, Android and iOS clients that pair with WHOOP 4.0 and 5.0/MG straps over Bluetooth, decode their vendor GATT frames, offload the strap’s history into local SQLite and compute recovery, strain, HRV and sleep on the device. A versioned protocol reference documents framing, checksums, commands and sensor records per strap family, building on the community my-whoop and goose projects. WHOOP 4.0 is the supported path; deeper WHOOP 5.0/MG metrics and Oura ring support remain experimental. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/noop-whoop-ble-client-case/))
- [nRF52 e-paper tag: reconstructing an undocumented display interface](https://github.com/niazlv/nrf52-eink-price-tag) — case-study · firmware, protocol — nRF52 e-paper tag reconstructs control of an undocumented electronic shelf label with an nRF52832 board and SSD1675A-class panel. The repository records the pinout, 9-bit SPI connection, waveform-table layout and refresh behavior, and provides replacement Zephyr firmware plus a portable C99 display driver. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/nrf52-eink-price-tag-case/))
- [NuVelocity Engine: reimplementing Reflexive's Velocity Engine](https://github.com/NuVelocity/NuVelocity) — case-study · game — NuVelocity Engine reimplements Reflexive Entertainment's Velocity Engine as a C++ and SDL3 game engine. It provides modular 2D rendering, asset and resource loading, audio playback, screen management, and a property-based object system, with decoder source included in the repository. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/nuvelocity-engine-case/))
- [Ocarina of Time: decompilation with MD5-checked ROM builds for 16 versions](https://github.com/zeldaret/oot) — case-study · game — Recreates a C source base for Ocarina of Time that rebuilds 16 Nintendo 64, GameCube and iQue Player ROM versions, using information from the game and static and dynamic analysis. Assets come from the user’s own ROM and each default build is checked against that version’s MD5; README states the project is not producing a PC port and still calls the repository a work in progress. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ocarina-of-time-decomp-case/))
- [open-appleremote: Siri Remote protocol reconstruction](https://github.com/a-funk/open-appleremote) — case-study · protocol — Reconstructs first-generation Siri Remote Bluetooth reports for Linux, decoding buttons, touch and tvOS-style gestures. The Python implementation and protocol notes distinguish measured behavior, historical reports and inferred meanings. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/open-appleremote-case/))
- [open-picpak: reconstructing the PicPak frame's BLE protocol and device behavior](https://github.com/GottZ/open-PicPak) — case-study · firmware, protocol — open-picpak documents the PicPak 4.2-inch e-ink frame's stock firmware behavior, BLE GATT protocol, and image conversion pipeline. The author reports deriving byte-level findings from the stock ESP-IDF image and a device client, then using those findings in custom ESP-IDF firmware, host tools, and a self-hosted backend. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/open-picpak-case/))
- [open-rknpu: Rockchip NPU task-program reconstruction](https://github.com/dnhkng/open-rknpu) — case-study · protocol — open-rknpu reconstructs a task-program interface for the Rockchip RV1103/RV1106 NPU from board experiments and black-box comparisons. Its Python compiler emits register commands, weight and quantization blocks, and a container for bounded static ONNX convolution graphs; a C runtime submits them through /dev/rknpu. Research files document register and numerical findings. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/open-rknpu-case/))
- [open_oura: a cloud-free Oura Ring client built on its reverse-engineered BLE protocol](https://github.com/Th0rgal/open_oura) — case-study · app, protocol — Reimplements the Bluetooth Low Energy protocol of the Oura Ring Gen 3/4/5 in Rust crates and an oura command-line client that pairs with, authenticates to and syncs a ring directly, with no Oura account or cloud service. The author reports recovering the GATT layout, packet framing and AES app-auth flow from the decompiled Android app, and porting history-event decoders and several on-device metrics, such as HRV, sleep debt and nightly temperature baselines, from Ghidra decompilations of the app's native libraries. Event bodies are stored raw in SQLite so they can be re-decoded as decoders improve. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/open-oura-ring-protocol-case/))
- [OpenAWE: an open reimplementation of Remedy’s Alan Wake engine](https://github.com/OpenAWE-Project/OpenAWE) — case-study · game — Reimplements Remedy Entertainment’s Alan Wake engine, later known as Northlight, in C++ and OpenGL, aiming to run Alan Wake and Alan Wake’s American Nightmare portably from the user’s game data, with Quantum Break and possibly Control as later goals. README documents only a free camera for moving through loaded game data, and the American Nightmare script table implements 16 of its 278 native functions. The repository also includes command-line tools for the games’ archive, texture, Havok and sound formats. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/openawe-alan-wake-engine-case/))
- [opendash: reconstructing the Scanmatik SM3 J2534 adapter protocol](https://github.com/PabloSoage/opendash) — case-study · protocol — Speaks the undocumented protocol of the Scanmatik SM3, a J2534 vehicle interface that otherwise talks only to its own Windows software, and exposes it to an Android phone as an ELM327 bridge with a diagnostics app on top. The author recovered the framing — a 16-byte header with sequence, CRC-based and sum-based check words, opcode and length — by capturing the device's bundled driver and measuring the replies, and docs/protocol.md separates what was established from what is still open. Reported results come from one Opel Astra J over five sessions with the car present. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/opendash-scanmatik-sm3-protocol-case/))
- [OpenDiablo2: Diablo 2 engine reimplementation](https://github.com/OpenDiablo2/OpenDiablo2) — case-study · game — OpenDiablo2 is a cross-platform ARPG engine written in Go that supports playing Diablo 2. The project is being split into a separate engine and toolset, called Abyss Engine, and a game project retaining the OpenDiablo2 name. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/opendiablo2/))
- [OpenDMCUFirm: a reimplemented Wii U DMCU display firmware](https://github.com/GaryOderNichts/OpenDMCUFirm) — case-study · firmware — Reimplements in C the firmware of the Wii U’s DMCU, the M68HC11-compatible display microcontroller in its AMD graphics controller, which in Wii mode reads the Video Interface state and configures display output, deinterlacing, scaling and overscan. The README says the work is based on reverse engineering of the original firmware but is not a byte-for-byte matching decompilation, and calls it a work in progress. The built image replaces the stock firmware in Wii mode when loaded through the evWii plugin. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/opendmcufirm-wii-u-dmcu-firmware-case/))
- [OpenDrop: an open reimplementation of Apple AirDrop](https://github.com/seemoo-lab/opendrop) — case-study · protocol — Python command-line reimplementation of Apple's AirDrop protocol from SEEMOO Lab's reverse-engineering research. It finds receivers via _airdrop._tcp DNS-SD, sends binary-plist /Discover and /Ask requests over HTTPS and uploads gzip-compressed CPIO archives to exchange files with iOS and macOS. It is not affiliated with Apple. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/opendrop-airdrop-reimplementation-case/))
- [OpenGOAL: recovering GOAL code and rebuilding the Jak games](https://github.com/open-goal/jak-project) — case-study · game — Reconstructs readable GOAL code from the original Jak binaries and compiles it with a new GOAL compiler for modern PCs. Its workflow combines a specialized decompiler, manually supplied type information, asset extraction and an interactive development runtime. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/opengoal-jak-case/))
- [OpenJooki: replacing the Jooki 2 software and recovery path](https://github.com/Guillain-RDCDE/OpenJooki) — case-study · firmware — OpenJooki replaces the Jooki 2 music speaker's embedded Linux core and provides phone-based control, updates and SD-card recovery. Its technical notes map the device's Ingenic Linux and ESP32 split, storage partitions, Mender update slots and recovery behavior, while the repository contains the replacement Lua services and installer tooling. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/openjooki-jooki2-case/))
- [OpenLara: Tomb Raider engine reimplementation](https://github.com/XProger/OpenLara) — case-study · game — Open-source engine for classic Tomb Raider, with a WebGL build and a standalone release. Its format code defines object types for Tomb Raider 1 through 4. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/openlara/))
- [OpenLF2: a Little Fighter 2 engine reimplementation](https://github.com/OpenLF2/OpenLF2) — case-study · game — OpenLF2 reimplements Little Fighter 2 v2.0a as a playable C++ and Lua engine. It loads resources from the original game installer and provides local modes, replays, mods and network matches between OpenLF2 players. The author states that gameplay compatibility is still being refined. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/openlf2-case/))
- [OpenMW: replacing Morrowind’s engine while reusing original content](https://github.com/OpenMW/openmw) — case-study · game — Provides an independent engine for playing Morrowind and an alternative editor, OpenMW-CS. It loads original game content through its own runtime and documents script and mod compatibility differences; it is an engine replacement rather than a recovered Bethesda source tree. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/openmw-engine-case/))
