- [OpenPeats: reconstructing SoundPEATS earbud controls](https://github.com/Wasabules/OpenPeats) — case-study · protocol — OpenPeats reconstructs the SoundPEATS earbud control protocol for desktop and Android clients. Its specification describes frames and opcodes derived from analysis of the official Android app and checked against SOUNDPEATS H3 hardware. Go and Kotlin implementations expose ANC, equalizer and multipoint controls, plus battery status. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/openpeats-case/))
- [OpenPigeon: reconstructing GamePigeon iMessage game messages](https://github.com/time-attack/OpenPigeon) — case-study · game, protocol — OpenPigeon is a TypeScript codec for reading GamePigeon iMessage game state and encoding caller-supplied moves for eleven turn-based games. Its protocol documentation describes balloon identity, encrypted move URLs, the length-seeded cipher and the state envelope; captured-message vectors support codec tests. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/openpigeon-case/))
- [OpenRA: an engine reimplementation reimagining early Westwood RTS games](https://github.com/OpenRA/OpenRA) — case-study · game — Provides a C# engine for early Westwood real-time strategy games, with mods that reimagine Command & Conquer: Red Alert, Tiberian Dawn and Dune 2000. It loads the original games’ content through its own file-system, MiniYAML rules and Lua mission layers rather than running or recovering Westwood code. Gameplay and interface deliberately follow OpenRA’s own design, so it is not a faithful recreation of the original executables. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/openra-engine-case/))
- [OpenRCT2: an open re-implementation of RollerCoaster Tycoon 2](https://github.com/OpenRCT2/OpenRCT2) — case-study · game — Re-implements RollerCoaster Tycoon 2, originally written in x86 assembly, as a C++ engine that runs on the original game’s data. It loads RCT2 saves and scenarios, and RCT1 scenarios when those files are present, while saving to its own documented park format. Modern-platform support, interface and AI changes, higher limits and multiplayer are deliberate extensions, not reproductions of the original. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/openrct2-engine-case/))
- [OpenRiseOfNations: a native macOS engine reimplementation](https://github.com/ben-wright-1/OpenRiseOfNations) — case-study · game — Reimplements the Rise of Nations: Extended Edition engine as a native macOS app with a Metal renderer, a deterministic simulation, a computer opponent and a compatible BigHugeScript compiler and virtual machine, loading art, maps, rules and scripts from the user’s own Windows installation. Subsystem specifications written from Ghidra study of the symbol-bearing executable, together with JavaScript reference tools, guided a C++17 implementation built with heavy Claude Code assistance. The alpha plays tutorials and scenarios, and its bit-exactness checks remain project-reported. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/openriseofnations-engine-case/))
- [OpenRW: an open re-implementation of the Grand Theft Auto III engine](https://github.com/rwengine/openrw) — case-study · game — Re-implements Rockstar Games’ Grand Theft Auto III (2001) as a cross-platform C++ engine that runs on the data of a legitimate PC copy. It has its own loaders for the game’s RenderWare models, textures and archives, a script machine for the original mission scripts, and an optional Qt data viewer. Playability is partial: the project’s mission tracker marks six early Portland story missions as completable. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/openrw-gta3-engine-case/))
- [OpenSC4: an early open-source reimplementation of SimCity 4](https://github.com/OpenSC4-org/OpenSC4) — case-study · game — Reimplements SimCity 4 as a Godot project in GDScript that reads DBPF archives, region folders and city saves from the user’s own SimCity 4 installation. The code draws a region view with city thumbnails and interface elements built from the game’s UI definitions, opens a saved city’s terrain and water in 3D, and includes a DAT explorer for previewing archive subfiles. It is an early, inactive engine case without simulation gameplay, not a playable replacement. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/opensc4-simcity-4-reimplementation-case/))
- [OpenTomb: Tomb Raider engine reimplementation](https://github.com/opentomb/OpenTomb) — case-study · game — OpenTomb is an open-source reimplementation of the classic Tomb Raider engine for playing levels from Tomb Raider 1–5 and custom TRLE levels. It is no longer maintained. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/opentomb/))
- [OpenTracks: a UWP port of OpenTracks7 BETA1](https://github.com/mediaexplorer74/OpenTracks) — case-study · app — OpenTracks is an early UWP port of the OpenTracks7 BETA1 Windows Phone music app informed by the author's reverse engineering. Its C# and XAML implementation updates page control access and replaces WP7 MediaPlayerLauncher with UWP media playback APIs. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/opentracks-uwp-case/))
- [OpenXcom: an open-source clone of X-COM: UFO Defense and Terror from the Deep](https://github.com/OpenXcom/OpenXcom) — case-study · game — Reimplements MicroProse’s UFO: Enemy Unknown (X-COM: UFO Defense) and X-COM: Terror from the Deep as a C++/SDL engine that runs on the user’s original game resources. Game data such as items, craft, research and missions is expressed in YAML rulesets, which also carry its standard mods and a wider modding ecosystem. It is a clone that reuses original content, not recovered MicroProse code. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/openxcom-engine-case/))
- [os-xgs-npu: reconstructing Sophos XGS coprocessor interfaces](https://github.com/AbdelmonemAwad/os-xgs-npu) — case-study · protocol — os-xgs-npu reconstructs interfaces to Sophos XGS appliance coprocessors through FreeBSD kernel modules and an OPNsense plugin. The work documents undocumented registers, PCIe device families and measured coprocessor behavior to expose front ports and offload firewall flows and IPsec associations. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/os-xgs-npu-case/))
- [OSIRIS: replacing Onkyo receiver firmware through MCU protocol research](https://github.com/ubis/OSIRIS) — case-study · firmware, protocol — OSIRIS replaces the DSP-board software of Onkyo TX-NR515 and TX-NR717 receivers with its own boot loader, Linux services and DSP code. The project documents the SPI exchange between the main MCU and DSP board, including 1032-byte transfers, frame structure and command meanings recovered from stock firmware and logs. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/osiris-onkyo-receiver-case/))
- [OWL: an open Apple Wireless Direct Link implementation](https://github.com/seemoo-lab/owl) — case-study · protocol — Reimplements Apple Wireless Direct Link (AWDL), the proprietary Wi-Fi ad hoc protocol Apple devices use for peer-to-peer links such as AirDrop, as a user-space C daemon for Linux and macOS; the README calls it the result of reverse engineering by the Open Wireless Link project at TU Darmstadt's SEEMOO lab. OWL runs the master election, synchronises availability windows and the channel sequence, parses and crafts AWDL action and data frames, and provides an awdl0 virtual interface with a link-local IPv6 address, so unmodified IPv6 programs such as SEEMOO's OpenDrop AirDrop client can reach Apple devices. The authors describe the recovered protocol in a MobiCom 2018 paper. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/owl-awdl-open-implementation-case/))
- [P.T. for PC: a behavior-based engine reimplementation that reads the PS4 data](https://github.com/LoreanXavier/pt-pc) — case-study · game — Reimplements P.T.’s game logic in C++ on a new Vulkan engine that reads levels, models, textures, sounds, scripts and cutscenes from the user’s own PS4 copy at run time. The author documents reverse-engineered file formats and used the shadPS4 emulator as a behavior and frame reference. Fidelity to the original is author-reported, and the use of AI coding tools is disclosed. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pt-pc-port-case/))
- [Paleblood: a functional decompilation of Bloodborne with its own runtime](https://github.com/Yharnam-Hunters/Paleblood) — case-study · game — Rewrites code from Bloodborne’s PS4 executable (CUSA03173 with update 1.09) as readable C++, one function at a time, aiming at a native PC source port on a clean-room runtime of its own. It is a functional rather than matching decompilation: each replacement is hooked in, compared with the original machine code on recorded and generated inputs, and independently reviewed. Work is at an early stage: README reports 21 of 157,757 functions verified, and the game does not yet run on Paleblood’s own runtime. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/paleblood-bloodborne-functional-decomp-case/))
- [Paper Mario: matching Nintendo 64 decompilation for four releases](https://github.com/pmret/papermario) — case-study · game — Reconstructs Paper Mario’s Nintendo 64 code as C source that rebuilds the US, Japanese, PAL and iQue ROMs. The build takes assets and comparison inputs from a user-supplied original ROM, then checks each output against a recorded SHA-1. Mutable project reports list no remaining nonmatching functions, while the README still calls the decompilation work in progress; it is distinct from the PaperBoat native port. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/paper-mario-matching-decomp-case/))
- [Paper Mario: The Thousand-Year Door: an early matching GameCube decompilation](https://github.com/doldecomp/ttyd) — case-study · game — A work-in-progress matching decompilation of Paper Mario: The Thousand-Year Door that targets the Japanese Rev 0 GameCube release (G8MJ01), rebuilding MarioSt.dol and 31 REL modules with the original Metrowerks compilers and checking each against a stored SHA-1. The build splits these binaries from a user-supplied disc image, and objdiff compares objects during development. decomp.dev reported 12.10% of code matched at the reviewed commit. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/paper-mario-ttyd-decomp-case/))
- [PaperBoat: a native Paper Mario port built on the decompilation](https://github.com/HarbourMasters/PaperBoat) — case-study · game — Compiles Paper Mario decompilation sources, credited to the pmret and Paper Mario DX projects, into a native US-version application on libultraship. Torch converts the user’s own ROM into a local asset archive, and the port adds rendering backends, enhancements and archive-based mods. Desktop releases are published, while Android, iOS and web builds are documented on the development branch. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/paperboat-paper-mario-port-case/))
- [Parking sensor kit: reconstructing its display protocol](https://github.com/kunkka-project/re_parking_sensor_protocol) — case-study · protocol — This parking-sensor investigation reconstructs the signal sent from a kit's controller to its display. Oscilloscope observations and tests with different sensor states identify fields in roughly 48-bit frames for sensor address, connection and proximity. A PlatformIO example for ATmega328P reads and reports the parsed frames. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/parking-sensor-protocol-case/))
- [Perfect Dark: matching Nintendo 64 decompilation](https://github.com/n64decomp/perfect_dark) — case-study · game — Reconstructs Perfect Dark’s Nintendo 64 code with a documented matching-ROM build. The project combines reconstructed source with assets from a user-supplied original ROM, then checks version-specific artifact hashes. It is a reproducible-build research case, distinct from the separate native PC port; matching results remain project-reported here. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/perfect-dark-matching-decomp-case/))
- [Petari: a matching Super Mario Galaxy decompilation](https://github.com/SMGCommunity/Petari) — case-study · game — Reconstructs Super Mario Galaxy’s Wii code as C++ that rebuilds, object by object, against main.dol files extracted from the user’s own Japanese, North American, PAL or Korean disc. Matched objects are linked from source and checked with objdiff and a target hash. The project describes itself as a work in progress, separate from any PC port, and says it does not accept AI-generated decompilation. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/petari-smg-decomp-case/))
- [Petkit Local: reconstructing feeder cloud and fountain BLE controls](https://github.com/Opcodeffm/petkit-local) — case-study · protocol — Petkit Local replaces selected Petkit D4 feeder cloud exchanges with a local Home Assistant server and relays Eversweet Max 2 CTW3 fountain messages over BLE. The integration implements device registration, heartbeats, schedules, events and commands based on observed device communication. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/petkit-local-case/))
- [Phantom Hourglass: an early-stage matching Nintendo DS decompilation](https://github.com/zeldaret/ph) — case-study · game — Recreates C++ source for The Legend of Zelda: Phantom Hourglass by hand-decompiling its European and US Nintendo DS releases, using the dsd toolkit to delink code from a user-supplied ROM and rebuild the ROM. objdiff tracks matches function by function, and the default build compares the output ROM with a stored SHA-1. The work is at an early stage: decomp.dev reported 2.82% of code matched at the reviewed commit. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/phantom-hourglass-ds-decomp-case/))
- [PhotoCraft: editor workflow reconstruction and PSD round-trip boundaries](https://github.com/storytold/photocraft) — case-study · app — The authors describe this Rust image editor as a clean-room reimplementation of Photoshop-style workflows. Its document engine, UI and automation interfaces share editing commands, while the source distinguishes PSD editor round trips from byte preservation in the standalone file codec. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/photocraft-case/))
- [Pikmin 2: a fully matched GameCube decompilation across five releases](https://github.com/projectPiki/pikmin2) — case-study · game — A fan decompilation of Pikmin 2 that rebuilds main.dol for five GameCube releases (USA retail, two USA demos, PAL and Japan) with the original Metrowerks compilers, checking each against a stored SHA-1. The build reads main.dol from the user’s own copy of each release. decomp.dev reported all code matched and fully linked for every version at the reviewed commit, and a non-matching mode builds the US DOL entirely from C++ for modding. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pikmin-2-matching-decomp-case/))
- [Pinyon Shift: a ShiftGlue static recompilation of Forza Horizon for PC](https://github.com/arcanite24/pinyon-shift) — case-study · game — Builds a native port of the Xbox 360 release of Forza Horizon by translating its PowerPC executable ahead of time into C++ with ShiftGlue, the author’s fork of ReXGlue, and compiling it on the player’s machine from their own disc. A renderer written for this game runs its GPU commands on Vulkan, and some translated library code, such as FMOD Ex DSP units, is replaced by independently written native code checked against the translated original. README presents it as a playable preview with rendering bugs and slowdowns. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pinyon-shift-forza-horizon-recomp-case/))
- [PixMob IR reverse engineering: controlling event wristbands at home](https://github.com/danielweidman/pixmob-ir-reverse-engineering) — case-study · protocol — Recovers enough of the infrared protocol used by PixMob event wristbands to light them at home, starting from Flipper Zero recordings made at concerts and games and replaying them to bracelets. Pulse-timing analysis found 38 kHz signals with bit intervals of roughly 700 µs, and targeted brute forcing from an ESP32 discovered hundreds of colour, fade, random and motion-mode commands, now sent through Arduino, Python and web tools. The README calls the result far from exhaustive and points to a later repository that decoded most of the full IR protocol through MCU dumping. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pixmob-ir-protocol-case/))
- [PlxNative: reconstructing a native webOS media-client interface](https://github.com/GLinnik21/plx-native) — case-study · app — An unofficial Plex client built after the author investigated LG webOS native media interfaces with Ghidra and coding agents. It draws its own Rust/OpenGL interface and uses the television’s decoder, with documented host, simulator and real-device test tiers. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/plx-native-case/))
- [pocket-client: a BLE client for the Pocket voice recorder](https://github.com/Enigma-Labs-Technology/pocket-client) — case-study · protocol — pocket-client implements the Pocket voice recorder's recovered BLE command protocol in a Swift library and CLI. It documents authentication, status and recording inventory exchanges, MP3 transfer and control commands, using findings from vendor-app HCI captures and live device probes. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pocket-client-case/))
- [PocketPrinter5890: reconstructing the Lidl 5890 printer protocol](https://github.com/dimer47/PocketPrinter5890) — case-study · protocol — PocketPrinter5890 reconstructs the BLE printing protocol of Lidl's Tronic, SILVERCREST, and Parkside model 5890 pocket printers. Its specification documents activation writes, flow-control credits, and raster bands; Swift, Kotlin, and TypeScript libraries implement printing for Apple devices, Android, and browsers. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pocketprinter5890-case/))
- [pokecrystal: a matching Pokémon Crystal disassembly](https://github.com/pret/pokecrystal) — case-study · game — Reconstructs the Game Boy Color game Pokémon Crystal as RGBDS assembly, with graphics, maps and data stored as editable sources. One tree builds the international 1.0 and 1.1 releases, the Australian release, two debug builds and a Virtual Console patch, each checked against a recorded SHA-1. Companion documentation covers script command formats and known bugs in the original game. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pokecrystal-disassembly-case/))
- [pokered: a matching Pokémon Red and Blue disassembly](https://github.com/pret/pokered) — case-study · game — Reconstructs the Game Boy games Pokémon Red and Blue as RGBDS assembly, with graphics and data kept as editable source files. One tree builds the USA/Europe Red and Blue ROMs, a Blue debug build and patches for the Virtual Console releases, each checked against a recorded SHA-1. It is a full disassembly rather than a decompilation into a higher-level language. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pokered-disassembly-case/))
- [pokeruby: a matching Pokémon Ruby and Sapphire decompilation](https://github.com/pret/pokeruby) — case-study · game — Reconstructs the Game Boy Advance games Pokémon Ruby and Sapphire as C source built with pret's agbcc compiler, alongside assembly data scripts and editable graphics. Matching builds cover the English 1.0, 1.1 and 1.2 revisions, debug builds and the first German editions, each checked against a stored SHA-1. A separate modern-GCC target is not expected to match. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pokeruby-decomp-case/))
- [Pokémon Emerald: a matching Game Boy Advance decompilation](https://github.com/pret/pokeemerald) — case-study · game — Reconstructs Pokémon Emerald for the Game Boy Advance as buildable C source, with game scripts and data kept as assembler directives. The default agbcc build produces pokeemerald.gba, and make compare checks it against the SHA-1 of the original English ROM; an optional modern-GCC build is not hash-checked. Graphics and audio sources are committed in the tree, so the documented build reads no base ROM. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pokemon-emerald-decomp-case/))
- [Pokémon FireRed and LeafGreen: a matching Game Boy Advance decompilation](https://github.com/pret/pokefirered) — case-study · game — Reconstructs the English Game Boy Advance releases of Pokémon FireRed and LeafGreen as buildable C source, with game scripts and data kept as assembler directives. Default agbcc builds produce six ROM images, covering both games' 1.0 and 1.1 releases and two variants labeled switch, each checked against its own SHA-1 manifest. Graphics and audio sources are committed in the tree, so the documented build reads no base ROM. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pokemon-firered-leafgreen-decomp-case/))
- [Pokémon HeartGold and SoulSilver: a work-in-progress, checksum-matched DS disassembly](https://github.com/pret/pokeheartgold) — case-study · game — Rebuilds the US Nintendo DS releases of Pokémon HeartGold and SoulSilver from a work-in-progress disassembly in which part of the code has been decompiled to C. Each default build checks the ARM9 and ARM7 binaries, the filesystem and the complete ROM against per-game SHA-1 manifests. README warns that, because decompilation is incomplete, edits that change code or data layout can crash the game. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pokemon-heartgold-soulsilver-disassembly-case/))
- [Pokémon Platinum PC Port: a native port built on the pret decompilation](https://github.com/cybervisi0n/pokeplatinum) — case-study · game — Builds native 64-bit Windows and Linux executables of Pokémon Platinum from a fork of pret’s decompilation, replacing NitroSDK with the author’s libntr libraries; the same tree keeps the DS ROM build and an experimental Switch target. README says the game should be playable from start to finish but may crash or show graphical bugs. Prebuilt binaries require the player’s own US ROM, and the fork’s default build no longer runs pret’s checksum tests. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pokemon-platinum-pc-port-case/))
- [Pokémon Platinum: a checksum-matched Nintendo DS decompilation](https://github.com/pret/pokeplatinum) — case-study · game — Reconstructs the US Nintendo DS release of Pokémon Platinum as C source that rebuilds revision 1 by default and revision 0 on request. The default make target runs Meson checks of the code binaries, filesystem and complete ROM against SHA-1 manifests. README still calls the project work in progress; its contributing guide says the code is entirely C but much of it remains obfuscated, leaving documentation and tooling as the main work. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pokemon-platinum-decomp-case/))
- [Power BI API Scraper: decoding a published report query format](https://github.com/artemiyDev/powerbi_api_scraper) — case-study · protocol, website — Power BI API Scraper reconstructs the web client's undocumented query and response format for published reports. It sends SemanticQueryDataShapeCommand requests, follows restart tokens and expands bitsets and ValueDicts into CSV rows. The included example is hardcoded for an Ohio liquor permit report. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/power-bi-report-query-case/))
- [Project Arwing (2): Star Fox 2 recompiled from a matching disassembly](https://github.com/carlosrmz009/arwing2) — case-study · game — Rebuilds Star Fox 2 from a coverage-seeded 65816 and SuperFX disassembly that reassembles to the original ROM, then translates it statically to C for native Windows, macOS and Linux builds. Unreached code and RAM code that differs from the ROM run through an interpreter, and the README reports identical per-frame hashes against interpreted runs. It accepts the SNES Classic release image, not the 1995 prototypes; results remain project-reported. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/project-arwing-2-star-fox-2-recomp-case/))
- [Project Arwing: Star Fox recompiled from a matching disassembly](https://github.com/carlosrmz009/arwing) — case-study · game — Disassembles Star Fox’s 65816 and SuperFX code from runtime coverage and recursive descent, reassembles it into the original ROM, then translates it statically to C on a from-scratch SNES runtime for Windows, macOS and Linux. Code never reached in recorded sessions, and RAM code that no longer matches the ROM, falls back to an interpreter; the README reports identical per-frame hashes for both paths. The generated C is a mechanical translation rather than hand-decompiled source, and results remain project-reported. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/project-arwing-star-fox-recomp-case/))
- [ProjectCane: a native PC port of Sly Cooper and the Thievius Raccoonus](https://github.com/theclub654/ProjectCane) — case-study · game — Rebuilds the NTSC PlayStation 2 release of Sly Cooper and the Thievius Raccoonus as a native C++ game for Windows and Linux, running on data extracted from the player's own disc image. The README reports the full game as playable while work continues on accuracy and remaining differences from the original. It does not describe its reverse-engineering method or relate the port to the separate sly1 decompilation. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/projectcane-sly1-pc-port-case/))
- [pw: a matching decompilation of the Pokéwalker firmware](https://github.com/lumirth/pw) — case-study · firmware, game — Reconstructs as C the firmware of the Pokéwalker, the pedometer accessory for Pokémon HeartGold and SoulSilver, for its Renesas H8/38606 microcontroller. With artwork from the user’s retail firmware image and a supported Renesas compiler suite, the README reports a byte-identical rebuild of the 48 KiB image; without that image, original placeholder art gives a non-matching reference build. The author describes disassembling and decompiling the retail binary, the HeartGold/SoulSilver ROMs and the candidate compilers, alongside published hardware research. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pokewalker-matching-decomp-case/))
- [pyatv: a reverse-engineered client for Apple TV and AirPlay protocols](https://github.com/postlund/pyatv) — case-study · protocol — Asyncio Python library and command-line tools that act as an independent client for Apple TV and AirPlay devices by reimplementing the DMAP/DACP, Media Remote Protocol, Companion Link, AirPlay and RAOP protocols, which its protocol documentation describes as reverse engineered. That page records the recovered binary formats, Zeroconf properties, the OPACK encoding, pair-setup and pair-verify exchanges and Companion commands, with some request headers taken from Wireshark captures of the iPhone Remote app, and the bundled atvproxy relays MRP, Companion and AirPlay sessions to print decrypted messages for further analysis. On this basis pyatv provides remote control, metadata with push updates, app and account switching, audio-output management and AirPlay streaming to Apple TVs, HomePods and third-party receivers. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pyatv-apple-tv-airplay-protocols-case/))
- [PyEilik: a client for the Eilik robot's USB protocol](https://github.com/aklto/PyEilik) — case-study · protocol — PyEilik implements control of an Eilik desk robot over a recovered USB serial protocol. Its C library and Python wrapper move four servos, read and update the 128×64 display, and play video with synchronized movement. The protocol reference documents 125000-baud framing, command IDs, checksums and acknowledgements. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pyeilik-case/))
- [pyhaseiq: reconstructing the older Hase iQ stove's WebSocket protocol](https://github.com/bbayszczak/pyhaseiq) — case-study · protocol — pyhaseiq reconstructs the local WebSocket protocol of Hase iQ wood stoves used with the flamemonitor app and implements an asynchronous read-only Python client. The specification records base64-encoded request and response frames, serial dialogue and observation status for each finding; the client reads combustion phase, temperature and performance. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pyhaseiq-case/))
- [pypush: a Python client for Apple’s push protocol](https://github.com/JJTech0130/pypush) — case-study · protocol — Reimplements the client side of Apple’s private Apple Push Notification service (APNs) protocol in platform-independent Python: it activates as an Apple device, connects to the APNs courier, mints app-scoped tokens and receives push notifications. The project began as the author’s proof of concept for iMessage reverse engineering, but the current rewrite has removed IDS and iMessage support for now. It uses Apple’s services outside Apple’s official clients. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pypush-apns-protocol-case/))
- [QuestPad: a USB gamepad from the Quest Touch radio protocol](https://github.com/Jakey-Jakey/questpad) — case-study · protocol — QuestPad implements the Quest Touch controllers' private 2.4 GHz radio protocol on nRF52840 firmware so a computer can use them as a USB gamepad. Its protocol reference documents pairing, AES-CCM encrypted controller packets, 2 ms link frames and input data; the firmware exposes generic gamepad, DS4 and XInput modes. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/questpad-case/))
- [R.E.L.I.V.E.: an Oddworld Abe’s Oddysee and Abe’s Exoddus engine reimplementation](https://github.com/AliveTeam/alive_reversing) — case-study · game — Reimplements the engine of Oddworld: Abe’s Oddysee and Abe’s Exoddus as an open-source replacement executable that runs from the user’s own game folder. The team decompiled the original executables and replaced their functions incrementally, using injected DLLs that hook reimplemented code into the patched retail executable, then rewrote the result into a standalone cross-platform engine. The project site says both games are playable in their entirety, with bug fixes and windowed, high-resolution and controller play. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/relive-oddworld-abe-engine-case/))
- [RaceWave46: an AI-assisted Wave Race 64 recompilation for Windows](https://github.com/DomazinUS/RaceWave46) — case-study · game — Recompiles the USA Rev 1 Nintendo 64 release of Wave Race 64 into a native Windows application with N64Recomp and RSPRecomp, on modified copies of the N64ModernRuntime, RecompFrontend and RT64 stacks. Game and microcode C is generated locally from the user’s ROM, after an included widescreen patch is applied to a temporary copy, and checked against recorded checksums. The port adds higher resolutions, frame interpolation, ultrawide views, rasterized and ray-traced reflections and shadows, split-screen or separate-window two-player play and RetroAchievements; the maintainer calls it an AI-assisted recompilation. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/racewave46-wave-race-64-recomp-case/))
- [Ratchet & Clank: a matching PS2 decompilation with a documented agent workflow](https://github.com/OpenRAC/rac1-decomp) — case-study · game — Reconstructs C source for SCES_509.16, the PAL v2.00 PlayStation 2 executable of Ratchet & Clank (2002), with every function linked at its retail address. Functions not yet decompiled are still built from disassembly generated from the user's own executable, and the build audits functions, layout and the whole image against retail. The project documents an LLM-agent matching workflow and written build-fidelity rules for what counts as a match. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rac1-matching-decomp-case/))
- [rauchg.com: rebuilding an RSC blog from captured Flight output](https://github.com/boyang-hu/website-rebuild-skill/blob/ac60c9d8a2ab40045a89b5403c3457f1404e92b3/skills/website-rebuild/references/rsc-reconstruction.md) — case-study · website — Documents a website-rebuild-skill case that decodes browser-delivered React Server Component Flight streams, reconstructs a buildable Next project and compares its output with a captured site. The reported rauchg.com campaign uses route-tree comparison and module-reference mapping; the new server source is inferred from observable output. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rauchg-rsc-reconstruction-case/))
- [RazerStream: reconstructing Stream Controller device control](https://github.com/ShoelessTim/RazerStream) — case-study · protocol — RazerStream replaces the retired control software for the Razer Stream Controller with a macOS app, Swift protocol library and CLI. Its documented device protocol runs over a USB serial port, performs a WebSocket upgrade, then exchanges framed commands and input events. The app configures touchscreen tiles, knobs, buttons and LEDs. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/razerstream-case/))
- [re-plants-vs-zombies: decompiling and modernizing Plants vs. Zombies GOTY](https://github.com/Patoke/re-plants-vs-zombies) — case-study · game — Reconstructs C++ source for Plants vs. Zombies on PopCap’s open-source SexyAppFramework, starting from an earlier community decompilation of version 0.9.9 and adding functionality reversed from the Game of the Year edition, with functions annotated by GOTY addresses. The project also modernizes the engine with current C++ and is replacing the DirectDraw and Direct3D 7 renderers with GLFW. It builds a fan executable that runs from an original game folder, aiming at a modernized, extensible game rather than an identical binary. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/re-plants-vs-zombies-decomp-case/))
- [re4: a byte-identical decompilation of the Resident Evil 4 GameCube debug build](https://github.com/adonis-singh/re4) — case-study · game — Reconstructs C and C++ source for the two-disc Resident Evil 4 GameCube debug prototype (G4BE08) and rebuilds main.dol and all 114 REL overlays with the compilers the README names, checking every output against a SHA-1 list. Function names are Capcom’s own from the debug build’s Bio4.sym files, and most struct and field names are aligned from the PS2 debug build’s debug information. The project’s research log says AI agents did the matching work; byte identity is project-reported. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/re4-gamecube-debug-decomp-case/))
- [REDRIVER2: reconstructing Driver 2 through a PlayStation compatibility layer](https://github.com/OpenDriver2/REDRIVER2) — case-study · game — Translates reverse-engineered Driver 2 MIPS routines into C/C++ and uses Psy-X/Psy-Cross to adapt PlayStation interfaces for desktop systems. The project documents symbol-assisted Ghidra/IDA work alongside intentional bug fixes and gameplay changes. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/redriver2-case/))
- [reflecticon-224: running Lexicon 224 reverb firmware on emulated hardware](https://github.com/joelanders/reflecticon-224) — case-study · firmware — Emulates the Lexicon 224, 224X and 224XL digital reverbs so that their own firmware runs in a browser page or a JUCE plugin. The repository layers KiCad schematics, gate-level and board-level Verilog models, an 8080-hosted C++ emulator and analog input/output models, and checks faster layers against slower ones using user-supplied ROMs. It is a work-in-progress hardware-and-firmware emulation whose documentation is still pending, not recovered algorithm source. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/reflecticon-224-lexicon-emulation-case/))
- [retruxx: reverse-engineered Hard Truck Apocalypse](https://github.com/mindflower/retruxx) — case-study · game — retruxx is a reverse-engineered Hard Truck Apocalypse project that builds the game executable and separate renderer, input, and sound DLLs. Its 32-bit build matches the original game's memory layouts. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/retruxx/))
- [RK M75: reconstructing wired and wireless RGB control](https://github.com/Lightning-13/rk-m75) — case-study · protocol — RK M75 reconstructs RGB control for the Royal Kludge RK M75 keyboard from wired USB HID captures and 2.4 GHz wireless experiments. A Python library sends per-key wired frames and grouped wireless frames; the repository also contains protocol notes, captures and a wired SignalRGB plugin. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rk-m75-case/))
- [RKD Telemetry Extractor: decoding Race-Keeper track-day recordings](https://github.com/sam-dumont/rkd-telemetry-extractor) — case-study · app — Documents the proprietary .rkd telemetry format written by Race-Keeper “Instant Video” recorders, whose bundled player runs only on Windows, and implements matching Python and Go parsers that export GPS and IMU channels as Telemetry Overlay CSV and GPX. The author credits Claude Code with the hex-dump analysis and hypothesis testing, anchored by known car IDs, circuit location and speeds. Field meanings rest on physical cross-checks from the author’s own recordings, and several record types remain partly unknown. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rkd-telemetry-extractor-case/))
- [RND Companion: reconstructing the RND Synth SysEx protocol](https://github.com/Enkerli/rnd-companion) — case-study · protocol — RND Companion reconstructs the Cymaforma RND Synth's SysEx control vocabulary and implements a C++ codec and companion plugin for patch seeds. The protocol notes identify framing and seed or status commands from a web app and one hardware capture. The client can use a direct MIDI port or host MIDI routing and stores a curated seed library. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rnd-companion-case/))
- [Roborock Local Server: a self-hosted stand-in for Roborock’s vacuum cloud](https://github.com/Python-roborock/local_roborock_server) — case-study · protocol — Runs a local HTTPS and MQTT stack in place of Roborock’s cloud, so supported vacuums keep live maps and controls on an isolated network without rooting or hardware changes. The maintainer’s technical write-up recounts earlier app and traffic analysis and a later investigation in which Claude Code and Codex worked against an emulated firmware environment. Compatibility is reported per model by the project and its users. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/roborock-local-server-cloud-case/))
- [RSDKv3 Decompilation: Sonic CD (2011) on a reconstructed Retro Engine v3](https://github.com/RSDKModding/RSDKv3-Decompilation) — case-study · game — Reconstructs Retro Engine v3, the engine behind the 2011 Sonic CD remaster, as a native C++ source port for Windows, Linux, macOS and Android. The engine runs the game's own scripts and assets from an official release, which the repository does not ship, and adds a mod loader, a settings file with remappable controls, support for the mobile versions' updated scripts and extra developer-menu debug keys. Its license describes the code as mostly functionally the same as the original rather than byte-matching; the same team's RSDKv5 decompilation is listed as a separate case. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rsdkv3-sonic-cd-decomp-case/))
- [RSDKv4 Decompilation: Retro Engine v4 and the Sonic 1 & 2 (2013) menus](https://github.com/RSDKModding/RSDKv4-Decompilation) — case-study · game — Decompiles Retro Engine (RSDK) v4, the engine behind Sonic the Hedgehog and Sonic the Hedgehog 2 (2013), together with the games’ natively coded menus, and builds it for Windows, Linux and Android. The engine runs the game scripts contained in the official releases’ data files, which users must supply. The project adds a script compiler, a mod loader and an experimental online mode for Sonic 2’s 2P VS; it is the predecessor of the same group’s RSDKv5 decompilation. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rsdkv4-sonic-1-2-decomp-case/))
- [RSDKv5 Decompilation: reconstructing Retro Engine v5 and v5U](https://github.com/RSDKModding/RSDKv5-Decompilation) — case-study · game — Reconstructs the Retro Engine (RSDK) v5 and v5 Ultimate runtime, the engine that runs Sonic Mania’s game code, and builds it for Windows, Linux, Switch and Android. RSDKv5U can also run RSDKv3 and RSDKv4 games such as Sonic CD and Sonic 1 & 2. The project adds a mod loader, a shader compiler and extra Windows backends, and ships no game data. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rsdkv5-engine-decomp-case/))
- [RTO eChalan: investigating a BankBot Android APK](https://github.com/Gautam-CyberSec/Android-Malware-Analysis-Report) — case-study · app — The RTO eChalan report investigates an Android APK impersonating an Indian government eChallan service through manifest review, JADX decompilation and sandbox analysis. The author reports identifying a BankBot variant, a REQUEST_INSTALL_PACKAGES permission, a BIND_VPN_SERVICE entry and extensive obfuscation that limited decompilation. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rto-echalan-android-analysis-case/))
- [Ruffle: a Rust reimplementation of Adobe Flash Player](https://github.com/ruffle-rs/ruffle) — case-study · game, website — Reimplements Adobe Flash Player in Rust so that SWF content, from ActionScript 1/2 (AVM1) to ActionScript 3 (AVM2), runs in a native desktop player or in browsers through WebAssembly, as a self-hosted script or a browser extension. Its contributor guide says behavior comes from observing Flash Player's output or from license-compatible sources such as Adobe's open avmplus code, and forbids decompiling Flash Player, Flash Professional or Animate. Regression tests run contributor-made SWFs and compare their trace output, and optionally rendered images, with captures from the official debug Flash Player. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ruffle-flash-player-case/))
- [Saints Reborn: a ReXGlue static recompilation of Saints Row](https://github.com/whompay/SaintsReborn) — case-study · game — An unofficial native Windows port of Saints Row (Xbox 360, 2006) that statically recompiles the game's PowerPC executable to C++ for x86-64 with the ReXGlue SDK, which also reimplements the console's kernel, GPU (on Direct3D 12), audio and input. A recompiler manifest, game-specific function overrides and an SDK patch make the result run, and each user builds it locally from their own disc. The README reports the game fully playable with built-in co-op and lists known issues. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/saints-reborn-recomp-case/))
- [Salamander UEFI: a disassembly-based UEFI port of the NES game](https://github.com/MikeWuPing/UEFI_Salamander) — case-study · game — Salamander UEFI ports the NES game Salamander to a native UEFI Shell application by translating its 6502 disassembly into a platform-independent C core. The repository includes a 6502 disassembler, a user-ROM resource extractor, host tests and a fidelity checklist. UEFI GOP, input and timing services replace the original console environment. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/salamander-uefi-case/))
- [San Andreas Unity: a GTA San Andreas engine reimplementation in Unity](https://github.com/in0finite/SanAndreasUnity) — case-study · game — Reimplements the Grand Theft Auto: San Andreas engine in Unity (C#), loading the archives, RenderWare models and textures, collision, animations, audio, text and item definitions from the user’s own PC installation. README says it will not be a complete reimplementation; it focuses on gameplay features, multiplayer and a framework for extending and modding the game through C# plugins. Development has stalled: the newest commit on any branch dates from 2022-09-30. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/san-andreas-unity-engine-case/))
- [Savage Wind Recomp: statically recompiling The Legend of Zelda: Breath of the Wild](https://github.com/Revivedx/SavageWindRecomp) — case-study · game — Savage Wind Recomp is an in-progress static recompilation of the Nintendo Switch version of The Legend of Zelda: Breath of the Wild for Windows and Linux. It builds native game code locally from the player's extracted files; the README reports playable early areas but says the full story remains unconfirmed. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/savagewindrecomp/))
- [Scooter Link: a Xiaomi scooter client built from BLE protocol research](https://github.com/desperado0044/xiaomi-scooter-link) — case-study · protocol — Scooter Link implements an Android client for Xiaomi Electric Scooter 5 Pro and 5 Max using a reconstructed BLE authentication protocol. Its research log covers GATT characteristics and investigation of the ECDH P-256, HKDF-SHA256, and AES-CCM exchange; the app reads ride and battery data and controls supported settings. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/scooter-link-case/))
- [ScooterLink: reconstructing Honda RoadSync’s handlebar-button protocol](https://github.com/gdorgian/ScooterLink) — case-study · app, protocol — Replaces Honda RoadSync’s Apple Music-only media control with an iOS app that pairs with a Honda PCX 2025 TFT dash over Bluetooth Low Energy and turns handlebar presses into system-wide commands for any player. The author documents the dash’s BLE services, key exchange, button registration and event bytes, reportedly worked out from the official Android app and logged traffic. Results are author-reported on one PCX125, and the protocol notes separate confirmed from unverified parts. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/scooterlink-honda-roadsync-case/))
- [scvk: replacing SimCity 4's renderer with Vulkan](https://github.com/aspctt/scvk) — case-study · game — scvk reconstructs SimCity 4's renderer interface as a native Vulkan DLL loaded through the game's GZCOM plugin system. It claims existing renderer class IDs and emulates the cIGZGDriver fixed-function API with pipeline state and shaders for terrain, buildings, lighting, textures and interface drawing. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/scvk-case/))
- [sead: a decompilation of Nintendo’s first-party C++ standard library](https://github.com/open-ead/sead) — case-study · game — Decompiles sead, the C++ standard library that Nintendo statically links into its first-party games, covering containers, heaps, strings, math, file devices, threads, the game framework and graphics. Compile-time presets target the recent versions used by Breath of the Wild, Super Mario Odyssey, Splatoon 3, Tears of the Kingdom and Super Mario Bros. Wonder, and game decompilations such as Breath of the Wild pull it in as a submodule. Names come from debugging symbols and assertions in retail titles, and the README warns that many inlined and templated names are guesses. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/sead-nintendo-library-decomp-case/))
- [SF2Recomp: statically recompiling Shadow Fight 2 for native platforms](https://github.com/bipinkrish/sf2recomp) — case-study · game — SF2Recomp converts a user's Shadow Fight 2 Nintendo Switch game files into native builds for Linux, Windows, macOS, and Android. Its converter combines translated game code with a runtime and user-supplied assets, adding controls, graphics options, sparring, and LAN play. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/sf2recomp-case/))
- [shark-k86-mac: a client for the Attack Shark K86 HID protocol](https://github.com/RaphaelCaputo2/shark-k86-mac) — case-study · protocol — shark-k86-mac reconstructs the Attack Shark K86 keyboard's vendor HID controls as a Python CLI and library for macOS and Linux. Its clean-room protocol notes describe 64-byte feature reports, checksums and opcodes; the client controls lighting and the TFT screen and reads device information. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/shark-k86-mac-case/))
- [Sheypoor MCP: mapping marketplace APIs from browser traffic](https://github.com/FarhamAghdasi/sheypoor-mcp) — case-study · website — Sheypoor MCP implements a client and MCP server for the Sheypoor classifieds site using API behavior documented from browser traffic. Its reference records observed endpoints, request headers, cookies and phone-based login. The server exposes listing search and details, account functions, chat room lists and unread message counts to MCP clients. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/sheypoor-mcp-case/))
- [Ship of Harkinian: a native Ocarina of Time port built on the decompilation](https://github.com/HarbourMasters/Shipwright) — case-study · game — Builds Ocarina of Time as a native PC application from the OoT decompilation’s source, with libultraship standing in for the N64 libultra SDK and DirectX 11, OpenGL or Metal rendering. No game assets are shipped: they are converted from the user’s supported ROM into an .o2r archive, and the port adds save states, alternate and custom assets and other enhancements. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ship-of-harkinian-oot-port-case/))
- [SiegeFX: a Dungeon Siege engine reimplementation in alpha](https://github.com/codingncaffeine/SiegeFX) — case-study · game — A C#/.NET 10 engine loads Dungeon Siege's original archives, scripts and models, with published alpha builds and an audit/parity ledger. The project also provides SiegeSmith modding tools, while describing remaining fidelity and playthrough risks. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/siegefx-dungeon-siege-engine-case/))
- [Sin & Punishment: a work-in-progress N64 static recompilation](https://github.com/maximilianoide/sin-punishment-recompiled) — case-study · game — Sin & Punishment: Recompiled uses N64Recomp and RSPRecomp to build an experimental native C++ port from a user-supplied Japanese N64 ROM and the repository's symbol maps, with RT64 rendering. The author reports that it reaches the title screen; menu navigation, gameplay, audio validation, and a public pipeline for its patched working image remain unfinished. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/sin-punishment-recompiled-case/))
- [Siriously: recovering macOS Writing Tools entry points](https://github.com/bo2themax/Siriously) — case-study · app, binary — Siriously investigates how macOS 27 changed access to Writing Tools and restores its popover through a Services client. The author used Objective-C runtime introspection and behavioral tracing to identify private WritingToolsUI classes, then passes selected text to the popover and writes accepted results back through Accessibility APIs. The findings concern one macOS build. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/siriously-writing-tools-case/))
- [Skate 3 Recomp: a native Xbox 360 recompilation through ReXGlue](https://github.com/mchughalex/skate3recomp) — case-study · game — Recompiles the Xbox 360 version of Skate 3 into native Windows, Linux and macOS builds with a Skate-specific fork of the ReXGlue SDK. Since v2.0.0 a native Direct3D 12 and Vulkan renderer with ports of the game’s own material shading replaces emulation of the Xbox 360 GPU, which remains as a fallback. Game files come from the user’s disc image; the author calls the native renderer early and has not tested the game end to end. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/skate3-recomp-case/))
- [Skate 3 Rust Engine: a Bevy rewrite built on Skate 3 reverse-engineering research](https://github.com/SK8-ENGINE/skate-3-rust-engine) — case-study · game — Reimplements Skate 3’s skating, tricks, grinds and offboard movement in a new Rust and Bevy engine that runs on assets converted from the user’s own Xbox 360 copy, adding difficulty settings and a .skate custom-map format. Physics notes cite functions and constants read from the original executable in IDA, and audio research reads traces from a research-hooks branch of a separate Skate 3 static recompilation. README says gameplay parity is still a work in progress, credits dumbad’s years of format research and states that AI coding tools were used to write the rewrite. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/skate-3-rust-engine-case/))
- [Skyward Sword: Wii decompilation with hash-checked relinking](https://github.com/zeldaret/ss) — case-study · game — Reconstructs C++ source for The Legend of Zelda: Skyward Sword’s Wii main.dol and REL modules, starting from the user’s NTSC-U 1.0 (SOUE01) disc files. Objects marked matching are compiled from source and linked with objects split from the original, and the result is checked against SHA-1 hashes; the repository ships no assets or assembly and is not a port. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/skyward-sword-decomp-case/))
- [Sly Cooper and the Thievius Raccoonus: matching PlayStation 2 decompilation](https://github.com/TheOnlyZac/sly1) — case-study · game — Reconstructs source code for SCUS_971.98, the NTSC-U PlayStation 2 executable of Sly Cooper and the Thievius Raccoonus, as a work-in-progress matching decompilation. Functions not yet matched are still assembled from code split out of the user's own executable, and the build checks the linked ELF's SHA-1. The result runs in PCSX2 with the original disc for assets; it is not a native PC port. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/sly1-matching-decomp-case/))
- [sm64ex: an enhanced Super Mario 64 PC port from the decompilation](https://github.com/sm64pc/sm64ex) — case-study · game — Builds native Super Mario 64 executables from decompiled source, extending the sm64-port fork of the n64decomp reconstruction with renderer, input, camera, save-format and external-data options. Game assets are extracted from the user’s own ROM at build time. It is a non-matching enhanced port, not a byte-identical ROM reconstruction. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/sm64ex-pc-port-case/))
- [Snap64 Recomp: a static-recompilation port of Pokémon Snap](https://github.com/JackandBeans/Snap64Recomp) — case-study · game — Uses N64Recomp to statically recompile the US Nintendo 64 release of Pokémon Snap into a native Windows, Linux, Steam Deck and macOS port rendered with RT64. The recompiler reads a relocatable ELF linked from the matching ethteck/pokemonsnap decompilation together with the user’s ROM, and game-side patches are modified copies of decompiled functions. The author reports building the port with Claude Code; results remain project-reported. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/snap64-recomp-case/))
- [Snowboard Kids 2: an LLM-assisted N64 matching decompilation](https://github.com/cdlewis/snowboardkids2-decomp) — case-study · game — A public matching decompilation of the N64 game Snowboard Kids 2, begun in 2024 with community decompilation tools. The author’s dated posts document Claude, Codex and GLM workflows, including headless one-shot loops, similarity-based function scheduling and hook-guarded, hash-checked builds, through a reported complete function match. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/snowboardkids2-decomp-case/))
- [Snowboard Kids: an agent-assisted N64 matching decompilation](https://github.com/cdlewis/snowboardkids-decomp) — case-study · game — A matching decompilation of the original Nintendo 64 Snowboard Kids that rebuilds SHA1-identical North American and Japanese ROMs from a user-supplied ROM. The author’s 84-day post-mortem describes coding agents run through his Nigel harness alongside m2c, the permuter and a shared file of compiler learnings, and declines to credit LLMs alone for the speed-up. It is a separate project from the Snowboard Kids 2 decompilation. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/snowboardkids-matching-decomp-case/))
- [Sonic Advance 1 & 2: a matching GBA decompilation with widescreen ports](https://github.com/SAT-R/sa2) — case-study · game — Reconstructs Sonic Advance and Sonic Advance 2 for the Game Boy Advance as C that rebuilds six hash-listed ROMs with SAT-R’s agbcc fork, and compiles the same code into widescreen SDL ports for Linux, macOS and Windows plus PSP and PS2 builds. README reports Sonic Advance 2 built entirely from C with about 60 functions still non-matching. Extracted graphics, music and data are committed, so the builds read no original ROM. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/sonic-advance-1-2-decomp-port-case/))
- [Sonic Advance 3: an AI-assisted match of one enemy module](https://gambiconf.substack.com/p/development-journey-on-game-decompilation) — case-study · game — Macabeus’s dated study describes prompting Claude Sonnet 3.5 and DeepSeek R1 with assembly, similar decompiled functions and structs to write matching C for Marun, one enemy module of the community Sonic Advance 3 GBA decompilation. The resulting pull request replaced that module’s assembly and was merged; the author calls the setting unusually AI-friendly, and the project now bars LLM-origin contributions. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/sa3-marun-ai-decomp-case/))
- [Sonic Mania: a game-code decompilation for the RSDKv5 engine](https://github.com/RSDKModding/Sonic-Mania-Decompilation) — case-study · game — Reconstructs Sonic Mania’s game code in C and builds it with the separately decompiled RSDKv5 or RSDKv5U engine for Windows, Linux, Switch and Android. Assets must come from an official Sonic Mania (Plus) release; the project adds a mod loader and can build several retail versions. Its license describes the code as mostly functionally the same as the original, not byte-matching. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/sonic-mania-decomp-case/))
- [Sony CDX-A250: reconstructing the LCD panel interface](https://github.com/emsyscode/lc75826-sony-cdx-a250) — case-study · protocol — Sony CDX-A250 LCD Panel reconstructs the serial control needed to drive the radio's detachable display independently through its LC75826W driver. Wiring notes, CCB transfer documentation, and an Arduino sketch cover display-data groups, control bits, test patterns, and experimental segment mapping. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/lc75826-sony-cdx-a250-case/))
- [Sony Headphones Client: a desktop client for Sony’s headset control protocol](https://github.com/Plutoberth/SonyHeadphonesClient) — case-study · app, protocol — Recreates part of Sony’s mobile-only Headphones app as a Windows, Linux and macOS desktop client that changes noise-cancelling, ambient-sound and virtual-sound settings over a Bluetooth RFCOMM connection to a paired headset. The author reports reverse engineering the phone app to define its control protocol, and the code rebuilds its message framing, with start and end markers, byte escaping, sequence numbers, a sum checksum and acknowledgements, together with the two set-parameter commands the client sends. Full support is reported for few models, mainly the WH-1000XM3, and the project is archived. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/sony-headphones-client-case/))
- [sony-bravia-linux: pre-Android BRAVIA platform and media behavior](https://github.com/danielcamposramos/sony-bravia-linux) — case-study · firmware — sony-bravia-linux investigates the Linux-based, pre-Android Sony BRAVIA platform and reconstructs selected media and portal behavior for owner-run services. Its documentation records observations from 2011–2012 AZ1, AZ2 and AZ3 sets, including remote input, browser media handling and 3D signalling. The repository also contains tools for H.264 3D SEI and related media workflows. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/sony-bravia-linux-case/))
- [Sound Blaster AE-9: reconstructing Linux control from Windows driver analysis](https://github.com/verzic/sb-ae9) — case-study · binary, protocol — Sound Blaster AE-9 Linux support reconstructs card and Audio Control Module behavior using static analysis of Creative's Windows drivers and hardware observations. The included investigations document CtxHda.sys, CtxHdb.sys, and the ACM's SysEx-framed UART protocol; the driver implements selected playback and controls. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/sb-ae9-case/))
- [Soundcore D3200: reconstructing BLE recording transfer and decryption](https://github.com/andrewseago/d3200-ble-note-downloader) — case-study · protocol — A Soundcore D3200 investigation reconstructs the BLE exchange used to list and transfer encrypted recordings. Its Python client performs a P-256 ECDH handshake, derives session and file keys, decrypts AES-256-CTR Opus packets and writes an Ogg/Opus file. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/soundcore-d3200-ble-case/))
- [SpaceCadetPinball: decompiled game logic adapted to SDL2](https://github.com/k4zmu2a/SpaceCadetPinball) — case-study · game — Converts the reverse-engineered Space Cadet pinball game into compilable C++ for desktop platforms. The README identifies the Windows XP executable and public PDB, a Full Tilt executable, and the work of recovering structures, naming variables and adapting the decompiled routines. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/space-cadet-pinball-case/))
- [SpaghettiKart: a native Mario Kart 64 port built on decompiled source](https://github.com/HarbourMasters/SpaghettiKart) — case-study · game — Builds a native Mario Kart 64 port for Windows, Linux, macOS and Switch from source that keeps the Mario Kart 64 decompilation’s file layout, running on libultraship’s reimplemented N64 SDK. Its bundled Torch extractor converts the user’s US ROM into an asset archive, and the port adds mods, custom tracks and an editor. The README does not name its upstream decompilation, and no matching or fidelity checks are published. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/spaghettikart-mk64-port-case/))
- [Spark Mail: rehosting the Windows desktop app on Linux with Wine shims](https://github.com/kyryl-bogach/spark-mail-linux) — case-study · app — spark-mail-linux rehosts the Windows Spark Mail desktop app on Linux with Wine, two compatibility DLL shims and launcher scripts. The project documents application startup, browser authentication, notifications, clipboard and file-opening behavior, then patches specific DLL imports and bridges those functions to the Linux desktop. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/spark-mail-linux-case/))
- [SpeedBreaker: statically recompiling Need for Speed: Most Wanted for Xbox 360](https://github.com/SpeedBreakerProject/speedbreaker) — case-study · game — Translates the Xbox 360 executable of Need for Speed: Most Wanted (2005) into native C++ with a patched XenonRecomp and runs it on a project runtime that stands in for the console’s kernel, Xenos GPU, XMA audio and controllers. Releases target SteamOS, Linux and Apple silicon Macs, with an experimental iPhone and iPad build, while all game data comes from the player’s own US disc. Rendering and frame-pacing improvements are project-reported. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/speedbreaker-nfs-most-wanted-case/))
- [Spider-Man 2 PS2 Recomp: a static recompilation checkpoint](https://github.com/saltyboosack-blip/Spider-Man-2-PS2-Recomp) — case-study · game — Spider-Man 2 PS2 Recomp applies static recompilation to the USA PlayStation 2 release SLUS-20776 for native PC execution. It combines a game-specific Ghidra function map with PS2Recomp source, runtime fixes and hardware comparison tests. The author reports that a native Windows build reaches the legal splash; the port is not playable. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/spider-man-2-ps2-recomp-case/))
- [ssbu-recomp: static recompilation with explicit mode limits](https://github.com/Denkishi/ssbu-recomp) — case-study · game — Translates Super Smash Bros. Ultimate 13.0.4 AArch64 code ahead of time and supplies a Windows runtime with Vulkan/OpenGL rendering. It also reconstructs selected fighter scripts as editable C++ and compares translated/source behavior through differential and deterministic tests. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ssbu-recomp-case/))
- [Stadiacast: reconstructing the Stadia Controller's Wi-Fi service](https://github.com/jkfran/stadiacast) — case-study · protocol — Stadiacast recreates the network service used by the Stadia Controller's Wi-Fi firmware so the controller can connect to a local computer. The receiver answers boot and discovery requests, completes WebRTC and DTLS sessions, decodes input and sends rumble commands. Protocol notes record findings from firmware reading and live controller runs, with protobuf schemas and receiver code in the repository. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/stadiacast-case/))
- [stagg-net: investigating the Fellow Stagg EKG kettle UART](https://github.com/linux4life798/stagg-net) — case-study · protocol — stagg-net investigates the Fellow Stagg EKG kettle's internal UART interface and documents its connector, serial settings, observed heartbeat packet, and startup message. The repository includes a logic capture, decoder source, probing scripts, and ESPHome configuration for an ESP32-C6 connection. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/stagg-net-case/))
- [Star Fox 64: a complete matching Nintendo 64 decompilation](https://github.com/sonicdcer/sf64) — case-study · game — Reconstructs C source for the US 1.1 (Rev A) Nintendo 64 release of Star Fox 64 that, compiled with the IDO compiler through a static recompilation, rebuilds a ROM with the published MD5; the README badge counts all 2,951 functions as matching. Code and assets are split from the user’s own ROM at build time, and the repository contains no assets or assembly. The separately curated Starship PC port compiles this C code, while Starfox 64: Recompiled takes headers and some functions from it for its patches. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/sf64-matching-decomp-case/))
- [Starfox 64: Recompiled — an N64Recomp static-recompilation port](https://github.com/sonicdcer/Starfox64Recomp) — case-study · game — Uses N64: Recompiled to statically recompile the US 1.1 (Rev A) Nintendo 64 release of Star Fox 64 into a native port for Windows, Linux and macOS, rendered with RT64. Its patches and enhancements draw on headers and some functions from the separate sf64 decompilation, but the port is not compiled from that decompilation's source. The README reports high-frame-rate, widescreen and mod support, with assets loaded from the user's own ROM. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/starfox64-recompiled-case/))
- [Starship: a Star Fox 64 PC port built from the decompilation](https://github.com/HarbourMasters/Starship) — case-study · game — A native PC port of Star Fox 64 that compiles the game's decompiled C code against libultraship, an N64 porting library that reimplements libultra and renders through Fast3D. On first run it uses the Torch tool to turn the user's US ROM into an asset archive, and it ships for Windows, Linux, macOS and Switch. The README of the separate Starfox 64: Recompiled static recompilation calls this port its sibling, built with different technology. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/starship-sf64-pc-port-case/))
- [SteelSeries Overlay Hook: mapping and hooking the GameOverlay swapchain](https://github.com/meowdiocre/ss-overlay-hook) — case-study · app, binary — SteelSeries Overlay Hook documents IDA analysis of SteelSeriesGameOverlay.dll’s window, object layout and Direct3D 11 swapchain, then uses recovered offsets in a C++ DLL hook. Its injector and hook render Dear ImGui content through the existing GameOverlay window by intercepting Present and ResizeBuffers. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/steelseries-overlay-hook-case/))
- [Stickwalker: porting Pokéwalker firmware to the Stick S3](https://github.com/lumirth/stickwalker) — case-study · firmware — Stickwalker ports a matching reconstruction of Pokéwalker firmware to the M5Stack Stick S3. The repository includes firmware source, build tooling and placeholder artwork; users can build with artwork extracted from their own ROM, and a BPV10NF photodiode supplies infrared reception. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/stickwalker-case/))
- [Super Mario 64 DS: matching decompilation with relocation checks](https://github.com/tangosdev/sm64ds-decomp) — case-study · game — Reconstructs Super Mario 64 DS functions as C/C++ and permitted assembly primitives, comparing compiled output with a user-supplied cartridge dump. Independent relocation-destination checks strengthen the matching gate; integration into the PC port is tracked separately. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/sm64ds-decomp-case/))
- [Super Mario 64: ROM reconstruction with explicit comparison gates](https://github.com/n64decomp/sm64) — case-study · game — Reconstructs Super Mario 64 source for Japanese, North American, European, Shindou and iQue releases. Its build files distinguish original-ROM hash comparison from nonmatching or modified configurations, while source naming and documentation remain ongoing. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/sm64-decomp-case/))
- [Super Mario Odyssey: per-function matching decompilation of the Switch executable](https://github.com/MonsterDruide1/OdysseyDecomp) — case-study · game — Reconstructs C++ source for Super Mario Odyssey’s Switch 1.0 main executable and checks each function against the original AArch64 machine code with the shared nx-decomp-tools checker. At the pinned commit its function list marks 18,996 of 74,171 functions matching. The README warns that the build is not a playable game, the repository ships no game assets, and Nintendo libraries such as sead and agl come from open-ead’s separate reconstructions. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/odyssey-decomp-case/))
- [Super Mario Sunshine (USA): an AI-assisted matching decompilation fork](https://github.com/chasem-dev/sms-english) — case-study · game — Extends the doldecomp Super Mario Sunshine decompilation to the North American GMSE01 release. The README says AI was used extensively, and the repository’s agent rules, work order and progress files describe batch-based Claude and Codex orchestration. Its rebuilt DOL is reported byte-identical, but unfinished objects are still linked from code extracted from the user’s disc. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/sms-english-gmse01-decomp-case/))
- [Super Mario Sunshine: a native PC port from an AI-assisted decompilation](https://github.com/chasem-dev/sms-pc-port) — case-study · game — Builds a native PC port of Super Mario Sunshine’s North American release from a pinned matching-decompilation fork, adding a host platform layer for graphics, disc, audio, input and OS services. The fork’s README says AI was used extensively to reconstruct and match its code; the port documents scripted regression hashes and lockstep comparisons with retail captures. Functions that do not yet match can still behave differently from retail, and the game must come from the user’s own disc image. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/sms-pc-port-case/))
- [Super Mario Sunshine: an in-progress matching GameCube decompilation](https://github.com/doldecomp/sms) — case-study · game — Reconstructs Super Mario Sunshine’s C++ source so that the original Metrowerks compiler reproduces byte-identical object files for four GameCube releases and the Switch 3D All-Stars build. The target binary comes from the user’s own disc image, and objdiff compares rebuilt objects function by function. The work is incomplete, and the repository publishes contribution rules for AI agents alongside its human workflow. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/sms-matching-decomp-case/))
- [Super Smash Bros. Brawl: an early matching Wii decompilation](https://github.com/doldecomp/brawl) — case-study · game — A work-in-progress matching decompilation of Super Smash Bros. Brawl that targets the USA Rev 2 Wii release (RSBE01_02), splitting main.dol and 126 REL modules from a user-supplied disc image and checking the rebuilt binaries against stored SHA-1 values. objdiff compares objects during development, and six other regional revisions are listed but commented out of the supported set. decomp.dev reported 1.27% of code matched at the reviewed commit. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/smash-bros-brawl-decomp-case/))
- [Super Smash Bros. Melee: a matching GameCube decompilation with a shiftable build](https://github.com/doldecomp/melee) — case-study · game — A matching decompilation of Super Smash Bros. Melee's US 1.02 release (GALE01) that rebuilds main.dol from C with the original Metrowerks compilers and checks it against the retail SHA-1. decomp.dev reports the code fully decompiled and linked at the reviewed commit, and the README says current work is mostly naming and cleanup. Because the build can be shifted, a separate non-matching mode lets modders add code to a DOL run with their own disc dump. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/melee-matching-decomp-case/))
- [Supermodel: initial Real3D Pro-1000 image-generator emulation](https://github.com/trzy/Supermodel) — case-study · firmware — Adds emulation of Real3D’s Pro-1000, the SCSI-attached image generator from which Sega’s Model 3 graphics hardware derives, to the Supermodel arcade emulator. It boots user-supplied Pro-1000 firmware from the SDK disc, emulates the board’s interrupt, JTAG and UART hardware, and exposes the firmware’s built-in monitor on the console. Nothing renders yet, and SCSI host communication is unimplemented. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/supermodel-real3d-pro1000-emulation-case/))
- [SVR2011-Recomp: a ReXGlue recompilation of WWE SmackDown vs. Raw 2011](https://github.com/shaneomac1337/SVR2011-Recomp) — case-study · game — Translates the Xbox 360 executable of WWE SmackDown vs. Raw 2011 to C++ ahead of time with ReXGlue and builds it as a Windows program on a Vulkan runtime derived from Xenia. A hand-maintained manifest adds function entry points the automatic pass misses, and project patches address frame pacing, a music-player delay and pipeline stalls. Exhibition play is author-tested on one AMD GPU; career mode and online remain untested. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/svr2011-recomp-case/))
- [SymphonyRecomp: a RecompOne static recompilation of Symphony of the Night](https://github.com/BlackLabelHQ/SymphonyRecomp) — case-study · game — Statically recompiles the North American PlayStation release of Castlevania: Symphony of the Night into C# with BlackLabelHQ’s RecompOne, whose runtime simulates the BIOS, memory-mapped I/O, GPU and CD-ROM so the game runs natively without a console BIOS. Per-overlay function maps and patches add widescreen, quality-of-life, randomizer and cheat features. References from the SOTN decompilation help name functions, but the project stresses that it is a recompilation, not a decompilation. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/symphonyrecomp-case/))
- [Syphon Filter: The Omega Strain — matching PS2 executable decompilation](https://github.com/zm2283145/syphon-filter-omega-strain-decomp) — case-study · game — Reconstructs much of the NTSC-U PlayStation 2 executable of Syphon Filter: The Omega Strain as C; with the remaining functions kept as split assembly, the build reproduces the retail SCUS_972.64 byte for byte. Committed objdiff reports track per-function matching against the user’s own executable. This is a matching-decompilation case, not a PC port; progress figures remain project-reported. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/syphon-filter-omega-strain-decomp-case/))
- [TCLimate: reconstructing selected TCL Home cloud controls](https://github.com/umarz317/TCLimate) — case-study · app, protocol — Implements selected TCL Home air-conditioner controls and electricity reporting in a native macOS menu-bar app. It adapts earlier community protocol work and documents an Android-module investigation to recover a separate statistics request. Reported validation checks cloud state, not independent physical operation or complete device equivalence. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/tclimate-cloud-controls-case/))
- [TeddyCloud: a self-hosted replacement for the Toniebox cloud](https://github.com/toniebox-reverse-engineering/teddycloud) — case-study · firmware, protocol — Reimplements the Boxine cloud services a Toniebox talks to as a self-hosted C server, so the box can fetch audio, settings and over-the-air content locally while the owner controls what reaches the manufacturer. It serves and caches tonie audio, converts other audio into the box’s TAF format, can pass requests through to the original cloud, and adds a web interface, MQTT and Home Assistant integration. Protobuf definitions in the repository describe the box’s freshness-check, RTNL log and TAF header messages. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/teddycloud-toniebox-server-case/))
- [TH04: rebuilding Lotus Land Story’s four PC-98 programs from source](https://github.com/N0zoM1z0/th04) — case-study · game — Reconstructs source for the Japanese PC-98 Touhou 4: Lotus Land Story and rebuilds all four DOS programs, MAIN.EXE, OP.EXE, MAINE.EXE and ZUN.COM, with a pinned Borland toolchain under Wine. Function acceptance against the original binaries is tracked separately from emulator replays that compare inputs, RNG, object pools and video with the original game. Complete-campaign, original-timing and byte-identity goals remain open. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/th04-reconstruction-case/))
- [TH08 Web: a WebAssembly port built from reconstructed source](https://github.com/N0zoM1z0/th08-web) — case-study · game — Compiles the reconstructed C++ source of the Japanese Touhou 8 1.00d release to WebAssembly with Emscripten, adding WebGL 2 rendering, Web Audio, browser input and IndexedDB saves behind the game’s D3D8-, DirectSound- and DirectInput-shaped interfaces. Players select their own retail DAT files locally. The web build makes no binary-exact claim and is a separate port from the curated TH08 reconstruction. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/th08-web-wasm-port-case/))
- [TH08: exact-source reconstruction and separately tested ports](https://github.com/N0zoM1z0/th08) — case-study · game — Reconstructs the original Japanese Touhou 8 version 1.00d against its binary and original compiler, then adapts the recovered code for other platforms. Source presence, strict function matching, whole-executable identity and playable-port checks are recorded as different milestones. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/th08-reconstruction-case/))
- [TH095: function-level reconstruction of Shoot the Bullet 1.02a](https://github.com/N0zoM1z0/th095) — case-study · game — Reconstructs source for the original Japanese Touhou 9.5: Shoot the Bullet 1.02a executable, accepting function exactness only through reproducible byte comparison of pinned Visual C++ .NET 2003 builds against one hash-attested target. The whole program also compiles and links into a playable Windows i386 executable, while README keeps playability, function matching and whole-executable identity as separate statuses. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/th095-reconstruction-case/))
- [The Darkness Recomp: an Xbox 360 static recompilation with a native D3D11 renderer](https://github.com/portingpete/The-Darkness-Recomp) — case-study · game — Builds a native Windows x64 port of The Darkness by translating its Xbox 360 executable to C++ ahead of time with a patched XenonRecomp. A D3D11 renderer draws from the engine’s original geometry, shader sources and device state instead of interpreting console GPU packets. Gameplay coverage and Linux results are project-reported, and the author discloses OpenAI Codex assistance. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/the-darkness-recomp-case/))
- [The Minish Cap: a GBA decompilation targeting five ROMs](https://github.com/zeldaret/tmc) — case-study · game — Recreates a C source base for The Legend of Zelda: The Minish Cap from information in the game and static or dynamic analysis, building five Game Boy Advance ROMs: USA, EU, JP and two demos. Each version takes its assets from a user-supplied original ROM, and normal builds check the output’s SHA-1 hash. The project remains a work in progress, and some parts of the ROM may not yet be shiftable. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/zelda-tmc-decomp-case/))
