- [The Simpsons Wrestling Recompiled: a psxrecomp PlayStation static recompilation](https://github.com/TekRantGaming/simpsons-wrestling-recompiled) — case-study · game — Translates the PlayStation game’s MIPS code to C with a pinned psxrecomp fork and builds Windows and Linux programs on its hardware-modelling runtime, with a launcher and a game-specific plugin. Without patching game code, the plugin adds 16:9 matches, 60 FPS by raising the emulated CPU clock and rescaling movement, intro skipping and unlocks. The user’s own NTSC-U disc image is required, and the measurements are author-reported. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/simpsons-wrestling-recompiled-case/))
- [The Sims: Bustin' Out: an AI-driven partial GBA matching decompilation](https://github.com/DaBrown95/tsbodp) — case-study · game — Rebuilds the USA/Europe Game Boy Advance release of The Sims: Bustin' Out byte for byte while gradually replacing ROM regions with matching C compiled by agbcc. The README calls it an AI-driven experiment in which almost all code, analysis and tooling are AI-generated. Most of the ROM is still copied from the user’s dump, so whole-ROM equality does not mean the game has been decompiled. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/sims-bustin-out-gba-decomp-case/))
- [The Two Towers Recompiled: an original Xbox static-recompilation port](https://github.com/Gumpolas/TooTawersRecomp) — case-study · game — Statically recompiles the x86 code of The Lord of the Rings: The Two Towers (Xbox, USA, 2002) into C with the bundled xboxrecomp toolkit, producing native Windows, Linux and experimental macOS builds. The recompiled sources are generated locally from the player's own default.xbe, and game data comes from their disc image or extracted folder. README reports the game as fully playable, with visual, frame-rate and mod issues listed. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/two-towers-recompiled-case/))
- [The Wind Waker: a GameCube decompilation with hash-checked relinking](https://github.com/zeldaret/tww) — case-study · game — Reconstructs source for The Wind Waker’s GameCube executable and REL modules across the USA/Korea, PAL and Japanese releases and a Japanese kiosk demo. Matched units are compiled from source and linked with objects split from the user’s disc, then checked against per-version SHA-1 hashes; the repository ships no assets or assembly and covers the GameCube game, not the HD remaster. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/wind-waker-decomp-case/))
- [thermalmaster: a Go client for ThermalMaster camera USB commands](https://github.com/xaionaro-go/thermalmaster) — case-study · protocol — thermalmaster implements a Go USB client for ThermalMaster P3 and P1 thermal cameras using documented VDCMD control transfers. It captures PNG frames, streams thermal and IR video to Linux v4l2loopback, and records USB command framing and model identifiers for interoperability. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/thermalmaster-case/))
- [ThinkPad IMX471: recovering camera tuning for Linux](https://github.com/cschalle/mipi-fixes-x1-aura) — case-study · firmware — Recovers selected camera-tuning structures from an Intel Windows driver package and uses them to repair Linux image capture on one ThinkPad model. Public CPFF/AIQB parsers, kernel patches and libcamera changes accompany the author’s Claude-assisted investigation and bounded before/after measurements. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/thinkpad-imx471-camera-case/))
- [Thyme: Command & Conquer Generals: Zero Hour reimplementation](https://github.com/TheAssemblyArmada/Thyme) — case-study · game — Thyme is an open source reimplementation of Command & Conquer Generals: Zero Hour. It rewrites the game from the ground up and uses the original binary for functions it has not yet replaced. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/thyme/))
- [tMock-Server: reconstructing Terraria server handshakes](https://github.com/StardustVulpine/tMock-Server) — case-study · game, protocol — tMock-Server is an unfinished C++ reimplementation of the Terraria server's initial network flow. It uses socket code and packet classes to accept connections, check protocol versions, request passwords, and assign player slots; the README records packet IDs, fields, directions, and structures informed by traffic analysis. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/tmock-server-case/))
- [TonyWasAProSkater: a THPS1 native port forked from a matching decompilation](https://github.com/LANCommander/TonyWasAProSkater) — case-study · game — Ports Tony Hawk’s Pro Skater (PlayStation, USA) to native Windows and Linux builds from a fork of an in-progress matching decompilation of the retail executable. The decompilation recompiles reconstructed functions with a Psy-Q toolchain and compares them byte for byte with the user’s retail executable, while the port replaces PlayStation hardware and libraries with SDL2 and OpenGL services. Play needs the user’s own disc image; matching and gameplay results remain project-reported. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/thps1-decomp-native-port-case/))
- [Touhou 6: reconstructing Embodiment of Scarlet Devil 1.02h source](https://github.com/GensokyoClub/th06) — case-study · game — Reconstructs C++ source for the Windows executable of Touhou 6: Embodiment of Scarlet Devil version 1.02h, aiming at an exact rebuild with the original Visual Studio .NET 2002 and DirectX 8 toolchain. reccmp and objdiff compare the rebuilt binary with the user’s original executable function by function. The committed progress badge reports every function implemented but 98.03% byte accuracy, so whole-binary identity is not claimed. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/th06-decomp-case/))
- [Touhou 7: reconstructing Perfect Cherry Blossom 1.00b source](https://github.com/some100/th07) — case-study · game — Reconstructs C++ source for the Windows executable of Touhou 7: Perfect Cherry Blossom 1.00b and its custom.exe configuration tool, built with the original Visual Studio .NET 2002 and DirectX 8 toolchain and compared function by function with the user’s originals through reccmp. The committed progress badges report th07.exe fully implemented at 99.78% accuracy and custom.exe fully matched. The README says the game should be fully playable, but a matching build trips the game’s integrity check, and the cross-platform ports live on separate branches. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/th07-decomp-case/))
- [touhou-engine: a Python reimplementation of Touhou gameplay](https://github.com/SaigyoujYuyuko/touhou-engine) — case-study · game — touhou-engine reimplements Touhou danmaku gameplay in Python, with TH07: Perfect Cherry Blossom as its reference game. The author says its engine logic was ported frame by frame against a decompilation, while the new architecture supplies an event stream, replay handling, headless stepping, and a pygame renderer. It reads original game archives supplied by the user. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/touhou-engine-case/))
- [TOZO Control: a Linux earbuds client built from the TOZO BLE protocol](https://github.com/GabriWar/tozo-control) — case-study · protocol — TOZO Control provides a Linux GTK4 client for TOZO earbuds using a BLE GATT protocol recovered from the vendor's Android app. It sends commands to characteristic B611, receives notifications on B612, and documents GET and SET packet layouts and parameter IDs. The author reports testing the T10 model for battery, game mode, equalizer and balance controls. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/tozo-control-case/))
- [trvision: implementing Thermalright Vision USB display control](https://github.com/0iroN/trvision) — case-study · protocol — trvision implements direct USB control of Thermalright Vision display panels using previously documented protocol research. It captures desktop frames, encodes JPEG or RGB565 according to the handshake reply, and sends framed data over bulk USB; a companion parser reads USBPcap captures. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/trvision-case/))
- [tuya-mobile: a Python reconstruction of Tuya app signing and API calls](https://github.com/AboveColin/tuya-mobile) — case-study · app, protocol — tuya-mobile reconstructs Tuya mobile-app request signing, encrypted API calls and MQTT signaling credential derivation in Python. It replaces the app's native libthing_security.so signing step with HMAC-SHA256, SHA256 and MD5 calculations and includes versioned Smart Life and Tuya Smart profiles. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/tuya-mobile-security-layer-case/))
- [Twilight Princess: a decompilation with fully matching GameCube code](https://github.com/zeldaret/tp) — case-study · game — Reconstructs Twilight Princess source that compiles back to binaries identical to the originals, building GameCube, Wii and Nvidia Shield releases from one codebase through conditional compilation. README reports the GameCube code as completely matching but not every translation unit linked yet, with Debug and Wii alignment ongoing; the repository ships no assets or assembly and will not produce a port. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/twilight-princess-decomp-case/))
- [Uncharted: The Nathan Drake Collection native Windows port](https://github.com/bc160201983/Uncharted-The-Nathan-Drake-Collection-PC-port) — case-study · game — Runs the PS4 Uncharted: The Nathan Drake Collection’s own x86-64 executables natively on Windows: a runtime supplies the PS4 system libraries and a shadPS4-derived Vulkan renderer executes the GPU work. The port adds a per-game launcher, resolution patches and modern upscaling, and its status table reports all three games playable with listed open issues. It is a compatibility-runtime port rather than a decompilation, and its results remain project-reported. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/uncharted-collection-pc-port-case/))
- [Underworld Exhumed: reconstructing Ultima Underworld I and II](https://github.com/abedegno/underworld-exhumed) — case-study · binary, game — Pairs reconstructed DOS sources with native ports of Ultima Underworld I and II. The upstream documents Exhume-based byte-matching builds, routine-level comparisons and recorded-session replay against DOS. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/underworld-exhumed-case/))
- [uniden-r8-ble: reconstructing the Uniden R8 radar detector's BLE telemetry](https://github.com/JeremyWhittaker/unidenr8_bluetooth) — case-study · protocol — Decodes the undocumented Bluetooth Low Energy output of a Uniden R8 radar detector into a receive-only collector for a Raspberry Pi, with live telemetry, alert start/update/end events, SQLite history and optional MQTT and Home Assistant publishing. The author worked the protocol out from the wire on a real detector, building on earlier R8w findings from AegisX86/UnidenR8wlink, and grades every claim in PROTOCOL.md by its evidence: measured on this R8, seen on another model, decompiled from an app but untested, or inference. Unknown fields are carried through raw rather than given invented meanings. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/uniden-r8-ble-telemetry-case/))
- [Unleashed Recompiled: translating Xbox 360 game code and shaders](https://github.com/hedge-dev/UnleashedRecomp) — case-study · game — Builds an unofficial Sonic Unleashed PC port by statically recompiling Xbox 360 PowerPC code with XenonRecomp and Xenos shaders with XenosRecomp. The port adds modern display and modding options while documenting original-engine limits and remaining compatibility issues. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/unleashed-recompiled-case/))
- [usque: an open reimplementation of Cloudflare WARP’s MASQUE mode](https://github.com/Diniboy1123/usque) — case-study · app, protocol — Reimplements the MASQUE mode of Cloudflare’s WARP client in Go, tunnelling IP traffic over QUIC/HTTP/3 with Connect-IP (RFC 9484) and exposing it as a native TUN device, SOCKS5 or HTTP proxies, TCP-only L4 proxies or port forwarding. RESEARCH.md describes how the author worked out device enrollment, TLS client authentication and Cloudflare’s non-standard cf-connect-ip variant by analyzing the official Android client with Frida, mitmproxy, JADX, IDA Free and Wireshark. It registers with and connects to Cloudflare’s service outside the official client. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/usque-warp-masque-client-case/))
- [UU Remote on Linux: an unofficial protocol implementation](https://github.com/VallierDev/uu-remote-linux) — case-study · app, protocol — Implements an unofficial native Linux host for NetEase UU Remote, using protocol behavior the maintainer says was recovered from official-client traffic and shipped programs. The Rust implementation connects selected desktop, input and auxiliary-tool channels to Linux services; an additional Linux viewer remains experimental. The author attributes development to Claude and Codex, without establishing REA use. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/uu-remote-linux-protocol-case/))
- [Valetudo: local replacement for robot-vacuum vendor clouds](https://github.com/Hypfer/Valetudo) — case-study · firmware, protocol — Replaces the vendor cloud of supported robot vacuums with a local service that runs on the rooted robot beside the vendor firmware and emulates the cloud endpoints that firmware expects, including a miio dummycloud and a counterpart for Midea's MSmart protocol. Its FAQ says each new vendor backend meant reverse engineering data formats, authentication and communication without vendor documentation; Valetudo converts the proprietary map formats into one ValetudoMap format and keeps Kaitai Struct descriptions of Dreame, Roborock and Viomi map files and a Midea protobuf schema as references. It adds a web interface, a REST API with Swagger UI and MQTT/Home Assistant integration, while feature parity with the vendor apps is an explicit non-goal. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/valetudo-robot-cloud-replacement-case/))
- [Vandal Hearts: native PC port built on matching decompilations](https://github.com/HalmyLyseas/VandalHearts-PcPort) — case-study · game — Builds on shao113’s matching decompilation of PlayStation Vandal Hearts, adds a Japanese matching tree, and carries both through to a native Windows and Linux port. SDL2, OpenGL or Metal and OpenAL backends replace the console’s GPU, GTE, CD-ROM/XA, SPU, MDEC and pad interfaces, and one executable runs the USA, Asia and Japan discs. The matching builds are still checked on every change; PC-only features such as Tactical Mode and HD packs are opt-in. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/vandal-hearts-pc-port-case/))
- [VanMoof BLE: a client for S5, A5 and S6 bicycle protocols](https://github.com/Knight1/vanmoof-ble) — case-study · firmware, protocol — VanMoof BLE implements a Python client for the S5, A5 and S6 bicycle BLE protocols. It authenticates with an Ed25519 certificate, reads and writes documented topics, decodes captured messages, and exposes controls and telemetry according to each model's message format. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/vanmoof-ble-protocol-case/))
- [vanmoof-certificates: decoding VanMoof bike certificates](https://github.com/Knight1/vanmoof-certificates) — case-study · protocol — vanmoof-certificates decodes the access certificates used with VanMoof SA5 and Series 6 bikes. It documents an Ed25519 signature followed by a CBOR payload containing bike serials, user identity, access role and expiry, and provides Go code to request, parse and check certificates. The source says the SA5 verification key was recovered from a bike BLE certificate. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/vanmoof-certificates-case/))
- [vf2-decomp: differentially validated Virtua Fighter 2 recovery](https://github.com/celsowm/vf2-decomp) — case-study · game — Recovers Virtua Fighter 2 Version 2.1’s Sega Model 2A i960 program into portable, non-matching C17, accepting each recovered path only when it matches a reference i960 executor at controlled boundaries. The repository adds ROM validation, i960 and 68000 analysis tools, a bounded Model 2A hardware model and snapshot-based differential runners. README states that it is not yet a playable port; match flow, combat, physics, AI, rasterization and much of the audio remain open. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/vf2-decomp-differential-recovery-case/))
- [Virtual MPC Sample: rehosting Akai MPC Sample firmware in QEMU](https://github.com/orrpunk-art/virtual-mpc-sample) — case-study · firmware, protocol — Virtual MPC Sample rehosts Akai MPC Sample 1.3.0.12 firmware in QEMU using a custom lab kernel and a browser panel for the device controls. The repository documents firmware and device architecture, emulates the panel's UART communication and provides a shell, tracing and mod testing against the user's own firmware files. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/virtual-mpc-sample-case/))
- [Visor: an independent Ray-Ban Meta glasses client](https://github.com/macedonga/visor) — case-study · app, protocol — Implements an Android replacement for the Meta AI companion app that speaks the Ray-Ban Meta glasses’ own Bluetooth and Wi-Fi Direct protocol, imports media, exposes settings and answers “Hey Meta” with a model the user chooses. The project wiki documents the link layers, encryption, services and Meta-side ownership steps, and lists 153 open questions. Testing is on real glasses rather than test suites, so behavior beyond the stated model and firmware remains unverified. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/visor-ray-ban-meta-client-case/))
- [viture-v2: a reconstructed VITURE Gen2 glasses protocol and driver](https://github.com/elasticjava/viture-v2) — case-study · protocol — viture-v2 documents and implements the USB HID protocol used by VITURE Gen2 XR glasses. The author matched usbmon captures to SDK frame logs, then wrote a Rust driver with hidraw, usbfs and io_uring transports for queries and IMU streams. Measurements and compatibility claims cover the VITURE Pro 2 XR. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/viture-v2-case/))
- [Void Client: rebuilding the Starblast.io client](https://github.com/Senko74/Void-Client) — case-study · game, protocol — Void Client is an unfinished JavaScript rewrite of the Starblast.io browser game client based on study of its network protocol. Its current Node.js and browser components provide game-mode selection, server discovery, local WebSocket communication, and parsing and JSON export of some received packets. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/void-client-case/))
- [Webamp: a browser reimplementation of Winamp 2](https://github.com/captbaritone/webamp) — case-study · app — Reimplements Winamp 2's main, equalizer and playlist windows in HTML5 and JavaScript, with Web Audio playback, volume, balance and equalizer, and renders original classic .wsz skins, which its documentation says match Winamp 2.9 pixel for pixel; Milkdrop presets run through the Butterchurn WebGL port. It ships as the webamp npm module for embedding in web pages, and the same monorepo runs webamp.org and the skins.webamp.org skin museum. Its webamp-modern prototype targets Winamp 3/5 modern skins with an interpreter for MAKI skin scripts, whose undocumented bytecode format the author reverse engineered and documents in the repository. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/webamp-winamp-reimplementation-case/))
- [WeChat: recovering SQLCipher keys through CommonCrypto hooks](https://github.com/Evanyuan-builder/wechat-4.1.10-macos-key) — case-study · app, binary — An investigation of WeChat 4.1.10 for macOS reports recovering per-database SQLCipher keys by breaking on CommonCrypto encryption calls in LLDB. It compares that approach with memory scanning and sqlite3_key breakpoints, and describes an offline check against a decrypted SQLite page. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/wechat-4-1-10-macos-key-case/))
- [Weeping of the Empire: statically recompiling Tears of the Kingdom for Windows](https://github.com/daniel-madrid-07/weeping-of-the-empire) — case-study · game — Weeping of the Empire builds a native Windows port of The Legend of Zelda: Tears of the Kingdom from the user's Switch game dump. It recompiles ARM64 code ahead of time to x86-64, supplies Switch services through a host runtime and renders through Vulkan. The engine documentation credits totk-recomp as the starting point and identifies the added Windows port, renderer, controls and menus. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/weeping-of-the-empire-case/))
- [whatsapp-web-reveng: documenting the legacy WhatsApp Web protocol](https://github.com/sigalor/whatsapp-web-reveng) — case-study · protocol, website — Documents and reimplements the WebSocket protocol WhatsApp Web used while browser sessions mirrored a paired phone: QR pairing, Curve25519/HKDF key setup, AES/HMAC-protected binary messages, the token-based node encoding and end-to-end media encryption. A Python backend, Node.js relay and browser front end decode a logged-in session’s messages into inspectable JSON, and the README lists independent reimplementations such as Baileys and go-whatsapp. This is an older protocol generation from before WhatsApp’s 2021 multi-device architecture, not a description of current WhatsApp Web. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/whatsapp-web-reveng-legacy-protocol-case/))
- [wii-ipl: a decompilation of the Wii Menu (System Menu 4.3)](https://github.com/koopthekoopa/wii-ipl) — case-study · firmware, game — A work-in-progress decompilation of the Wii Menu, the console’s System Menu (IPL), targeting version 4.3 in its USA, European, Japanese and Korean builds. It recovers the menu’s own code together with the Revolution SDK, NintendoWare (NW4R), EGG and middleware libraries linked into it, matched unit by unit against the original executable with decomp-toolkit and objdiff. The repository holds no assets or assembly; a build needs the user’s own Wii Menu WAD and yields only main.dol. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/wii-menu-ipl-decomp-case/))
- [Wind Waker HD decompilation: functions verified against the recompiled Wii U game](https://github.com/ZeldaWWHDDecomp/wwhd) — case-study · game — Decompiles the USA Wii U release of The Wind Waker HD function by function into readable C++ in zeldaret/tww’s style, and verifies each function by running it beside the original, statically recompiled from the user’s own copy, comparing return values, memory writes and calls on generated and recorded inputs, with coverage and mutation checks. README reports 28,244 of the game’s 39,713 functions verified, including 22,068 of 22,215 GameCube-derived functions. The code calls undecompiled functions by address, so it runs only inside the ZeldaWWHDRecomp port, not as a standalone game build. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/wind-waker-hd-decomp-case/))
- [Wind Waker HD Recomp: a native port by static recompilation of the Wii U game](https://github.com/ZeldaWWHDRecomp/ZeldaWWHDRecomp) — case-study · game — Statically recompiles The Wind Waker HD’s Wii U PowerPC code to C ahead of time, reimplements the Cafe OS libraries the game uses and implements GX2 graphics on Metal or Vulkan, producing native macOS, Linux, Windows and Android builds without a Cemu runtime. All game code and data are generated locally from the user’s own dump; an experimental macOS-only build can instead run functions from the separate ZeldaWWHDDecomp/wwhd decompilation. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/wind-waker-hd-recomp-case/))
- [wine-uwp: running packaged Windows Store apps on a patched Wine](https://github.com/designgears/wine-uwp) — case-study · app — Extends Wine 11.17 so that signed MSIX Store apps, with Shapr3D as the main target, install and run on Linux without their source or symbols. About 49,000 changed lines add a DirectComposition implementation, composition swap chains and an app-model host, while Microsoft’s own XAML and app-model DLLs, harvested from a Windows 11 image on the user’s machine, run unmodified. The author says Claude did most of the reverse engineering and code; app support is uneven and checked by a project regression suite. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/wine-uwp-store-apps-case/))
- [Wino Mail: recreating Windows Mail and Calendar](https://github.com/bkaankose/Wino-Mail) — case-study · app — Native Windows client that aims to preserve the familiar Windows Mail and Calendar experience while rebuilding its functionality. The upstream README documents a WinUI 3 interface, mail and calendar providers, contacts, tasks and offline workflows. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/wino-mail-case/))
- [WordCraft: document workflow reconstruction and bounded DOCX compatibility](https://github.com/storytold/wordcraft) — case-study · app — The authors describe WordCraft as a clean-room Rust implementation of familiar Microsoft Word workflows. It provides its own document model, layout engine and DOCX reader/writer, with desktop and browser interfaces; source tests exercise selected editing and file-preservation behavior. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/wordcraft-case/))
- [WristBeam: reconstructing Garmin’s watch protocol for a Mac companion](https://github.com/kongdayan/wristbeam) — case-study · app, protocol — Implements a Garmin watch companion as a native macOS menu-bar app that pairs over Bluetooth, receives live health notifications and original FIT files, and can upload them to Garmin Connect. Source comments attribute its GFDI framing, handshake, file-transfer and status fields to analysis of a supplied Garmin Android package, cross-checked against community implementations such as Gadgetbridge. Device verification covers one watch model, and the opt-in MCP and HTTP interfaces expose only locally cached data. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/wristbeam-garmin-watch-protocol-case/))
- [xbloom-ble: a client for the xBloom Studio BLE protocol](https://github.com/Janczykkkko/xbloom-ble) — case-study · protocol — xbloom-ble implements a Python client for the xBloom Studio coffee machine's recovered Bluetooth LE protocol. The author documents capturing app traffic and comparing GATT command frames; the CLI and terminal UI load recipes, program dial presets, optionally start brewing and display live scale telemetry. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/xbloom-ble-case/))
- [Xiaomi Kettle BLE protocol: reconstructing a smart kettle's control interface](https://github.com/anna-oake/xiaomi-kettle) — case-study · protocol — Documents the closed Bluetooth Low Energy GATT protocol of the Xiaomi Kettle, which the author reconstructed by decompiling the Mi Home Android app and disassembling its native encryption library. The write-up covers the connection and authentication handshake, the layout of status notifications and the writable settings characteristics, with a small Go reimplementation of the cipher helpers. The author built a kettle-to-MQTT bridge for Home Assistant from it but did not publish that code, and gives no guarantee that the notes work for others. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/xiaomi-kettle-ble-protocol-case/))
- [xoreos: an open reimplementation of BioWare’s Aurora engine family](https://github.com/xoreos/xoreos) — case-study · game — Reimplements BioWare’s Aurora engine and its later derivatives as a portable C++ engine that runs on the user’s installed game data, targeting nine games from Neverwinter Nights to Dragon Age II. Resource management, many Aurora file formats, graphics and sound are in place, and every target shows partial in-game areas in a free-flying spectator mode. README states that normal gameplay is not implemented yet, so this is an early-stage engine case, not a playable replacement. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/xoreos-aurora-engine-case/))
- [z2rs: an incremental Rust reconstruction of Zelda II checked against an emulator](https://github.com/troyedwardsjr/z2rs) — case-study · game — Rebuilds Zelda II: The Adventure of Link (NES) in Rust for desktop, browser and Android by replacing the game’s 6502 routines one at a time. A built-in interpreter still runs the user’s ROM and hands ported routines to Rust, while a verifier compares RAM, sprites, palette and frames with a reference emulator. It is a behavioral, clean-room-style reconstruction guided by a community disassembly, not a decompilation or a finished standalone engine. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/z2rs-zelda2-reconstruction-case/))
- [ZCode-Android: reconstructing the ZCode remote-control client](https://github.com/Damianjiang/ZCode-Android) — case-study · app, protocol, website — ZCode-Android recreates ZCode remote control on Android from captured traffic and analysis of its official web page. Its Kotlin client implements WebSocket relay, HMAC pairing, IPC encoding, fragmented RPC frames and Conversation V4 session messages. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/zcode-android-case/))
- [zee-rp: reconstructing supported Axure RP 11 document operations](https://github.com/LilZeeCN/zee-rp) — case-study · app — Reads and rewrites supported native Axure RP 11 documents through a binary-record codec, reference-aware cloning and constrained interaction writers. CLI and MCP interfaces expose inspected edits while keeping offline structure checks separate from native application acceptance. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/zee-rp-case/))
- [Zelda 64: Recompiled — a native Majora’s Mask port without a full source decompilation](https://github.com/Zelda64Recomp/Zelda64Recomp) — case-study · game — Uses N64Recomp to translate Majora’s Mask into a native application and RT64 for rendering. The project separates static recompilation from source decompilation while reusing decompilation headers and selected functions for patches and enhancements. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/zelda64recomp-case/))
- [Zemismart Blind Controller Protocol: RF command reconstruction](https://github.com/blark/zemismart-blind-protocol) — case-study · protocol — Zemismart Blind Controller Protocol documents the 433.92 MHz OOK PWM messages used by Zemismart motorized blind remotes. It records pulse timing, payload fields and formulas for channel and button commands, with Python scripts to capture IQ samples and encode or decode transmissions. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/zemismart-blind-protocol-case/))
- [zlt: MTN ZLT T10D MAX router API reconstruction](https://github.com/exbyte-dev/zltrouter) — case-study · protocol — zlt reconstructs the LAN JSON API of the MTN ZLT T10D MAX router from its served JavaScript and observed responses. Its Python client handles reqproc requests, login and CSRF behavior, while a CLI and local dashboard expose signal, network mode, SMS and USSD operations. The protocol document identifies firmware-specific fields and porting limits. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/zltrouter-case/))

<a id="agent-mcp"></a>
## Independent MCP integrations

- [adb-mcp](https://github.com/FlashZ/adb-mcp) — integration · app — adb-mcp gives agents structured Android Debug Bridge tools for investigating app behavior on a device. It can launch packages, inspect the UI hierarchy, capture logcat records and screenshots, retrieve files, and set up port forwarding. Its documentation shows a command sequence for investigating an in-app action through UI interaction and logs. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/adb-mcp/))
- [apktool-mcp-server](https://github.com/zinja-coder/apktool-mcp-server) — integration · app — Python MCP server that drives a local Apktool install so agents can decode APKs; list, read, search and edit smali and resource files; read AndroidManifest.xml and apktool.yml; and rebuild the project. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/apktool-mcp-server/))
- [Binary Ninja Headless MCP](https://github.com/mrphrazer/binary-ninja-headless-mcp) — integration · binary — A third-party, headless-only MCP server by Ruben Marks (mrphrazer) that gives agents a large Binary Ninja tool surface the project counts as 181 tools across 36 feature groups, including disassembly, intermediate languages, cross-references, types, search, patching with undo/redo transactions, and arbitrary scripting through binja.eval and binja.call. It speaks MCP over stdio or TCP, ships a schema-derived command-line client (binja_cli), and is meant to run in the same container as the agent runtime. A fake-backend mode runs the CLI, tests and a feature fuzzer without a Binary Ninja license, while real analysis needs a headless-capable license. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/binary-ninja-headless-mcp/))
- [Binary Ninja MCP (fosdickio)](https://github.com/fosdickio/binary_ninja_mcp) — integration · binary — Community Binary Ninja plugin and bridge for MCP clients, including workflows that switch among multiple open binaries. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/binary-ninja-community-mcp/))
- [Binary Ninja MCP (official)](https://docs.binary.ninja/guide/mcp.html) — integration · binary — Binary Ninja's own MCP interface exposes functions, symbols, disassembly, intermediate languages and decompiled text to agents. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/binary-ninja-official-mcp/))
- [Burp Suite MCP Server](https://github.com/PortSwigger/mcp-server) — integration · app, website — PortSwigger's Burp Suite extension serves an MCP endpoint, plus a packaged stdio proxy, so agents can query proxy HTTP and WebSocket history, send HTTP/1.1 or HTTP/2 requests, and open Repeater or Intruder tabs. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/burp-suite-mcp-server/))
- [Cheat Engine MCP Bridge](https://github.com/miscusi-peek/cheatengine-mcp-bridge) — integration · binary, game — Connects MCP clients to Cheat Engine through a Lua bridge script and a Python MCP server, so an agent can inspect a process the user runs under Cheat Engine: memory reads and value scans, pointer-chain resolution, structure dissection, RTTI class names, disassembly, cross-references and hardware or DBVM breakpoints. Further tool groups write memory, inject DLLs or code, automate input and use Cheat Engine's kernel-mode driver. The README limits it to educational and research use and disclaims multiplayer cheating and Terms of Service violations. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/cheatengine-mcp-bridge/))
- [Chrome DevTools MCP](https://github.com/ChromeDevTools/chrome-devtools-mcp) — integration · website — Connect an agent to Chrome for live page inspection, network debugging, and performance traces. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/chrome-devtools-mcp/))
- [DecompilerServer](https://github.com/pardeike/DecompilerServer) — integration · app, game — Stdio MCP server built on the ICSharpCode.Decompiler engine that lets agents load managed .NET assemblies, including a Unity game's Assembly-CSharp.dll located from its game directory, then search symbols, string literals and attributes, read decompiled C# or IL, and trace callers, callees, usages and inheritance. Several assemblies stay loaded under aliases, so an agent can compare two versions structurally or diff individual method bodies, the README's example being RimWorld mod porting. Modding-oriented tools generate Harmony patch skeletons, detour stubs and transpiler anchor suggestions as text from the inspected members. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/decompilerserver-dotnet-mcp/))
- [dos-mcp](https://github.com/abedegno/dos-mcp) — integration · binary, game — dos-mcp connects AI agents to DOS programs running in js-dos for interactive reverse engineering. Its MCP tools send keyboard and mouse input, capture frames, exchange files with the guest and read or search emulated memory; the documentation shows a save-format comparison workflow using Ultima Underworld. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/dos-mcp/))
- [Exoproc](https://github.com/w0ldan/exoproc) — integration · binary — Exoproc exposes Windows x64 process inspection and instrumentation to agents through a CLI and MCP server. It enumerates processes, reads and scans memory, installs function detours, redirects thread contexts and compiles C snippets for execution in a target process. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/exoproc/))
- [frida-mcp (yfe404)](https://github.com/yfe404/frida-mcp) — integration · app, game — Frida MCP server with persistent instrumentation sessions, runtime inspection and Android static-to-dynamic navigation. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/frida-mcp-yfe404/))
- [Ghidra Headless MCP](https://github.com/mrphrazer/ghidra-headless-mcp) — integration · binary — Headless-only MCP server that drives Ghidra through PyGhidra and is meant to share a container with the agent rather than attach to a Ghidra GUI. Its 212 tools in 34 groups cover program and project lifecycle, disassembly, decompilation and P-code, patching and memory edits, symbols, references, types and structure layouts, search and graph extraction, with raw Python, Java-API and script access for anything else. Programs open read-only by default, edits run in transactions with undo and redo, a fake backend lets development and CI run without Ghidra, and a ghidra_cli client exposes the same tools to shells and agents without MCP. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ghidra-headless-mcp/))
- [Ghidra MCP Server (bethington)](https://github.com/bethington/ghidra-mcp) — integration · binary — A Ghidra GUI plugin, standalone headless server and Python MCP bridge exposing a 209-tool catalog: decompilation, cross-references, renaming, typing, structures, comments and batch edits, plus P-code emulation, live debugging through Ghidra’s TraceRmi framework, shared Ghidra Server repository and version-control operations, and hash-based transfer of function documentation between binary versions. The bridge loads three tool groups on connect and lets the model search for and load the rest, while naming and type conventions are enforced in the tool layer. Its NOTICE says it began as a derivative of LaurieWired’s GhidraMCP in August 2025 and has since been substantially rewritten and extended. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ghidra-mcp-bethington/))
- [GhidraMCP](https://github.com/LaurieWired/GhidraMCP) — integration · binary — Ghidra extension and MCP bridge exposing decompilation, symbol navigation and renaming to agents, with GUI and headless modes. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ghidramcp/))
- [GhidrAssistMCP](https://github.com/symgraph/GhidrAssistMCP) — integration · binary — Ghidra extension that runs an MCP server inside Ghidra over streamable HTTP and legacy SSE, with 100 explicitly named tools (93 enabled by default) for pseudocode, disassembly, P-code, control-flow and call graphs, cross-references, strings, imports and exports, plus renaming, retyping, comments and structure inference. Since version 3.0.0 the server starts with the project manager, so agents can create, open, save and close projects and open programs before a CodeBrowser exists, while one shared server tracks whichever CodeBrowser window has focus. Six program resource templates, seven analysis prompts and a bundled script for running the server under analyzeHeadless complete the package. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ghidrassistmcp/))
- [Hex-Rays IDA MCP](https://github.com/HexRaysSA/ida-mcp) — integration · binary — Official Hex-Rays MCP server for agent access to IDA databases, with headless operation and optional GUI integration. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/hex-rays-ida-mcp/))
- [IDA Pro MCP (mrexodia)](https://github.com/mrexodia/ida-pro-mcp) — integration · binary — Community IDA integration useful for existing setups and migration context; its maintainer now recommends the official Hex-Rays MCP server. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ida-pro-mcp-community/))
- [IDA Pro MCP Server (fdrechsler)](https://github.com/fdrechsler/mcp-server-idapro) — integration · binary — An IDA Pro plugin and a TypeScript MCP server by Florian Drechsler: the plugin starts an HTTP server inside IDA on 127.0.0.1:9045, and the stdio MCP server forwards agent requests to it. Its twelve tools run IDAPython scripts passed inline or by file path, list functions, exports and strings, search names, immediate values and byte sequences, return cross-references to and from an address, and disassemble address ranges. It is a separate codebase from the mrexodia and Hex-Rays IDA MCP servers listed here. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/mcp-server-idapro-fdrechsler/))
- [ida-mcp-rs](https://github.com/blacktop/ida-mcp-rs) — integration · binary — Headless MCP server written in Rust that drives IDA Pro through idalib, so agents such as Claude Code, Codex CLI, Gemini CLI or Cursor can open and analyze binaries without the IDA GUI. Apple targets are single tool calls, including universal Mach-O slice selection, dSYM/DWARF loading and dyld_shared_cache module loading; a 28-tool lean profile sits beside the full tool set, run_script executes IDAPython and returns JSON, and editing tools such as rename, comments, patches and type application each take exactly one address or exact symbol name. save_idb writes checkpoints, and crashed or wedged IDA workers are replaced automatically. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ida-mcp-rs/))
- [ida-multi-mcp](https://github.com/MeroZemory/ida-multi-mcp) — integration · binary — MCP server that lets an agent analyze several IDA Pro databases at once through one endpoint. Each database with its plugin registers itself when opened and headless idalib sessions can be added, and a router exposes up to 76 tools for triage, decompilation and disassembly, cross-references and call graphs, search, types, renaming, comments and patching, plus analysis-wait gating so agents can wait for IDA's auto-analysis to settle; its IDA tool implementations come from the ida-pro-mcp project. Function-similarity search compares functions within or across binaries using name-independent instruction MinHash, API, string and constant anchors and control-flow shape, with optional local neural recall, and nothing is uploaded. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ida-multi-mcp/))
- [JADX MCP Server (Qtty)](https://github.com/Qtty/jadx-mcp-server) — integration · app — MCP server for Android APK analysis in which a Python FastMCP process talks to the agent over stdio and drives a long-lived local JVM daemon that embeds JADX 1.5.6 as a library, so no JADX GUI is involved and a loaded APK stays warm between sessions. Its 23 tools return classes, decompiled sources with size-capped outlines, methods, fields, smali, cross-references and callees read from a dex-derived index, type hierarchies and overrides, exported and deep-link components, the manifest and resource files, plus a whole-APK sweep for framework sink call sites grouped by impact category. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/qtty-jadx-mcp-server/))
- [JADX-AI-MCP](https://github.com/zinja-coder/jadx-ai-mcp) — integration · app — JADX plugin paired with a Python MCP server to inspect decompiled Android classes, resources, cross-references and debugger context. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/jadx-ai-mcp/))
- [JEB-MCP](https://github.com/flankerhqd/jebmcp) — integration · app — JEB-MCP is an MCP server and JEB Pro script that lets MCP clients inspect and rename elements of Android apps in JEB. Its documented commands include reading decompiled code, examining classes and methods, and renaming them. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/jebmcp/))
- [lisa.py](https://github.com/ant4g0nist/lisa.py) — integration · binary — LLDB extension that adds an MCP bridge to a debugging session, adapted from ida-pro-mcp: the plugin's mcp start command serves 18 debugger functions over local JSON-RPC, and a FastMCP stdio server generated from those functions exposes them to MCP clients such as Claude Desktop. The tools create, launch or attach to targets, step, manage breakpoints, read backtraces, variables, disassembly and memory, evaluate expressions and run arbitrary LLDB commands. The same script keeps lisa.py's earlier exploit-development helpers, including checksec, a register and stack context view, Mach-O header and load-command display, Capstone disassembly and an exploitable crash-triage command. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/lisa-lldb-mcp/))
- [LLDB MCP](https://lldb.llvm.org/use/mcp.html) — integration · app, binary — LLDB's official MCP interface gives agents debugger sessions and commands for breakpoints, memory inspection and stepping. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/lldb-mcp/))
- [mcp-windbg](https://github.com/svnscha/mcp-windbg) — integration · binary — Independent, community-built MCP server that runs Microsoft's cdb.exe and kd.exe headless, so agents can open and triage user-mode and kernel crash dumps, attach to cdb remote debug servers and KD kernel targets, and run arbitrary debugger commands in separately addressed sessions. Its README points users who want AI inside WinDbg to Microsoft's official WinDbg MCP and positions this project as an alternative for other MCP clients, remote HTTP use and batch dump triage; it calls itself a wrapper that runs real debugger commands, not an automatic fix. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/mcp-windbg/))
- [MindNode MCP Server](https://github.com/jyuwaaw/mindnode-mcp) — integration · app — MindNode MCP Server connects agents to MindNode Next mind maps on macOS. Its reader decodes the app's SQLite library, protobuf snapshots, Apple LZ4 compression and CRDT operations into Markdown outlines; other MCP tools return previews or create maps through MindNode's Markdown importer. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/mindnode-mcp/))
- [OGhidra](https://github.com/llnl/OGhidra) — integration · binary — OGhidra is an AI integration for analyzing binaries with Ghidra through natural language. It supports function analysis, automated renaming, malware pattern detection, and either an MCP or PyGhidra backend. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/oghidra/))
- [OpenBaud](https://github.com/Leonezz/openbaud) — integration · protocol — Rust MCP server and Codex plugin that gives coding agents audited access to local serial devices, from identifying ports by USB metadata and sending framed requests to recording every received and transmitted byte in lossless .obcap captures. Device behaviour confirmed during exploration is kept as YAML device profiles, typed commands and workflows that validate checksums and decode scalars, bit fields and record arrays, and the same commands can be replayed against a capture without hardware. Its worked demo decodes OBP/1, a small binary protocol the author wrote for an ESP32-S3 test board, not an existing vendor protocol. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/openbaud-serial-device-mcp/))
- [Playwright MCP](https://github.com/microsoft/playwright-mcp) — integration · website — Drive browser interactions through structured accessibility snapshots to investigate reproducible web behavior. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/playwright-mcp/))
- [pyghidra-mcp](https://github.com/clearbluejar/pyghidra-mcp) — integration · binary — PyGhidra-based MCP server that analyzes every binary in a Ghidra project and exposes decompilation, cross-references, call graphs, symbol and string search, indexed semantic code search, and renaming, typing and comment edits. A separate HTTP CLI client and an optional mode driving a Ghidra GUI it launches are included. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pyghidra-mcp/))
- [r0idamcp](https://github.com/r0ysue/r0idamcp) — integration · binary — r0idamcp is a single-file MCP server that connects IDA Pro to an MCP-capable assistant for automated reverse engineering. Its tools expose functions, strings, decompilation, cross-references, comments, and renaming through an SSE connection. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/r0idamcp/))
- [Radare2 MCP Server](https://github.com/radareorg/radare2-mcp) — integration · binary — Native radare2 MCP server offering binary-analysis tools, reusable sessions and configurable read-only access. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/radare2-mcp/))
- [RenderDoc MCP](https://github.com/scarletfantasy/renderdoc-mcp) — integration · app, game — Expose existing RenderDoc captures to an agent for bounded frame, pipeline, resource, and shader inspection. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/renderdoc-mcp/))
- [ReVa (Reverse Engineering Assistant)](https://github.com/cyberkaida/reverse-engineering-assistant) — integration · binary — Ghidra extension that runs an MCP server inside Ghidra, giving agents decompilation with namespace and cross-reference context, symbol, string, data-type, structure and call-graph tools, program diffing, renaming and PyGhidra scripting. Its README attributes the project's distinction to tool design for LLM use: many small tools whose schemas tolerate other input, redirect correctable mistakes and return short fragments linked to related information. Assistant mode serves an open Ghidra project to several clients at once; headless mode starts Ghidra itself with session-scoped projects. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/reva-ghidra-mcp/))
- [Reversecore MCP](https://github.com/sjkim1127/Reversecore_MCP) — integration · binary — MCP server that puts radare2 with r2ghidra, LIEF, Capstone, YARA, CAPA, Detect It Easy, binwalk, angr, Volatility3, Scapy, Sleuth Kit and other engines behind 177 typed tools for AI clients such as Claude and Cursor. The tools cover disassembly and decompilation, binary parsing, string, IOC and capability extraction, ESIL emulation and symbolic execution, YARA scanning, memory, PCAP and disk forensics, source-code pattern scanning and session reports with MITRE ATT&CK mapping, alongside 22 guided analysis prompts and per-binary MCP resources. File access is confined to a configured sample workspace, and the server runs over stdio or HTTP/SSE, typically from the project's Docker image. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/reversecore-mcp/))
- [SOMCP](https://github.com/bilieebiliee1-design/SOMCP) — integration · app, binary — SOMCP is an Android MCP server for analyzing and modifying native SO libraries. It exposes ELF analysis, disassembly, patching, and optional dynamic analysis tools to MCP clients. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/somcp/))
- [Super Trouper](https://github.com/crissyfield/super-trouper) — integration · app, game — Single-binary Frida MCP server for discovering devices, managing process sessions and loading instrumentation scripts. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/super-trouper/))
- [usb-rea](https://github.com/emirmrkaljevic/usb-rea) — integration · protocol — Python CLI and stdio MCP server for inferring the USB protocol of a device whose only control software is a vendor app. While the user clicks through the app, it captures traffic with usbmon or USBPcap, takes a label for each action, diffs the packets per action and infers message layouts: constant bytes, rolling counters, scaled 8- to 32-bit values, lookup tables, packed bit fields and sum, XOR or CRC checksums. It also decodes device replies, handshakes with session tokens and image streams sent to device screens, links each inferred field to Wireshark frame numbers in a report, and generates a pyusb driver and a Wireshark Lua dissector. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/usb-rea/))
- [WireMCP](https://github.com/0xKoda/WireMCP) — integration · protocol — Node.js MCP server that runs Wireshark's tshark for LLM clients, aimed in its README at threat hunting, network diagnostics and anomaly detection. Three tools capture a few seconds of live traffic and return either a fixed set of IP, port, TCP-flag and HTTP fields, tshark's protocol-hierarchy statistics or TCP conversation statistics; analyze_pcap returns IPs, ports, HTTP URLs and protocol stacks from a saved capture. The remaining tools match captured IPs against the URLhaus blocklist and scan a capture for HTTP Basic, FTP, Telnet and Kerberos credentials, beside the dissection output. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/wiremcp/))
- [Wireshark MCP](https://github.com/bx33661/Wireshark-MCP) — integration · protocol — MCP server that wraps tshark, and optionally other Wireshark command-line tools, so agents can query saved captures through Wireshark's dissectors: packet lists and details, stream following, arbitrary field extraction, protocol hierarchies and conversations, decode-as rules for non-standard ports, protocol preferences, TLS and WPA decryption with user-supplied keys, and one tool that applies preset fields and filters for 25 protocols such as Modbus, S7comm, MQTT, Zigbee and BLE. Separate security-heuristic, live-capture and capture-editing tools sit beside the dissection tools. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/wireshark-mcp/))
- [x64dbg MCP](https://github.com/HxT9/x64dbg_mcp) — integration · binary — x64dbg MCP gives agents debugger control over Windows binaries through a native x64dbg plugin and a C# stdio MCP server. The plugin serves local HTTP requests to x64dbg's bridge API, while MCP tools expose disassembly, memory and register access, modules, execution control, and breakpoints. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/hxt9-x64dbg-mcp/))
- [x64dbg MCP (x64dbgMCP)](https://github.com/Wasdubya/x64dbgMCP) — integration · binary — MCP server that connects LLM clients to the x64dbg and x32dbg debuggers through a native plugin and a Python MCP script. It exposes more than 40 tools built on the x64dbg SDK and was tested with Claude and Cursor; agents can query registers, search byte patterns in a module and drive debugging sessions. The README advises giving the model the executable's full path so it can restart a crashed or hung target through the command-execution tool, and starting from GetModuleBase so that queries use valid addresses. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/x64dbgmcp/))
- [x64dbg MCP Server Plugin](https://github.com/SetsunaYukiOvO/x64dbg-mcp) — integration · binary — An MCP server plugin for x64dbg and x32dbg that lets external applications and AI agents control debugging through HTTP. It provides tools for execution, memory, registers, breakpoints, and disassembly, along with debugger context resources and workflow prompts. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/x64dbg-mcp-setsuna/))
- [x64dbg-MCP Server](https://github.com/duty1g/x64dbg-mcp-server) — integration · binary — Native x64dbg plugin, written in Zig for both x32dbg and x64dbg, that serves an MCP endpoint over streamable HTTP and SSE from inside the debugger. Agents can load or attach to a process, set software, hardware, memory and conditional breakpoints, step and trace, read registers, call stacks, threads, memory maps, imports, strings and cross-references, and run arbitrary x64dbg commands. A bundled SKILL.md guide tells agents to confirm the debugger state before every action. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/x64dbg-mcp-server/))

<a id="agent-workflows"></a>
## Independent agent workflows

- [aiDAPal](https://github.com/atredispartners/aidapal) — integration · binary — aiDAPal is an IDA Pro plugin that uses a locally hosted language model trained for Hex-Rays pseudocode to assist with code analysis. It presents generated changes for acceptance before updating the pseudocode. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/aidapal/))
- [Android Reverse Engineering Skill](https://github.com/SimoneAvogadro/android-reverse-engineering-skill) — integration · app — Claude Code plugin and agent skill that drives jadx and Fernflower/Vineflower to decompile Android APK, XAPK, JAR and AAR files and document their HTTP APIs. A fingerprint pass first identifies the app's framework, HTTP stack, obfuscation level and native libraries, then shell scripts extract endpoints and authentication patterns across Retrofit, OkHttp, Volley, Ktor and Apollo/GraphQL, trace call flows from UI down to the network layer, and rebuild original Kotlin class names from the metadata R8 leaves behind. It installs through the bundled Claude Code marketplace, and its scripts also run standalone with a /decompile slash command. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/android-reverse-engineering-skill/))
- [AutoProber](https://github.com/GainSec/AutoProber) — integration · firmware — Flying-probe control stack meant to let a coding agent or an operator take a circuit board on a GRBL 3018-style CNC from locating it on the plate, through XYZ-tagged USB-microscope frames and an annotated map of pads, pins and chips, to probing targets the operator has approved. Python wrappers, a Flask dashboard and AGENTS.md safety rules run every move under an oscilloscope-monitored optical endstop that triggers feed hold and a STOP state with no automatic recovery. The published code covers device control, camera-to-motion calibration and map and probe-candidate review; the README presents locating, stitching and annotating the board as agent tasks. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/autoprober/))
- [BinAssist](https://github.com/symgraph/BinAssist) — integration · binary — Binary Ninja plugin by Jason Tang, who also wrote GhidrAssist and IDAssist, that adds a BinAssist sidebar for LLM-assisted analysis of the open binary. Its tabs explain functions at the LLIL, MLIL and HLIL levels and individual instructions, keep chats with context macros, MCP tool calling and an optional ReAct agent mode, propose function and variable renames, retypes and structures with confidence scores for review before they are applied, and build a semantic graph of functions and calls with community detection and source-to-sink taint paths, alongside retrieval over reference documents the analyst adds. A SymGraph tab imports and publishes names, graph data and documents through the hosted symgraph.ai service. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/binassist/))
- [BINNY](https://github.com/openbin-ai/binny) — integration · app, binary, firmware — An open-source terminal agent harness from OpenBin that runs a user-chosen hosted or local model over binary-analysis data. It reads decompiled functions, strings, imports and data from an OpenBin or OpenAPK project or a local Ghidra container run, follows triage, investigation and drafting playbooks, and saves a draft malware-analysis or vulnerability-research report for human review. It is an analysis harness with draft outputs, not a reconstruction project. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/binny-agent-harness/))
- [bn](https://github.com/banteg/bn) — integration · binary — Agent-oriented command-line interface for the Binary Ninja database already open in the GUI, reached through a companion plugin that listens on a local Unix-domain socket. Agents read decompilation, IL, disassembly, cross-references, callsites, types and data, export multi-function bundles and run unrestricted Python inside Binary Ninja; renames and prototype, structure and type edits can be previewed, are verified after they apply and roll back on failure. Large text output spills to hashed artifact files, and a packaged Codex skill describes the workflow. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/bn-binary-ninja-agent-cli/))
- [Claude Intercept](https://github.com/whaesemeyer/claude-intercept) — integration · app, protocol — A local man-in-the-middle proxy and dashboard that captures HTTP/S traffic from the user’s own computer and devices, stores it in SQLite and hands it to Claude Code through an /intercept skill and CLI. Exports such as API documentation and traffic summaries support investigating request patterns and undocumented endpoints. It is an agent-facing capture tool for authorized testing, not a reconstruction case, and it has no established REA connection. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/claude-intercept-traffic-capture/))
- [Claude Reverse Engineering Skill](https://github.com/EncidXVII/claude-reverse-engineering-skill) — integration · app, binary — Community agent skill for triage, headless exports and evidence-based RE reports; explicitly documents workflows with REA and other MCP servers. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/claude-reverse-engineering-skill/))
- [DAILA](https://github.com/mahaloz/DAILA) — integration · binary — Decompiler-independent AI assistant for function summaries and naming across IDA, Ghidra, Binary Ninja and angr-management. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/daila/))
- [decomp.wtf](https://github.com/i12bp8/decomp.wtf) — integration · game — A discontinued local app and MCP server that hands AI coding agents one function of a user-owned console game at a time, compiles each C attempt with the game’s period compiler and accepts it only when the linked result matches the original bytes. It can start a project from a ROM or disc image, or work inside forks of public decompilation projects listed on decomp.dev, where objdiff judges the result. Its final commit, dated 15 October 2026, marks it discontinued in response to opposition in the decompilation community; the code stays under MIT without further fixes or releases. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/decomp-wtf-agent-decompilation/))
- [DecompAI](https://github.com/louisgthier/decompai) — integration · binary — Gradio chat application whose LangGraph ReAct agent analyses an uploaded x86 Linux ELF binary: it lists and disassembles functions, decompiles them with Ghidra, drives radare2 and gdb, and runs shell commands and Python scripts in a privileged Kali Linux runner container. Workspaces are keyed by the binary’s SHA-256, so uploading the same file again restores the session. It came out of a school research project, and its CTF results are the authors’ own report. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/decompai/))
- [DeepExtract (DeepExtractIDA)](https://github.com/marcosd4h/DeepExtractIDA) — integration · binary — IDA Pro 9.x plugin and PowerShell batch extractor that turn Windows PE files into material AI coding agents can read: a per-binary SQLite database of PE metadata, signatures, disassembly, Hex-Rays pseudocode, cross-references, strings, stack frames, loops and dangerous API calls, plus optional grouped C++ and assembly files with per-function caller, callee and string headers and JSON indexes. It runs headless over directories, file lists or the modules of a running process, or interactively on the open IDA database, keeping the analyst’s renames and comments. The agent commands that work on this output come from the separate DeepExtractRuntime project. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/deepextract-ida/))
- [Gepetto](https://github.com/JusticeRage/Gepetto) — integration · binary — IDA Pro plugin that sends Hex-Rays pseudocode to a hosted or local language model the user selects, to explain a function, comment it or propose new variable and function names, which the user picks from before they are applied. A chat console inside IDA also lets the model call tools that read disassembly, decompilation, cross-references, strings and types, rename and comment the database and run Python. The README warns that these general-purpose models may well be wrong and asks users to treat every result critically. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/gepetto/))
- [ghidra-rpc](https://github.com/cellebrite-labs/ghidra-rpc) — integration · binary — ghidra-rpc is a CLI integration that lets AI coding assistants use a persistent Ghidra session for binary analysis. It returns structured JSON for tasks such as decompilation, symbol annotation, type recovery, patching, and binary version comparison. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ghidra-rpc/))
- [GhidraGPT](https://github.com/weirdmachine64/GhidraGPT) — integration · binary — Ghidra extension that sends one function’s decompiled C to a hosted or local language model the user selects and streams the answer into a GhidraGPT console. Its three actions, offered in the Decompiler and Listing context menus, explain the function; rewrite it by writing recovered function, parameter and variable names, types, the prototype and inline comments back into the program; or audit it for likely security issues. Every action uses only the current function’s decompiler output, and the README calls audit findings leads to verify rather than sound static analysis. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ghidragpt/))
- [GhidrAssist](https://github.com/symgraph/GhidrAssist) — integration · binary — Ghidra extension by Jason Tang, who also wrote GhidrAssistMCP, that adds a tabbed GhidrAssist window to CodeBrowser for LLM-assisted analysis of the open program. Its tabs explain decompiled or disassembled functions with a security summary, keep persistent chats with context macros, MCP tool calling and an optional ReAct agent mode, propose function and variable renames, retypes and structures for review before they are applied, and build a semantic knowledge graph of functions, calls and security features with community detection, alongside Lucene retrieval over reference documents the analyst adds. A SymGraph tab imports and publishes names, types, graph data and documents through the hosted symgraph.ai service. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ghidrassist/))
- [IDA NO MCP](https://github.com/P4nda0s/IDA-NO-MCP) — integration · binary — IDA Pro exporter that turns an analyzed binary into plain files for AI coding tools such as Cursor or Claude Code instead of serving it over MCP. It writes each decompiled function as a .c file, or as .asm when decompilation fails, under a header listing its name, address, callers and callees, along with the string, import and export tables, 1 MB hexdump chunks of every segment and logs of fallbacks, failures and skipped library functions. It ships as an IDA plugin (Export for AI, Ctrl-Shift-E) for small and medium files and as a Rust command-line binary for large or batch exports, which can skip IDA's auto-analysis. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ida-no-mcp/))
- [IDA-Skill](https://github.com/miunasu/IDA-Skill) — integration · binary — Agent skill that has an AI agent triage malware samples statically in IDA Pro through headless idat. An initialization script builds the IDA database and exports a summary, imports, exports, entry-point decompilation and filtered strings, and an executor runs IDAPython snippets or files against that database. SKILL.md sets a static-only rule, a top-down method and a rule to hand large string dumps to a sub-agent, and bundled scripts add cryptographic-constant detection, an export-size check and REAI, which sends decompiled functions to a configured OpenAI-compatible model to describe them and rename them with an AI_ prefix. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ida-skill-malware-analysis/))
- [IDASQL](https://github.com/allthingsida/idasql) — integration · binary — SQL interface to IDA Pro databases that exposes a binary's analysis as more than 30 live virtual tables for agents and scripts. The tables cover functions, segments, names, imports, cross-references, basic blocks, instructions, strings, types, decompilation and annotations, and part of the database can be edited through them. It runs as a standalone CLI that can open raw binaries through idalib, as a plugin in IDA's command-line dropdown, as an HTTP REST or MCP server, or through a skills plugin for Claude Code, GitHub Copilot CLI and Codex, and it can work across several databases at once. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/idasql/))
- [IDAssist](https://github.com/symgraph/IDAssist) — integration · binary — IDA Pro plugin by Jason Tang, who also wrote GhidrAssist, that adds a dockable PySide6 panel for LLM-assisted analysis of the open database. Its tabs explain decompiled or disassembled functions with a security summary, keep persistent chats with context macros and an optional ReAct agent mode, propose function, variable and type renames for review before they are applied, and build a semantic knowledge graph of functions, calls, data flows and security traits, with retrieval over reference documents the analyst adds. Models can call built-in IDA tools or external MCP servers, and a SymGraph tab imports and publishes names, types and graph data through the hosted symgraph.ai service. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/idassist/))
- [Kong](https://github.com/amruth-sn/kong) — integration · binary — Command-line pipeline that runs Ghidra in-process through PyGhidra and asks Claude, OpenAI or OpenAI-compatible models to name the functions, types and parameters of a stripped binary, working bottom-up through the call graph so callers see already-named callees. It matches known library and cryptographic signatures first, sends functions whose decompilation looks obfuscated through a tool-using deobfuscation pass with z3-based simplification, applies results to the Ghidra program and exports annotated C and an analysis.json file. Its README calls it the first agentic reverse engineer; that label and its accuracy and cost figures are the author’s claims. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/kong-agentic-reverse-engineer/))
- [LeanREA](https://github.com/aicodeking/leanrea) — integration · app, binary — An independent agent skill that routes application and binary investigations through ordinary command-line tools. It supplies batched triage recipes, targeted source/disassembly reading and explicit seen/inferred/unknown evidence labels, accompanied by a small author-reported comparison against an REA MCP workflow. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/leanrea-skill/))
- [Mizuchi](https://github.com/macabeus/mizuchi) — integration · binary, game — Plugin pipeline for matching decompilation that loops over functions in an existing decomp project: m2c drafts C, decomp-permuter mutates it, Claude rewrites it through a compile-and-diff tool, and objdiff accepts a result only when the compiled object matches the target assembly. A local Decomp Atlas web app indexes matched and unmatched functions with call graphs and code embeddings, helps pick the next function and builds prompts, while HTML reports summarise each run. An optional integrator places matched C into a git worktree, checks the build and can commit, push or open a PR. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/mizuchi-matching-decompilation-pipeline/))
- [OpenOCD SPI Dump Plugin](https://github.com/lukejenkins/claude-openocd-spi-dump) — integration · firmware — OpenOCD SPI Dump Plugin guides an agent through extracting SPI flash or EEPROM contents using a target MCU's SWD/JTAG debug connection. It supplies MCU register maps, a RAM-resident C reader, linker and OpenOCD Tcl templates, and a workflow for JEDEC identification and dump checking. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/claude-openocd-spi-dump/))
- [r2ai](https://github.com/radareorg/r2ai) — integration · binary — AI plugins inside radare2 for function explanations, naming suggestions and model-assisted decompilation using local or remote models. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/r2ai/))
- [re-shell](https://github.com/schlarpc/re-shell) — integration · app, binary, protocol, website — re-shell is a Nix flake development environment that provides Claude Code with reverse engineering tools and workflows. Its skills select guidance for Windows binaries, Android packages, and web captures based on the files being analyzed. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/re-shell/))
- [ReAgent](https://github.com/Dryxio/reagent) — integration · binary, game — An open-source Python agent, published on PyPI as auto-re-agent, that drafts C/C++ implementations of functions in a compiled program from Ghidra evidence and has a separately configurable checker model review each draft in a bounded fix loop. A candidate is accepted only after a structural comparison with the decompile, assembly, control-flow and P-code evidence, the project’s own build, test and optional differential commands, and 11 heuristic parity signals. The README calls these gates conservative evidence, not proof of equivalence, and the tool writes candidates to an overlay rather than patching the source tree. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/reagent-cpp-reconstruction-agent/))
- [RevEng.AI Ghidra Plugin](https://github.com/RevEngAI/plugin-ghidra) — integration · binary — Connects Ghidra to RevEng.AI for function similarity, recovered names, AI explanations and agent-assisted annotation. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/revengai-ghidra/))
- [Reverify](https://github.com/2akouwu/reverify) — integration · binary — Python CLI and stdio MCP server that checks a model’s structured claims about a binary, such as bytes or typed values at an offset, strings, imports and exports, instruction mnemonics, emulation results and tested or Z3-proved equivalence, and returns VERIFIED, REFUTED or INCONCLUSIVE with the evidence the tools observed. Grounded and refuted results are kept in a per-binary ledger on disk, so a reset or later session resumes from them instead of from a summary, and a reconstruct loop has a model propose claims until an informative set verifies. A separate rollover command replaces context compaction in Claude Code, Codex, Gemini CLI or OpenCode with file hand-offs and fresh sessions. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/reverify-claim-verifier/))
- [Reverse API Engineer](https://github.com/kalil0321/reverse-api-engineer) — integration · website — Python CLI that turns the HTTP traffic a website actually produces into a typed API client. A browser session, driven by the user through Playwright or by an AI agent through Playwright or Chrome DevTools MCP servers or Vercel's agent-browser CLI, is recorded to a HAR file; a coding agent run through the Claude Agent SDK, OpenCode, Cursor or GitHub Copilot then reads the capture and writes a client, README and usage example in one of ten languages. A separate collector mode gathers structured JSON/CSV data with web search and fetch. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/reverse-api-engineer/))
- [reverse-workbench-skill](https://github.com/Asaiuta/reverse-workbench-skill) — integration · app, binary, firmware, website — reverse-workbench-skill is a multi-agent-harness skill bundle that routes investigations to workflows for APKs, native binaries, firmware, frontend JavaScript, and network traffic. Its documented paths include Ghidra headless exports, binary diffing, and evidence recording, alongside broader security-analysis skills and helper scripts. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/reverse-workbench-skill/))
- [ReverserAI](https://github.com/mrphrazer/reverser_ai) — integration · binary — Binary Ninja plugin that suggests function names from HLIL decompiler output with a language model run locally through llama.cpp; the README says it operates entirely offline once the model is downloaded. It can rename the current function, every function still carrying a default sub_ name in call-graph post-order, or only those referencing strings and imported or library symbols. The author presents it as a research proof of concept whose only implemented task is function naming. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/reverser-ai/))
- [Rikugan](https://github.com/buzzer-re/Rikugan) — integration · binary — IDA Pro and Binary Ninja plugin that runs its own agent loop inside the disassembler: a model the user chooses streams its answers and calls more than 60 in-process tools for navigation, decompilation, disassembly, cross-references, strings, types, annotations, scripting and host IL or microcode edits. An orchestrator can map a binary and spawn parallel subagents, findings persist in a RIKUGAN.md file beside the database, and experimental commands apply plain-language patches or, on Binary Ninja, remove control-flow flattening and other obfuscation. README says it was vibecoded with Claude Code. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rikugan/))
- [SpecterOps Reverse Engineering Skills](https://github.com/SpecterOps/skills/tree/main/plugins/reverse-engineering) — integration · binary — Focused Binary Ninja and Ghidra analysis skills that describe MCP tool use, analysis recipes and annotation practices. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/specterops-re-skills/))
- [ToCode](https://github.com/buzzer-re/ToCode) — integration · app, binary — Python CLI that exports a binary, IDA database, Android APK, .NET assembly or whole application folder into a source-like project tree for coding agents: recovered C (Java for APKs, C# and IL for .NET) with matching assembly and function summaries, recovered headers and types, section dumps, JSON indexes of functions, strings, imports, exports, reachability and call-graph clusters, and generated AGENTS.md and CLAUDE.md files. It drives IDA, Binary Ninja, radare2 with r2ghidra or angr, and for folders links each import to the defining file's function using the platform's loader rules. Its tocode mcp server keeps the decompiler open so an agent can record names, types and comments and re-render only the affected files. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/tocode/))
- [Universal Modder](https://github.com/rehan-remade/universal-modder) — integration · game — Packages agent skills, three role briefs, a Python CLI and preconfigured fal MCP settings for modding PC games the user owns. Its reverse-engineering guidance sends managed, native and data-format work to external decompilers and debuggers, and requires a round-trip check before a reverse-engineered file format is used; one worked example ships an Age of Empires II sprite writer built this way. It is a modding toolkit with agent-written field notes, not a game decompilation, and its example results remain project-reported. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/universal-modder-agent-toolkit/))
- [website-to-cli](https://github.com/dennisonbertram/website-to-cli) — integration · website — website-to-cli is an agent skill for deriving terminal clients from captured website traffic. Its scripts record a HAR and filter requests to identify endpoint methods, paths, parameters, payload shapes and authentication headers. The skill then uses a Bun CLI template to build a client. The repository includes clients for crates.io and Hacker News search. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/website-to-cli/))
- [WPeGPT](https://github.com/WPeace-HcH/WPeGPT) — integration · binary — IDA plugin, formerly WPeChatGPT and inspired by Gepetto, that sends the Hex-Rays pseudocode of a function to an OpenAI-compatible model (OpenAI, DeepSeek or another compatible endpoint) and writes the model's analysis back into IDA as comments. Interactive actions explain a function's purpose and behavior, suggest variable names, re-express small routines such as XOR decoding in Python and flag potential vulnerabilities. Version 3.0 adds WPeServer, a TCP command server inside IDA through which an external controller or the separate wpegpt-analyzer skill runs headless light, full or vulnerability-focused passes, with string classification, network-indicator extraction, function suspiciousness scoring and JSON plus Markdown reports. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/wpegpt/))

<a id="game-analysis"></a>
## Game analysis tools

- [.AUP-File-Type-Decryptor-and-Viewer](https://github.com/PredatorIWD/.AUP-File-Type-Decryptor-and-Viewer) — tool · game — .AUP-File-Type-Decryptor-and-Viewer is a local browser tool for inspecting Dragon Soul .aup game asset files. It implements the documented LZSS variant, previews image, audio and text formats, and exports files while preserving folder structure.
