Its Python tools decompress DLZ with byte swapping and RLE, parse versioned DL records into pandas DataFrames, and decode CAN messages using supplied DBC files. The documentation records the proprietary 29-bit CAN identifier layout. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/holley-efi-parser/))
- [Impacket](https://github.com/fortra/impacket) — tool · protocol — Impacket is a Python library for constructing and parsing network packets and working with protocol implementations. It covers protocols including SMB and MSRPC and includes example tools. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/impacket/))
- [inspectrum](https://github.com/miek/inspectrum) — tool · protocol — Desktop analyzer for recorded radio signals, primarily captures from software-defined radio receivers, which the README says handles files of 100 GB and more. A zoomable spectrogram with a tunable filter feeds sample, amplitude, frequency, phase and threshold plots, and cursors measure period and symbol rate and extract symbol values to stdout or the clipboard. Selected time spans, filtered samples and demodulated traces can be exported, and SigMF annotations can be displayed over the spectrogram. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/inspectrum/))
- [libimobiledevice](https://github.com/libimobiledevice/libimobiledevice) — tool · app, protocol — C library and command-line tools that speak iOS devices' native service protocols without Apple's proprietary libraries or a jailbreak. Over usbmuxd or Wi-Fi sync it implements lockdownd pairing and services such as AFC file access, backup, app installation, crash reports, syslog, debugserver and WebKit inspection, plus Cython bindings. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/libimobiledevice/))
- [LogicAnalyzer](https://github.com/gusmanb/logicanalyzer) — tool · protocol — LogicAnalyzer is a hardware and software tool for capturing digital signals and decoding communication protocols. It supports triggered capture, multiple channels, and Sigrok protocol decoders. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/logicanalyzer/))
- [Lutron Protocols](https://github.com/alxgmpr/lutron-protocols) — project · app, binary, firmware, protocol — Lutron Protocols researches CCA radio packets, CCX Thread and CoAP messages, the LEAP processor API and IPL Designer integration frames for Lutron lighting systems. Its documentation covers device behavior and firmware, while TypeScript tools capture and decode traffic and parse Designer project files. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/lutron-protocols/))
- [Mirage](https://github.com/RCayre/mirage) — tool · protocol — Python framework from IoT-security research at LAAS-CNRS for analysing wireless communications, with lightweight protocol stacks for Bluetooth Low Energy, Bluetooth, Enhanced ShockBurst, Mosart, Zigbee, Wi-Fi and infrared that a command-line interpreter drives through chainable modules. For protocol analysis it offers per-protocol scan, information and sniffing modules, BLE ATT/GATT discovery that saves the discovered attribute table, HCI monitoring of an Android phone through its btsnoop log over ADB, PCAP reading and writing, and an experimental HackRF software-radio receive path with FSK demodulators and BLE and Zigbee decoders. Its author has deprecated it in favour of the WHAD tool suite. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/mirage-wireless-framework/))
- [NearDrop](https://github.com/grishka/NearDrop) — tool · protocol — macOS menu bar app that partially implements Nearby Share and Quick Share to exchange files and links with Android devices. Its protocol document describes the protocol as understood from Chromium sources and a decompiled Google Services Framework APK. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/neardrop/))
- [Netzob](https://github.com/netzob/netzob) — tool · protocol — Model communication protocols and infer message layouts from traces, with configured active learning for protocol state machines. Python APIs expose message fields, symbols and automata for parsing, traffic generation and fuzzing. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/netzob/))
- [opendbc](https://github.com/commaai/opendbc) — tool · protocol — comma.ai's repository of DBC files describing the CAN messages and signals of many car makes, with a Python library that parses and builds CAN messages from those definitions; its porting guide has contributors reverse engineer new messages from recorded drives in the cabana tool. Per-brand car ports built on it parse vehicle state, identify models from ECU firmware versions and generate openpilot's steering, gas and brake messages, while safety code for comma's panda interface restricts which messages can be sent. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/opendbc/))
- [Packetry](https://github.com/greatscottgadgets/packetry) — tool · protocol — Great Scott Gadgets' USB 2.0 protocol analysis application for live capture with the Cynthion analyzer or for exploring previously captured traffic, working from physical-layer packet data at High, Full and Low Speed. Its GTK 4 interface offers a hierarchical view that expands high-level events into their transactions and packets, plus chronological transaction and packet views, while a device pane summarizes each observed device from its decoded descriptors, including HID report descriptors. Captures are opened and saved as pcap files with the LINKTYPE_USB_2_0 link type, and v0.5.0 added pcapng with custom blocks for non-packet events. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/packetry/))
- [pbtk](https://github.com/marin-m/pbtk) — tool · app, protocol — Recover Protobuf message definitions from compiled programs as readable .proto files. Extractors cover the Java runtimes in JAR, DEX and APK files (including ProGuard-obfuscated code), binaries that embed Protobuf reflection metadata, and web apps using the Protobuf-URL format. A GUI then binds a recovered message type to an HTTP endpoint, decodes hex-encoded captured samples into an editable field tree and resends edited messages, so analysts can relate each field to the response. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pbtk/))
- [PiCCANTE](https://github.com/Alia5/PiCCANTE) — tool · protocol — Raspberry Pi Pico firmware and hardware designs for a low-cost CAN bus interface aimed at exploring and reverse engineering vehicle networks. It runs two CAN 2.0B channels on RP2040 boards or three on RP2350 boards through the PIO-based can2040 library and exposes them over USB as a GVRET device for SavvyCAN and as per-channel SLCAN devices for Linux SocketCAN via can-utils. A configuration shell, a partial ELM327 emulator for OBD-II PID requests with ISO-TP multi-frame replies, and Wi-Fi, Bluetooth and a web interface on Pico W boards complete the firmware, alongside perfboard and KiCad PCB designs with 3D-printable cases for an OBD-II dongle. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/piccante/))
- [Protobuf Decoder](https://github.com/pawitp/protobuf-decoder) — tool · protocol — Pawit Pornkitprasan's React web application for decoding Protocol Buffers data without the original .proto files, offered as a hosted page or run locally, with all decoding done in the browser. Pasted hex or base64 input, or an uploaded file, is split into a table of byte ranges, field numbers, wire types and contents: varints are shown as unsigned, two's-complement and zigzag integers, fixed32 and fixed64 values as integers and floating-point numbers, and length-delimited fields as nested messages when they parse cleanly, otherwise as UTF-8 strings or bytes. It skips a leading gRPC message header and can optionally split a stream of varint length-delimited messages. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pawitp-protobuf-decoder/))
- [protobuf-inspector](https://github.com/mildsunrise/protobuf-inspector) — tool · protocol — Parses Protocol Buffers blobs (proto2 or proto3) without their .proto definition and prints a colored view of the fields in wire order, trying each length-delimited chunk as a nested message, then a packed array, a string and finally a hexdump. Analysts record what they work out in a protobuf_config.py file, with field types and names, message and enum types and custom native types, until the parser no longer has to guess. Field names, zig-zag encoding, signedness and integer-versus-float choices cannot be read from the wire, and the README says guesses can be wrong, especially for small chunks. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/protobuf-inspector/))
- [protodump](https://github.com/arkadiyt/protodump) — tool · binary, protocol — Go command-line utility that recovers Protocol Buffers schemas from a compiled binary or any other file: it scans the raw bytes for serialized FileDescriptorProto records embedded by generated code, locating them through their .proto file names, and writes each one back out as a .proto source file under its original descriptor path. The regenerated files use proto2, proto3 or editions syntax and include the package, imports, common file options, messages with reserved ranges, nested enums, oneofs, maps and default values, and services with their RPC methods, giving analysts message and API definitions to work with when decoding Protobuf traffic. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/protodump/))
- [PULSAR](https://github.com/hgascon/pulsar) — tool · protocol — Research prototype by Hugo Gascon and colleagues that learns a model of an unknown network protocol from recorded traffic. Messages extracted from PCAP traces are embedded as token or n-gram vectors, clustered by matrix factorization through the PRISMA R module, and turned into a Markov-chain state model with message templates and rules linking fields to earlier messages, written out as model files and a Graphviz state graph. The same models drive the tool's session simulator and stateful fuzzer, described in the authors' SecureComm 2015 paper; this entry covers only the protocol-learning part. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pulsar/))
- [RoflLens](https://github.com/ss26367098/rofllens) — tool · binary, game, protocol — RoflLens is a Python library and CLI for analysing League of Legends ROFL v2 replay containers and network transport. It exports blocks and payloads, and offers patch-specific decoders for events such as movement, spells and deaths when the client version, protocol digest and executable hash match. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rofllens/))
- [rtl_433](https://github.com/merbanan/rtl_433) — tool · protocol — Receives and decodes transmissions from ISM-band devices such as weather sensors, remote controls, doorbells and alarm sensors, using RTL-SDR or SoapySDR receivers or recorded I/Q files, and emits decoded fields as JSON, CSV, MQTT and other outputs. For signals no built-in decoder recognizes, a pulse analyzer reports OOK/FSK timings and hints for a flexible -X decoder that slices frames into bits; its analysis guide leaves mapping those bits to values to the analyst, working from known readings. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rtl-433/))
- [SavvyCAN](https://github.com/collin80/SavvyCAN) — tool · protocol — Capture or load CAN bus traffic and investigate frames with sniffer, graphing, file-comparison and ISO-TP/UDS decoding views; loaded or analyst-edited DBC files map raw bytes to named signals. Separate custom-sender, fuzzing and UDS-scan windows transmit frames on a live bus. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/savvycan/))
- [Scapy](https://github.com/secdev/scapy) — tool · protocol — Script packet dissection, construction, request/reply matching and capture-file analysis in Python. Custom packet fields and layer bindings let analysts encode understood wire formats and investigate protocol behavior. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/scapy/))
- [SigDigger](https://github.com/BatchDrake/SigDigger) — tool · protocol — Qt graphical digital signal analyzer by Gonzalo José Carracedo Carballal, built on his Suscan and Sigutils libraries; its user manual names reverse engineering of radio signals with unknown parameters as the main use. It receives from SoapySDR-supported SDRs or plays back recorded I/Q and WAV files, shows spectrum and waterfall views, and lets analysts measure captured stretches in a time window with carrier recovery, transforms and symbol sampling. Inspector tabs run user-tunable PSK, FSK and ASK demodulators with parameter estimation and can forward demodulated samples; it also decodes analog video and previews AM, FM and SSB audio. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/sigdigger/))
- [Sniffle](https://github.com/nccgroup/Sniffle) — tool · protocol — NCC Group's sniffer for Bluetooth 5 and 4.x Low Energy, made of firmware for TI CC1352/CC26x2 radio boards and Python host tools. It captures advertising and connection traffic on the 1M, 2M and coded PHYs, follows connections through channel-map, connection-parameter and PHY changes, captures BT5 extended advertising, and filters by MAC address, IRK, RSSI or advertisement content; for a filtered target it hops all three primary advertising channels, which the README says makes connection detection nearly three times more reliable than single-channel sniffers. Captures are saved as Ubertooth-compatible PCAP, a Wireshark extcap plugin starts captures from the Wireshark GUI, and command-line decoders print advertising data, including Apple and Microsoft manufacturer-specific fields. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/sniffle/))
- [SteamKit](https://github.com/SteamRE/SteamKit) — tool · game, protocol — .NET library that implements the client side of Valve’s Steam network protocol: connection-manager sessions, authentication, friends, unified service and Game Coordinator messages, Web API calls and CDN depot downloads. Message classes are generated from Steam protobufs tracked in a submodule and from the project’s own SteamLanguage definitions. Its Resources tree adds protocol-research tools: NetHook2, a Windows DLL that hooks a running Steam client to dump the network messages it exchanges, NetHookAnalyzer2 for viewing those dumps, and ProtobufDumper for recovering protobuf definitions from executables. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/steamkit/))
- [termshark](https://github.com/gcla/termshark) — tool · protocol — Graham Clark's terminal user interface for tshark, inspired by Wireshark and aimed at inspecting captures on a remote machine, for example over SSH, without copying them back to a desktop. It reads pcap files, fifos or standard input, or captures live from one or more interfaces, and shows a packet list filtered with Wireshark display filters next to the dissected packet structure and a hex view. Analysis views reassemble TCP and UDP streams with search and client- or server-only display, list Ethernet, IPv4, IPv6, UDP and TCP conversations that can be turned into display filters, and search packet contents; Wireshark-style profiles can reuse a linked Wireshark profile's packet colors. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/termshark/))
- [Universal Radio Hacker (URH)](https://github.com/jopohl/urh) — tool · protocol — Demodulate and decode signals captured with software-defined radios, with automatic modulation-parameter detection and customizable decodings. Analysts assign protocol fields and message types by hand or review rule-based automatic field inference; a fuzzer targets stateless protocols and a simulator supports stateful exchanges. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/universal-radio-hacker/))
- [USBPcap](https://github.com/desowin/usbpcap) — tool · protocol — Tomasz Moń's open-source USB packet capture for Windows. A filter driver installed in the Windows USB device stack records the USB Request Blocks (URBs) exchanged with devices on each root hub, and USBPcapCMD writes them to pcap files in the LINKTYPE_USBPCAP format, which Wireshark dissects. Since 1.2.0.0 the project has recommended running USBPcapCMD as a Wireshark extcap: each root hub then appears as a capture interface, with options to capture selected, all or newly connected devices and to add the descriptors of devices that are already attached. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/usbpcap/))
- [VanBus](https://github.com/0xCAFEDECAF/VanBus) — tool · protocol — Arduino library for ESP8266/ESP8285 and ESP32 boards that receives and transmits packets on the VAN (Vehicle Area Network) comfort bus that PSA fitted to Peugeot and Citroën models until about 2009. It decodes the signal from a GPIO pin, wired through a CAN transceiver or a receive-only resistor divider, in software into packets with identifier, command flags, data and a 15-bit CRC that it can check or try to repair by flipping single bits, and reports queue and error statistics. Example sketches dump raw packets, send a packet, and parse comfort-bus traffic into readable fields on the serial port or a live web page. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/vanbus/))
- [Walksnail Ascent VRX UART Protocol](https://github.com/maks421400-lgtm/Walksnail-Ascent-VRX-UART) — documentation · protocol — Walksnail Ascent VRX UART Protocol documents serial control messages for Walksnail video receivers and supplies a C reference transmitter with an STM32 example. It specifies framing, payload checksums, remote keys, channel selection and status queries, while recording contradictions and unknown reply layouts. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/walksnail-ascent-vrx-uart/))
- [Wireshark](https://gitlab.com/wireshark/wireshark) — tool · app, firmware, protocol — Inspect captured application and device traffic as decoded protocol fields, with filtering and shared GUI/TShark dissection. Custom dissectors let an analyst encode newly understood packet formats and compare protocol implementations. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/wireshark/))
- [Локальный аудиостриминг на Яндекс.Станцию](https://github.com/volodarskij/yandex-station-local-streaming) — documentation · protocol — Local audio streaming on Yandex Station is a technical guide to the device’s Glagol WebSocket control channel and its HTTP audio-stream behavior. It documents radio_play, token acquisition, message formats and MP3 CBR stream requirements, including an exact Content-Length. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/yandex-station-local-streaming/))

<a id="native-analysis"></a>
## Native analysis tools

- [010 Editor](https://www.sweetscape.com/010editor/) — tool · binary — SweetScape Software's commercial hex and text editor whose Binary Templates, C/C++-like struct definitions with control flow and functions, parse binary files into hierarchical, editable structures. Templates can run when a file opens and are shared through an online repository, scripts with C/C++-like syntax automate edits and batch runs, and the tools include a Capstone-based disassembler for x86, ARM, MIPS, PowerPC and other code, byte-by-byte comparison, checksums and hashes, and editing of drives and, on Windows, process memory. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/010-editor/))
- [angr-management](https://github.com/angr/angr-management) — tool · binary — Cross-platform graphical binary analysis tool powered by the angr platform, with disassembly and decompilation views. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/angr-management/))
- [AngryGhidra](https://github.com/Nalen98/AngryGhidra) — tool · binary — Ghidra extension that runs angr symbolic execution from a CodeBrowser panel. The analyst marks a destination address, an optional start address and addresses to avoid with hotkeys, and can make program arguments, memory ranges or registers symbolic, write concrete values into memory and registers, hook instructions or apply bytes patched in Ghidra to the angr project. A bundled Python script runs angr's explorer on the program's original file, the listing highlights the instructions along the path it finds, and the panel shows the argument, memory and stdin values that reach the destination. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/angryghidra/))
- [Bin-Hunter](https://github.com/Emirzonee/Bin-Hunter) — tool · binary — Bin-Hunter is a Python command-line tool for initial static inspection of binary files. It identifies formats from magic bytes, calculates whole-file and peak-region Shannon entropy, extracts readable strings, and finds URLs, IP addresses, email addresses, and suspicious keywords in those strings. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/bin-hunter/))
- [BinAbsInspector](https://github.com/KeenSecurityLab/BinAbsInspector) — tool · binary — Static vulnerability scanner from Tencent Keen Lab that runs as a Ghidra extension or headless script and applies abstract interpretation to Ghidra's P-code rather than to assembly, covering x86, x64, ARMv7 and AArch64 binaries. Its flow- and context-sensitive interprocedural analysis, with configurable value-set size and call-string depth, optional Z3 constraint solving and models of external functions, feeds checkers for 13 CWE classes, including command injection, buffer overflows and out-of-bounds access, format strings, integer overflow, double free, use-after-free and NULL dereference. Warnings list the instruction address and its call context, in the Ghidra console or as JSON. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/binabsinspector/))
- [Binary BPE Tokenizer for Executable Analysis](https://github.com/mjbommar/binary-tokenizer-paper) — research · app, binary — Binary BPE Tokenizer for Executable Analysis studies byte-pair encoding of ELF, PE, Mach-O and APK files for transformer-based binary analysis. The repository provides paper sources, 4K to 64K tokenizer vocabularies, Python evaluation scripts and a sample archive. The author reports 2.89 bytes per token for the 64K vocabulary. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/binary-bpe-tokenizer-research/))
- [Binary Ninja](https://binary.ninja/) — tool · binary, firmware — Vector 35's commercial interactive disassembler, decompiler and debugger, whose decompiler is built on the BNIL family of intermediate languages and shows either C or BNIL for every officially supported architecture. Depending on the edition it decompiles x86/x86-64, ARMv7/Thumb2, AArch64, PowerPC, MIPS, RISC-V, MSP430, 6502, nanoMIPS, TriCore, C-SKY, M·CORE, Hexagon, NDS32 and TMS320C6x, and it loads raw binaries and PE/COFF, ELF, Mach-O, TE, Intel HEX, Motorola S-Record, TI-TXT, MD1ROM and VxWorks files. C++, Python and Rust APIs automate analysis inside or outside the UI; the open-source debugger runs locally or remotely on Windows, Linux and macOS and also connects to GDB, LLDB and iOS debugserver targets; patches can be made by editing assembly, in the hex editor or with the bundled SCC compiler; and 6.0 added an MCP server. Ultimate adds Firmware Ninja for embedded firmware, which finds code and data regions by entropy and detects memory-mapped I/O accesses, while the EFI Resolver workflow types protocol usage in UEFI PEI, DXE and SMM modules. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/binary-ninja/))
- [Binary Ninja API](https://github.com/Vector35/binaryninja-api) — tool · binary, firmware — Vector 35’s public C++, Python and Rust APIs for Binary Ninja, with their documentation, examples and the open-source plugins built on them. Binary Ninja itself is a closed-source commercial application, and this repository does not contain its analysis core. The open plugins cover the x86/x86_64, ARM64, ARMv7, PowerPC, MIPS, RISC-V and MSP430 architectures, ELF, PE/COFF, Mach-O and Apple shared-cache and kernel-cache loaders, DWARF, PDB and IDB import, and Objective-C, RTTI and SVD analysis, while the examples walk the low- and medium-level intermediate languages and extend the analysis pipeline through workflows. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/binary-ninja-api/))
- [Binary Ninja Security Analysis Plugin](https://github.com/meerkatone/rust_binary_analysis) — tool · binary — Binary Ninja Security Analysis Plugin batches binary analysis from a Rust plugin and exports Parquet reports. It records architecture, hashes, functions, strings, segments, cyclomatic complexity, entropy and references to selected process-execution functions. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/binary-ninja-security-analysis-plugin/))
- [Binary Refinery](https://github.com/binref/refinery) — tool · binary — Large collection of composable Python command-line units for transforming binary data: encodings, compression, encryption, archive and executable-format extraction, carving and pattern search. Each unit reads stdin and writes stdout, so units chain into shell pipelines, while framing, multibin arguments and meta variables let one pipeline process many chunks, and the binref command searches unit help. The author describes it as a command-line take on CyberChef focused on malware triage; the README also points to a separate binref/agent repository of instructions for language-model agents that use refinery. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/binary-refinery/))
- [BinaryInsight](https://github.com/ismailtsdln/BinaryInsight) — tool · binary — BinaryInsight inspects ELF, PE, and Mach-O executables and libraries in an interactive terminal view or a text-report CLI. Its Rust core uses goblin to parse binary structure and exposes sections, symbols, printable strings, and security mitigation indicators such as PIE, NX, RELRO, ASLR, and DEP. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/binaryinsight/))
- [BinCAT](https://github.com/airbus-seclab/bincat) — tool · binary — Static binary code analysis toolkit designed for reverse engineers. Starting from a chosen address and initial state, it computes register and memory values, taint and reconstructed types, with forward and backward analysis and heap use-after-free and double-free detection; bits it cannot determine are shown as unknown. It is driven from an IDA plugin, where analysts can override values or taint, skip functions or replace instructions with NOPs before re-running, or from Python and an .ini configuration for automation. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/bincat/))
- [binglide](https://github.com/wapiflapi/binglide) — tool · binary — Visual reverse-engineering tool that gives a quick overview of the data types present in a file. It reads no particular format, so it also works when headers are missing or corrupted or the format is unknown. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/binglide/))
- [bingrep](https://github.com/m4b/bingrep) — tool · binary — bingrep is a command-line tool for inspecting and colorizing binary files. It displays details from ELF, Mach, Unix and BSD archives, and PE files, with experimental views of hexadecimal data and file offset ranges. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/bingrep/))
- [BinNavi](https://github.com/google/binnavi) — tool · binary — Google's open-sourced binary analysis IDE for inspecting, navigating, editing and annotating the control-flow graphs and call graphs of disassembled code. It also collects and combines execution traces and keeps track of analysis results among a group of analysts. Disassemblies come from IDA Pro through a bundled export plugin that writes the PostgreSQL database BinNavi uses for disassemblies, comments and traces; the README notes that no exporter exists yet for other disassemblers. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/binnavi/))
- [BinScan](https://github.com/Mraxzist/BinScan) — tool · binary — BinScan scans files in bulk and emits per-file JSON containing format identification, structural metadata, hashes and entropy. Its C++ library includes parsers for PE, ELF and Mach-O executables alongside archives, packages, documents and images, with a staged read, analyze and write pipeline. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/binscan/))
- [binsider](https://github.com/orhun/binsider) — tool · binary — Terminal user interface for inspecting ELF binaries. It shows file information and linked shared libraries in the manner of stat and ldd, browses ELF headers, notes, sections, segments, symbols, dynamic symbols and relocations much like readelf, extracts strings with an adjustable minimum length, and offers a searchable hexdump view. Optional dynamic analysis runs the binary and traces its system calls like strace, with a summary of time and call counts per system call; the interface can also be embedded as a Rust library in Ratatui applications. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/binsider/))
- [BinSync](https://github.com/binsync/binsync) — tool · binary — Decompiler plugin for collaborative reverse engineering that records each analyst's work in a shared Git repository, one branch per user, so teammates can pull, push or auto-push changes across IDA Pro, Binary Ninja, Ghidra and angr-management. Synced artifacts are function headers (names, arguments and types), stack and global variables, structs, enums and comments, including user-defined types. It is built by mahaloz, the angr team and the SEFCOM research lab and is used by the Shellphish hacking team; scripting goes through the separate DecLib library. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/binsync/))
- [Bloaty](https://github.com/google/bloaty) — tool · binary — Bloaty is a binary size profiler that attributes file and memory usage to sources such as symbols, compilation units, and sections. It can compare sizes between binaries and supports ELF and Mach-O, with experimental PE/COFF and WebAssembly support. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/bloaty/))
- [Boomerang Decompiler](https://github.com/BoomerangDecompiler/boomerang) — tool · binary — Fork of the SourceForge Boomerang project, a general machine-code decompiler that translates executables into C. The README lists x86 (IA-32 only), PowerPC and ST20 code in ELF, PE, DOS MZ, DOS/4GW LE and Mach-O files; instruction semantics are defined in SSL specification files, and library signature files plus printf- and scanf-style format strings help recover types. It provides a boomerang-cli command-line tool and a Qt boomerang-gui application, with unit, regression and recompilation test suites. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/boomerang-decompiler/))
- [BTD (Bin To DNN)](https://github.com/monkbai/DNN-decompiler) — tool · binary — Research artifact for the USENIX Security 2023 paper “Decompiling x86 Deep Neural Network Executables”. BTD decompiles x86 executables emitted by the DNN compilers TVM, Glow and NNFusion into model specifications: operator types, network topology, dimensions and parameters such as weights and biases. It splits IDA disassembly into functions, infers each operator's type with a trained classifier over assembly tokens, records Intel Pin traces filtered by taint analysis, recovers dimensions and parameters with a trace-based symbolic execution engine, then rebuilds the model in PyTorch and compares its output with the original executable. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/btd-dnn-decompiler/))
- [capa](https://github.com/mandiant/capa) — tool · binary — Mandiant FLARE tool that reports what an executable can do by matching a YAML rule set against features extracted from PE, ELF and .NET files or 32/64-bit shellcode, such as API calls, imports, strings and constants. Matches are grouped into capability namespaces with ATT&CK and MBC tags, and verbose output shows the function or basic-block addresses and features behind each match, pointing analysts to code worth studying. It also reads CAPE, DRAKVUF and VMRay sandbox reports for dynamic features and can use vivisect, IDA Pro, Ghidra or Binary Ninja for analysis, with explorer plugins for IDA and Ghidra and a browser viewer for its JSON results. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/capa/))
- [CFG Visualizer](https://github.com/zhameersheraz/cfg-visualizer) — tool · binary — CFG Visualizer extracts function control-flow graphs from ELF, PE and Mach-O binaries with radare2 and presents them in an interactive 3D browser view. Its FastAPI backend returns graph JSON, while the frontend shows branch edges and node disassembly. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/cfg-visualizer/))
- [Class Informer](https://github.com/kweatherman/IDA_ClassInformer_PlugIn) — tool · binary — IDA Pro plugin that scans Microsoft Visual C++ binaries for virtual function tables (vftables) through their C++ run-time type information (RTTI), names, labels and comments the vftables it finds, defines the RTTI data structures and identifies single, multiple and virtual inheritance in each class hierarchy. It also processes static initializer and terminator tables, and opens a list window of the vftables with their method counts and class hierarchies, where each line jumps to its vftable. Its author notes that IDA 9's built-in RTTI plugin now finds nearly all of this data, so the current version only sets types that IDA missed and serves mainly as a gathering and browsing view. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/class-informer/))
- [class-dump](https://github.com/nygard/class-dump) — tool · app, binary — Command-line utility that reads the Objective-C runtime metadata in Mach-O executables, frameworks and bundles and prints it as ordinary Objective-C declarations for the classes, categories and protocols it finds, the same information otool -ov shows in a more compact form. It can write one header file per class, recurse into the frameworks a binary uses, choose one architecture from a universal binary, filter or sort the output, and annotate it with instance-variable offsets and implementation addresses. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/class-dump/))
- [classdump-dyld](https://github.com/limneos/classdump-dyld) — tool · app, binary — classdump-dyld is a command-line tool that generates Objective-C headers from Mach-O app binaries, libraries, frameworks, bundles, and images in the dyld shared cache. It can dump a single class or process compatible files recursively. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/classdump-dyld/))
- [cocos2d-dumper](https://github.com/d1xus/cocos2d-dumper) — tool · binary — cocos2d-dumper extracts defined function symbols from ELF shared libraries and demangles C++ names with GNU binutils. It produces a map of ELF symbol values and names for inspecting unstripped cocos2d-x binaries and other native libraries. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/cocos2d-dumper/))
- [Cpp2CS](https://github.com/MetaMikuAI/Cpp2CS) — tool · binary — Cpp2CS recovers managed methods from Android ARM64 Unity IL2CPP ELF binaries and matching metadata, emitting CIL assemblies, C# source and per-method diagnostics. Its pipeline models machine IR, IL2CPP runtime idioms, memory SSA and type recovery while marking unresolved portions. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/cpp2cs/))
- [Cutter](https://github.com/rizinorg/cutter) — tool · binary, game — Rizin-powered graphical reverse-engineering workspace with extensible views and Ghidra decompiler integration. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/cutter/))
- [cwe_checker](https://github.com/fkie-cad/cwe_checker) — tool · binary, firmware — Static analyzer from Fraunhofer FKIE that searches binary executables, mainly Linux and Unix ELF files, for code paths matching Common Weakness Enumeration bug classes such as buffer overflows, use-after-free and double free, NULL pointer dereferences, format strings and integer overflows. Ghidra disassembles the binary into a common intermediate representation on which its checks run, from simple heuristics to abstract-interpretation data-flow analysis, so it covers the CPU architectures Ghidra supports, including x86, ARM, MIPS and PowerPC. Warnings are printed as text or JSON, and a bundled Ghidra script annotates them in the disassembly for manual review. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/cwe-checker/))
- [CyberChef](https://github.com/gchq/CyberChef) — tool · binary, protocol — GCHQ's browser-based workbench for decoding, decrypting, decompressing, parsing and carving data by chaining operations into a recipe, with all processing done client-side. The pinned operation catalogue lists 506 operations, among them XOR brute force, AES, DES and Blowfish decryption, x86 and ARM disassembly, ELF info, file-type detection, file extraction, entropy and strings, YARA rules, Protobuf decoding and Ethernet, IPv4, TCP and UDP header parsing. The Magic operation tries to detect nested encodings automatically, and breakpoints step through a recipe one operation at a time to show the data at each stage. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/cyberchef/))
- [Ddisasm](https://github.com/GrammaTech/ddisasm) — tool · binary — GrammaTech's Datalog disassembler, which turns ELF and PE binaries for x86-32, x86-64, ARM32, ARM64 and MIPS32 into the GTIRB intermediate representation. It decodes a superset of possible instructions, then applies Soufflé Datalog rules and weighted heuristics to infer code locations, symbolization and function boundaries; gtirb-pprinter turns the result into reassemblable assembly or a rebuilt binary, and the GTIRB APIs or gtirb-rewriting can modify it in between. User hint files add Datalog facts or change heuristic weights where inference goes wrong. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ddisasm/))
- [de4py](https://github.com/Fadi002/de4py) — tool · app, binary — Python deobfuscation and analysis toolkit with a PySide6 GUI and a CLI mode, aimed at analysts examining obfuscated or packed Python programs. It bundles deobfuscators for named Python obfuscators such as Jawbreaker, BlankOBF, PlusOBF, Wodx, Hyperion and pyobfuscate, and an AI-assisted "Onyx" engine that pairs a local Ollama LLM (default qwen2.5-coder:1.5b) with AST cleaning and pattern matching. A file analyzer detects packers such as PyInstaller, computes hashes, looks up suspicious strings and extracts metadata, while a behavior monitor and a Python-code execution shell support dynamic inspection of a target process. It has a plugin API and can be used as a library. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/de4py/))
- [Decompiler Explorer](https://dogbolt.org) — tool · binary — Web service at dogbolt.org that runs one small uploaded executable through many decompilers and shows their C-like output side by side, described as Compiler Explorer in reverse. The pinned source has runners for angr, Binary Ninja, Boomerang, dewolf, Ghidra, Hex-Rays, Kuna, REC Studio, Reko, Relyze, RetDec, rev.ng and Snowman, which helps compare how different decompilers render the same code. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/decompiler-explorer/))
- [Decompyle++ (pycdc)](https://github.com/zrax/pycdc) — tool · app, binary — C++ disassembler and decompiler for compiled Python bytecode that aims to support bytecode from any Python version. pycdas prints the disassembly of a .pyc file, and pycdc prints reconstructed Python source to stdout with errors on stderr; both also accept raw marshalled code objects when the Python version is given on the command line. The source carries opcode tables for Python 1.0 through 3.13. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pycdc/))
- [decompyle3](https://github.com/rocky/python-decompile3) — tool · app, binary — Reworking of uncompyle6 by the same author that translates Python 3.7 and 3.8 bytecode, including some PyPy 3.7 and 3.8 bytecode, back into equivalent Python source; older bytecode stays with uncompyle6. It disassembles code objects with the companion xdis library, then tokenizes and parses the instruction stream with a refactored grammar into a tree from which source is printed, and it can decompile between given start and stop offsets or, through decompyle3-code, only a file's lambdas, generators and comprehensions. The README says the grammar rewrite does not by itself fix uncompyle6's control-flow problem, which the author is addressing with dominator-based analysis in a separate, non-public project. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/decompyle3/))
- [Dependencies](https://github.com/lucasg/Dependencies) — tool · binary — Dependencies is a Windows PE dependency analysis tool with command-line and graphical interfaces. It shows imports, exports, and DLL load dependencies to help diagnose loading problems. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/dependencies-pe/))
- [Detect It Easy](https://github.com/horsicq/Detect-It-Easy) — tool · binary, game — Format-aware file identification and static inspection using parsers, signatures and programmable heuristics. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/detect-it-easy/))
- [dewolf](https://github.com/fkie-cad/dewolf) — tool · binary — Research decompiler from Fraunhofer FKIE, developed with Singapore's DSO National Laboratories between 2019 and 2021, that runs as a Binary Ninja plugin and starts from Binary Ninja's Medium Level IL. A pipeline of preprocessing, SSA, data-flow and control-flow stages restructures code following the DREAM/DREAM++ approach and emits C-like output. Results appear in a Binary Ninja dock widget, or decompile.py prints or writes them for a whole binary or one named function in batch use. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/dewolf/))
- [Driver Buddy Reloaded](https://github.com/VoidSec/DriverBuddyReloaded) — tool · binary — IDA Pro Python plugin for triaging Windows kernel drivers, building on NCC Group's original DriverBuddy. Its auto-analysis identifies the driver model (WDM, KMDF, UMDF, WDF, Mini-Filter, Stream Minidriver, AVStream or PortCls), locates the device-control dispatch routines, labels IRP, IO_STACK_LOCATION and WDF structures and calls, and discovers and decodes IOCTL codes through Hex-Rays ctree, switch-table and immediate-operand scans. It also reports device names, symbolic links and device ACLs, pool tags, flagged APIs and opcodes, name-based call chains and heuristic vulnerability leads with per-IOCTL risk scores, in an IDA findings window, a JSON file and an HTML report. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/driver-buddy-reloaded/))
- [dyld_cache_extract](https://github.com/macmade/dyld_cache_extract) — tool · app, binary — macOS application and command-line tool that reads a dyld_shared_cache file from macOS or iOS, shows its header, mappings and image list, and copies one library or every library out to a directory. The CLI offers --info, --extract and --extract-all; the GUI presents the same header details and lets users select images to extract. The README marks the project obsolete and points to the author's dyld-cache-dump. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/dyld-cache-extract/))
- [ERESI](https://github.com/thorkill/eresi) — tool · binary — Reverse Engineering Software Interface built around ELFsh, an interactive and scriptable shell for reading and modifying ELF objects, and e2dbg, an embedded ELF debugger. ELFsh analyses sections, edits headers and symbol and relocation tables, disassembles through libasm, draws control-flow graphs and injects code into ELF files. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/eresi/))
- [esp-idf-injection-ng](https://github.com/mahdamin/esp-idf-injection-ng) — research · binary — esp-idf-injection-ng documents an investigation of ESP-IDF's compiled libnet80211.a frame-type check. The author traces why symbol overrides fail in v5 and later, then supplies an objcopy patch, demonstration firmware and captures for transmitting additional 802.11 management frames through esp_wifi_80211_tx. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/esp-idf-injection-ng/))
- [FA (Firmware Analysis)](https://github.com/doronz88/fa) — tool · binary, firmware — Python framework, mainly used as an IDA plugin, that turns the manual steps of locating symbols, such as finding a string, following its cross-reference and taking the function start, into reusable signatures. Hjson SIG files, grouped into per-platform projects, chain commands that search bytes, strings, immediates or Keystone-assembled opcodes, filter by operands and cross-references, and then set names, types, constants, enums and struct members; Python scripts can drive the same interpreter. In IDA it adds an FA menu and toolbar to find all of a project's symbols, draft a temporary signature from the current function and export symbols plus a C header, and a separate loader runs signatures over a raw ELF file without IDA. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/fa-firmware-analysis/))
- [FindCrypt - Ghidra Edition](https://github.com/d3v1l401/FindCrypt-Ghidra) — tool · binary — Ghidra script that ports Ilfak Guilfanov's FindCrypt plugin for IDA, searching a program's memory or the current selection for constants of cryptographic and compression algorithms. Its serialized signature database keeps the original plugin's 79 algorithm constant sets and, by the README's count, holds 122 detectable constants for hash functions, block and stream ciphers, Curve25519 code, Keccak and zlib; when a whole constant is absent, the script also looks for its individual elements and reports partial matches above a detection threshold. Hits become Ghidra bookmarks and a results table from which matching data can be defined. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/findcrypt-ghidra/))
- [findcrypt-yara](https://github.com/polymorf/findcrypt-yara) — tool · binary — IDA Pro plugin that scans the loaded database with YARA rules for cryptographic constants and similar signatures, lists the matches in a results window and names each matching address after its rule. Its bundled findcrypt3.rules has 126 rules covering constants and tables of hash, cipher and checksum algorithms such as MD5, SHA-1, SHA-256, SHA-512, RIPEMD-160, Whirlpool, DES, Blowfish, RC6, TEA and CRC32, big-number and prime constants, crypto API names and functions of libraries such as OpenSSL, Crypto++, MIRACL and FGint; users can add their own .rules files. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/findcrypt-yara/))
- [FLARE IDA Pro utilities](https://github.com/mandiant/flare-ida) — tool · binary — Archived collection of IDAPython plugins and scripts from Mandiant's FLARE team for malware and native-code analysis in IDA Pro. It searches code for precomputed API-name hashes used by shellcode, recovers strings built on the stack (statically, or by emulation with flare-emu in ironstrings), applies function types to structure fields and indirect calls, annotates imports with MSDN function, argument and constant information, and uses idb2pat to generate pattern files from an IDB's functions for building FLIRT signatures. Other scripts track static arguments to functions such as string decoders, link Objective-C selector references to their implementations in Mach-O files, and graft stub implementations of common Windows API imports so that decoding or unpacking code can run under IDA's Bochs emulation. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/flare-ida/))
- [FLARE-VM](https://github.com/mandiant/flare-vm) — tool · binary — Mandiant FLARE installer that sets up and maintains a Windows virtual machine for reverse engineering and malware analysis. Its PowerShell script uses Boxstarter and Chocolatey to install packages from the separate VM-Packages feed and the Chocolatey community feed, chosen in a GUI or a custom config.xml that also sets environment paths, the taskbar layout and post-install registry, service and path settings. The reviewed default configuration lists 137 packages, among them Ghidra, IDA Free, x64dbg, dnSpyEx, capa, FLOSS, PE-sieve, FakeNet-NG and Wireshark; scripts in its virtualbox folder build, snapshot and export FLARE-VM and REMnux VMs in VirtualBox. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/flare-vm/))
- [FLOSS (FLARE Obfuscated String Solver)](https://github.com/mandiant/flare-floss) — tool · binary — Mandiant FLARE string extractor for malware analysis that is used like strings.exe but also recovers obfuscated strings. Besides static ASCII and UTF-16LE strings it extracts stack strings, tight strings and strings decoded by functions: it heuristically identifies decoding routines, recovers their arguments through control-flow analysis, emulates them and diffs memory before and after emulation. It also extracts Go and Rust strings and accepts PE, ELF and raw 32- or 64-bit shellcode; JSON results can be turned into import scripts for IDA Pro, Ghidra, Binary Ninja, radare2 and x64dbg. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/flare-floss/))
- [fq](https://github.com/wader/fq) — tool · binary, protocol — fq is a command-line tool and query language for inspecting and decoding binary formats. It provides a structural hex viewer, bit-level decoding, nested format parsing, and an interactive query interface. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/fq/))
- [Ghidra](https://github.com/NationalSecurityAgency/ghidra) — tool · binary, firmware, game — Open-source analysis suite for disassembly, decompilation, graphs and Java/Python automation across executable formats. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ghidra/))
- [Ghidra Ninja ghidra_scripts](https://github.com/ghidraninja/ghidra_scripts) — tool · binary — Thomas Roth's (Ghidra Ninja) collection of Ghidra Python scripts, which appear under a Ghidra Ninja category in the Script Manager. binwalk.py and yara.py run binwalk or bundled YARA crypto-constant rules over the loaded program and bookmark the findings, YaraSearch.py does the same with a rule file the user chooses, swift_demangler.py demangles Swift function names, golang_renamer.py restores function names in stripped Go binaries from the .gopclntab table, and export_gameboy_rom.py writes a working ROM back out of a Game Boy program imported with GhidraBoy. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ghidra-ninja-scripts/))
- [ghidra-c6000](https://github.com/geepot/ghidra-c6000) — tool · binary — ghidra-c6000 adds Texas Instruments C64x, C67x and C674x DSP support to Ghidra 12.x. Its SLEIGH languages decode full-width and compact instructions, supply p-code for decompilation, and add C6000 calling conventions and packet-aware analyzers for ELF or raw images. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ghidra-c6000/))
- [ghidra-delinker-extension](https://github.com/boricj/ghidra-delinker-extension) — tool · binary, game — Ghidra extension that delinks a selected part of a program back into a relocatable object file. Its relocation table synthesizer analyzer reconstructs, from the Ghidra database, the relocations a linker normally discards, and its exporters write COFF (x86, x86-64), ELF (x86, x86-64, MIPS) or OMF (x86) objects with symbols and relocation tables that a toolchain can link again. The README lists binary patching through the linker, software ports, format conversion, library extraction and decompilation projects that reimplement a program one object file at a time among its uses. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ghidra-delinker-extension/))
- [ghidra-nanomips](https://github.com/nccgroup/ghidra-nanomips) — tool · binary, firmware — Ghidra processor module that adds nanoMIPS disassembly and decompilation through a SLEIGH specification of the little-endian 32-bit nanoMIPS instruction set, including its multithreading instructions. NCC Group's announcement says it was written while reverse engineering MediaTek 5G baseband firmware, implements the instructions met in that firmware and leaves some complex P-Code emulation unimplemented, yet decompiles most of the baseband code the team analyzed. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ghidra-nanomips/))
- [Ghidrathon](https://github.com/mandiant/Ghidrathon) — tool · binary — Mandiant FLARE's Ghidra extension that embeds the user's local CPython 3 interpreter in Ghidra through Jep, as an alternative to Ghidra's Jython-based Python 2.7 scripting. It adds an interactive Python 3 interpreter window, Python 3 scripts in the Script Manager and Python 3 post-scripts in headless analyzeHeadless runs, with GhidraScript state such as currentProgram() and FlatProgramAPI methods available as Python builtins. Because the configured interpreter or virtual environment is used as is, scripts can import installed packages; the README names capa, Unicorn and angr as motivating examples and shows Unicorn emulating ARM code inside Ghidra. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ghidrathon/))
- [ghidrecomp](https://github.com/clearbluejar/ghidrecomp) — tool · binary — Python command-line decompiler that drives Ghidra through pyghidra to decompile every function of a binary into separate C files, or into one C file and header with Ghidra's C++ exporter, keeping the Ghidra project so later runs can reuse the analysis. Options filter functions by regex, apply PDB symbols from a local file or from Microsoft, Chromium, Mozilla, Intel, NVIDIA and AMD symbol servers, apply extra GDT data-type archives, write Mermaid call-graph Markdown, generate BSim function signatures, and scan the decompiled code with Semgrep rules into SARIF reports. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ghidrecomp/))
- [Glaurung Models](https://github.com/mjbommar/glaurung-models) — research · binary — Glaurung Models contains tokenizer and model experiments for executable analysis. Its documented 64K BPE tokenizer encodes compiled PE and ELF bytes using Latin-1, with x86 and ARM64 training coverage; an accompanying analysis compares learned SSE and AVX instruction patterns across two tokenizers. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/glaurung-models/))
- [GolangAnalyzerExtension](https://github.com/mooncat-greenpy/Ghidra_GolangAnalyzerExtension) — tool · binary — Ghidra extension whose Golang Analyzer reads the runtime header and module data in Go binaries built with go1.6 through go1.27 to identify the Go version, rename functions, correct their arguments, comment source files and line numbers, add Go types to the Data Type Manager and label Go strings. A plugin window lists the recovered functions, types, source files and strings with links to Go source, and WebAssembly builds are handled when a separate Wasm loader such as ghidra-wasm-plugin is installed. A bundled script also renames functions that decode gobfuscate-style obfuscated strings and comments the decoded text. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ghidra-golanganalyzerextension/))
- [GoReSym](https://github.com/mandiant/GoReSym) — tool · binary — Recover Go build metadata, function names and address ranges, source file and line tables, embedded types and strings from Go binaries, including stripped and some malformed unpacked samples. JSON output can be imported into IDA Pro with the included IDAPython script. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/goresym/))
- [GREASE](https://github.com/GaloisInc/grease) — tool · binary — Galois's command-line tool, Ghidra plug-in and Haskell library for checking properties of binaries with under-constrained symbolic execution. It lifts AArch32, PowerPC or x86_64 ELF executables and shared objects, or LLVM bitcode, into a control-flow graph, simulates each function from fully symbolic registers and grows the inferred memory layout after out-of-bounds accesses, then reports likely undefined behavior such as division by zero or invalid memory access together with concretized arguments. Built-in overrides, or user overrides written in Crucible's S-expression language, model external library functions, and the plug-in presents results in Ghidra. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/grease/))
- [heimdall-rs](https://github.com/Jon-Becker/heimdall-rs) — tool · binary — Rust toolkit by Jonathan Becker for analyzing EVM smart-contract bytecode, particularly of contracts without verified source. Its CLI disassembles bytecode, builds control-flow graphs, decompiles it through per-function symbolic execution and heuristic post-processing passes into an ABI plus beta Solidity or Yul source, decodes raw calldata, dumps the storage slots a contract accesses over a block range, and inspects transactions by decoding their calldata, traces and logs. Targets can be raw bytecode, files, contract addresses or ENS names; function selectors are resolved against the OpenChain signature database unless that step is skipped. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/heimdall-rs/))
- [Hex Fiend](https://github.com/HexFiend/HexFiend) — tool · binary — Open-source macOS hex editor that inspects and edits binary files of any size without loading them into memory. It supports insertions and deletions, fast search and a data inspector that reads integers and floating-point values as signed or unsigned and big- or little-endian. File > Compare shows the differences between two files while accounting for inserted or deleted bytes, and Tcl binary templates display a file's structure next to its bytes; bundled templates cover COFF, ELF, Mach-O and MS-DOS EXE headers, 6502 disassembly and Commodore 64 PRG files, plus archives, file systems, partition tables and media formats. Its hex and data views are also available as an embeddable framework. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/hex-fiend/))
- [HexRays Toolbox](https://github.com/patois/HexraysToolbox) — tool · binary — IDAPython scripts that find code patterns in Hex-Rays decompiler output, independent of the binary's processor architecture, by running user-written lambda queries over the decompiled ctree. Queries run from the hxtb-shell GUI inside IDA, from IDAPython through functions such as find_expr() and find_item(), from interactive command-line helpers or in batch mode over a set of files. The README's use cases include finding vulnerabilities and their variants, locating code patterns from previously reversed executables in newly decompiled code, malware variant analysis and finding patterns across binaries built for different architectures. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/hexrays-toolbox/))
- [HexRaysCodeXplorer](https://github.com/REhints/HexRaysCodeXplorer) — tool · binary — Hex-Rays decompiler plugin for navigating and reconstructing C++ code in IDA's pseudocode window. It reconstructs C++ object types from a selected pointer variable into IDA's local types, identifies virtual function tables and generates structures for them, lets the analyst click through virtual calls once objects are typed, visualizes a function's ctree as a graph, jumps from a pseudocode line to its disassembly and dumps ctrees and types to files. Its Object Explorer lists vtables with cross-references and RTTI parsing, and a batch mode processes many files without user interaction. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/hexrays-codexplorer/))
- [HexRaysPyTools](https://github.com/igogo-x86/HexRaysPyTools) — tool · binary — IDAPython plugin for IDA Pro's Hex-Rays decompiler that helps rebuild structures and classes from pseudocode. Its structure builder collects the fields a variable accesses within one function or, with deep scan, across the functions it is passed to, marks virtual tables, resolves conflicting fields and finalizes a C declaration applied to every scanned variable; a Classes view edits classes and their virtual functions, and a graph shows how local types relate. Pseudocode helpers recast and rename across assignments and calls, propagate names, rewrite out-of-bounds pointers as CONTAINING_RECORD for a containing structure, edit function signatures, rename functions from assert strings and untangle if statements. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/hexrayspytools/))
- [Hopper Disassembler](https://www.hopperapp.com/) — tool · app, binary — Cryptic Apps' commercial disassembler, decompiler and debugger for macOS and Linux, which reads Mach-O (macOS and iOS), PE and ELF executables built for 32- and 64-bit Intel, ARMv6, ARMv7, ARM64 and PowerPC processors. Each procedure can be viewed as assembly, as an interactive control-flow graph that exports to PDF or as C-like pseudocode, with Swift name demangling and extraction of Objective-C selectors and messages, and binaries can be patched through the hex editor, the built-in assembler or Python. Debugging of Mac applications uses LLDB or GDB, Python scripts reach most features, an SDK adds file-format parsers and CPU backends, and Hopper 6 brought a new decompiler and an MCP server for LLM clients. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/hopper-disassembler/))
- [hrtng](https://github.com/KasperskyLab/hrtng) — tool · binary — IDA plugin published by Kaspersky that extends the Hex-Rays decompiler with tools its author collected for reverse-engineering work, including malware analysis. It decrypts strings, data and constants with built-in ciphers or in bulk through IDA's Appcall debugger feature, resolves API-name hashes, unflattens control flow and forces decompilation of obfuscated code, and recognizes library code through microcode signatures, inlined-function detection and PAT files built from an IDA database. Pseudocode helpers cover automatic renaming, retyping and enum substitution, structure creation, virtual and indirect call navigation, and patching. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/hrtng/))
- [Human-Readable Decompilation](https://github.com/aaronwb/human-readable-decompilation) — tool · binary — Human-Readable Decompilation restructures C produced by Ghidra's binary decompiler using an ANTLR parser and AST transformations. It recovers switches from conditional chains, introduces guard clauses, flattens nested branches and simplifies selected expressions. A GUI can launch Ghidra headlessly before transformation, while a CLI handles existing C output. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/human-readable-decompilation/))
- [iaito](https://github.com/radareorg/iaito) — tool · binary — Official Qt-based graphical interface for the radare2 reverse-engineering framework, aiming for parity with radare2's commands, features and keybindings so that existing r2 plugins and scripts keep working. Its README lists forensics, binary diffing and binary patching alongside disassembly; iaito was the original name before the project was forked as Cutter. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/iaito/))
- [iblessing](https://github.com/Soulghost/iblessing) — tool · app, binary — Toolkit for analyzing iOS apps and arm64 Mach-O binaries, built on Capstone, Unicorn and Keystone. Its scanners extract app-bundle metadata such as URL schemes and transport-security settings, locate Objective-C class references and NSPredicate uses, detect symbol wrappers, and partially emulate arm64 code with Unicorn to recover objc_msgSend cross-references, including those passed through blocks. Generators turn the reports into IDA Pro scripts that add cross-references and rename wrappers, or into a local query server, and a shared core library with a plug-in interface supports custom tools. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/iblessing/))
- [IDA iOS Helper](https://github.com/yoavst/ida-ios-helper) — tool · app, binary — IDA Pro plugin that cleans up Hex-Rays pseudocode and adds navigation helpers for iOS user-mode binaries and the kernelcache, enabling its components according to the loaded file. For Objective-C and Swift code it hides reference-counting calls, selectors and static class arguments, collapses os_log sequences, turns property helpers into plain assignments or field reads, lists cross-references to a method's selector, and adds Swift type declarations and readable stack strings. For kernelcaches it types the results of safeMetaCast and typed operator new, names and types kalloc_type_view globals, fixes this arguments and lists the possible implementations of a virtual call, while shared helpers remove __break calls, rewrite ranged conditions, collapse block initializers and mark outlined functions. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ida-ios-helper/))
- [IDA Pro](https://hex-rays.com/ida-pro) — tool · app, binary, firmware, game — Hex-Rays' commercial interactive disassembler, decompiler and debugger, which turns compiled code for more than 70 processor families into annotated, cross-referenced assembly and decompiled pseudocode. Its 15 decompilers cover 32- and 64-bit x86, ARM, MIPS, PowerPC and RISC-V as well as ARC, V850, TriCore, Hexagon and Android DEX; the Dalvik decompiler, new in IDA 9.5 alongside TriCore and Hexagon, renders DEX bytecode as Java pseudocode. Documented loaders include PE, ELF and Mach-O with Apple dyld shared caches and kernelcaches, DEX, UEFI firmware images, WebAssembly modules and console formats such as Xbox XBE, N64, SNES ROM and PlayStation PSX executables, and the processor list names console CPUs from the NES and Game Boy to the PlayStation 3 Cell, Xbox 360 Xenon and PSP Allegrex. The debugger runs locally or through remote debug servers for Windows, Linux, macOS and Android, and analysis can be scripted with IDAPython, IDC and the C++ SDK, run without a GUI through the idalib library, or driven from MCP clients through IDA MCP. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ida-pro/))
- [ImHex](https://github.com/WerWolv/ImHex) — tool · binary, firmware, game — Hex editor with a binary-pattern language, structured data inspection and visual tools for understanding file formats. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/imhex/))
- [imprint](https://github.com/hamodywe/imprint) — tool · binary — imprint analyzes Windows PE files by parsing import, delay-import and export directories without executing the image. It maps imported Windows APIs to categories and MITRE ATT&CK techniques, then reports capability patterns with the imports that triggered each finding in text or JSON. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/imprint-pe-triage/))
- [innoextract](https://github.com/dscharrer/innoextract) — tool · binary — Unpacks Inno Setup installers on non-Windows systems without running them. It also handles modified variants, including GOG.com's Inno Setup-based game installers. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/innoextract/))
- [IR Lifting Benchmark](https://github.com/Prajwal-kp-18/ir-analysis) — tool · binary — IR Lifting Benchmark compares binary lifting workflows across Ghidra P-code, angr VEX, BAP BIL and optional LLVM IR paths. Shell and Python scripts run analyses on binary samples, collect performance and control-flow metrics, check lifted semantics and generate reports and visualizations. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ir-lifting-benchmark/))
- [Joern](https://github.com/joernio/joern) — tool · app, binary — Static code analysis platform that parses source code, JVM bytecode and executables into code property graphs held in a graph database and queried with a Scala-based language from an interactive shell or server mode. Frontends cover C/C++, Java, JavaScript, Python, Kotlin, PHP, Go, Ruby, Swift, C#, Rust and ABAP source, JVM bytecode and APK/DEX files through Soot, and binaries loaded through an embedded Ghidra, which the documentation lists as x86/x64 support. It adds a static taint-tracking engine, the joern-scan query database of vulnerability patterns, and export of AST, CFG, PDG or whole-graph views to DOT, GraphML, GraphSON or Neo4j CSV. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/joern/))
- [Kaiju](https://github.com/nightwarelabz/kaiju) — tool · binary — Kaiju is a Rust reverse-engineering framework with a headless CLI for binary loading and analysis. Its current crates parse selected ELF, PE and Mach-O metadata, extract strings, decode a limited x86-64 instruction subset and inspect saved network evidence. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/kaiju-re-framework/))
- [Keypatch](https://github.com/keystone-engine/keypatch) — tool · binary — IDA Pro plugin built on the Keystone assembler engine that patches a binary from typed assembly, fills an address range with instructions or raw bytes and searches the loaded binary for assembly instructions. It assembles Arm, Arm64, Hexagon, MIPS, PowerPC, SPARC, SystemZ and 16-, 32- and 64-bit x86 code, shows the encoding while you type, can pad with NOPs up to the next instruction boundary, keeps the original instruction in an IDA comment and can undo the last patch. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/keypatch/))
- [knife](https://github.com/bl4ckr0ss3/knife) — tool · binary — Static reverse-engineering toolkit in Rust, installed as the knife command, that parses PE, ELF, Mach-O and WebAssembly files without running them. It triages hashes, sections, mitigations, imports, strings, indicators and crypto constants; recovers functions, call graphs and CFGs through recursive-descent disassembly for x86/x64 and AArch64; and lifts functions to experimental pseudocode. Analysts' names, notes, types and staged patches persist in a per-binary database shared by the CLI, a terminal UI and an MCP server, and further passes rank risky API call sites by argument provenance and summarize Windows driver dispatch and IOCTL surfaces. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/knife/))
- [ktool](https://github.com/0cyn/ktool) — tool · app, binary — Pure-Python toolkit for static Mach-O and Objective-C analysis, used as the ktool command line, a terminal browser (ktool open) or a library, with no compiled dependencies. Its commands print load commands, linked libraries, symbol tables, imports and exports, Objective-C classes and protocols, Swift types, function starts and code-signing entitlements, and dump reconstructed Objective-C headers or .tbd stubs. It can also list and extract kexts from kernel caches, split and combine fat binaries, change install names and insert load commands. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ktool/))
- [Kuna](https://github.com/Noelo-Lab/kuna) — tool · binary — Agent-first decompiler written in Rust that began as a port of Ghidra’s C++ decompiler and SLEIGH compiler, and has since diverged with option-gated features, some ported from angr. A single self-contained kuna binary decompiles one function or exports a whole binary to C, header and assembly files, and the same engine runs in the browser through WebAssembly or as the decompiler backend inside Ghidra. The project calls itself an experiment in having LLM agents refine the decompiler; most code is agent-written and steered by regression and benchmark results. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/kuna/))
- [LazyIDA](https://github.com/L4ys/LazyIDA) — tool · binary — Single-file IDA plugin of convenience actions first written for CTF work. In the disassembly and hex views it dumps a selection as a string, hex string, C/C++ array or Python list of bytes, words, dwords or qwords and copies the result to the clipboard, shows a selection XORed with one byte, fills it with NOPs, and copies or jumps to addresses and file offsets by hotkey. With Hex-Rays it toggles a function’s return type to void, copies item names and jumps to the member function behind an obj->func call on double-click, while a heuristic scan lists printf-family calls whose format argument is not a read-only constant. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/lazyida/))
- [LuaDec](https://github.com/viruscamp/luadec) — tool · binary, game — Lua bytecode decompiler that turns compiled chunks back into Lua source, with a disassembly mode, selection of single nested functions by number and a guesser for local variable declarations that can be overridden by hand. Its -fc option recompiles each decompiled function and compares it instruction by instruction with the original. It targets Lua 5.1, marks Lua 5.2 and 5.3 support as experimental, and continues Hisham Muhammad’s LuaDec for Lua 5.0 and Zsolt Sztupak’s LuaDec51. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/luadec/))
- [Lucid](https://github.com/gaasedelen/lucid) — tool · binary — IDA Pro plugin by Markus Gaasedelen for studying how the Hex-Rays decompiler transforms its microcode intermediate language. Opened from a Pseudocode window with View microcode, its explorer stays synchronized with the pseudocode and lets the user scroll a selected microinstruction through each microcode maturity level, tracing sub-operands back to their origins and changing the cursor color when the traced code has been optimized away. Nested sub-instructions can be shown as tree graphs; the author labels it a developer-oriented prototype and code resource, not a general-purpose reverse-engineering tool. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/lucid-microcode-explorer/))
- [m0rph: tracing an obfuscated ELF flag checker](https://github.com/UmbertoDellaMonica/m0rph-CTF-CyberChallenge.IT) — case-study · binary — The m0rph investigation examines an obfuscated x86-64 ELF CTF flag checker with readelf, strings, Cutter and Radare2. Its write-up traces indirect CALL RAX execution into allocated memory and describes recovering expected characters by breaking at the call and inspecting RDI during each loop iteration. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/m0rph-ctf-analysis-case/))
- [m68kdasm](https://github.com/jenska/m68kdasm) — tool · binary — m68kdasm disassembles Motorola 68000-family machine code in Go and returns assembly with structured instruction metadata. It supports selectable CPU variants, optional FPU and PMMU decoding, symbol and label formatting, streaming input and helpers for 68000 ELF binaries. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/m68kdasm/))
- [MachOExplorer](https://github.com/everettjf/machoexplorer) — tool · binary — MachOExplorer is a desktop and command-line tool for inspecting Mach-O binaries, Universal binaries, static archives, and dyld shared caches. It displays file structures, symbols, disassembly, and Apple-specific metadata, and can export analysis as text or JSON. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/machoexplorer/))
- [Malware Deobfuscator](https://github.com/X-Abhishek-X/malware-deobfuscator) — tool · app — Malware Deobfuscator analyzes obfuscated malware scripts and generates code, behavior and indicator reports. A deterministic pass extracts indicators with regexes and maps keyword signatures to MITRE ATT&CK; an OpenAI or Ollama model supplies deobfuscated code and execution-flow explanations. The CLI handles individual files or directories and can emit JSON. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/malware-deobfuscator/))
- [Mergen](https://github.com/NaC-L/Mergen) — tool · binary — Function-level lifter that symbolically lifts one x86-64 function from a Windows PE binary into LLVM IR, then iterates LLVM and custom passes to a fixpoint so obfuscated or virtualized code reduces to simpler control flow and semantics for analysis. Its scope document lists 119 instruction handlers, two-way branches and tested jump-table shapes; README examples lift small test functions the authors protected with VMProtect and Themida, and the IR can be compiled for viewing in a decompiler. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/mergen/))
- [nibkit](https://github.com/tokenbleed/nibkit) — tool · app — Decode compiled iOS/macOS interface archives into object trees, Outlet/Action connections, custom classes and navigation relationships. Exports JSON or Mermaid diagrams and exposes read-only inspection commands through MCP. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/nibkit/))
- [NullSec BinGaze](https://github.com/bad-antics/nullsec-bingaze) — tool · binary — NullSec BinGaze analyses ELF executables by parsing headers, listing sections and calculating entropy. It also reports NX stack, PIE, RELRO, stack-canary and RWX-segment properties through command-line modes for full analysis, section listing or security assessment. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/nullsec-bingaze/))
- [oletools](https://github.com/decalage2/oletools) — tool · binary — Python package by Philippe Lagadec for analyzing Microsoft OLE2 (Compound File Binary) files such as Office 97–2003 documents, MSI files and Outlook messages, as well as RTF and OpenXML documents, mainly for malware analysis, forensics and debugging. Its command-line tools detect and extract VBA and Excel 4 (XLM) macros, embedded OLE and Flash objects and DDE links (olevba, MacroRaptor, oleobj, rtfobj, pyxswf, msodde), flag characteristics common in malicious files with oleid and ftguess, and show directory entries, metadata, timestamps and sector maps with oledir, olemeta, oletimes and olemap. It is built on the same author's olefile parser. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/oletools/))
- [ollvm-unflattener](https://github.com/cdong1012/ollvm-unflattener) — tool · binary — Python command-line deobfuscator for the control-flow flattening applied by Obfuscator-LLVM (OLLVM). Given a flattened function's address, it uses Miasm's symbolic execution to track the dispatcher's state variable, records which blocks each state value selects and which state follows it, and writes a copy of the binary in which the function is reassembled with Keystone in its recovered order; an optional mode also follows the function's calls. The README covers 32- and 64-bit x86 Windows and Linux binaries and contrasts this approach with MODeflattener's static one. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ollvm-unflattener/))
- [PackerScope](https://github.com/salmanmallah/packerscope) — tool · binary — PackerScope scans Windows PE executables for packer signatures and structural indicators, including entropy, sections, imports and entry-point instructions. It can invoke supported unpackers and check PE integrity, entropy and import-table recovery before generating reports. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/packerscope/))
- [pcode2code](https://github.com/Big5-sec/pcode2code) — tool · binary — VBA p-code decompiler based on pcodedmp, written for Office documents whose macro source has been stomped to leave only bytecode. It takes a document or a previously saved pcodedmp dump and also works as a Python module. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pcode2code/))
- [PE Disassembler](https://github.com/ddeeduck/Pe-disassembler) — tool · binary — PE Disassembler is a WPF application that extracts the .text section of 32-bit Windows PE executables and displays decoded x86 instructions. It checks MZ and PE signatures, handles a limited set of movement, arithmetic and control-flow opcodes, and can save the listing as text. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pe-disassembler-ddeeduck/))
- [PE Tree](https://github.com/blackberry/pe_tree) — tool · binary — Python module that shows Portable Executable files in a tree view using pefile and PyQt5, as a standalone application and as a plugin for IDA Pro, Ghidra, Volatility, Rekall and minidump. It reads PE images from files, ZIP archives, Windows memory dumps and analysis databases. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pe-tree/))
- [PE-bear](https://github.com/hasherezade/pe-bear) — tool · binary — Qt GUI to view and edit Windows Portable Executable files, meant as a fast first look for malware analysts and robust against malformed PE32 and PE64 files. It shows headers and section layout, follows raw, RVA and VA addresses, disassembles from any offset with Capstone, matches packer signatures, compares two files and unmaps dumped images. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pe-bear/))
- [pestudio](https://www.winitor.com/) — tool · binary — Winitor's Windows tool for initial malware assessment, which statically inspects Windows executable files and reports key indicators such as hard-coded URLs and IP addresses, flagged imports, resources and overlay data. The free basic edition also detects file signatures, debug information and TLS callbacks, computes entropy and imphash, shows the rich header and .NET metadata and looks up VirusTotal scores by hash; pestudio-pro adds MITRE ATT&CK mapping, XML reports and the pestudiox.exe command-line version. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pestudio/))
- [Pharos](https://github.com/cmu-sei/pharos) — tool · binary — Carnegie Mellon SEI's framework of automated static analysis tools for binary programs, built on LLNL's ROSE compiler infrastructure for disassembly, control-flow analysis and instruction semantics.
