Its best-known tool, OOAnalyzer, recovers C++ classes, methods and other object-oriented constructs from 32-bit x86 executables built with Microsoft Visual C++ by applying Prolog rules to facts about how object pointers move between functions, as described in a CCS 2018 paper, and exports JSON for an included IDA plugin or the separate Kaiju Ghidra plugin. Other tools find API call sequences (ApiAnalyzer), report static API-call parameters (CallAnalyzer), produce function hashes and YARA signatures for similarity analysis (FN2Hash, FN2Yara) and work out the conditions for reaching one address from another (PathAnalyzer). ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pharos/))
- [pixd](https://github.com/FireyFly/pixd) — tool · binary — Terminal tool that visualises binary data like a hexdump, but draws each octet as a coloured square from a palette. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pixd/))
- [PLASMA](https://github.com/plasma-disassembler/plasma) — tool · binary — Interactive terminal disassembler, formerly named Reverse, for x86 and x86-64 with partial ARM and MIPS/MIPS64 support, reading ELF, PE and raw binaries. Its visual mode shows functions as indented, colored pseudo-code and lets the analyst reshape the flow by inverting conditional jumps, while a Python API runs scripts for strings, cross-reference call graphs, crypto-constant detection and Keystone assembly. Development stopped in 2019, and the repository has no releases. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/plasma-disassembler/))
- [Porosity](https://github.com/msuiche/porosity) — tool · binary — Decompiler that turns Ethereum Virtual Machine bytecode into readable Solidity-syntax contracts, supported on Linux and macOS. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/porosity/))
- [PyInstaller Extractor (pyinstxtractor)](https://github.com/extremecoders-re/pyinstxtractor) — tool · app, binary — Single-file Python script that extracts the contents of executables built with PyInstaller, including Linux ELF builds without other tools. It locates the PyInstaller archive in the executable, reports the PyInstaller and Python versions and likely entry-point scripts, and writes out the bundled CArchive files and the modules in the PYZ archive. It fixes the headers of the extracted .pyc files so that Python bytecode decompilers such as uncompyle6 and Decompyle++ (pycdc) recognize them. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pyinstxtractor/))
- [pyinstxtractor-ng (PyInstaller Extractor Next Generation)](https://github.com/pyinstxtractor/pyinstxtractor-ng) — tool · app, binary — Fork of PyInstaller Extractor (pyinstxtractor) by the same author that extracts the bundled CArchive files and PYZ modules from Linux ELF and Windows PE executables built with PyInstaller, writing .pyc files with repaired headers for a separate bytecode decompiler. Unlike the original script, it unmarshals the PYZ archive with the xdis library, so it does not have to run under the Python version that built the executable, and it automatically decrypts PYZ entries that PyInstaller's optional AES encryption protected, using the key module bundled in the same executable. A one-directory mode extracts the PYZ next to the other files so the result can be run directly, and --info prints archive metadata such as versions, entry points and encryption without writing files. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pyinstxtractor-ng/))
- [PyLingual](https://github.com/syssec-utd/pylingual) — tool · app, binary — Research decompiler for CPython bytecode from Python 3.6 onward that pairs per-version transformer models with control-flow reconstruction. A segmentation model splits bytecode into statements, a statement model translates each into source, and templates rebuild loops, conditionals, exception handlers, with blocks and match statements. The result is recompiled with the target Python version and its control flow compared with the original bytecode, and failing segments are retried with alternative segmentations; the design is described in the IEEE S&P 2025 paper “PyLingual: Toward Perfect Decompilation of Evolving High-Level Languages”. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pylingual/))
- [Qu1cksc0pe](https://github.com/CYB3RMX/Qu1cksc0pe) — tool · app, binary — All-in-one malware triage tool that statically analyses Windows PE, Linux ELF (including Go binaries), macOS Mach-O and Android APK, JAR and DEX files as well as Office and PDF documents, scripts, shortcuts, archives, e-mails and PCAPs, reporting imports and API categories, sections, strings and IOCs, packers, embedded executables, Android permission risk, YARA matches and, for Windows and Linux, MITRE ATT&CK mappings. VBA/VBScript, JavaScript and PowerShell are run through bounded in-memory emulators instead of being executed, and --watch modes monitor live Windows, Linux and Android processes. A CLI, a Flask web UI and an MCP server expose the analyses, with optional LLM summaries of the JSON reports. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/qu1cksc0pe/))
- [Quokka](https://github.com/quarkslab/quokka) — tool · binary — Quarkslab’s binary exporter, inspired by BinExport, saves a disassembler’s analysis to a protobuf .quokka file so functions, basic blocks, data, cross-references, segments and optionally decompiled code can be used later without the disassembler. Its Python bindings load an export together with the original binary, decode instructions at runtime with Capstone, or optionally lift them to P-code with pypcode, and expose programs, functions and control-flow graphs through one API. The main branch adds Ghidra and Binary Ninja exporters beside the IDA Pro plugin, a parallel quokka-cli batch exporter and name and type edits that can be written back to an IDA database. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/quokka-binary-exporter/))
- [r2dec](https://github.com/wargio/r2dec-js) — tool · binary — Decompiler plugin for radare2 that turns the disassembly of an analyzed function into pseudo-C through the pdd command. Its decompiler is written in JavaScript, with one module per architecture, compiled to QuickJS bytecode and embedded in a native radare2 core plugin; output can sit beside the assembly or offsets, show only scope blocks, casts or cross-references, render as HTML, or be written back into radare2 as comments. The README lists x86/x64, 16/32/64-bit ARM, MIPS, PowerPC (including VLE), SPARC, AVR, V850 and Dalvik, plus experimental 6502, 8051, BPF, m68k, SuperH, WebAssembly and RISC-V support. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/r2dec/))
- [r2pipe](https://github.com/radareorg/radare2-r2pipe) — tool · binary — radare2's official scripting interface, with implementations for more than two dozen languages in one repository. Every binding sends radare2 command strings and returns their text output, the design behind r_core_cmd_str(), and the README recommends asking for JSON output and decoding it into native objects instead of using the C API. Bindings connect through the R2PIPE_IN/R2PIPE_OUT pipes of a script run from inside r2, a spawned r2 -q0 process, TCP, HTTP or radare2's RAP protocol, many offer asynchronous calls, and r2pipe scripts can also implement radare2 plugins such as disassemblers, IO backends or ESIL syscall handlers. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/r2pipe/))
- [radare2](https://github.com/radareorg/radare2) — tool · binary, game — Scriptable command-line framework for inspecting, disassembling, debugging and modifying binaries across many architectures. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/radare2/))
- [readpe (formerly pev)](https://github.com/mentebinaria/readpe) — tool · binary — Command-line toolkit, formerly named pev, for analyzing 32- and 64-bit Windows PE files. readpe prints the DOS, COFF and optional headers, sections, data directories, imports and exports, while companion tools disassemble code (pedis), hash the file, headers or sections with ssdeep and imphash among others (pehash), list and extract resources (peres), match packer signatures (pepack), flag traits such as TLS callbacks, high entropy or an unusual entry point (pescan), report ASLR, DEP/NX, SEH and stack-cookie flags and dump certificates (pesec), find strings and convert between file offsets and RVAs. Output plugins write text, CSV, XML, HTML or JSON, and the bundled libpe C library provides the parser. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/readpe/))
- [REDasm](https://github.com/redasm-dev/redasm) — tool · binary, game — Cross-platform interactive disassembler whose version 4 is a plain-C rewrite: a C17 engine library, the Qt6 GUI kept in this repository, and loader, processor and analyzer plugins loaded as shared libraries. The listing supports renaming, comments and typed data, with a synchronized control-flow graph, cross-references, string detection, a hex view, patching and saved projects. Besides PE and ELF, loaders cover retro and console formats such as DOS MZ, Win16 NE, OS/2 LE/LX, PlayStation 1, original Xbox and ZX Spectrum, with x86/x86-64, MIPS32, ARM/Thumb, ARM64, Z80 and 6502 processors. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/redasm/))
- [Redress](https://github.com/goretk/redress) — tool · binary — Command-line tool, built on the GoRE library, that recovers metadata from stripped binaries produced by the Go compiler. It reports the compiler version, build ID, GOROOT, main-package path and Go module information; lists project, standard-library and vendored packages; prints interfaces, structs and their methods as Go type declarations; and projects the source tree as packages, files and functions with estimated line ranges. Run inside radare2 through r2pipe, it adds function and type flags and analyzes main.init and main.main. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/redress/))
- [REHex](https://github.com/solemnwarning/rehex) — tool · binary — Cross-platform hex editor for reverse engineering that handles files over 1 TB. It adds inline disassembly, integer and floating-point decoding, byte-range annotations, side-by-side comparison, virtual address mapping and bitmap visualisation, with Binary Templates that annotate data automatically and Lua scripting. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rehex/))
- [Reko](https://github.com/uxmal/reko) — tool · binary, firmware — General-purpose decompiler that loads executables or raw images, scans their machine code into a machine-independent register transfer language, groups it into procedures and decompiles them into high-level C code. The reviewed source has 64 architecture directories, from 6502, Z80 and PDP-11 to ARM, MIPS, PowerPC, RISC-V and x86, and loaders for formats including ELF, PE/MZ, Mach-O, COFF, Amiga Hunk, VMS, WebAssembly, Xbox XBE and Xbox 360 XEX. It runs as a Windows Forms GUI, a command-line client or a .NET library, and project files and scripts let users add names, types and comments. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/reko/))
- [restore-symbol](https://github.com/tobefuturer/restore-symbol) — tool · app, binary — macOS command-line tool that writes a stripped iOS app's Mach-O executable back out with a rebuilt symbol table, so a debugger such as lldb can break on and display Objective-C method names. It recovers method names and addresses from the binary's Objective-C metadata through class-dump, and can merge extra name and address pairs from a JSON file, such as the block symbols found by the bundled IDA script. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/restore-symbol/))
- [RetDec](https://github.com/avast/retdec) — tool · binary — Avast's retargetable machine-code decompiler built on LLVM. It takes ELF, PE, Mach-O, COFF, AR, Intel HEX or raw code for 32-bit x86, ARM, MIPS, PIC32 and PowerPC or 64-bit x86-64 and ARM64, recovers functions, types and C++ class hierarchies, and emits C or a Python-like language with call and control-flow graphs. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/retdec/))
- [RetroWrite](https://github.com/HexHive/retrowrite) — tool · binary — HexHive's static binary rewriter for x64 and aarch64 Linux ELF binaries, which turns a binary into symbolized, reassemblable assembly so instrumentation can be inserted without source code and without heuristics. Bundled passes add a binary-only AddressSanitizer and AFL coverage instrumentation, plus a coarse-grained control-flow-integrity pass on aarch64, and the symbolized assembly can also be edited by hand or by other tools before reassembly. The KRetrowrite variant of the x64 rewriter instruments Linux kernel modules with BASan or kcov for kernel fuzzing. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/retrowrite/))
- [rev.ng](https://github.com/revng/revng) — tool · binary — Open-source decompilation pipeline that lifts x86-64, AArch64, i386, ARM and MIPS code with QEMU, runs LLVM-based analyses and emits C designed to be valid and recompilable. Data layout analysis infers structures from pointer use; an editable YAML model and Python or TypeScript wrappers support automation. Its VSCode-based UI is a separate closed-beta product, with commercial plans announced. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/revng/))
- [Rizin](https://github.com/rizinorg/rizin) — tool · binary, game — Command-line reverse-engineering framework that began as a fork of radare2. The rizin shell analyzes and disassembles binaries, debugs programs and works as a scriptable hex editor, while rz-* tools cover binary-format information, assembly, diffing, hashing, pattern search and FLIRT signatures. The README lists dozens of architectures, bytecode formats and file formats from ELF, PE and Mach-O to dyld caches and BIOS/UEFI images; rzpipe scripts it from Python, Rust, Go and other languages. Cutter is the separate GUI built on it. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rizin/))
- [RpcView](https://github.com/silverf0x/RpcView) — tool · binary — Open-source Windows tool that explores and decompiles the RPC functionality present on a Microsoft system. New rpcrt4.dll runtime versions are added by extending its version table or by reversing the runtime's internal structures. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rpcview/))
- [rust-deobf](https://github.com/VAHPEM/rust-deobf) — tool · binary — rust-deobf analyzes compiled, mainly Rust, ELF, PE and Mach-O binaries to recover hidden strings and related artifacts. It combines static call-site analysis with Unicorn function emulation, identifies Rust metadata and library functions, and offers unpacking and Ghidra annotations. CLI and MCP interfaces expose the core analysis; deeper control-flow and virtualization modules have separate licensing. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rust-deobf/))
- [rust-sig-gen](https://github.com/st0de/rust-sig-gen) — tool · binary — rust-sig-gen generates IDA FLAIR library signatures from compiled Rust crates for executable analysis. Its Python script obtains the top 100 crates, builds them for Linux and x86-64 Windows, and processes the outputs with sigmake, pelf and pcf. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rust-sig-gen/))
- [rz-ghidra](https://github.com/rizinorg/rz-ghidra) — tool · binary — Rizin plugin that embeds Ghidra's C++ decompiler and Sleigh disassembler, so no Java Ghidra installation is needed. Its pdg commands decompile the current function, optionally side by side with offsets or as XML or JSON, can return the output to Rizin as comments, and list the loaded Sleigh languages; config variables control formatting and can override the auto-detected Sleigh language. The decompiler draws on Rizin's loaded binary, types and comments, and an optional plugin brings it into the Cutter GUI. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rz-ghidra/))
- [SIC/XE Disassembler and Linking Loader](https://github.com/MN294/SICXE-Disassembler) — tool · binary — SIC/XE Disassembler and Linking Loader converts SIC/XE object code into assembly listings and reconstructed symbols. Its first pass identifies instruction boundaries and addressing modes; its second emits assembly, while a separate loader resolves external symbols and applies relocation records. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/sicxe-disassembler/))
- [SiMBA](https://github.com/DenuvoSoftwareSolutions/SiMBA) — tool · binary — Research tool from Denuvo that simplifies linear mixed Boolean-arithmetic (MBA) expressions, which combine bitwise and integer operations and are used in code obfuscation. Like MBA-Blast and MBA-Solver it works algebraically, using the fact that a linear MBA is fully determined by its values on inputs of zeros and ones: it evaluates the expression on every such combination and rewrites it as a linear combination of basic bitwise expressions, without first translating it to 1-bit space. Python scripts simplify single expressions or whole datasets, can check that an input is linear and can prove the result equivalent with Z3. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/simba-mba-simplifier/))
- [SimpleParserPE](https://github.com/wmitq/SimpleParserPE) — tool · binary — SimpleParserPE is a C++ command-line inspector for PE32 Windows executables. It reads DOS and NT headers, section metadata, data directories, and imported DLL functions, translating RVAs through the section table without Windows headers or external libraries. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/simpleparserpe/))
- [Spelunking](https://github.com/gaelic-ghost/spelunking) — research · app, binary — Spelunking studies Apple apps, private frameworks and system services with Swift and Objective-C probes. It inventories runtime classes, symbols, notifications and MediaRemote state, while publishing target-specific writeups and raw captures for Messages, Phone, MediaRemote and UserNotifications. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/spelunking/))
- [tasm-rs](https://github.com/nktkt/tasm-rs) — tool · binary — tasm-rs implements TVM bytecode assembly and disassembly in Rust, with a tdisasm CLI, instruction tables, Fift parsing and trace and coverage reports. It uses checked-in conformance fixtures to compare output with the upstream TypeScript implementation; the author reports 959 matching fixtures. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/tasm-rs/))
- [uncompyle6](https://github.com/rocky/python-uncompyle6) — tool · app, binary — Cross-version decompiler that translates Python bytecode back into equivalent Python source, accepting bytecode from Python 1.0 through 3.8 as well as some PyPy bytecode and Dropbox's Python 2.5. It disassembles code objects with the companion xdis library and parses the instruction stream into a grammar-based tree from which source is printed; it can also deparse just the fragment around a given bytecode offset, which the author's trepan debuggers use for exact location reporting. It succeeds the older decompyle, uncompyle and uncompyle2 forks. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/uncompyle6/))
- [Universal Extractor 2](https://github.com/Bioruebe/UniExtract2) — tool · binary, game — Unofficial, extended continuation of Universal Extractor that unpacks files from standard archives, application installers, disk images and game archives. Version 2 adds batch mode, a scan-only mode that detects a file's type, password lists and silent mode. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/universal-extractor-2/))
- [Viper](https://github.com/viper-framework/viper) — tool · binary — Viper is a framework for binary analysis and for organizing malware samples, exploit samples, and research scripts. The Viper 1.x repository is archived and development has ended. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/viper-framework/))
- [Vivisect](https://github.com/vivisect/vivisect) — tool · binary, firmware — Pure-Python reverse-engineering framework that combines the vivisect static analyzer, the vtrace debugging library and the vdb debugger. Vivisect loads PE, ELF, Mach-O, Intel HEX, S-record and raw blob files into workspaces, disassembles and emulates code through envi architecture modules for x86, AMD64, ARM, Thumb, AArch64, MSP430, H8 and Z80, and adds symbolic execution, a Qt GUI and a remote workspace server. vtrace drives processes on Windows, Linux, FreeBSD, macOS and Solaris and has GDB-stub and VMware backends. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/vivisect/))
- [VulHunt Community Edition](https://github.com/vulhunt-re/vulhunt) — tool · binary, firmware — Open-source Community Edition of Binarly REsearch's VulHunt engine for hunting vulnerabilities in POSIX binaries and UEFI firmware on x86 and ARM. Lua rules run on the Rust BIAS framework, which lifts code to Ghidra's P-code and bundles Ghidra's C++ decompiler, and combine taint and dataflow tracking, Weggli-based queries over decompiled code, byte patterns, FLIRT signatures and type libraries; findings are written as JSON with annotated decompiled code. The CLI scans single binaries, Binarly BA2 archives or Binary Ninja databases, and can also run as an MCP server that lets AI assistants open a project, query it with Lua, rename functions and keep notes. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/vulhunt/))
- [XLMMacroDeobfuscator](https://github.com/DissectMalware/XLMMacroDeobfuscator) — tool · binary — Command-line tool and Python library that extracts and decodes obfuscated Excel 4.0 (XLM) macros from xls, xlsm and xlsb files. An internal XLM emulator interprets the macros without fully performing the code, and results can be exported as JSON. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/xlmmacrodeobfuscator/))
- [Xori](https://github.com/endgameinc/xori) — tool · binary — Automation-ready disassembly and static analysis library for shellcode and PE binaries that outputs disassembly, functions and imports as JSON. It offers a light emulation mode that enumerates all paths and a slower full emulation mode, simulated TEB and PEB structures, and FLIRT-style signatures. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/xori/))
- [XPEViewer](https://github.com/horsicq/XPEViewer) — tool · binary — Qt-based PE file viewer and editor for Windows, Linux and macOS by horsicq, the author of the catalog's Detect It Easy. Its PE view parses the DOS, NT and optional headers, sections, normal, delay-load and bound imports, exports, resources including manifests, version data and string tables, relocations, TLS callbacks, exception, debug and load-configuration data, certificates, the Rich header and .NET metadata tables. Hex, disassembly, strings, entropy, hash, memory-map and extractor views sit beside Detect It Easy, Nauz File Detector and YARA scans and an optional VirusTotal lookup. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/xpeviewer/))
- [Yagi](https://github.com/airbus-cert/Yagi) — tool · binary — Airbus CERT plugin that embeds Ghidra's native decompiler in IDA Pro and IDA Free: pressing F3 on a function shows Ghidra's C output inside IDA, and edits made there, covering global symbols such as function prototypes and the names and types of stack and register variables, are saved to the IDA database. The README lists x86, x86-64, ARM, AArch64, PowerPC, MIPS, SPARC, AVR8, 6502, Z80 and eBPF as supported, while other Ghidra processors such as Dalvik, JVM, RISC-V and 68000 are not yet enabled. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/yagi-ghidra-decompiler-for-ida/))
- [YARA](https://github.com/VirusTotal/yara) — tool · binary — VirusTotal's pattern-matching tool for identifying and classifying files such as malware samples with rules that combine text, hex and regular-expression strings with a Boolean condition. It scans a file, a folder or a running process from the command line, through the libyara C API or from Python with yara-python. Modules expose parsed structure to rules: pe and elf expose most fields of PE and ELF headers, dotnet adds .NET metadata, math adds calculations such as entropy, and the optional magic and cuckoo modules add libmagic file types and Cuckoo sandbox behavior reports. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/yara/))
- [YARA-X](https://github.com/VirusTotal/yara-x) — tool · binary — VirusTotal's Rust rewrite of YARA for identifying and classifying files with pattern-matching rules. It is meant to replace YARA, with more detailed error reports, faster regular-expression and hex-pattern matching and a more capable command line. Rules keep YARA's syntax of text, hex and regular-expression patterns joined by a Boolean condition, and modules parse PE, ELF, Mach-O, .NET, Android DEX, Chrome CRX and Windows LNK files; the CLI can also print a module's parsed output as JSON or YAML without any rule, which makes it usable as a file-dissection tool. Rust, C, Go and Python APIs, a rule formatter and a language server with a Visual Studio Code extension are included. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/yara-x/))

<a id="dynamic-analysis"></a>
## Debugging and dynamic analysis

- [8086 Register Command Debugger](https://github.com/Usman-Azfar/8086-assembly-debugger) — tool · binary — 8086 Register Command Debugger is an MS-DOS machine-code inspection tool written in 8086 assembly. It loads a program into a 64 KB segment, decodes 8086 instructions and lets users inspect or edit memory and registers while stepping through real CPU instructions using the Trap Flag. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/8086-register-command-debugger/))
- [advantage](https://github.com/rtfmkiesel/advantage) — tool · app — advantage is a Windows research tool for inspecting Lenovo Vantage and add-in RPC commands. It injects a stub into a Lenovo-signed process, supplies an RPC JSON payload from a local server, and returns the command response through a CLI or web interface; the repository also contains Frida contract and request dump scripts. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/advantage/))
- [AppMon](https://github.com/dpnishant/appmon) — tool · app — Frida-based framework by Nishant Das Patnaik, shown at Black Hat Arsenal 2016, for watching the system API calls that native macOS, iOS and Android apps make. Its Sniffer attaches to or spawns an app, loads hook scripts from a bundled library covering areas such as crypto, keychain, files, SQLite, HTTP, IPC and WebView, and records each intercepted call in a per-app SQLite database that a local Flask web interface displays. Further components trace Java classes, methods and argument types in Android apps and repackage IPAs or APKs with Frida Gadget so they can be inspected on non-jailbroken or non-rooted devices. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/appmon/))
- [BREAD](https://github.com/Theldus/bread) — tool · binary, firmware — Injectable real-mode x86 debugger for legacy BIOS code, boot code and DOS programs on real hardware or in a virtual machine. A small 16-bit assembly stub placed in the target code installs interrupt handlers, single-steps the processor and talks over a serial line to a Linux bridge, which translates its simple packets into the GDB remote protocol so GDB can read and write memory and registers, step, continue and set one hardware breakpoint and one watchpoint at a time. A companion script turns an address–label list into a minimal ELF symbol file for GDB. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/bread-bios-debugger/))
- [Brida](https://github.com/federicodotta/Brida) — tool · app, protocol — Burp Suite extension by Federico Dotta and Piergiovanni Cipolloni that bridges Burp and Frida, so an analyst can run an app's own functions on the HTTP messages it exchanges with its backend instead of reimplementing its encryption, encoding or signing. A graphical plugin engine connects Frida-exported functions to Burp: they can process requests and responses in every Burp tool, or run from custom message-editor tabs, context-menu entries and buttons, and a Generate Stubs tool emits Java or Python code for external Burp extensions. An analysis tab shows the app's Java and Objective-C classes, methods, imports and exports as a tree, from which hooks that log arguments and return values, or replace return values, can be added; an editor holds the analyst's own Frida exports. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/brida/))
- [CAPE Sandbox (CAPEv2)](https://github.com/kevoreilly/CAPEv2) — tool · binary — Malware sandbox derived from Cuckoo v1 that runs samples in isolated Windows guests and records API-hook behavior, created and modified files, network traffic as PCAP, screenshots and memory dumps. Its capemon monitor adds automated unpacking, capturing injected, extracted or decompressed payloads and dumping process images, plus a debugger that YARA signatures or submission options can program with breakpoints and short instruction traces. Results are classified with YARA, Suricata and behavioral signatures, and malware configurations are extracted by parsers maintained in the separate CAPE-parsers package. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/capev2/))
- [Cheat Engine](https://github.com/cheat-engine/cheat-engine) — tool · binary, game — Memory scanner, debugger, disassembler and assembler for inspecting and changing running games and other applications; its README calls it a development environment focused on modding games and applications for personal use. The project site lists a memory scanner for finding the variables a game uses, a debugger, disassembler, assembler, speedhack, trainer maker, Direct3D manipulation and system inspection tools, and the README's build notes add Mono, .NET and Java runtime inspection, a VEH debugger interface, Lua and C code in scripts and an optional kernel-mode driver. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/cheat-engine/))
- [Chisel](https://github.com/meta/chisel) — tool · app — Collection of LLDB commands for debugging iOS apps. Commands print view and view-controller hierarchies, find views by class-name regex, show or hide views without resuming the process, mask or border views, set symbolic breakpoints on a class's method and watchpoints on instance variables, and can be extended with custom commands. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/chisel-lldb/))
- [Cuckoo Sandbox](https://github.com/cuckoosandbox/cuckoo) — tool · binary — Cuckoo Sandbox is an automated malware analysis system that executes suspicious files in an isolated environment and reports their behavior. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/cuckoo-sandbox/))
- [Cuckoo3](https://github.com/cert-ee/cuckoo3) — tool · binary, website — Cuckoo3 is a malware analysis tool that tests suspicious files and links in sandboxed platform emulators. It reports what the files or websites did during the test. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/cuckoo3/))
- [decomp2dbg](https://github.com/mahaloz/decomp2dbg) — tool · binary — Decompiler-to-debugger bridge that serves function names, global variables, stack and register variable names and decompiled code from IDA Pro, Binary Ninja, Ghidra or angr-management to GDB over an XML-RPC connection. Synced functions and globals become ordinary GDB symbols for breakpoints and memory examination, local variables appear as convenience variables, and each breakpoint stop prints the decompiled line for the current address. It works with vanilla GDB, GEF and pwndbg, and can map a shared library's symbols once its load range is supplied. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/decomp2dbg/))
- [deREferencing](https://github.com/danigargu/deREferencing) — tool · binary — IDA Pro plugin that adds register and stack views to the debugger in which pointers are dereferenced and colored, similar to the context displays of GDB plugins such as PEDA, GEF and pwndbg. It supports x86, x86-64, ARM, ARM64, MIPS32 and MIPS64; both views open from Debugger > Debugger Windows or with Alt-Shift-D and Alt-Shift-E, and a saved desktop layout reopens them in later debugging sessions. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/dereferencing/))
- [Dobby](https://github.com/jmpews/Dobby) — tool · binary — Lightweight, modular hook framework for Windows, macOS, iOS, Android and Linux on x86, x86-64, ARM and ARM64. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/dobby/))
- [DRAKVUF](https://github.com/tklengyel/drakvuf) — tool · binary — Tamas K Lengyel's virtualization-based, agentless black-box binary analysis system: running on the Xen hypervisor through LibVMI, it traces execution in Windows and Linux guests, from kernel functions to user-mode code, without installing software inside the analyzed virtual machine. Plugins log system calls, API calls and file, registry, process, socket and memory activity, extract files from memory before they are deleted and dump process memory, while an injector starts programs in the guest without an in-guest helper. CERT Polska's separate DRAKVUF Sandbox builds an automated sandbox with a GUI on top of it. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/drakvuf/))
- [DRAKVUF Sandbox](https://github.com/CERT-Polska/drakvuf-sandbox) — tool · binary — CERT Polska’s automated black-box malware analysis system built on the DRAKVUF engine, which observes a Windows guest from the Xen hypervisor through virtual machine introspection rather than an agent installed in the guest. Samples submitted through the web interface or the CLI run in a restored snapshot, and the report covers the process tree, WinAPI, file and network activity, a PCAP, intercepted TLS keys, memory dumps, screenshots and capa-based TTP indicators. A drakrun installer builds the Windows guest, its snapshot and the VMI profile. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/drakvuf-sandbox/))
- [Dwarf](https://github.com/iGio90/Dwarf) — tool · app, binary — PyQt5 debugger built on Frida that attaches to or spawns processes on Android and iOS devices, the local machine or a remote Frida server. A session offers breakpoints, watchpoints, register context, backtraces and threads, a Capstone disassembly view for x86, x64, ARM and ARM64, a hex editor and panels for modules, memory ranges and memory search, while Java explorer, inspector and trace panels and an Objective-C inspector cover mobile app runtimes. User Frida scripts and Python plugins extend a session, and companion commands trace Java methods or native addresses (dwarf-trace), log syscalls from one module on arm64 (dwarf-strace) and scaffold scripting projects with Dwarf API typings (dwarf-creator). ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/dwarf-debugger/))
- [DynamoRIO](https://github.com/DynamoRIO/dynamorio) — tool · binary — Runtime code manipulation system for building dynamic tools that observe or transform any part of an unmodified program while it runs. Its API and IA-32/AMD64/ARM/AArch64 instruction manipulation library allow arbitrary changes to application instructions, not only inserted callouts. The release package bundles tools including drcov for code coverage, drltrace for library-call tracing, drstrace for Windows system calls, drmemtrace instruction and memory traces and the drdisas disassembler. It is a framework for building instrumentation tools, distinct from Frida's scripting of live processes. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/dynamorio/))
- [Dyninst](https://github.com/dyninst/dyninst) — tool · binary — Binary instrumentation, analysis and modification libraries from the Paradyn project. DyninstAPI inserts and changes instrumentation in a running program, or rewrites an executable or library on disk and writes the patched copy back, through one machine-independent interface that also supports static and dynamic analysis. Separately documented components handle symbol tables, line numbers and types (SymtabAPI), instruction decoding and disassembly (InstructionAPI), parsing into blocks, functions and loops (ParseAPI), dataflow analyses such as slicing (DataFlowAPI), process control, stack walking and patching. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/dyninst/))
- [E9Patch](https://github.com/GJDuck/e9patch) — tool · binary — Static binary rewriter for x86-64 Linux ELF executables and shared objects that inserts instrumentation without control-flow recovery and writes a drop-in replacement binary, following the PLDI 2020 paper Binary Rewriting without Control Flow Recovery. The e9tool front end selects instructions with a matching language over disassembly attributes and patches them with built-in trampolines, calls into C or C++ functions compiled with e9compile.sh, or hand-written plugin trampolines; the e9patch back end accepts JSON-RPC commands so other tools can drive it. Optional modes add heuristic control-flow recovery for faster rewritten binaries or SIGILL-based fallbacks for full patch coverage. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/e9patch/))
- [edb-debugger](https://github.com/eteran/edb-debugger) — tool · binary — Qt-based graphical debugger for AArch32, x86 and x86-64 programs, inspired by OllyDbg, with Linux as its only officially supported platform. Its interface docks disassembly, register, stack and data views, adds a memory-regions window, process attaching, symbols, an expression syntax and an analysis bar similar to IDA Pro's, and its default plugins include Analyzer, Assembler, BinarySearcher, BreakpointManager, FunctionFinder, HardwareBreakpoints, HeapAnalyzer, OpcodeSearcher, ROPTool and SymbolViewer. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/edb-debugger/))
- [ElleKit](https://github.com/tealbathingsuit/ellekit) — tool · binary — Function hooking toolkit for arm64 XNU devices: a C function hooker that patches memory pages directly, an Objective-C function hooker, an arm64 assembler, JIT inline assembly for Swift and a reimplementation of the Substrate and libhooker APIs. The README lists the Dopamine, palera1n and meowbrek2 jailbreaks as users. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ellekit/))
- [FakeNet-NG](https://github.com/mandiant/flare-fakenet-ng) — tool · binary, protocol — Mandiant FLARE's dynamic network analysis tool for malware analysts, a successor to the original Fakenet. Its Diverter redirects traffic from local processes on Windows, or from other hosts in Linux MultiHost mode, to configurable listeners that simulate DNS, HTTP and HTTPS, SMTP, FTP, TFTP, IRC, POP and raw TCP or UDP services, with process and host filters, custom responses and an option to launch a command such as a debugger on a connection. Sessions can be saved as PCAP and end with an interactive HTML report of network-based indicators grouped by process and protocol. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/fakenet-ng/))
- [Fermion](https://github.com/FuzzySecurity/Fermion) — tool · app, binary — Ruben Boonen's Electron desktop application that wraps frida-node and the Monaco editor into one window for writing, injecting and refining Frida scripts. The editor brings linting, IntelliSense and Frida API type definitions, a device menu reaches local, USB and remote Frida servers to attach to or spawn processes, scripts reload in the target with a keyboard shortcut and their output appears beside the code. A prototype CALL tracer follows every call a thread makes from a chosen pointer or module symbol until it returns and renders the result as a Graphviz SVG graph, and Frida's JavaScript API documentation is built in. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/fermion/))
- [fishhook](https://github.com/meta/fishhook) — tool · app — Small library that dynamically rebinds symbols in Mach-O binaries running on iOS, on device and in the simulator, by updating the lazy and non-lazy symbol pointers that dyld uses. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/fishhook/))
- [FLEX](https://github.com/FLEXTool/FLEX) — tool · app — FLEX is an in-app tool for exploring and debugging running iOS applications. It can inspect and change views and object state, browse app files and databases, and show network request history. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/flex-ios/))
- [fpicker](https://github.com/ttdennis/fpicker) — tool · app, binary — Frida-based fuzzing suite for closed-source programs, listed here for its in-process instrumentation and coverage layer. A JavaScript harness injected with Frida starts Stalker at an analyst-chosen function and records the basic blocks executed in the target module on each call, feeding either AFL++'s coverage bitmap or fpicker's own standalone mode. Its passive mode works as a tracer: it attaches to a running process without sending inputs and stores each input that reaches the function together with new coverage, on the local host or on USB and network devices running frida-server. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/fpicker/))
- [Frida](https://github.com/frida/frida) — tool · app, game — Dynamic instrumentation toolkit for observing and scripting the behavior of live native and managed processes. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/frida/))
- [Frida Workbench for VSCode](https://github.com/ChiChou/vscode-frida) — tool · app — Unofficial Visual Studio Code extension for Frida work. A sidebar lists apps and processes on local, Apple Simulator, USB and remote devices to attach, spawn or kill, and runtime panels browse an attached process's native modules and exports and its Java and Objective-C classes, methods and protocols; selected items generate Interceptor, Objective-C or Java hook scaffolds, optionally with GitHub Copilot-inferred native signatures, and Objective-C headers or Java class stubs can be dumped. A language server completes class, selector and module names from a live target, and the extension also scaffolds Frida agent and C-module projects, script-debugging configurations, syslog streaming and Android frida-server setup. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/vscode-frida/))
- [frida-scripts (0xdea)](https://github.com/0xdea/frida-scripts) — tool · app, binary — Marco Ivaldi's (raptor) collection of Frida JavaScript instrumentation scripts for reverse engineering mobile apps and Linux programs. The main Android and iOS tracers hook Java or Objective-C classes and methods selected by name, wildcard or regular expression, plus native functions matched by Frida ApiResolver patterns such as exports:*!open*, and log each call's arguments and return value; they can also print backtraces. Companion scripts enumerate loaded classes and methods, and a Linux pair traces and enumerates functions in an ELF binary and its shared libraries. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/raptor-frida-scripts/))
- [frida-trace (NowSecure library)](https://github.com/nowsecure/frida-trace) — tool · app, binary — JavaScript library for Frida agents that traces native functions from a declarative description: each function is listed with its return type and input and output arguments, including pointers, UTF-8 and UTF-16 strings and byte arrays whose length is bound to another argument or the return value, with conditions such as reading an output pointer only when the call succeeds. It resolves the functions from a module's exports or a vtable, attaches Frida's Interceptor and hands one event per call to callbacks. Helper scripts in the same repository generate trace boilerplate from C headers through libclang. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/nowsecure-frida-trace/))
- [Fridump](https://github.com/Nightbringer21/fridump) — tool · app, binary — Small Python command-line tool that attaches to a running process through Frida and writes its accessible memory ranges to files, run from a Windows, Linux or macOS host against iOS, Android or Windows applications. By default it saves ranges that are readable and writable, -r also takes read-only ones, ranges above a size limit (20 MiB unless set with --max-size) are split into chunks, and -s runs a basic strings pass that collects printable text from every dump into strings.txt. Its README presents it for penetration testers and developers. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/fridump/))
- [GDB](https://sourceware.org/gdb/) — tool · binary — Native and remote debugger for controlling execution and inspecting live or crashed program state. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/gdb/))
- [GDB dashboard](https://github.com/cyrus-and/gdb-dashboard) — tool · binary — Standalone .gdbinit file written with GDB's Python API that shows a modular status interface each time the debugged program stops, reducing the commands needed to inspect its state. No GDB command is redefined; its features sit under a single dashboard command. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/gdb-dashboard/))
- [gdbgui](https://github.com/cs01/gdbgui) — tool · binary — Browser-based frontend to the GNU debugger, gdb. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/gdbgui/))
- [GEF (GDB Enhanced Features)](https://github.com/hugsy/gef) — tool · binary — Single-file Python extension, gef.py, that adds dynamic-analysis commands to GDB. Each stop shows a context view of registers, stack, code, threads and call trace, and commands cover memory maps, pointer dereferencing, searching process memory for patterns, ELF and security-property information, process status and glibc heap inspection. An architecture abstraction layer covers x86-32/64, ARMv5–v7, AArch64, SPARC, MIPS and PowerPC, and gef-remote enriches remote sessions through gdbserver or the GDB stubs of qemu-user and qemu-system. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/gef/))
- [Grapefruit](https://github.com/ChiChou/grapefruit) — tool · app — Inspect and hook running iOS and Android apps from a Frida-based web interface, with method and crypto-API hooks, filesystem and SQLite browsers, iOS keychain and UI-hierarchy views, and HTTP capture from common networking libraries. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/grapefruit/))
- [gxpc](https://github.com/ReverseApple/gxpc) — tool · app, protocol — macOS command-line XPC sniffer, inspired by xpcspy, that injects a Frida agent into an attached or newly spawned process and logs the XPC messages it sends and receives, labelled with the libxpc function, connection name, peer PID and process name. The agent recursively converts xpc_object_t dictionaries, arrays and scalar values into readable output and decodes binary-plist payloads, while a Go host built on frida-go selects a local, USB or remote Frida device, filters connections by name or PID and can save the trace to a file. It observes how Apple-platform apps and services communicate over XPC and does not alter or inject messages. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/gxpc/))
- [Hollows Hunter](https://github.com/hasherezade/hollows_hunter) — tool · binary — Windows command-line memory scanner by hasherezade that runs the PE-sieve engine across many processes, or the whole system, instead of a single PID. It recognizes and dumps replaced or implanted PE modules, shellcode, hooks and other in-memory patches for further analysis, selecting processes by PID list, name list or creation time relative to the scan. Scans can repeat in a loop or, in the 64-bit build, run as an ETW listener whose INI profile chooses the triggering events. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/hollows-hunter/))
- [HyperDbg](https://github.com/HyperDbg/HyperDbg) — tool · binary — Hypervisor-assisted Windows debugger that virtualizes the running system with Intel VT-x and Extended Page Tables to debug kernel and user mode, either locally as a tracing and introspection tool or from a host attached over a serial connection to a guest virtual machine. Its events include EPT-based hidden breakpoints and hooks, memory read, write and execute monitoring without the limits of hardware debug registers, monitoring of system calls, CPUID, MSRs, I/O ports, MMIO, exceptions and interrupts, and Intel PT and LBR branch tracing. A kernel-side script engine, PDB symbol support and an SDK automate analysis. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/hyperdbg/))
- [iMonitor](https://github.com/Trivexion/iMonitor-System-Activity-Monitor) — tool · app — iMonitor is a Windows endpoint activity monitor for examining running software. It records process, file, registry and network events, adds DLL-load analysis, and supports custom columns and matching rules through plugins and scripts. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/imonitor-system-activity-monitor/))
- [Interruptor](https://github.com/FrenchYeti/interruptor) — tool · app, binary — Frida library, taken from the Dexcalibur project, that uses Frida’s Stalker to trace a process’s system calls and print strace-like lines with the calling thread, module and offset, decoded flag values and file paths resolved from descriptors. A trace can be limited to chosen modules and syscalls by name or pattern, started when a given library loads, extended with Interceptor-style per-syscall callbacks, and used to write drcov basic-block coverage files. The README documents Linux and Android on AArch64 and x64; the source also contains AArch32 Linux and macOS arm64 and x64 entry points. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/interruptor/))
- [kdemulator](https://github.com/000nico/kdemulator) — tool · binary — kdemulator executes Windows x86-64 kernel-driver binaries outside a live kernel for IOCTL analysis. It maps PE sections into Unicorn, intercepts imported NT API calls and supplies minimal kernel-object and memory stubs. The CLI can invoke DriverEntry, send one IOCTL or run a mutation loop over an IOCTL input buffer. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/kdemulator/))
- [Lighthouse](https://github.com/gaasedelen/lighthouse) — tool · binary — Code-coverage explorer plugin for IDA Pro and Binary Ninja that loads execution traces of native programs and paints the executed code across the disassembler's views, without needing symbols or source. Its coverage overview lists per-function coverage, and a composing shell combines loaded coverage sets with logical operators to diff runs, filter functions and jump to addresses, while coverage cross-references show which of many batch-loaded sets executed a block. It reads DynamoRIO drcov logs, module+offset, absolute-address and Tenet traces, writes basic HTML coverage reports, and ships a prototype Intel Pin tool and a Frida script that produce drcov-compatible logs. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/lighthouse/))
- [mal_unpack](https://github.com/hasherezade/mal_unpack) — tool · binary — mal_unpack is a Windows dynamic unpacker based on PE-sieve. It runs a packed malware executable, waits for its payload to unpack, dumps the payload, and terminates the original process. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/mal-unpack/))
- [malware-jail](https://github.com/HynekPetrak/malware-jail) — tool · binary, website — Node.js sandbox for semi-automatic analysis of JavaScript malware: it runs a sample in Node's vm module against emulated Windows Script Host and ActiveX objects such as WScript.Shell, MSXML2.XMLHTTP, ADODB.Stream, Scripting.FileSystemObject and WMI, plus a partial browser context with selectable browser profiles. It logs the objects created and methods called, keeps every eval() argument to help deobfuscation, records requested URLs and WMI queries, and saves the files a dropper tries to write so payloads can be examined. When the run ends, the whole sandbox context is dumped to JSON for review. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/malware-jail/))
- [MAMBO](https://github.com/beehive-lab/mambo) — tool · binary — Dynamic binary modification framework from the University of Manchester that runs unmodified Linux user-space programs on 32-bit Arm (A32/T32), AArch64 and RV64GC RISC-V, translating their code into a code cache as it executes. An event-driven C plugin API registers callbacks on code-scanning and execution events and emits instrumentation; sample plugins count instruction mixes, branches and blocks, trace memory accesses and system calls, find hotspots, simulate caches, check memory errors and detect data races. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/mambo/))
- [mobdevim](https://github.com/DerekSelander/mobdevim) — tool · app — Derek Selander's macOS command-line utility for inspecting and controlling iOS devices connected over USB or Wi-Fi through Apple's private MobileDevice framework. It lists devices and installed apps with their Info.plist keys and entitlements, streams the device console, fetches crash logs, shows provisioning profiles and developer certificates, copies an app's data container off the device and back where the sandbox allows, and installs, uninstalls and launches apps with arguments and environment variables. With a Developer Disk Image mounted it also lists and kills processes and simulates location, and output can be printed as plist for scripts. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/mobdevim/))
- [MonkeyDev](https://github.com/AloneMonkey/MonkeyDev) — tool · app — Based on iOSOpenDev, MonkeyDev adds a MonkeyApp project template to Xcode for instrumenting iOS apps the developer is authorized to analyze. From Xcode it injects a developer-written dylib into a supplied decrypted app, builds Objective-C or Logos (CaptainHook) hooks, and re-signs, installs and debugs the result with source-level breakpoints; optional build settings restore Objective-C symbols, export the target's Objective-C headers and enable Reveal view inspection. The current version also builds jailbreak Logos/CaptainHook tweaks and command-line tools for rootless, rootful or roothide environments. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/monkeydev/))
- [Noriben](https://github.com/Rurik/Noriben) — tool · binary — Python script that drives Sysinternals Process Monitor to record what a program does while it runs, filters out routine system activity and writes a text report of the processes it created and its file, registry and network activity. It works as a lightweight malware sandbox and can also log events while the analyst runs a sample by hand, with command-line options, user interaction or under a debugger; created files can be scanned with YARA rules and their hashes looked up on VirusTotal. Version 2.1.0 adds DLL-load monitoring, an ASCII process tree and JSON output, and NoribenSandbox.py automates runs inside a guest VM. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/noriben/))
- [OllyDbg](https://www.ollydbg.de/) — tool · binary — 32-bit assembler-level analyzing debugger for Windows that emphasizes binary code analysis for programs without source; its site says development is frozen, and the newest version it lists is 2.01 of 27 September 2013. Its analysis recognizes procedures, loops, API calls, switches, tables, constants and strings and decodes calls to more than 1,900 standard API and 400 C functions; it attaches to running programs, debugs DLLs directly, sets conditional, logging, memory and hardware breakpoints and writes patches back to the executable. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ollydbg/))
- [Patch-0xC0000022](https://github.com/Harryson3001/Patch-0xC0000022) — tool · app — Patch-0xC0000022 instruments the running Windows SysMain service by changing its NtCreateFile response to STATUS_ACCESS_DENIED. The C++ program finds the service process, resolves the remote function address and applies a six-byte patch to study the effect on prefetch behavior. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/patch-0xc0000022/))
- [PE-sieve](https://github.com/hasherezade/pe-sieve) — tool · binary — Windows process scanner by hasherezade that finds and dumps implants in one running process: replaced or injected PE modules, shellcode, inline and IAT hooks and other in-memory patches, with the README naming Process Hollowing, Process Doppelgänging and reflective DLL injection among the techniques it detects. Options add shellcode detection by patterns or statistics, scans of thread call stacks, non-executable pages and encrypted or obfuscated content, a choice of dump mode with import-table recovery for dumped PEs, minidumps and JSON reports. It builds as an EXE, as a DLL with a small API that Python, Go and Rust bindings use, or as a static library, and is the engine behind HollowsHunter and MalUnpack. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pe-sieve/))
- [PINCE](https://github.com/korcankaraokcu/PINCE) — tool · binary, game — Linux front end for the GNU Debugger (GDB), focused on games but usable for other reverse engineering; the name stands for “PINCE is not Cheat Engine”. It scans process memory and pointers through libmemscan into a typed address table, and its memory view adds disassembly, an on-the-fly assembler, code dissection for referenced calls, jumps and strings, function, region and instruction searches, and structure overlays, including structures generated from Mono/IL2CPP class dissection of native Linux or Wine/Proton processes. Debugging covers chained breakpoints and watchpoints, tracking of the instructions that access an address, tracing and stack traces, alongside a GDB console and the reusable libpince Python library. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pince/))
- [Ponce](https://github.com/illera88/Ponce) — tool · binary — IDA Pro plugin, described by its repository as the winner of the 2016 IDA plugin contest, that brings the Triton framework's taint analysis and symbolic execution into IDA's debugger for x86 and x64 programs. After the analyst taints or symbolizes registers, memory or inputs such as argv or a buffer read with fread, Ponce single-steps the debugged program, analyzes each instruction with Triton and renames the functions that the input influences. Its symbolic engine can solve a branch condition, or negate it and inject a satisfying input into memory, while execution snapshots restore earlier state to try that input; builds with Hex-Rays support also show the results in decompiler pseudocode. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ponce/))
- [ProbeDroid](https://github.com/ZSShen/ProbeDroid) — tool · app — Dynamic Java-method instrumentation kit for Android 5.0 that lets analysts write their own tracing and profiling tools for apps whose source code they do not have. A launcher uses ptrace to inject the native engine library and a user-built instrumentation APK into a newly started target app, and the tool subclasses the Instrument API to hook framework or app-defined Java methods and inspect or change their arguments and return values. The README's demos use sample tools that log strings built through StringBuilder and StringBuffer and the activities and services an app starts. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/probedroid/))
- [ProcDump for Linux and macOS](https://github.com/microsoft/ProcDump-Linux) — tool · binary — Linux and macOS reimagining of the Sysinternals ProcDump tool that writes process dumps in response to performance, runtime, signal and resource-tracking triggers, including native dumps through a Rust ELF writer and managed dumps through .NET diagnostics IPC. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/procdump-linux/))
- [Process Dump](https://github.com/glmcdona/Process-Dump) — tool · binary — Process Dump is a Windows command-line tool that saves code from running processes to disk for malware analysis. It can find hidden modules and loose executable code, reconstruct PE headers and imports, and use a clean-hash database to skip known modules. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/process-dump/))
- [pwndbg](https://github.com/pwndbg/pwndbg) — tool · binary — Python plug-in that loads into GDB, or runs as a REPL around LLDB, to make live program state easier to inspect. Each stop prints a context of registers, stack, call frames and annotated disassembly with recursively dereferenced pointers; commands cover memory maps, search, telescoping, heap allocators and Go values, with Capstone/Unicorn emulation and decompiler integration for IDA, Binary Ninja, Ghidra and angr-management. The README lists Linux ELF, macOS Mach-O, QEMU user/system and embedded Cortex-M or RISC-V/32 debugging. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pwndbg/))
- [QBDI](https://github.com/QBDI/QBDI) — tool · app, binary — Quarkslab's modular dynamic binary instrumentation (DBI) framework, which JIT-recompiles a program's basic blocks inside its own process so that an instrumentation tool can register callbacks before or after instructions, on memory reads and writes, and on VM events. The engine is a library that can start an instrumented execution anywhere in a process, scripted from C, C++, Python (PyQBDI) or Frida JavaScript. It has no preferred injection method: it bundles the LD_PRELOAD-based QBDIPreload for Linux and macOS dynamic executables and is integrated with Frida. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/qbdi/))
- [QEMU](https://www.qemu.org/) — tool · binary, firmware — Machine and user-space emulator that runs code built for one CPU architecture on another through its TCG dynamic translator. System emulation boots a complete emulated machine, such as an ARMv7 board on an x86_64 PC, and user-mode emulation runs Linux or BSD binaries built for another ABI by translating their system calls. For analysis, its gdbstub lets GDB stop the guest, examine registers and memory and set breakpoints and watchpoints, TCG plugins passively instrument execution down to single instructions and memory accesses (bundled examples count instructions, trace system calls and log execution), and system emulation supports deterministic record/replay debugging. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/qemu/))
- [Qiling](https://github.com/qilingframework/qiling) — tool · binary, firmware — Python emulation framework that adds OS, filesystem and API behavior around CPU emulation for instrumented binary analysis. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/qiling/))
- [QIRA](https://github.com/geohot/qira) — tool · binary — QIRA is a tool for examining program execution traces. Its interface lets users navigate instruction changes, register values, memory reads and writes, and forks. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/qira/))
- [r2frida](https://github.com/nowsecure/r2frida) — tool · app, binary — Use radare2 commands and Frida instrumentation together to inspect local or remote process memory, symbols, classes, and runtime calls on desktop and mobile targets. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/r2frida/))
- [ReClass.NET](https://github.com/ReClassNET/ReClass.NET) — tool · binary, game — Memory structure editor for reconstructing the classes and data structures of a running x86 or x64 process, a .NET port of ReClass with many additions. Users lay out memory as typed nodes, such as integers, floats, vectors and matrices, text, pointers, arrays, unions, virtual tables and function pointers, with automatic node dissection, highlighting of changed memory, types from PDB debug symbols and RTTI class names, then generate C++ or C# code from the result. It also offers a memory viewer and scanner, a module and section dumper, a debugger that finds what writes to or accesses an address, and plugins that can supply other memory-access methods or game-specific class information, such as for Frostbite games. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/reclass-net/))
- [ret-sync](https://github.com/bootleg/ret-sync) — tool · binary — Reverse-Engineering Tools SYNChronization: plugins that keep IDA, Ghidra or Binary Ninja graph and decompiler views following a live WinDbg, GDB, LLDB, OllyDbg 1.10/2 or x64dbg session, rebasing addresses on the fly so ASLR does not break the mapping. Debugger commands push comments, labels, rebased addresses and command output into the disassembler database and resolve symbols from it, while disassembler hotkeys set breakpoints and step the debugger; several databases can be synced at once and the two sides may run on different hosts. A standalone Python library offers the same position syncing to custom tracing tools. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ret-sync/))
- [rr](https://github.com/rr-debugger/rr) — tool · binary — Records process execution and replays it under GDB, enabling repeatable inspection and reverse execution. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rr/))
- [Runtime Mobile Security (RMS)](https://github.com/m0bilesecurity/RMS-Runtime-Mobile-Security) — tool · app — Frida-based web interface for observing Android and iOS apps at run time on a test device. It lists the classes and methods loaded in memory, traces method arguments and return values, generates hook templates for a selected class, finds class instances on the heap, and on Android monitors categorized framework APIs and a few libc file functions; a beta file browser covers the app's private folders. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rms-runtime-mobile-security/))
- [ScyllaHide](https://github.com/x64dbg/ScyllaHide) — tool · binary — User-mode (ring 3) anti-anti-debug library for 32- and 64-bit Windows programs that hooks system functions so the program being debugged cannot detect the debugger. Its documentation covers options for PEB debug flags such as BeingDebugged, NtGlobalFlag and heap flags, threads hidden with ThreadHideFromDebugger, debugger windows and processes found through window and process-list queries, invalid-handle NtClose checks, input blocking, timing checks, hardware-breakpoint detection and exception-based checks; extra options inject DLLs, prevent thread creation and dump processes started RunPE-style. Plugins integrate it with x64dbg, OllyDbg v1 and v2, IDA and TitanEngine, and the standalone InjectorCLI injects its hook library into a process under any debugger. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/scyllahide/))
- [Sogen Dart bindings](https://github.com/Wdestroier/sogen_dart) — tool · binary — Sogen Dart bindings expose Windows and Linux binary emulation through a Dart FFI package and native bridge. The API covers execution, snapshots, memory and registers, process views, callbacks and low-level hooks; Windows API calls and Linux symbols can also be intercepted. Its example hooks a Sleep call in an emulated executable. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/sogen-dart-bindings/))
- [Speakeasy](https://github.com/mandiant/speakeasy) — tool · binary — Mandiant's Windows malware emulation framework, which runs PE executables, DLLs, kernel drivers and raw x86/amd64 shellcode on the Unicorn CPU emulator inside a modeled Windows runtime instead of a full VM. Hand-written handlers and a signature database built from win32metadata and phnt emulate Windows API calls, process and thread behavior, the filesystem, registry and network, and each run is recorded as a structured JSON report of API events with named arguments, dropped files and optional memory snapshots. It runs from the speakeasy CLI or as a Python library, and a built-in GDB stub lets an analyst step through an emulated sample. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/speakeasy/))
- [System Informer](https://github.com/winsiderss/systeminformer) — tool · binary — Windows process viewer and system inspector, formerly Process Hacker, for examining running programs and the system around them. It gives a highlighted overview of system activity, searches the handles and loaded DLLs of processes to find which one is using a file, shows active network connections and real-time disk access, displays stack traces with kernel-mode, WOW64 and .NET support, and creates, edits and controls services. Version 4.0 adds a Process Monitor that logs process, thread, image, registry, file, handle and silo events from its kernel driver, and plugins add .NET assembly and performance tabs, a window explorer and Hybrid Analysis lookups. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/system-informer/))
- [Tenet](https://github.com/gaasedelen/tenet) — tool · binary — IDA Pro plugin for exploring execution traces of a binary: it loads an externally recorded trace and lets the analyst step forwards and backwards through time while the disassembly shows the execution flow in both directions. Execution, memory and region breakpoints mark every execution of an instruction or every read and write of an address or range along a trace timeline, register seeking jumps to the instruction that set a register's value, and a timestamp shell seeks to a position or percentage of the trace. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/tenet/))
- [tenet_tracer](https://github.com/mahmoudimus/tenet_tracer) — tool · binary — tenet_tracer uses Intel Pin to record instruction-level execution traces for Tenet. It captures program counters, register changes and memory accesses, stores them in SQLite tables, and provides a script to export the trace text. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/tenet-tracer/))
- [Tiny Tracer](https://github.com/hasherezade/tiny_tracer) — tool · binary — Intel Pin tool by hasherezade that traces a 32- or 64-bit program while it runs on Windows or Linux, logging API calls (with arguments and return values for watched functions), chosen local and statically linked functions, inline system calls, RDTSC, CPUID and INT instructions, and transitions between sections of the traced module, which helps find the original entry point of a packed module. It can follow code the module unpacks into new memory, disassemble selected ranges and, on Windows, flag the anti-debug and anti-VM checks a sample performs. Each run writes a .tag file of RVA;event lines that PE-bear reads natively and that the wiki shows loading into IDA Pro, Binary Ninja, x64dbg, Ghidra and Cutter to annotate code. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/tiny-tracer/))
- [Valkyrie](https://github.com/rifatardarslan/Valkyrie) — tool · binary — Valkyrie analyzes suspect binaries through static inspection and Docker-based execution. Its documented pipeline examines PE headers, entropy and packing, then captures system calls, file activity and PCAP traffic for reports and threat-intelligence output. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/valkyrie-malware-analyzer/))
- [ViperMonkey](https://github.com/decalage2/ViperMonkey) — tool · binary — VBA parser and emulation engine, created by Philippe Lagadec and developed since 2017 mainly by Kirk Sayre and other contributors, for analyzing and deobfuscating malicious macros in Microsoft Office files such as Word, Excel, PowerPoint and Publisher documents, as well as VBScript files and HTA files carrying VBScript. The vmonkey command reads the macros through oletools' olevba, emulates them from their auto-run entry points or from functions the analyst names, and reports the recorded actions, such as process creation, along with optional intermediate IOCs and the files the code would write; vbashell is an interactive VBA shell. A Python API evaluates VBA snippets, walks and emulates individual code blocks, replaces functions and deobfuscates code. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/vipermonkey/))
- [Virtuailor](https://github.com/0xgalz/Virtuailor) — tool · binary, firmware — IDAPython tool that reconstructs C++ virtual tables in IDA Pro for 32- and 64-bit Intel and AArch64 code by observing indirect calls while the program runs under IDA's debugger. Its static part finds indirect calls and hooks the assignment of their targets with conditional breakpoints; when those breakpoints hit, the dynamic part creates vtable structures, renames vtables and virtual functions, adds structure offsets to the call instructions and cross-references from each indirect call to the virtual functions it reached, and for AArch64 firmware tries to fix undefined vtables and their functions. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/virtuailor/))
- [Voltron](https://github.com/snare/voltron) — tool · binary — Extensible Python debugger UI toolkit that attaches utility views to LLDB, GDB, VDB and WinDbg/CDB sessions. Each view retrieves data from the debugger host and can run in its own terminal, so users can assemble a custom debugger interface; built-in views cover registers, disassembly, stack, memory, breakpoints and backtrace, and any debugger command can be split off into a view highlighted with a chosen Pygments lexer. The views complement the debugger's command line rather than replace it. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/voltron/))
- [WinDbg](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-hardware/drivers/debugger/) — tool · binary — Microsoft's WinDbg debugger and the Debugging Tools for Windows, documented on Microsoft Learn, for debugging live user-mode and kernel-mode code, analyzing crash dumps and examining CPU registers and memory. The current app keeps the engine, commands and extensions of WinDbg (Classic) and adds Time Travel Debugging, which records a user-mode process and replays it in both directions, JavaScript and NatVis scripting and Linux ELF dump analysis; since 1.2610.1001.0 it also offers WinDbg MCP for supported AI clients. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/windbg/))
- [x64dbg](https://github.com/x64dbg/x64dbg) — tool · binary — Windows binary debugger with disassembly, memory views and plugins for analyzing programs without source code. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/x64dbg/))
- [x64dbg-tenet](https://github.com/gr0grig/x64dbg-tenet) — tool · binary — x64dbg-tenet is a native x64dbg plugin that records execution steps in Tenet trace format. Commands start and stop logging or trace a specified number of steps, with an option to stop when execution leaves the current module. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/x64dbg-tenet/))
- [xAnalyzer](https://github.com/ThunderCls/xAnalyzer) — tool · binary — x64dbg plugin that annotates the static code of the debugged x86 or x64 executable with function-call, argument and loop analysis. It adds argument and type information from over 13,000 API definitions for almost 200 DLLs, handles undefined and register-based calls generically and detects loops automatically. Analysis runs on a selection, a function or a whole module from menus or the xanal commands, can run automatically at the entry point like OllyDbg's initial analysis, and users can extend its .api and header definition files. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/xanalyzer/))
- [xpcspy](https://github.com/ab-rizk/xpcspy) — tool · app, protocol — Frida-powered command-line tool that observes XPC messages exchanged between processes on iOS and macOS in both directions, for understanding how Apple-platform apps and services talk over XPC. It can spawn or attach to a target over a USB or remote Frida connection, filter the trace by direction (incoming or outgoing) and by service name with wildcards, and optionally parse the binary-plist payloads carried in XPC dictionaries, with bplist00 and bplist16 described as officially supported and bplist15 and bplist17 as experimental. A Python CLI drives a TypeScript Frida agent that is compiled to JavaScript. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/xpcspy/))

<a id="android"></a>
## Android tools

- [abc-decompiler](https://github.com/ohos-decompiler/abc-decompiler) — tool · app — Decompiler for HarmonyOS Ark bytecode (.abc), the format HarmonyOS app packages carry in modules.abc, built by adapting the jadx decompiler and reading files through a patched copy of the abcde library. Because much of Ark bytecode is semantically close to DEX, it reuses jadx's GUI, IR and passes: an .abc file dropped into the jadx-based window is shown as decompiled code. The README reports that all 227 usable instructions of the 282 Ark opcodes are implemented, most special instructions as pseudo-functions. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/abc-decompiler/))
- [ABCDE](https://github.com/Yricky/abcde) — tool · app — Kotlin toolkit for OpenHarmony reverse engineering that parses Ark bytecode (.abc) files, the format HarmonyOS app packages carry in modules.abc, along with HAP packages and resource index (.index) files. Its Compose desktop viewer browses classes, methods, fields, literal arrays, imports and exports and disassembles methods, showing imported modules and resource references in readable form; an experimental mode translates some methods literally into JavaScript, and a CLI dumps class lists and resource indexes. The parsing core is also published as a JVM library, and the separate abc-decompiler builds on a patched fork of it. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/abcde/))
- [Androguard](https://github.com/androguard/androguard) — tool · app — Python toolkit for APK/DEX metadata, bytecode and reference analysis; the v5 source branch also documents agent skills and MCP. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/androguard/))
- [Android Reverse](https://github.com/UltraSina/androidReverse) — tool · app — Android app, published only as release APKs, that runs an APK reverse-engineering workflow entirely on the phone, from package extraction and Java decompilation to native analysis, editing and rebuilding. It extracts APK, XAPK and split APKS packages from storage or installed apps, decompiles each class with Jadx (with fallback and IR modes), CFR, Procyon, JD-Core, Krakatau or Vineflower, analyzes native libraries through an embedded radare2 with pseudo-C, assembly, hex, control-flow-graph and call-graph views, and since v20 edits resources, binary XML, the manifest and smali before rebuilding and signing the APK. A built-in MCP server lets MCP clients edit those project files while rebuilding stays a manual step; the README also lists Flutter Dart AOT analysis through Unflutter and Unity il2cpp metadata parsing. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ultrasina-android-reverse/))
- [Android Security Toolkit](https://github.com/abhirampatel/android-security-toolkit) — tool · app — Local Python APK triage tool that drives apktool and strings, checks manifest settings and source/native-library strings, and produces TXT/JSON findings for manual review. Its rules flag possible issues without proving exploitability or reconstructing application behavior. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/android-security-toolkit/))
- [Android Unpacker](https://github.com/strazzere/android-unpacker) — tool · app — Code and slides from the Defcon 22 talk "Android Hacker Protection Level 0". It holds a gdb and adb script for Bangcle and SecShell, a native unpacker for APKProtect, Bangcle, LIAPP and Qihoo Packer, tweaks that hide QEMU and debuggers from APKProtect, and a Corellium-based dynamic unpacking approach with automation. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/android-unpacker/))
- [Android Unpinner](https://github.com/mitmproxy/android-unpinner) — tool · app, protocol — Tool from the mitmproxy project that removes certificate pinning from an Android APK you own or are authorized to test, without a rooted device. It marks the app debuggable, touching only AndroidManifest.xml, and injects a Frida Gadget over ADB so an interception proxy can read the app's traffic, reusing HTTP Toolkit's unpinning script. It bundles the Android SDK helpers it needs and can also handle split APKs delivered as XAPK. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/android-unpinner/))
- [Android-Disassembler](https://github.com/yhs0602/Android-Disassembler) — tool · app, binary — On-device disassembler and binary viewer for Android, built on Capstone, that opens ELF shared libraries and executables, object files, Windows PE files, .NET assemblies, dex files and APKs chosen from storage or from installed apps. It shows ELF details and symbol tables, PE import and export tables, disassembly with jump following and navigation by symbol or address, a hex view, ASCII string search and byte statistics such as entropy and chi-square tests to help tell whether data is encrypted. Analyses are kept as projects and can be exported as raw, text, JSON or database files. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/android-disassembler/))
- [Andromeda](https://github.com/secrary/Andromeda) — tool · app — Interactive command-line tool by Lasha Khasaia for first-pass reverse engineering of Android APKs, written in C/C++ for speed.
