It downloads and unpacks IPSWs, OTAs and KDKs and compares builds; inspects Mach-O load commands, symbols, signatures and entitlements with ARM64 disassembly; finds symbols and cross-references in dyld shared caches, extracts dylibs and dumps Objective-C headers and Swift metadata; extracts KEXTs and compares kernelcaches; and parses IMG4, decrypts AEA archives and inspects iBoot and coprocessor firmware. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ipsw/))
- [JTAGulator](https://github.com/grandideastudio/jtagulator) — tool · firmware — Joe Grand's open-source hardware tool and Parallax Propeller firmware for locating on-chip debug interfaces among an unfamiliar board's test points, vias, component pads or connectors. Driven from a serial terminal, its 24 level-shifted channels try pin permutations to identify JTAG pinouts by IDCODE or BYPASS scans, SWD pinouts by IDCODE and UART pinouts with their baud rates. With a known JTAG pinout the firmware can also read device IDs, discover instruction and data registers and map a chip's pins to boundary-scan positions with EXTEST, and other modes pass UART traffic through, act as a SUMP-compatible logic analyzer or emulate a Bus Pirate so OpenOCD can use the board as a JTAG adapter. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/jtagulator/))
- [m1n1](https://github.com/AsahiLinux/m1n1) — tool · firmware — Asahi Linux's bootloader and hardware-research environment for Apple Silicon Macs. It bridges Apple's boot chain to Linux by loading appended device trees, initramfs and kernels; in proxy mode a USB link lets a Python client probe hardware live and run macOS or Linux under m1n1's hypervisor while tracing its hardware accesses. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/m1n1/))
- [ME Analyzer](https://github.com/platomav/MEAnalyzer) — tool · firmware — Parse Intel Management Engine (CS)ME, TXE and SPS firmware, Graphics System Controller firmware and their independent PMC, PCHC, PHY and option-ROM images to report family, version, release, SKU, platform, size and health status. Research options print flash partition, BPDT/IFWI and CSE layout tables, validate RSA signatures and checksums, and unpack the supported CSE and GSC code and file systems. A bundled database identifies known images and reports those not yet listed in the community firmware repositories. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/me-analyzer/))
- [me_cleaner](https://github.com/corna/me_cleaner) — tool · firmware — me_cleaner is a Python script that modifies Intel ME firmware images to reduce the firmware's ability to interact with the system. Depending on the ME generation, it removes the firmware or retains only modules needed for boot. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/me-cleaner/))
- [mithril](https://github.com/nmatt0/mithril) — tool · firmware — Offline C++ scanner for the contents of firmware and IoT software, pointed at a single file or an already unpacked root filesystem, that reports embedded secrets, a software bill of materials, known CVEs, open-source licenses, boot-security configuration and weak or publicly known keys, each finding with its evidence and a confidence level. Its SBOM also recovers components from ELF version banners, libc filenames and the kernel banner in stripped images and is written as CycloneDX and SPDX, while the CVE pass joins it against a local OSV and NVD mirror annotated with CISA KEV and EPSS. It does not unpack images; the author's companion tool moria handles identification and extraction. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/mithril-iot-scanner/))
- [MS4X](https://github.com/therudywolf/MS4X) — tool · firmware — MS4X analyzes Siemens MS42 and MS43 engine-control firmware for BMW M52TU and M54 engines. Its Python tools disassemble and assemble C166 instructions and inspect image checksums, stock-code differences and TunerPro patchlists; companion documents cover memory maps and findings in the factory programs. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ms4x/))
- [OFRAK](https://github.com/redballoonsecurity/ofrak) — tool · binary, firmware — Recursively identify, unpack, analyze, modify, and repack firmware and binary formats using a resource-tree GUI and Python API, with optional disassembler backends. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ofrak/))
- [OpenOCD](https://github.com/openocd-org/openocd) — tool · firmware — OpenOCD is a tool for programming and debugging embedded targets through hardware debug adapters. It supports flash programming, breakpoints, single stepping, and remote debugging through GDB. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/openocd/))
- [Pawn](https://github.com/google/pawn) — tool · firmware — Linux command-line tool from Google that reads the BIOS/UEFI firmware image out of the SPI flash of Intel-based workstations and laptops. Running as root, it identifies the chipset's LPC bridge over PCI, maps the chipset's root-complex registers through /dev/mem, and reports the flash descriptor regions and the BIOS write- and read-protection settings it finds. It then reads the flash through the SPI controller's hardware sequencing into a single image file, which the README suggests processing further with tools such as UEFITool. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/google-pawn/))
- [payload dumper](https://github.com/vm03/payload_dumper) — tool · firmware — Python script that extracts the partition images, such as boot.img and system.img, from the payload.bin inside an Android OTA update package. It handles full OTAs directly and incremental OTAs with --diff by applying the update to original images that the user copies from a full OTA or dumps from the device, and it can also run from a Docker image. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/payload-dumper/))
- [payload-dumper-go](https://github.com/ssut/payload-dumper-go) — tool · firmware — Go command-line tool and library that extracts partition images from Android A/B OTA payloads, reading either a raw payload.bin or the OTA zip that contains it in place. It decompresses operations in parallel, can list partitions or dump only selected ones, and verifies operation data, source images and final images with SHA-256, exiting non-zero on any mismatch. Incremental (delta) payloads are applied onto previously extracted base images, and it regenerates the dm-verity hash tree and FEC parity that delta payloads omit, so the README reports bit-exact output. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/payload-dumper-go/))
- [PCM Explorer](https://github.com/dspl1236/PCM-Explorer) — tool · firmware — PCM Explorer is a read-only Python browser for Porsche PCM and Audi MMI head-unit images. It parses QNX4/QNX6 drives, compressed firmware, update discs, persistence flash and HBM5 HMI files, and supports search, image comparison, extraction and filesystem checks. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pcm-explorer/))
- [Penguin](https://github.com/rehosting/penguin) — tool · firmware — Rehost embedded Linux firmware from an extracted root filesystem using a generated, editable configuration of emulation transformations, then refine it through a guest root shell, forwarded network services and recorded dynamic-analysis output. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/penguin/))
- [PIC microcontrollers disassembler](https://github.com/c-not-around/pic-disasm) — tool · firmware — PIC microcontrollers disassembler converts Intel HEX dumps for MidRange and Enhanced MidRange PIC12/PIC16 devices into disassembly listings, HI-TECH PICC assembly files, or both. Processor descriptions and instruction definitions are supplied as JSON configuration files. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pic-disasm/))
- [PMCA-RE RELOADED](https://github.com/masterflai/pmca-re-reloaded) — tool · firmware — PMCA-RE RELOADED is a Rust desktop and CLI toolkit for Sony cameras that includes experimental analysis of where menu settings reside in camera settings memory. It also parses firmware files, backup images and usage logs offline, and provides USB access to camera information, apps, firmware updates and settings. Supported operations depend on the camera generation. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pmca-re-reloaded/))
- [premium5](https://github.com/mnaberez/premium5) — tool · firmware — premium5 emulates the Volkswagen Premium 5 car radio at the register and signal level so its original firmware can run without patches. It models the NEC microcontroller, LCD controller, EEPROM and selected vehicle interfaces, with live disassembly, memory inspection and execution controls for firmware research. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/premium5-radio-emulator/))
- [PulseView](https://github.com/sigrokproject/pulseview) — tool · firmware, protocol — Examine captured logic and analog waveforms with sigrok's graphical frontend. Protocol decoders and decoder stacking expose bus messages and higher-level device operations, making hardware communication traces useful for firmware and interoperability investigations. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pulseview/))
- [Pyrrha](https://github.com/quarkslab/pyrrha) — tool · binary, firmware — Quarkslab's firmware cartography tool, which indexes an extracted firmware filesystem into a database browsed with NumbatUI, Quarkslab's fork of the Sourcetrail code explorer. The fs mapper uses LIEF to map ELF and PE files, their imports and exports and the symlinks that point to them; fs-cg disassembles every executable with IDA Pro or Ghidra and links their call graphs across binaries, and decomp indexes one executable's decompiled functions with call cross-references. JSON exports can be post-processed in Python, for example to diff two firmware versions. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pyrrha/))
- [qemu-rtd2841](https://github.com/rubenthampy890-del/qemu-rtd2841) — tool · firmware — qemu-rtd2841 adds a Realtek RTD2841/RTK284x TV machine model to QEMU for bare-metal diagnostics and local firmware experiments. Its board profile models AArch32 Cortex-A53 cores, GICv2, timers, SDHCI, Ethernet, UART, and a framebuffer, with diagnostic assembly and a staged test runner. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/qemu-rtd2841/))
- [rbasefind](https://github.com/sgayou/rbasefind) — tool · firmware — Multithreaded Rust command-line tool that brute-forces the load address of a flat 32-bit firmware image, reimplementing mncoppola's basefind.py and rsaxvc's basefind.cpp. It records the offsets of ASCII strings, reads every 32-bit word as a possible pointer, and ranks candidate base addresses by how many strings each one would turn into pointer targets. The README calls it a simple heuristic that works rather well on some ARM (non-Thumb) binaries and will not work miracles. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rbasefind/))
- [RETracker](https://github.com/patois/RETracker) — tool · firmware — Python reverse-engineering framework for the Polyend Tracker music device that patches the vendor's stock firmware, replacing an unused USB RAWHID handler with its own command handler. With the patched firmware flashed, a host CLI reads, hex-dumps and disassembles device memory in ARM or Thumb mode, writes memory, runs code at an address, assembles and executes position-independent "Polyp" modules such as a pad text scroller and screen fades, and copies files to the SD card over USB. Its fwtool.py also unpacks and repacks the Intel HEX .ptf firmware images for loading into a disassembler. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/retracker/))
- [Shannon Modem Loader for IDA Pro](https://github.com/alexander-pick/shannon_modem_loader) — tool · firmware — IDA Pro loader and post-processing scripts for Samsung Exynos "Shannon" baseband modem images that carry a TOC header, including crash dumps. The loader maps the TOC entries to aligned segments and entry points; after auto-analysis the post-processor restores debug-trace structures, names functions from strings and debug-log calls, rebuilds the MPU table and memory map, comments MMU-related coprocessor instructions, performs the ARM scatter-load with its decompression, and labels platform-abstraction-layer and task-initialization functions. The README adds the author's notes on ShannonOS, the PAL and the move from Cortex-R to Cortex-A cores. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/shannon-modem-loader/))
- [sniffROM](https://github.com/alainiamburg/sniffROM) — tool · firmware, protocol — Python tool that reconstructs serial flash contents from passive bus captures: it parses SPI or I²C analyzer exports from Saleae Logic, recorded while a device reads and writes its own flash chip, and writes the observed data into a binary image at the original memory addresses. It recognizes more than 100 SPI flash commands from vendors such as Winbond, Macronix, Spansion and GigaDevice and currently parses 12 of them, reports manufacturer and device IDs seen in JEDEC ID reads, and can print a command summary, show a visual map of the captured address ranges and plot the timing of reads and writes. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/sniffrom/))
- [Sony-PMCA-RE](https://github.com/ma1co/Sony-PMCA-RE) — tool · firmware, protocol — USB tool built from reverse engineering Sony digital cameras. On supported models it reads camera information and installs Android apps through the PlayMemories Camera Apps mechanism, while its firmware-updater and service-mode shells can dump firmware, run Linux commands and change settings. The repository's notes describe the app-installation exchange between the Sony app store, the browser plugin and the camera. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/sony-pmca-re/))
- [SPRD Flash Tool](https://github.com/ajsb85/sprdflash-rs) — tool · firmware, protocol — SPRD Flash Tool implements the SPRD/UNISOC PAC download protocol in Rust for RDA8910 and UIS8910 devices. Its analysis functions read partitions or whole flash through READ_FLASH and infer a proposed BMAConfig layout from U-Boot, LuatOS and filesystem regions; the author reports byte comparisons on an Air724UG. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/sprdflash-rs/))
- [SVD-Loader for Ghidra](https://github.com/leveldown-security/SVD-Loader-Ghidra) — tool · firmware — Ghidra script from leveldown security that reads a CMSIS-SVD file, the XML format describing a microcontroller's memory-mapped peripherals and their registers, and applies it to firmware already loaded in Ghidra. It creates memory blocks for the peripheral address ranges, a structure type for each peripheral with its registers as fields, and a label at each peripheral base address, so register accesses can be read by peripheral and register name. It targets little-endian Arm Cortex-M devices and bundles a fork of the cmsis-svd parser ported to Ghidra's Jython. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/svd-loader-ghidra/))
- [T2 platform research](https://github.com/macintog/t2-platform-research) — research · firmware, protocol — T2 platform research maps Apple T2 firmware, hardware and Linux-facing interfaces using static analysis, hardware observations and driver comparisons. Its chapters cover Secure Enclave services, boot and recovery, graphics, audio, sensors, storage and other subsystems. Machine-readable findings and board indexes connect claims to their evidence, with Python reference code for selected message formats. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/t2-platform-research/))
- [ThisFirmwareDoesNotExist](https://github.com/ThisFreakDoesntExist/ThisFirmwareDoesNotExist) — tool · firmware — ThisFirmwareDoesNotExist maps Arturia MicroFreak firmware 5.0.0.2084 and provides freakpatch to modify a user's official update file. Its notes cover the update format and firmware subsystems, while Ghidra scripts and assembly sources support the documented patch locations. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/thisfirmwaredoesnotexist/))
- [Tigard](https://github.com/tigard-tools/tigard) — tool · firmware, protocol — Open-hardware board built around the FTDI FT2232H that connects a computer to a target device's UART, SPI, I2C, JTAG and SWD interfaces through level shifters that operate from 1.8 to 5.5 V. One of the chip's two ports is dedicated to UART and the other is shared by the remaining interfaces; switches select the reference voltage and the SPI/JTAG or I2C/SWD wiring, and a logic-analyzer header lets an external analyzer watch the traffic. Tigard has no software of its own: it is designed to work with existing x232H tools, and the README gives settings for reading SPI flash, JTAG debugging with OpenOCD or Black Magic Debug, SWD debugging with OpenOCD, boundary scan, and iCE40 or AVR programming. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/tigard/))
- [Titan M tools (Quarkslab)](https://github.com/quarkslab/titanm) — tool · firmware, protocol — Tools Quarkslab published from its research on Google's Titan M security chip in Pixel phones: a Ghidra loader for Titan M (Citadel) firmware files, nosclient for tracing and sending the chip's protobuf-based commands from Android with a black-box fuzzer built on libprotobuf-mutator, a parser for sigrok SPI captures, and scripts that convert firmware images to the rec files of the chip's SPI rescue feature. The repository also holds the Black Hat Europe 2021 white paper and slides, the ROOTS'21 paper and the Black Hat USA 2022 slides, which describe reverse engineering the firmware, sniffing its SPI communication with the application processor, and black-box and emulation-based fuzzing; the authors say the vulnerabilities they found were disclosed to Google. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/quarkslab-titanm-tools/))
- [UBI Reader](https://github.com/onekey-sec/ubi_reader) — tool · firmware — Python module and command-line scripts that read UBI and UBIFS images, including ones inside raw NAND dumps, by locating the first UBI or UBIFS data in a file. They extract files or whole UBI and UBIFS images, list and copy single files, display UBI headers, volume tables and the UBIFS super and master nodes, and search physical erase blocks when debugging failed extractions. ubireader_utils_info also derives the mtd-utils parameters, written as a shell script and config file, needed to rebuild an image to the same specification. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ubi-reader/))
- [UEFI Firmware Parser](https://github.com/theopolis/uefi-firmware-parser) — tool · firmware — Python library and uefi-firmware-parser command for identifying, parsing and extracting PC firmware images: UEFI firmware volumes, capsules, firmware file systems, files and sections, Intel PCH flash descriptors, Intel ME modules and Dell PFS update packages. An AutoParser picks the format, or a brute-force scan looks for volumes and other known objects inside an unidentified blob; results print as an object hierarchy or dump to files, with Tiano/EFI and LZMA sections decompressed by a bundled C extension. Helper scripts replace a section or file by GUID inside a volume, generate an FDF flash description from parsed volumes and output GUID labels for IDA scripts. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/uefi-firmware-parser/))
- [uefi_retool](https://github.com/yeggor/uefi_retool) — tool · firmware — Python command-line tool and IDA Pro plugin for UEFI firmware reverse engineering. uefi_retool.py unpacks a firmware image with uefi-firmware-parser, collects its executable modules and runs IDA in batch mode on each one, writing their boot-service calls and protocol GUIDs to JSON; a separate command lists protocols whose GUIDs are absent from its EDK2, AMI and vendor GUID tables as proprietary. Inside IDA the plugin applies EFI system-table types and names, lists protocols in an explorer window and, from the JSON, shows a dependency browser and graph across the firmware’s modules. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/uefi-retool/))
- [UEFITool](https://github.com/LongSoft/UEFITool) — tool · firmware — Parse UEFI firmware images into an inspectable tree and check their structure. Companion UEFIExtract and UEFIFind tools extract parsed contents and locate patterns, supporting analysis of firmware components and vendor formats. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/uefitool/))
- [unblob](https://github.com/onekey-sec/unblob) — tool · firmware — Recursively extracts archives and filesystems from binary blobs while reporting chunk boundaries and unknown regions. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/unblob/))
- [vmlinux-to-elf](https://github.com/marin-m/vmlinux-to-elf) — tool · binary, firmware — Rebuild an analyzable ELF with function and variable symbols from a Linux kernel image, whether a raw or compressed vmlinux, vmlinuz, zImage or bzImage blob, certain Android boot.img files or a stripped kernel ELF, by locating and decoding the embedded kallsyms symbol table. It detects the compression format, instruction set, endianness, word size, entry point and an approximate base address and writes an ELF for IDA Pro or Ghidra; a kallsyms-finder listing, a standalone decompressor and a GTK GUI are included. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/vmlinux-to-elf/))

<a id="binary-libraries"></a>
## Binary analysis libraries

- [1C 7.7 Configuration Parser (MD Parser)](https://github.com/bigsprut/1CMDParser) — tool · app — 1C 7.7 Configuration Parser inspects 1C:Enterprise 7.7 1cv7.md configuration containers. Its Win32 interface displays OLE streams, decompresses ZLib data, removes the documented XOR layer and parses Main MetaData Stream into an object hierarchy. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/1cmdparser/))
- [amoco](https://github.com/bdcht/amoco) — tool · binary — Python package for symbolic analysis of binaries. It combines an instruction-decoding framework for adding architectures, a symbolic algebra module that describes instruction semantics, an abstract memory and execution model, and disassembly techniques from linear sweep and recursive traversal to solver-based path-predicate exploration. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/amoco/))
- [angr](https://github.com/angr/angr) — tool · binary — Python binary-analysis framework combining control-flow recovery, symbolic execution, data-flow analysis and decompilation. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/angr/))
- [B2R2](https://github.com/B2R2-org/B2R2) — tool · binary — Binary analysis framework written in F# for .NET by KAIST's SoftSec Lab. It parses ELF, PE, Mach-O and WebAssembly files, parses, disassembles and lifts instructions into its own IR, recovers control-flow graphs and assembles code; the README's feature table covers x86/x86-64, ARMv7/AArch64, MIPS, PowerPC, RISC-V, SPARC, S390 and other ISAs as well as CIL, EVM, eBPF, WebAssembly and Python bytecode. Its b2r2 launcher starts a readelf-like format scanner, a linear-sweep disassembler, a recursive-descent explorer with a web GUI, an IR and assembly REPL and an assembler. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/b2r2/))
- [BARF](https://github.com/programa-stic/barf-project) — tool · binary — Python binary analysis framework that loads ELF and PE programs, disassembles 32- and 64-bit x86 and 32-bit ARM code with Capstone and translates it into the REIL intermediate language, so its analyses do not depend on the source architecture. Modules recover control-flow and call graphs, emulate REIL and turn code fragments into Z3 or CVC4 formulas for checking constraints on registers and memory; release 0.6.0 added basic symbolic execution. Bundled command-line tools recover CFGs and call graphs, replay x86 execution traces and search, classify and verify ROP gadgets. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/barf/))
- [bddisasm](https://github.com/bitdefender/bddisasm) — tool · binary — Bitdefender’s dependency-free C decoder for x86/x64 instructions, reporting explicit and implicit operands with their access modes, CPUID feature flags, flag accesses and valid modes, and printing Intel/MASM-style text. A minimal API with a 64-byte instruction structure trades detail for speed, and the companion bdshemu library emulates basic x86/x64 code in a raw buffer to flag shellcode-like behaviour such as NOP sleds, reading the instruction pointer, self-modification, TEB/PEB access and system calls. The disasmtool command-line example decodes or emulates files and hex strings. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/bddisasm/))
- [Binary Analysis Platform (BAP)](https://github.com/BinaryAnalysisPlatform/bap) — tool · binary — OCaml binary analysis platform from Carnegie Mellon University. Its bap command loads a program, disassembles it, lifts instructions into an architecture-neutral intermediate representation, builds the control-flow graph and runs analysis passes, while objdump, raw-loader and mc modes handle linear disassembly, raw code blobs and single encodings. The README lists x86, x86-64, ARM, MIPS and PowerPC support, an interpreter, a microexecution interpreter, a symbolic executor and the Primus Lisp language for analyses, function stubs and SMT-solver queries; analyses are written as OCaml plugins or by embedding BAP as a library, with C bindings and minimal Python support in separate repositories. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/bap/))
- [Binlex](https://github.com/c3rb3ru5d3d53c/binlex) — tool · binary — Binary trait-lexer framework that extracts instructions, basic blocks and functions from executables and emits searchable JSON traits for similarity, hunting and signature work. It models code with a genomics-inspired hierarchy and computes features such as MinHash, TLSH, SHA256, entropy and wildcard byte patterns, so samples can be compared, clustered and turned into signatures at scale. It ships a CLI suite, a Rust API, Python bindings and an IDA plugin, can generate YARA rules from its patterns, and runs processors locally or over IPC and HTTP. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/binlex/))
- [BinStruct Inspector](https://github.com/1051093860/BinStruct-Inspector) — tool · binary — BinStruct Inspector is a GUI for examining binary file layouts through user-defined field trees. It parses files as structures are edited, shows parsed values beside synchronized hex and JSON views, and tracks byte-level insertions, deletions, and replacements; plugins add custom data types. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/binstruct-inspector/))
- [Capstone](https://github.com/capstone-engine/capstone) — tool · binary — Disassembly library with architecture-neutral APIs and instruction details for custom binary-analysis pipelines. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/capstone/))
- [CoBRA (Coefficient-Based Reconstruction of Arithmetic)](https://github.com/trailofbits/CoBRA) — tool · binary — Trail of Bits' C++23 simplifier for Mixed Boolean-Arithmetic (MBA) expressions, an obfuscation technique that rewrites simple computations as interleaved arithmetic, bitwise and shift operations. Given an expression and a bit width from 1 to 64, cobra-cli returns a simpler equivalent: a worklist scheduler combines signature-vector and change-of-basis techniques, semilinear handling of constant masks, polynomial recovery, decomposition of mixed products and subexpression lifting, and each result is checked on random inputs or, optionally, with a Z3 equivalence proof. The same engine is available as a library and as an optional LLVM pass plugin that simplifies MBA expressions it detects in LLVM IR. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/cobra-mba-simplifier/))
- [cupertino-files](https://github.com/den-frie-vilje/cupertino-files) — tool · app — cupertino-files is a TypeScript library for reading and writing modern Apple Pages, Numbers, and Keynote documents. It models their undocumented file structures from real documents, exposes editing APIs and CLI tools, and publishes format notes and a language-neutral conformance suite. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/cupertino-files/))
- [diStorm3](https://github.com/gdabah/distorm) — tool · binary — Lightweight x86/AMD64 disassembler library in C that the README describes as a decomposer: besides formatted text, distorm_decompose returns a binary structure per instruction with its opcode, operands, prefixes, flow-control class and optionally affected CPU flags. It decodes 16-, 32- and 64-bit code, can stop at calls, returns, branches, interrupts or privileged instructions, and its generated instruction tables cover integer, x87, MMX, 3DNow!, SSE through SSE4.2 and SSE4a, AVX, FMA, AES, CLMUL, VMX and SVM sets. The repository includes the distorm3 Python module, plus example Java and .NET wrappers and sample programs for Linux, Win32 and a Windows kernel driver. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/distorm/))
- [flare-emu](https://github.com/mandiant/flare-emu) — tool · binary — Mandiant FLARE Python library that pairs IDA Pro, Rizin or Radare2 with the Unicorn emulator so analysts can script emulation of the binary they are analyzing, for 32- and 64-bit x86, ARM and ARM64. Its interfaces emulate an instruction range or function with chosen register and stack values, force execution down the branches needed to reach target addresses or every cross-reference to a function, walk all paths through a function, run a standalone byte blob, or emulate from an address until nothing is left, with hooks on instructions and calls. A common use in its README is decrypting malware strings by emulating the decryption routine at each call site and commenting the results. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/flare-emu/))
- [flashkit](https://github.com/bitalizer/pyflashkit) — tool · binary — flashkit is a Python toolkit for examining Adobe Flash SWF files and AVM2 bytecode. It parses and rebuilds SWF and ABC structures, disassembles instructions, constructs call and reference indexes, and decompiles classes or methods to ActionScript 3 through a control-flow pipeline. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/flashkit/))
- [goblin](https://github.com/m4b/goblin) — tool · binary — Rust crate for zero-copy, endian-aware parsing of ELF32/64, 32/64-bit Mach-O and multi-architecture fat containers, PE32/PE32+, COFF objects, Terse Executable (TE) images and Unix/BSD archives; Object::parse picks the format from its magic bytes. Parsed structures cover ELF headers, sections, symbols, dynamic entries, relocations and notes, Mach-O load commands, imports and exports, and PE imports, exports, relocations, TLS, resources, exception and unwind data, Authenticode certificates and the .NET CLR header. Feature flags can reduce it to std-free #[repr(C)] struct definitions for kernels and other no_std code. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/goblin/))
- [GTIRB](https://github.com/GrammaTech/gtirb) — tool · binary — GrammaTech's intermediate representation for passing disassembly, analysis and rewriting results between binary tools, modeled on the role LLVM-IR plays for compilers. An IR holds modules for executables or libraries with their sections, byte intervals, code and data blocks, symbols and symbolic operands, plus an inter-procedural CFG and AuxData tables for analysis results, all referenced by UUID; instruction semantics are deliberately left out, so machine code stays as raw bytes for Capstone/Keystone or any other IL. It serializes as protobuf, with C++, Python and Common Lisp APIs and a partial Java API; the separately listed ddisasm produces it. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/gtirb/))
- [Hachoir](https://github.com/vstinner/hachoir) — tool · binary — Hachoir is a Python library for viewing and editing binary streams as trees of fields, down to individual bits. Its command-line tools can search binary files for text, extract or remove metadata, and display file contents in text mode. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/hachoir/))
- [iced](https://github.com/icedland/iced) — tool · binary — x86 instruction library for 16-, 32- and 64-bit code that decodes, formats, assembles and encodes instructions, with masm, nasm, GNU AT&T and Intel XED output syntaxes. Its instruction-info API reports the registers, memory operands and RFLAGS bits an instruction reads or writes, its CPUID feature flags and control-flow type, and the encoder can re-encode decoded instructions at any address. Rust, C# (.NET) and Java implementations live in one repository, with Python, JavaScript (WebAssembly) and Lua bindings built on the Rust crate. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/iced/))
- [inkterop](https://github.com/cable729/inkterop) — tool · app — inkterop converts native handwriting files from note apps and e-ink devices through a documented intermediate representation. It reads stroke data including pressure and tilt where present, implements readers for formats such as GoodNotes and Notability, and publishes format investigation notes and conversion fidelity modes. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/inkterop/))
- [Kaitai Struct](https://github.com/kaitai-io/kaitai_struct) — tool · binary, firmware, protocol — Turn documented binary layouts into reusable parsers for files and network packets. A .ksy specification can be inspected with visualizers and compiled to multiple languages, helping preserve reverse-engineered format knowledge in interoperable implementations. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/kaitai-struct/))
- [libPeConv](https://github.com/hasherezade/libpeconv) — tool · binary — libPeConv is a Windows library for loading and manipulating PE files. It supports tasks such as section remapping, relocation, import loading, resource parsing, and rebuilding import address tables from memory dumps. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/libpeconv/))
- [LIEF](https://github.com/lief-project/LIEF) — tool · binary — Library for structured parsing and modification of executable formats including ELF, PE and Mach-O. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/lief/))
- [Manticore](https://github.com/trailofbits/manticore) — tool · binary — Trail of Bits' now-archived symbolic execution tool for Linux ELF binaries on x86, x86-64, AArch64 and ARMv7, Ethereum smart contracts as EVM bytecode, and WebAssembly modules. It runs a program on symbolic inputs to explore the states it can reach, generates concrete inputs that lead to a chosen state and detects crashes and other failures, solving constraints with an external Yices, Z3 or CVC4 solver. The manticore CLI writes generated test cases to an mcore_ workspace, while the Python API adds instruction hooks, event callbacks and custom initial states; manticore-verifier checks contract properties written in the contract's own language. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/manticore/))
- [Metasm](https://github.com/jjyg/metasm) — tool · binary — Metasm is a Ruby suite for assembling, disassembling, debugging, and modifying executable binaries. It supports formats including PE, ELF, Mach-O, and shellcode. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/metasm/))
- [Miasm](https://github.com/cea-sec/miasm) — tool · binary — Python reverse-engineering framework for analyzing, modifying and generating binary programs. It reads, modifies and generates 32- and 64-bit PE and ELF files, assembles and disassembles x86, ARM, AArch64, MIPS, PowerPC, SH4, MSP430 and MeP code, and lifts it into its own intermediate language for symbolic execution, SSA and data-flow analysis, dependency graphs and expression simplification aimed at deobfuscation. Its JIT emulator runs shellcode or parts of binaries through LLVM, GCC, Clang or pure-Python backends, with Python callbacks that stand in for library functions, sandboxes with selected Windows API and Linux emulation, and dynamic symbolic execution through Z3. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/miasm/))
- [pdbinspector](https://github.com/nikaiw/pdbinspector) — tool · binary — pdbinspector analyzes Microsoft PDB files to list structures, classes, enums, functions, public symbols and exports. It demangles MSVC C++ names, decodes MSVC string literals and emits text, JSON or tab-separated reports through a CLI; a WebAssembly version runs online. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pdbinspector/))
- [rabbitizer](https://github.com/Decompollaborate/rabbitizer) — tool · binary, game — MIPS instruction decoder written in C that never allocates on the heap, with Python, C++ and Rust bindings in the same repository and the stated aim of producing matching assembly. It decodes one instruction word at a time, focusing on MIPS I to III with partial MIPS IV support, and also handles the N64 RSP, the PlayStation's R3000 GTE, the PSP's R4000 Allegrex and the PS2's R5900; it offers validation checks, instruction-grouping queries, configurable pseudo-instructions and register names, and small helpers for building pointer and symbol detection. Matching-decompilation tooling builds on it: splat and spimdisasm both list it as a Python dependency. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rabbitizer/))
- [Rellic](https://github.com/lifting-bits/rellic) — tool · binary — Trail of Bits implementation of the pattern-independent structuring algorithm from the No More Gotos paper, turning LLVM bitcode into goto-free C as a small, hackable decompiler back end that interoperates with LLVM and Remill. rellic-decomp translates a bitcode module to C in batch, rellic-xref serves a web interface for choosing refinement passes and linking C AST nodes to the bitcode that produced them, and rellic-headergen emits C layouts of data structures from a module's DWARF metadata. Machine code must first be lifted to bitcode by another tool; a bundled IDA script uses Anvill for that step. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rellic/))
- [Remill](https://github.com/lifting-bits/remill) — tool · binary — Static binary translation library that lifts AArch64, SPARC32, SPARC64, x86 and amd64 machine code, including AVX and AVX512, into LLVM bitcode, with AArch32 support underway. It focuses on lifting individual instructions accurately and is meant as a library for other tools such as McSema; its documentation walks through how an instruction is lifted, how instruction semantics are implemented and the overall design. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/remill/))
- [SMDA](https://github.com/danielplohmann/smda) — tool · binary — Python recursive disassembler library built on Capstone that recovers control-flow graphs from PE, ELF and Mach-O files, .NET assemblies, raw DEX files and memory dumps with a known base address, through x86/x64, AArch64, 32-bit ARM/Thumb, CIL and Dalvik backends. Its JSON reports list functions, basic blocks, instructions and the edges between them, enriched with symbols from imports, exports, PDB, Go, Rust and Delphi metadata, referenced strings, Windows API references inferred with the ApiScout method and position-independent code hashes. Function discovery deliberately favours completeness over precision; the README names SMDA as capa's disassembler backend and MCRIT's code-analysis layer, and IDA Pro or Binary Ninja analyses can be exported into the same report format. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/smda/))
- [sqlce](https://github.com/boykopovar/sqlce) — tool · app — sqlce reads SQL Server Compact .sdf database files using a cross-platform C++ parser informed by analysis of decompiled SQL CE components. Python and WebAssembly bindings expose table names, schemas and rows, including files encrypted with a supplied password. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/sqlce/))
- [Triton](https://github.com/JonathanSalwan/Triton) — tool · binary — C++ library with Python bindings for dynamic binary analysis of x86, x86-64, ARM32, AArch64 and RISC-V 32/64 code. Callers process instructions one at a time, optionally with concrete register and memory values; Triton builds symbolic expressions from each instruction's semantics, tracks taint and solves path constraints through Z3 or Bitwuzla. It also synthesizes and simplifies expressions and lifts them to LLVM IR or Z3 and back, for building analysis tools, automating reverse engineering, software verification or emulating code. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/triton/))
- [Unicorn Engine](https://github.com/unicorn-engine/unicorn) — tool · binary, firmware, game — Multi-architecture CPU emulator for executing selected instruction ranges with register, memory and hook control. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/unicorn/))
- [VTIL-Core](https://github.com/vtil-project/VTIL-Core) — tool · binary — C++20 core of the VTIL (Virtual-machine Translation Intermediate Language) project, an intermediate language and optimizing compiler that the README describes as built for binary de-obfuscation and de-virtualization. Unlike LLVM IR, VTIL keeps the native stack, physical registers and non-SSA form, so code from register or stack machines can be lifted into it, native instructions can stay inline, and code can be emitted back at any virtual address. The library provides the IL with routines, basic blocks and serialization, a symbolic expression engine and simplifier, optimizer passes such as dead-code elimination, propagation and stack pinning, and Capstone/Keystone-based AMD64 and ARM64 disassembly and assembly helpers. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/vtil-core/))
- [Zydis](https://github.com/zyantific/zydis) — tool · binary — Lightweight C11 x86/x86-64 disassembler and code-generation library. Its decoder reports each instruction's operands, accessed CPU and FPU flags, ISA set and extension and branch type; the formatter prints Intel, MASM or AT&T syntax, and the encoder assembles instructions from encoder requests, which can also be derived from decoded instructions. ZydisDisasm and ZydisInfo command-line tools are included, and the official Rust and Python 3 bindings live in separate repositories. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/zydis/))

<a id="binary-diffing"></a>
## Binary comparison tools

- [BinDiff](https://github.com/google/bindiff) — tool · binary, game — Compares disassembled binaries to find matching functions and changed code across versions. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/bindiff/))
- [BinExport](https://github.com/google/binexport) — tool · binary — The exporter component of BinDiff: plugins for IDA Pro and Binary Ninja and an extension for Ghidra that write an analyzed disassembly to the Protocol Buffer format BinDiff requires. The BinExport2 schema covers instructions with their operands and expressions, basic blocks, flow graphs, the call graph, sections, strings, comments and data references; the exporter can also write a statistics text file or a text dump for debugging. Exports can be scripted through IDC or IDAPython in IDA and through a BinExport.java script that also runs in Ghidra's headless analyzer. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/binexport/))
- [Diaphora](https://github.com/joxeankoret/diaphora) — tool · binary, game — IDA-based binary diffing that matches functions and transfers names, comments and types between analyzed versions. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/diaphora/))
- [ghidriff](https://github.com/clearbluejar/ghidriff) — tool · binary, firmware — Command-line binary diffing engine that drives Ghidra headlessly through pyghidra: it imports and analyzes an old and a new binary, then reports added, deleted and modified functions, with patch diffing as its main use. The default VersionTrackingDiff engine combines several Ghidra Version Tracking correlators with BSim matching, alongside simpler symbol-name and structural-graph engines and a GhidraDiffEngine base class for custom differs. Results are written as JSON and Markdown with Mermaid charts and meta diffs of strings, called and calling functions and binary metadata, plus optional side-by-side HTML. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ghidriff/))
- [Kam1n0](https://github.com/McGill-DMaS/Kam1n0-Community) — tool · binary — Server platform from McGill University's Data Mining and Security Lab that indexes collections of disassembled binaries into shared repositories and searches them for cloned assembly functions. Its applications cover subgraph clone search within one instruction-set family (Asm-Clone), cross-architecture semantic search over VEX IR using symbolic and concrete execution (Sym1n0), Asm2Vec representation learning aimed at compiler optimization and obfuscation, and executable classification built on Asm2Vec. An IDA Pro plug-in sends functions to the server and shows flow-graph, text-diff and clone-group views; the README's accuracy and query-time figures come from the authors' own papers. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/kam1n0/))
- [Karta](https://github.com/CheckPointSW/Karta) — tool · binary, firmware — Source-code-assisted IDA Python plugin from Check Point Research that finds and matches open-source libraries compiled into large binaries and firmware. Its identifier script reports which supported libraries, such as OpenSSL, zlib, libpng and net-snmp, are present and fingerprints their versions; the matcher then uses a JSON configuration built from that library version to locate its compiled files by their original function order, match their functions and the external libc functions they call, and export selected matches as IDA names. The bundled Thumbs Up scripts improve IDA's function analysis beforehand, including ARM/Thumb separation in firmware. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/karta/))
- [Pigaios](https://github.com/joxeankoret/pigaios) — tool · binary — Pigaios is a tool that matches C source code against binaries in IDA. It extracts function features from source code, finds corresponding functions in an IDA database, and imports symbols, structures, and enumerations. It can work with partial source code that does not compile. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pigaios/))
- [wMatcher](https://github.com/earthsworth/wMatcher) — tool · binary — wMatcher compares two Java archives to match classes renamed or obfuscated between releases. Its desktop workflow loads old and new JARs, accepts dependency JARs, lets the analyst correct uncertain matches and saves the comparison as a .wmatch project. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/wmatcher/))

<a id="learning"></a>
## Learning resources

- [Android App Reverse Engineering 101](https://www.ragingrock.com/AndroidAppRE/) — learning-resource · app, binary — Maddie Stone's free English workshop introducing static reverse engineering of Android apps, published as GitHub Pages. Its chapters cover app components and entry points, Dalvik bytecode and smali, decompiling DEX with jadx, finding and reading JNI native libraries in Ghidra, and string deobfuscation. Seven exercises with written solutions apply this to triaging malware samples and spotting command-execution flaws in pre-installed apps. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/android-app-re-101/))
- [AndroidReverse101](https://github.com/Evil0ctal/AndroidReverse101) — learning-resource · app — A Chinese-language Android reverse-engineering course by Evil0ctal, planned as 100 daily lessons. It starts from number systems, ARM assembly, the Dalvik/ART runtime and APK loading, then covers APK and DEX structure, Smali editing and repackaging, Frida and Xposed hooking, JNI and native-library analysis, traffic analysis, obfuscation and app hardening, with commands and code for tools such as jadx, apktool, readelf, GDB/LLDB, Frida and Burp Suite. A Crackmes folder adds copies of OWASP's UnCrackable and r2pay practice apps, and a community Indonesian translation follows the published lessons. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/androidreverse101/))
- [Corkami pics](https://github.com/corkami/pics) — learning-resource · binary — Collection of posters and diagrams that dissect file formats byte by byte, published as PNG and PDF images. The binary posters cover executables (ELF, DOS COM and EXE, NE, LE, PE, Mach-O and fat Mach-O, Java class, Dalvik DEX, Python bytecode, SWF, GameCube/Wii DOL and Switch NRO) along with image, video, archive, document, sound and serialization formats, x86 and x64 opcode charts and Dalvik, JVM and .NET opcode tables. The more detailed 101 series explains PE, ELF, Mach-O, ZIP, Java class, PDF, COM and WAV files and includes Inkscape sources and proof-of-concept files. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/corkami-pics/))
- [CrackMes](https://github.com/ghostware0x00/CrackMes) — learning-resource · binary — CrackMes is a set of walkthroughs for purpose-built crackme binaries from crackmes.one. The supplied Linux x86-64 examples identify ELF executables, inspect program disassembly, use breakpoints and runtime observations, and explain how candidate input is checked. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/crackmes-writeups/))
- [Decomp Academy](https://decomp-academy.dev/) — learning-resource · binary, game — A free, English, in-browser course in matching decompilation maintained by Jack Price-Burns: learners read a function's target assembly, write C, and the original console compiler grades the result byte for byte. Lessons build from reading registers and arithmetic through control flow, structs, floating point, the ABI, globals, optimizer behaviour and 64-bit integers, then finish with functions adapted from open-source decompilation projects. The curriculum now spans GameCube PowerPC, Game Boy Advance Thumb and Nintendo 64 MIPS courses, and the frontend source is on GitHub. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/decomp-academy/))
- [DecompileBench (Findings of ACL 2025)](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-acl.1194/) — research · binary — A benchmark combining functions from real programs, runtime-aware validation, and LLM-assisted assessment of decompiled-code usability. Helps separate functional correctness from perceived understandability when comparing decompilers. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/decompilebench-paper/))
- [Embedded Hacking (mytechnotalent)](https://github.com/mytechnotalent/Embedded-Hacking) — learning-resource · firmware — A free English course by mytechnotalent on developing, debugging and reverse engineering firmware for the Raspberry Pi Pico 2 (RP2350, Arm Cortex-M33). A fourteen-week syllabus with slides, Markdown notebooks and a 40-chapter PDF textbook follows a write, debug, then hack pattern from hello-world through variables, data types, constants, operators, conditionals, structures and functions, using GDB with OpenOCD and Ghidra, with Binary Ninja and IDA versions of the notebooks from week 4; an I2C LCD, GPS, LoRa radio, servo and IR remote supply the exercises. Graded CTFs, story-driven classified-brief challenges and a secure-boot custom bootloader ship as author-built binaries alongside their source. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/mytechnotalent-embedded-hacking/))
- [Emproof workshop: Firmware Reverse Engineering](https://github.com/emproof-com/workshop_firmware_reverse_engineering) — learning-resource · binary, firmware — Slides and 14 self-contained exercises from Emproof's firmware reverse-engineering workshop at ScapyCon Automotive 2025, designed by Tim Blazytko for a technical audience with little security experience. The firmware and binary tasks move from reading ELF metadata with binutils and first steps in Ghidra or Binary Ninja, through unpacking two embedded-Linux CTF images with binwalk, to bare-metal analysis: identifying the microcontroller, toolchain and memory map of a model car's firmware ELF, and rebasing a raw STM32F103 flash dump with segments taken from the datasheet. Other tasks decrypt strings statically in a Mirai sample and trace a hardcoded AES key back to its data section. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/emproof-firmware-re-workshop/))
- [Finding Jingle Town: Debugging an N64 Game without Symbols](https://blog.chrislewis.au/finding-jingle-town-debugging-an-n64-game-without-symbols/) — learning-resource · game — A walkthrough from the Snowboard Kids 2 decompilation project on attaching gdb-multiarch to the Ares emulator while the matching build still cannot carry debug information. It breaks on a function by its ELF symbol name and reads one structure byte by hand to test whether that byte selects the level overlay. A short method note on checking decompilation hypotheses at runtime, not a reconstruction result. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/finding-jingle-town-n64-debugging/))
- [FLARE Learning Hub](https://github.com/mandiant/flare-learning-hub) — learning-resource · binary — Free English reverse-engineering and malware-analysis modules from Mandiant's FLARE team, published as Google Docs, with lab binaries, IDA databases and scripts kept in this repository. The Malware Analysis Crash Course builds from x86 basics to reading Windows API use through assembly-patching exercises and labs with worked solutions; the other modules introduce WinDbg Time Travel Debugging for malware triage and provide a reference to how Go 1.24 programs look in Windows executables. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/flare-learning-hub/))
- [Frida Boot: a beginner binary instrumentation workshop](https://github.com/leonjza/frida-boot) — learning-resource · binary — A beginner workshop by leonjza on binary instrumentation with Frida, served as offline pages from a Docker container that has gdb with GEF and frida-tools preinstalled. Three chapters move from a small Linux C program, LD_PRELOAD hooks and how library calls resolve through the PLT and GOT, to Frida’s Interceptor for reading arguments and changing return values, NativeFunction for calling a program’s own functions, Python injector tools, RPC exports, TypeScript agents, frida-server and frida-gadget. It deliberately leaves out Frida’s mobile-app uses, and every exercise targets sample programs shipped with the workshop. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/frida-boot-workshop/))
- [Frida Labs](https://github.com/DERE-ad2001/Frida-Labs) — learning-resource · app — Eleven Android challenge apps with step-by-step write-ups for learning Frida instrumentation, progressing from setting up frida-server and hooking a Java method through calling static methods, changing fields, creating instances and hooking constructors to hooking, calling and patching native functions. The author presents them as beginner-to-intermediate exercises rather than hard CTF challenges: each app hides a flag, but the stated goal is learning Frida’s common APIs and being able to read scripts written by others. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/frida-labs/))
- [Frida Snippets](https://github.com/iddoeldor/frida-snippets) — learning-resource · app — A single large README that collects hand-written Frida instrumentation examples, grouped into Native, Android and iOS sections, for observing how a running app behaves. The examples show patterns such as attaching interceptors, enumerating loaded modules and classes, describing class members, tracing method arguments and return values, printing backtraces, logging SQLite queries and I/O, and scanning or dumping process memory. A scripts directory adds helper code, including Python generators that emit hook stubs from a decompiler and a frida-server install script, plus editor snippets for Sublime and Vim. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/frida-snippets/))
- [Frontkick vs Rikugan: a CREBench agent comparison](https://www.synacktiv.com/en/publications/ufc-666-frontkick-vs-rikugan) — learning-resource · binary — Matthieu Breuil’s Synacktiv article explains tool-calling and code-executing agent designs and describes Frontkick, a CodeAct agent that runs generated Python inside Binary Ninja. It compares Frontkick with the open-source Rikugan plugin on two CREBench cryptographic binary tasks, then attributes most of Frontkick’s advantage to a prompt that listed installed Python libraries rather than to its architecture. It is a small evaluation write-up with per-run results, not a released tool or a general benchmark finding. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/frontkick-rikugan-crebench-comparison/))
- [Ghidra Snippets](https://github.com/HackOvert/GhidraSnippets) — learning-resource · binary — A single-page collection of 30 commented Python examples for scripting Ghidra, grouped by task: projects, programs, functions and cross-references, basic blocks, instructions, stack variables, decompiler output, comments, P-code emulation and dumps, call graphs and program slices. It starts from the Flat Program, Flat Decompiler and Flat Debugger APIs, then shows where a task needs Ghidra's fuller API; most snippets include an example of their output. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ghidra-snippets/))
- [Hacking the Xbox — 日本語訳](https://github.com/Nico-Tech-Shenzhen/hacking-the-xbox-ja) — learning-resource · firmware — Hacking the Xbox — 日本語訳 publishes a Japanese translation of a book on Xbox reverse engineering. The repository includes translated introductory material and chapters 1–6 with figures; later chapters and appendices remain pending. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/hacking-the-xbox-ja/))
- [ICSim (Instrument Cluster Simulator)](https://github.com/zombieCraig/ICSim) — learning-resource · protocol — OpenGarages' Instrument Cluster Simulator for practising CAN bus reverse engineering on a Linux virtual CAN interface instead of a car. One SDL window draws a dashboard whose speedometer, turn signals and door-lock indicators react to the CAN frames it sniffs, while a separate controls program turns gamepad or keyboard input into those frames and replays a recorded sample of generic CAN traffic as background noise. In training mode the arbitration IDs and byte positions behind each signal are randomized from a seed shared by both programs, and the highest difficulty level adds further random data to the target messages, so learners have to find the relevant messages themselves. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/icsim/))
- [iOS App Reverse Engineering](https://github.com/iosre/iOSAppReverseEngineering) — learning-resource · app — Freely published 2015 book by snakeninny and hangcom on reverse engineering iOS apps, distributed in its repository as a 442-page English PDF. Its four parts cover the iOS filesystem and file types; OS X and on-device tools such as class-dump, Theos, Reveal, IDA, Cycript and LLDB with debugserver; an Objective-C and ARM assembly methodology for locating the code behind a feature; and four worked practices that locate the relevant code in Notes, Mail, WeChat and Messages and then extend those apps with tweaks. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ios-app-reverse-engineering-book/))
- [iOSRE: iOS Reverse Engineering reference](https://github.com/kpwn/iOSRE) — learning-resource · app, binary, firmware — A community-maintained reference on iOS reverse engineering started by kpwn, built from a wiki, a curated index of public technical papers and talks, and a few environment-setup scripts. The wiki pages introduce iOS internals such as the Mach-O executable format, the XNU kernel and IOKit, the boot chain and a history of platform mitigations, while the README states the project is an early, ongoing effort and several pages remain stubs. The repository hosts notes and external links rather than any Apple firmware, device images or app binaries. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/kpwn-iosre/))
- [JPCERT/CC: APK analysis and API probing on attack servers](https://blogs.jpcert.or.jp/ja/2026/10/web_api_attacks.html) — learning-resource · app — JPCERT/CC’s 15 October 2026 blog post on the infrastructure behind a series of unauthorized-access incidents in Japan. It reports that the attackers used AI for known-vulnerability scanning and API attacks, and that attack servers held APKs, jadx- and Blutter-related files, APK-extraction directories and an SSRF test script used to probe app APIs. It is a defender’s account of observed attacker tooling with mitigation advice, not a published AI-assisted analysis method or reproducible workflow. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/jpcert-apk-api-attack-infrastructure-report/))
- [Link Pi ENCx encoders (unofficial documentation)](https://github.com/matiaspl/LinkPi-ENCx) — learning-resource · firmware — A single-README notebook, kept since 2021 by an owner with no ties to the vendor, on the internals of LinkPi ENC-series video encoders and the TBS 2603SE, which share one firmware line; the author's first units use the F3520D board with a HiSilicon Hi3520DV400. It covers the Linux system and PHP web interface, undocumented features and internal pages, intercom and tally interfaces, default accounts and network services with hardening steps, HiSilicon media-pipeline diagnostics, board pinouts including the UART, firmware recovery, and translated release notes for firmware updates from 2020 to 2026. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/linkpi-encx-unofficial-docs/))
- [linux-re-101](https://github.com/michalmalik/linux-re-101) — learning-resource · binary — Michal Malik’s single-README reading list for Linux reverse engineering, with about 230 links and book titles that run from quick references on system calls, ELF and processor ABIs through 101 and 201 tiers on program start-up, linking and relocations. Later sections gather material on packers and obfuscation, anti-debugging, ELF infection, kernel modules and rootkits, exploitation, crackmes, radare2 walkthroughs, detection tools and books. It is a lightly annotated link list last changed in January 2022, useful as a background syllabus rather than a maintained course. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/linux-re-101/))
- [LLM4Decompile (EMNLP 2024)](https://aclanthology.org/2024.emnlp-main.203/) — research · binary — Research on direct binary decompilation and refinement of Ghidra output with language models. Evaluates re-executability on HumanEval and ExeBench; useful for distinguishing readable output from functional recovery. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/llm4decompile-paper/))
- [Malware Analysis & CTI Reports](https://github.com/Spellskite-coding/Binary_Analysis) — collection · binary — Malware Analysis & CTI Reports collects ten PDF investigations of malware, security tools and binaries with disputed detections. The author describes static inspection with Detect It Easy, strings and imports, followed by manual disassembly or decompilation in Ghidra or radare2. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/malware-analysis-cti-reports/))
- [MCP Reversing Dataset (mrexodia)](https://github.com/mrexodia/mcp-reversing-dataset) — learning-resource · binary — Three worked examples by mrexodia of a language model reverse-engineering crackme binaries in IDA Pro through his IDA Pro MCP server. Each folder holds the input binary, a README giving its origin, the prompt and the model, the agent's own report and the exported session transcript, so readers can follow every tool call, rename, retype and comment the agent made on the database. The tasks cover a small Linux password check, an obfuscated Linux flag checker and a Windows crackme that, according to the agent's report, routes its checks through vectored exception handling. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/mcp-reversing-dataset/))
- [Nintendo Switch Reverse Engineering](https://github.com/dekuNukem/Nintendo_Switch_Reverse_Engineering) — learning-resource · firmware, protocol — Notes begun by dekuNukem in 2017 and extended by contributors on how the Joy-Con, Pro Controller and parts of the Switch console and dock work, worked out from teardowns, logic-analyzer captures and packet logs. Markdown files cover the Joy-Con board test points and rail-connector pinout, the wired serial handshake with its CRC-8 checksum, Bluetooth HID input and output reports with 32 documented subcommands, the USB HID commands of the Pro Controller and charging grip, the SPI flash memory map with stick and 6-axis calibration, IMU conversion formulas and rumble encoding tables. The repository also keeps STM32 test firmware that plays the console or a Joy-Con on the rail, a Wireshark dissector for the Bluetooth traffic, and the captures behind the notes. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/nintendo-switch-joycon-re-notes/))
- [OpenSecurityTraining2](https://ost2.fyi/) — learning-resource · binary — Free self-paced security courses with learning paths for reverse engineering, firmware and systems foundations. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ost2/))
- [osx & ios re 101](https://github.com/michalmalik/osx-re-101) — learning-resource · app, binary — A single-README reading list for macOS (OS X) and iOS reverse engineering, kept by Michal Malik from 2015 to 2019 and labelled a work in progress. Its 98 entries in nine sections run from must-read blogs and Mach-O, ABI and system-call references through Mach-O basics, malware, anti-debugging and obfuscation write-ups, kernel, sandbox and jailbreak research, kernel-extension development and kernel debugging, and forensics and monitoring material, to crackmes and six books. Entries are bare links or titles, mostly with no annotation. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/osx-re-101/))
- [OWASP Firmware Security Testing Methodology (FSTM)](https://scriptingxss.gitbook.io/firmware-security-testing-methodology/) — learning-resource · firmware — A free English methodology, maintained by Aaron Guzman and published on GitBook, for security testing and reverse engineering embedded-device firmware. It is organized as nine stages: information gathering, obtaining firmware, analyzing the image, extracting the filesystem, static analysis of its contents, emulation, dynamic testing, runtime analysis and binary exploitation, with example commands for tools such as binwalk, unblob, FACT, EMBA, Firmwalker, QEMU and Ghidra. An October 2025 revision added C/C++ static-analysis tools, SBOM generation and a mapping to the OWASP IoT Security Verification Standard (ISVS). ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/owasp-fstm/))
- [OWASP Mobile Application Security Testing Guide (MASTG)](https://mas.owasp.org/MASTG/) — learning-resource · app — OWASP’s open manual for Android and iOS app security testing and reverse engineering, published at mas.owasp.org. Chapters on platform internals, test setup and app tampering and reverse engineering sit beside modular, individually referenced tests, techniques, tool pages, knowledge articles, best practices and runnable demos, all mapped to MASVS controls and MASWE weaknesses. OWASP’s UnCrackable crackme apps serve as practice targets and examples. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/owasp-mastg/))
- [Practical Malware Analysis](https://nostarch.com/malware) — learning-resource · binary — No Starch Press book, published in February 2012, that teaches hands-on malware analysis from basic static and dynamic techniques in virtual machines through x86 disassembly with IDA Pro, debugging with OllyDbg and kernel debugging with WinDbg. Later chapters cover malicious Windows programs, covert launching, data encoding, network signatures, anti-disassembly, anti-debugging and anti-VM techniques, unpacking, shellcode, C++ and 64-bit malware, and hands-on labs with solutions have readers dissect malware samples. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/practical-malware-analysis/))
- [pwn.college: Reverse Engineering](https://pwn.college/program-security/reverse-engineering/) — learning-resource · binary — Hands-on reverse-engineering module covering functions, data access, static tools and dynamic tools with hosted exercises. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/pwn-college-re/))
- [RE-Workstation](https://github.com/SUNYT772/RE-Workstation) — learning-resource · app, binary, firmware, website — RE-Workstation is a Chinese reference library for reverse-engineering workflows and related security research. Its topic pages cover native binaries, APKs, firmware, JavaScript, patch diffing and malware analysis, with indexes, evidence templates and pattern references for locating relevant methods. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/re-workstation/))
- [ReSym (CCS 2024)](https://doi.org/10.1145/3658644.3670340) — research · binary — Combines language models and program analysis to recover variable and user-defined data-structure symbols from stripped binaries. The authors provide training, inference, evaluation, and posterior-reasoning artifacts. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/resym-paper/))
- [Reverse Engineering (mytechnotalent)](https://github.com/mytechnotalent/Reverse-Engineering) — learning-resource · binary, firmware — A free, English lesson index by mytechnotalent that grows almost daily: 490 numbered lessons, most of which have learners write small programs and then debug and patch ("hack") them, across x86, x64, 32/64-bit ARM, RISC-V and AVR. Early courses start from number systems, registers, memory and the stack; later ones cover microcontroller firmware on Raspberry Pi Pico/Pico 2, STM32F401, ESP32-C3, ATmega328P and micro:bit boards, plus Windows, Go, Rust and WebAssembly programs. The README is a table of links; the lesson text lives in a GitBook and in the author's other repositories and PDF books. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/mytechnotalent-reverse-engineering/))
- [Reverse Engineering for Beginners](https://beginners.re/) — learning-resource · binary — Dennis Yurichev's free book on understanding compiled programs and assembly, available in multiple languages. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/re-for-beginners/))
- [Reverse Engineering iOS Applications](https://github.com/ivRodriguezCA/RE-iOS-Apps) — learning-resource · app, binary — Ivan Rodriguez's free English course (version 1.1) on reverse engineering iOS applications, aimed at beginners in iOS app security. Five Markdown modules cover setting up a jailbroken test device, decrypting App Store apps for analysis, static analysis of an IPA's files, frameworks, Objective-C classes and ARM binary in Hopper or Ghidra, runtime analysis with Cycript and Frida, and binary patching in Hopper. Exercises use CoinZa, a deliberately insecure Objective-C app the author built from vulnerability classes he reports finding in production App Store apps. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/re-ios-apps-course/))
- [Reverse Engineering Reading List](https://github.com/onethawt/reverseengineering-reading-list) — learning-resource · binary — A short reading list, compiled by One Thawt mostly in 2015–2016, of about 58 links to reverse-engineering tools, books, papers, talks and blog posts, kept in a README and a separate malware-analysis page. Sections run from disassemblers and debuggers, beginner talks and classic books through data-structure recovery papers, instruction-set references, iOS, Android and Windows material, unpacking, deobfuscation and protocol inference from network traces. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/onethawt-re-reading-list/))
- [Reverse-Engineering a Bluetooth-Enabled Radar Detector](https://github.com/brandonasuncion/Reverse-Engineering-Bluetooth-Protocols) — learning-resource · protocol — Write-up of how the author decoded the Bluetooth RFCOMM protocol of a Cobra iRAD 900 radar detector so that a Raspberry Pi 3 could receive alerts without the iOS or Android app. Traffic was logged on a jailbroken iPhone with BTServer and filtered in Wireshark; the README then documents the detector's half-second status payload and the 9-byte reply whose ACK byte must echo the detector's sequence byte, and replays recorded responses to the initial settings sync instead of decoding it. A short beginner's case study on RFCOMM traffic analysis with a rough proof-of-concept script, not a maintained client. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/cobra-irad-bluetooth-protocol-walkthrough/))
- [RPISEC Malware Analysis course (CSCI 4976)](https://github.com/RPISEC/Malware) — learning-resource · binary — Materials from the student-run Malware Analysis course that RPISEC taught at Rensselaer Polytechnic Institute in Fall 2015, built around the book Practical Malware Analysis. Twelve lectures run from basic static and dynamic analysis, x86 and IDA through Windows internals, malware behavior, data encoding, covert launching, anti-disassembly, anti-VM and anti-debugging, and packing and unpacking to the Windows kernel and rootkit techniques, with ten labs and four projects that include runtime process manipulation, unpacking automation and an APT sample analysis. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/rpisec-malware-analysis-course/))
- [Supplementary AST-Based Deobfuscation Materials](https://github.com/SteakEnthusiast/Supplementary-AST-Based-Deobfuscation-Materials) — learning-resource · website — Companion code for the ReverseJS blog's 2022 series on deobfuscating JavaScript by manipulating abstract syntax trees with Babel. Each folder pairs a small obfuscated sample with a deobfuscator script and its output, covering string concealing (escape sequences, concatenation, string arrays and string encryption), constant folding including JSFuck-style code, substitution of constant variables, bracket-to-dot property access, dead-code removal, proxy functions and sequence expressions. The README presents them as short learning examples rather than a production deobfuscator. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/reversejs-ast-deobfuscation-materials/))
- [The Decompilation Wiki](https://decompilation.wiki/) — learning-resource · binary, game — Community wiki explaining how decompilers work, from disassembly, lifting and control-flow recovery to type recovery, control-flow structuring, quality evaluation and neural decompilation, citing the research behind each technique. It also keeps directories of decompilers, plugins, blogs and talks, pages on applied research such as symbol recovery, code similarity and vulnerability discovery, and a table of about 240 game reconstruction projects by original platform. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/decompilation-wiki/))
- [The Official Radare2 Book](https://book.rada.re/) — learning-resource · binary — Official practical reference for radare2 analysis, disassembly, scripting, debugging and related plugins. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/radare2-book/))
- [tiny-dec](https://github.com/ZhangZhuoSJTU/tiny-dec) — learning-resource · binary — Zhuo Zhang's small educational decompiler for 32-bit RISC-V (RV32I) ELF files, written in Python to teach how decompilation works rather than to compete with production decompilers. Its 19 deliberately separate stages run from ELF loading, decoding and p-code lifting through SSA, stack, type, variable and range recovery to control-flow structuring and C output, and the CLI can stop at any stage to print that stage's intermediate result. The README also offers a Claude Code plugin whose AI tutor walks learners through the stages with quizzes and exercises. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/tiny-dec/))
- [Windows Malware Analysis training, volume 1 (malware_training_vol1)](https://github.com/hasherezade/malware_training_vol1) — learning-resource · binary — Slides and exercises for a Windows malware analysis training that builds an understanding of common malware techniques through programming, reverse engineering and Windows internals, and shows how analysis tools are used for particular goals. Module 1 covers compilation, the PE format, processes, WoW64, shellcode and PE loaders; module 2 introduces malware missions and tactics, hooking, persistence and banking trojans; module 3 introduces evasion and self-defence and fingerprinting, with obfuscation, anti-hooking, deobfuscation and kernel-mode components planned. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/malware-training-vol1/))
- [Z0FCourse Reverse Engineering](https://github.com/0xZ0F/Z0FCourse_ReverseEngineering) — learning-resource · binary — A free English course by Z0F on reverse engineering 64-bit Windows programs, which its README pitches as taking learners from beginner to intermediate. Markdown lessons move from number systems, x64 registers, memory layout and calling conventions to x64dbg and Ghidra, then reverse small samples and an author-written DLL, recovering a Player class and calling the DLL's exports from a program of one's own. The unfinished later chapters introduce Windows internals and reverse ntdll's generic-table functions. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/z0f-reverse-engineering-course/))

<a id="related-lists"></a>
## Related resource lists

- [AI Rebuilds](https://ai-rebuilds.com/) — collection · app, binary, game — A website and GitHub list of 81 projects that rebuild existing software or games without the original source: AI-built clean-room apps such as the ArtCraft suite, software rewritten with coding agents, AI-assisted game decompilations, game engine reimplementations, human-led decompilations and ports, clean-room classics such as Wine and ReactOS, and tools for rebuilders. Each entry says what it rebuilds and carries a method label and an AI-involvement label, and the site can be searched, filtered and sorted by stars or recent activity. A separate guide describes a clean-room rebuild workflow with REA. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/ai-rebuilds-directory/))
- [Awesome AI Reverse Engineering](https://github.com/darbra/awesome-ai-reverse) — collection · app, binary, website — A single-README, Chinese-language list of AI-assisted reverse-engineering projects, grouped into JavaScript reversing, binary and native analysis, Android and iOS security, and browser automation and traffic analysis. Its entries are mostly MCP servers, agent skills for Claude, Cursor and Codex, and IDE plugins that connect large language models to tools such as IDA Pro, Ghidra, Binary Ninja, JADX and Frida. Each row is a project name, a link and a one-line Chinese blurb. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/awesome-ai-reverse/))
- [Awesome Android Reverse Engineering](https://github.com/user1342/Awesome-Android-Reverse-Engineering) — collection · app — An awesome list of Android reverse-engineering training, tools and resources started in 2023 by James Stevenson, with 92 one-line entries, 16 of them starred as great and up to date. It covers courses, videos and books; static analysis and deobfuscation, networking and Frida-based instrumentation, decompilers and malware analysis tools; documentation and case studies; CTFs and crackmes; obfuscation and anti-reversing; and a short firmware and kernel section. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/awesome-android-reverse-engineering/))
- [Awesome CAN Bus](https://github.com/iDoka/awesome-canbus) — collection · firmware, protocol — A single-README list of CAN bus tools, hardware and resources for automotive and embedded work, grouped into sections for reverse-engineering tools, test equipment and simulators, higher-layer protocols (OBD-II, UDS, ISO-TP, J1939, J2534), general and Linux utilities, CAN database formats (DBC, KCD) with converters, and hardware for ARM, Arduino, ESP32, single-board computers and FPGA cores. Each entry links out to an external project or resource with a short description. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/awesome-canbus/))
- [Awesome CAN ID](https://github.com/iDoka/awesome-automotive-can-id) — collection · protocol — A single-README list that gathers links to CAN message identifier and payload decodings in one place, grouped by vehicle make and model under about 30 car brands and brand groups plus BMW, Ducati and KTM motorcycles. Its links lead to DBC files, decoding spreadsheets, wiki pages, sniffing notes and small libraries maintained elsewhere, after a Common section of general databases such as comma.ai’s opendbc and the OpenGarages CAN ID references. ([entry](https://awesomerea.org/resources/awesome-automotive-can-id/))
