- [No More Gotos: Decompilation Using Pattern-Independent Control-Flow Structuring and Semantics-Preserving Transformations](https://www.ndss-symposium.org/ndss2015/ndss-2015-programme/no-more-gotos-decompilation-using-pattern-independent-control-flow-structuring-and-semantics/) (NDSS 2015, 2015) — DREAM is a decompiler whose control-flow structuring recovers loops, conditionals and switch statements from a recovered control-flow graph without matching predefined region patterns. For acyclic regions it computes each node's reaching condition, then refines the resulting conditions into if-then-else and switch constructs; cyclic regions are structured as loops whose type and continuation condition are inferred. Semantics-preserving transformations first rewrite cyclic regions with multiple entries or successors into single-entry, single-successor regions, and a Boolean variable keeps a repeated test equivalent when its operands may change. On GNU coreutils the authors report no goto statements, against thousands from Hex-Rays and Phoenix, and more compact code than Hex-Rays for 72.7% of functions and than Phoenix for 98.8% in one-to-one comparisons.
- [Reverse Engineering Mobile Application User Interfaces with REMAUI (T)](https://ieeexplore.ieee.org/abstract/document/7372013) (ASE 2015, 2015) · [code](https://github.com/soumikmohianuta/pixtoapp) — Reconstructs native Android UI code from screenshots or design bitmaps using OCR, computer vision, and layout heuristics; evaluates visual similarity and view hierarchies on 488 screenshots from over 100 Android and iOS applications.
- [Avatar: A Framework to Support Dynamic Security Analysis of Embedded Systems’ Firmwares](https://www.ndss-symposium.org/ndss2014/ndss-2014-programme/avatar-framework-support-dynamic-security-analysis-embedded-systems-firmwares/) (NDSS 2014, 2014) · [code](https://github.com/avatarone/avatar-python) — Avatar runs embedded firmware in an instrumented emulator, S²E on QEMU, while forwarding memory-mapped I/O to the real device and injecting the device's interrupts back into the emulator, so firmware that depends on undocumented peripherals can still be analyzed dynamically. The device is reached through JTAG via OpenOCD or through a small debugging stub over a serial line. Because forwarding every access over a 38400-baud serial link is very slow, about five instructions per second in one of the authors' measurements, Avatar also keeps memory that never behaves like I/O local to the emulator, migrates selected code to run natively on the device, and switches state between the two. With S²E's selective symbolic execution, the authors probe a hard-disk mask-ROM bootloader for hidden commands, an IEEE 802.15.4 sensor node for an injected overflow, and the SMS decoding routines of a GSM feature phone.
- [Towards Automated Protocol Reverse Engineering Using Semantic Information](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/2590296.2590346) (ACM ASIA CCS 2014, 2014) · [code](https://github.com/netzob/netzob) — Infers the vocabulary of an undocumented protocol, its message types and formats, from communication traces. The approach uses contextual information embedded in the messages to improve message clustering and the identification of field boundaries; the HAL record lists contextual clustering and semantic sequence alignment as keywords. The authors motivate the work with parsers and traffic generators for network security products, and compare it with their own reimplementations of ASAP, Discoverer and ScriptGen. The first two authors are the authors of Netzob, the open-source protocol reverse-engineering framework.
- [Understanding JavaScript Event-Based Interactions](https://doi.org/10.1145/2568225.2568268) (ICSE 2014, 2014) · [code](https://github.com/saltlab/clematis/tree/7b2479a09721699eaaa24f6e258df5e2beb35d0a) — Clematis helps developers understand a JavaScript web application's runtime behavior from a recorded user session. A proxy instruments JavaScript on the fly to log DOM events, timeouts, XMLHttpRequest phases, function calls and DOM mutations. The trace is grouped into episodes, each with its triggering event, executed functions and resulting DOM changes, linked by temporal and causal edges and shown in an interactive visualization. In an industrial study with 20 developers on the Phormer photo gallery, the authors report 61% higher task accuracy and 47% less completion time than developers using their preferred tools.
- [Guided GUI Testing of Android Apps with Minimal Restart and Approximate Learning](https://doi.org/10.1145/2509136.2509552) (OOPSLA 2013, 2013) · [code](https://github.com/wtchoi/SwiftHand/tree/b2b45acd636ab63beb0969401d1ee2c77fa0530d) — SwiftHand generates test inputs for Android apps while learning an approximate GUI model during testing. It instruments Dalvik bytecode to read the GUI component tree and derive each screen's enabled touch, scroll, text, Back and Menu inputs; screens with the same enabled inputs are treated as equivalent states of an extended deterministic labeled transition system. The learned model steers execution toward unexplored frontier states and avoids restarts, since reinstalling an app took about 30 seconds. On 10 F-Droid apps, the authors report higher branch coverage than random and L*-based testing within the time budget, reached faster.
- [Crawling Ajax-Based Web Applications through Dynamic Analysis of User Interface State Changes](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/2109205.2109208) (ACM Transactions on the Web, 2012) · [code](https://github.com/crawljax/crawljax) — Crawljax infers a state-flow graph of an Ajax web application by driving it in a real browser. It scans the DOM for candidate clickable elements, fires events on them, compares the resulting DOM against known states with configurable comparison and similarity thresholds, and records new states and the event transitions between them, backtracking by reloading and replaying paths. A concurrent variant partitions the crawl across browser instances. The Java tool uses Selenium WebDriver and plugins; one plugin writes a static mirror of the crawled states. The authors evaluate six open-source and industrial sites and measure how the number of concurrent browsers affects crawling runtime on Google AdSense.
- [Prospex: Protocol Specification Extraction](https://ieeexplore.ieee.org/document/5207640) (IEEE S&P 2009, 2009) — Prospex infers message formats and a protocol state machine from execution traces of a Linux server handling client sessions. Dynamic taint analysis splits each session into messages and recovers their formats; messages are then clustered into types by input-format, execution and server-impact similarity, such as the system calls, outputs and file paths a message triggers. The observed type sequences form an augmented prefix tree acceptor, which prerequisite and end-state heuristics label before Exbar computes a minimal consistent automaton. The resulting specifications are translated for the Peach fuzzer to reach later protocol states. The evaluation covers the Agobot command-and-control protocol, SMTP on Sendmail, SMB on Samba and SIP on Asterisk.
- [Tupni: Automatic Reverse Engineering of Input Formats](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/1455770.1455820) (ACM CCS 2008, 2008) — Tupni infers an input format from an execution trace of an x86 program parsing one input, using byte-level dynamic taint tracking. It segments the input into fields from the instructions that operate on tainted bytes, finds record sequences from parsing loops, groups records into types by the code that handles them, and emits an enhanced BNF grammar with length, value and some inter-message constraints. Grammars from several inputs can be merged into a more complete specification. The evaluation covers five file formats (WMF, BMP, JPG, PNG, TIF) and five network protocols (DNS, RPC, TFTP, HTTP, FTP), and the output is used for zero-day vulnerability signature generation with ShieldGen.
- [Polyglot: Automatic Extraction of Protocol Message Format using Dynamic Binary Analysis](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/1315245.1315286) (ACM CCS 2007, 2007) — Polyglot infers the format of a message that a program receives by monitoring how the program binary processes it, an approach the authors call shadowing. An execution monitor taints network input and records an instruction trace; offline analyses then find direction fields, such as length and pointer fields, from tainted memory accesses and loop conditions, separators from comparisons between tainted bytes and constants, multi-byte fixed-length fields from how instructions group input bytes, and protocol keywords from successful comparisons. It works on stripped IA-32 binaries without source code or debugging information.
- [GUI ripping: reverse engineering of graphical user interfaces for testing](https://ieeexplore.ieee.org/document/1287256) (WCRE 2003, 2003) — GUI ripping reverse engineers a test model from a running desktop application rather than from its source code or specifications. Starting from the top-level windows, a ripper opens each child window by programmatically clicking executable widgets, records every window’s widgets, properties and values, and builds a GUI forest of windows. From that forest it derives event-flow graphs, which record which events can follow one another within a component, and an integration tree of component invocations. The paper describes rippers for Microsoft Windows applications, using window handles and the Windows API, and for Java Swing applications, using Java’s AWT and Swing APIs. It applies them to WordPad, Notepad, Yahoo Messenger and four applications of the TerpOffice student office suite, with the models feeding automated test generation in the authors’ GUITAR and DART systems.
